# Adult Hip Dysplasia

*Recon · 1 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — abnormal hip development → **shallow acetabulum**, deficient **anterior + lateral** coverage

**Incidence** — US **3–5%** · **~10% of all THA** done for dysplasia · **48% of THA in patients <50 yr** are for dysplasia

**Most common cause of THA <50 yr** (excluding osteonecrosis) — **DDH**\
✗ Wrong options: FAI · SCFE · Perthes/MED · femoral neck fracture

**Demographics** — **females > males** (2–4× relative risk)

**Risk factors** — **breech**, **female**, **primiparity**, **family history**

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — undercoverage → load concentrated on **superolateral acetabular rim** → ↑ contact stress → **degenerative labral tear**, cartilage delamination → **secondary OA**

**Labrum** — **hypertrophied**, intrasubstance tearing (labrum stabilises the hip → overloaded)

**Arthritis timing** — untreated → THA typically in **30s–40s**

### Anatomic abnormalities

| | Dysplastic hip |
|---|---|
| **Acetabulum** | **Shallow, lateralized, anteverted** · deficient **anteriorly + superiorly** (superolateral) · may be **retroverted** with superolateral bone loss (OB) |
| **Femoral head** | **Small**, aspherical |
| **Femoral neck** | **Short**, **↑ anteversion** (+5° to +16°, rises with subluxation) · **coxa valga** (↑ neck-shaft angle) |
| **Greater trochanter** | **Posteriorly placed** |
| **Femoral canal** | **Small, narrow, straight** · thick cortices |
| **Contact area** | **↓ head–acetabulum contact area** |

**"Most common abnormalities"** → ✓ **excessive femoral anteversion + posterior greater trochanter**\
✗ Wrong options: medialized acetabulum · large contact area · large head/long neck · ↓ neck-shaft angle

**Femoral concern before THA** → ✓ **excessive anteversion**\
✗ Wrong options: osteopenia · varus · bowing · stove-pipe femur

**Rotational deformity site** — **diaphysis between lesser trochanter and isthmus**\
✗ Wrong options: metaphyseal torsion

**Normal values** — acetabulum **anteversion 15°, abduction 45°** · femoral neck anteversion **15°** · neck-shaft angle **125°** · head centre at level of **greater trochanter**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — insidious **groin/hip pain** · worse with hip flexion or ER in weight-bearing · **lateral hip pain + limp / Trendelenburg** (abductor fatigue)

**Exam** — ↑ **internal rotation in flexion** (↑ femoral anteversion) · generalized laxity (**Beighton**) · ± FADIR pain (labral)

**Anterior apprehension sign** — lateral decubitus, hip extended, progressive **ER + adduction** → apprehension/pain

**Prone external rotation test** — anterior force on posterior greater trochanter

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP pelvis**, lateral, **false-profile view**

**Findings** — femoral head **asphericity** · **coxa valga** · **narrow femoral canal**

**CT** — preoperative planning only (ordered by treating surgeon) · include **distal femur** if clinical anteversion · canal diameter **over-estimated on AP, under-estimated on lateral**

![Lateral centre-edge angle of Wiberg](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5008-lcea.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – radiographic parameters

| Measure | View | Dysplastic | Borderline | Normal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LCEA (Wiberg)** — vertical through head centre vs line to lateral acetabular edge | **AP** | **<20°** | **20–25°** | **25–39°** |
| **Tönnis angle** (acetabular roof / sourcil inclination) | **AP** | **>10°** | — | **<10°** |
| **ACEA (Lequesne)** — anterior coverage | **False-profile** | **<20°** | — | **25–40°** · **>40° = FAI** |
| **FEAR index** | AP | — | — | **<5° = stable hip, no treatment** |
| **Extrusion index** (uncovered head / head width) | AP | **>27%** suggests dysplasia (TO) | — | — |

**LCEA cut-off variants** — Orthobullets **<20° dysplastic**, OB **<25° dysplastic** (LCEA or ACEA) · borderline/mild dysplasia **18–25°** (OB) · Wiberg (TO): **>25° normal, 20–25° uncertain, <20° pathological** · CE angle reliable only **>5 yr** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: <12 yr

**Tönnis angle = acetabular index / acetabular roof angle** — used in **hip dysplasia** (TO) · line teardrop-to-teardrop + line from medial to lateral edge of **sourcil** (weight-bearing subchondral condensation)\
✗ Wrong options: genu varum · Blount · recurvatum

**Paediatric acetabular index** — Hilgenreiner's line + acetabular surface · **<25° after 6 months** (TO)

**Other TO angles** — **MCE** (head centre → medial sourcil) · **acetabular arc** = LCE + MCE

### Acetabular version signs (TO)

**Normal** — slight anteversion · **anterior wall projects medial** to posterior wall · posterior wall descends **through femoral head centre**

**Retroversion** — **anterior wall lateral to posterior wall** · signs: **crossover sign**, **posterior wall sign**, **ischial spine sign** (spine protrudes into true pelvis) · also narrow ischium, laterally protruding iliac wing

**Crossover sign** — anterior rim crosses posterior wall below superolateral margin → **anterior overcoverage OR whole-acetabulum retroversion OR posterior deficiency — any alone or combined** (TO)

**Posterior wall sign** — posterior wall runs **medial to head centre** → posterior deficiency

**Posterior wall lateral to head centre** — prominent posterior wall → **excessive anteversion**

![Posterior wall sign](../../MCQ/attachments/q219941-illc-posterior-wall-sign.jpg)

### Coxa profunda vs protrusio (TO)

| | Coxa profunda | Protrusio acetabuli |
|---|---|---|
| Relation to **ilioischial line** | **Acetabular fossa** medial to line, head is **not** | **Femoral head** crosses medial to line |
| Meaning | Overcoverage **or normal variant (women)** | Excess depth, overcoverage, **pincer** |

### Crowe classification

| Grade | Proximal displacement (% pelvic height) | Femoral head subluxation |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **<10%** | **<50%** of head vertical diameter (head–neck junction from inter-teardrop line) |
| **II** | **10–15%** | **50–75%** |
| **III** | **15–20%** | **75–100%** |
| **IV** | **>20%** | **>100%** (high dislocation) |

![Crowe classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5008-crowe-classification.jpg)

### Hartofilakidis classification

| Type | Head position | Acetabulum |
|---|---|---|
| **A — Dysplasia** | Within acetabulum despite subluxation | **Segmental superior wall deficiency** · inadequate depth |
| **B — Low dislocation** | **False acetabulum** superior to true | **Superior wall absent** · inadequate depth |
| **C — High dislocation** | Completely uncovered, migrated **superior + posterior** | **Complete deficiency** · excessive anteversion of true acetabulum |

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic adult hip dysplasia
├─ Concentric, congruent, joint space preserved → PAO (Bernese/Ganz)
│      └─ Retroverted acetabulum → reverse (anteverting) PAO
├─ Unreduced / incongruous joint → salvage (Chiari, shelf)
├─ Borderline/mild only → ± arthroscopy + labral repair + capsular plication
└─ End-stage OA → THA (cup in true acetabulum)
       └─ Lengthening >4 cm (Crowe IV) → + subtrochanteric shortening osteotomy
```

### Nonoperative

**Supportive** — weight loss, NSAIDs, activity modification, intra-articular injection · **limited role** (premature OA) · PT + steroid injection not for lasting relief in 20s

**No remodelling** — triradiate closes ~10 yr · at 18 yr **no pelvic growth left**\
✗ Wrong options: "continued remodelling (observe")

### Periacetabular osteotomy (PAO) indications

**Indication** — **symptomatic dysplasia**, adolescent/adult, **concentrically reduced hip + congruous joint space** · preserved ROM · **minimum 2 mm joint space** (OB) · best: **very low VCE 0–15°**, concentric through ROM

**Typical stem** — young woman, LCEA ~15°, ACEA ~14–18°, Tönnis 15°, labral tear, **no chondral lesion** → ✓ **Bernese PAO**\
✗ Wrong options: surgical dislocation + osteoplasty (for FAI) · proximal femoral osteotomy (paediatric/abnormal NSA) · Salter · THA (needs advanced OA)

**Intraop test** — need **≥90° flexion + 15° IR** after correction, otherwise add femoral osteotomy (prevent FAI)

**Advantages** — **hyaline cartilage coverage** · **posterior column intact → WBAT** · large multidirectional corrections · preserves external rotators · delays THA

**Outcome** — **92% survivorship at 15 yr** avoiding THA · **>90% at 10–15 yr** · **better long-term survivorship than arthroscopy** (✓) · retroversion series: 10-yr survivorship **PAO 79% vs rim trimming 23%**

**Secondary instability** — after arthroscopic labral debridement, **not after PAO**

**Technique** — **modified Smith-Petersen** approach · osteotomies of **pubis (superior pubic root), ilium, ischium** · **avoids posterior column** (fracture → pelvic discontinuity → plate)

**Later THA after PAO** — ↑ risk of **retroverted cup**

**Reverse (anteverting) PAO** — **acetabular retroversion**: ✓ **LCEA <20° + positive posterior wall sign**\
✗ Wrong options: LCEA 21° + negative PWS · LCEA 23° + Tönnis 8 · LCEA 30°

![Bernese periacetabular osteotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5008-ganz-osteotomy.jpg)

### Pelvic osteotomy descriptions

| Osteotomy | Description | Patient |
|---|---|---|
| **Bernese / Ganz PAO** | **Multiple cuts: pubis, ilium, ischium** near acetabulum ✓ | Skeletally mature |
| **Salter** | **Above acetabulum through ilium to sciatic notch** · opening wedge · hinges on **mobile symphysis / triradiate** | Immature |
| **Pemberton** | From **AIIS posteriorly** → ends at **ilioischial limb of triradiate** | Immature |
| **Dega** | Acetabular roof → triradiate · **incomplete** pericapsular cuts · **does not violate sciatic notch** · volume-reducing | **Neuromuscular** DDH |
| **Chiari** (salvage) | Cut **above acetabulum to sciatic notch**, **shift ilium laterally** · relies on **fibrocartilage metaplasia** · **shortens limb** | Incongruous joint |
| **Staheli shelf** (salvage) | **Extra-articular buttress** of bone over subluxed head · fibrocartilage cover | Incongruous joint |

**Salvage outcome** — **84% survivorship at 17 yr** (advanced OA endpoint) · poor with advanced DDH, head asphericity

### Hip arthroscopy failure in dysplasia

**Role** — controversial · **adjunct to PAO** (chondral, labral, cam lesions; abandon PAO if major chondral damage) · **contraindicated in moderate–severe dysplasia** · alone only for **borderline/mild** (with **labral repair + capsular plication**)

**Prevalence** — dysplasia in **~13%** of hip arthroscopy patients (mostly borderline/mild)

**Outcome vs no dysplasia** — ✓ **greater failure rate** · **accelerated arthritis** (~⅓) · ↑ **femoral head subluxation/migration** · **reoperation ~½** · inferior function · **iatrogenic instability**\
✗ Wrong options: decelerated arthritis · ↓ subluxation · ↓ reoperation · greater improvement

**Missed DDH in FAI patients** → poor outcomes after arthroscopic osteoplasty

### Hip resurfacing

**Indication** — **Crowe I or II** · **cannot address LLD** · **10% revision at 6 yr** · higher revision/complications than general population · posterior approach · complication: **femoral neck fracture**

### THA in dysplasia – cup coverage, shortening osteotomy, nerve palsy

**Indication** — **end-stage OA** secondary to dysplasia ✓\
✗ Wrong options: hemiarthroplasty (acetabular arthritis) · redirectional osteotomies (best before arthritis)

**Cup goal** — **true acetabulum** (low + medial) · **small reamers 38–44 mm**, **small cup <50 mm** · no jumbo cup (reams away columns, medial wall blow-out)

**Coverage rule** — **uncemented cup OK if <30% uncovered** → ✓ accept **25% superolateral uncoverage** and proceed · insufficient coverage = **<60–70%** covered\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ abduction to 60° (↓ coverage, ↑ wear) · breach medial wall · elevate hip centre · cement augments

**Options for deficient coverage** — downsize cup · **medialize** · elevate hip centre · **trabecular metal / cement augments** · superolateral bone graft · tantalum revision shells

**Pseudoacetabulum** — in **ilium**, poor support · cup there → **loosening, malposition, ilium fracture** (avoid)

**High hip centre** — gains bony support but **↓ abductor tension, ↑ JRF, ↑ loosening, ↑ dislocation** · bone poorer higher in ilium

**Cemented cup** — PMMA poor in **shear** → high loosening (avoid)

**Bearing** — avoid **MoM** in young · **ceramic-on-PE** longest survivorship, fewest complications (OB)

**Femur** — **modular stems** correct rotational deformity · trochanteric osteotomy aids exposure in **Crowe III/IV**

### Femoral shortening & sciatic nerve palsy

**Risk** — restoring true hip centre in high dislocation → **over-lengthening** → **sciatic palsy (peroneal division)** → **foot drop** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: hip centre too inferior (concern is too superior) · over-medialization (over-lateralization is the concern) · acetabular fracture (less likely)

**Threshold** — lengthening **>4 cm** → palsy (Orthobullets, OB) · **2–4 cm** associated with palsy (OB)

**Muscle at risk** — ✓ **EHL** (peroneal)\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps (femoral) · lateral gastrocnemius/tibialis posterior/FHL (tibial) · adductor magnus · semitendinosus

**Prevention** — ✓ **subtrochanteric femoral shortening osteotomy** · also corrects **angular/rotational deformity** · obviates GT osteotomy · proximal + **diaphyseal-engaging** fit, no extra hardware · alternatively **downsize components**\
✗ Wrong options: upsizing

**Not preventive** — hip extension + knee flexion during exposure (helps exposure only) · **anterior approach** (no ↓ palsy; ↑ femoral nerve risk) · ↑ offset (does not change length) · navigation · ↑ medialization · anterior retractor placement · EMG · hamstring/quadriceps lengthening · GT osteotomy with progressive shortening (↑ nonunion, needs hardware)

![Crowe classification and reconstruction options](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5008-recon-options.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Sciatic palsy** | **10× ↑** incidence (**5–15%**) · lengthening **>4 cm** · peroneal division **80%** · only **35–40%** recover fully |
| **GT osteotomy nonunion** | **29%** · ↓ with **subtrochanteric osteotomy** + trochanteric advancement |
| **Dislocation** | **5–10%**, esp. **high hip centre** |
| **Loosening** | ↑ with **high hip centre** cup · Crowe III/IV revision rates > non-dysplastic |
| Others | Periprosthetic femur fracture · infection · neurovascular injury |

**Long-term** — higher THA revision rate in dysplastic hips · Crowe I–II short-term = primary OA

**Sciatic palsy risk factors** — **dysplasia**, revision, **female**, post-traumatic OA, **lengthening** · causes: posterior-approach trauma, stretch, haematoma, cement heat

## X vs Y

| | **Adult hip dysplasia** | **FAI** |
|---|---|---|
| LCEA | **<20°** | Normal/↑ (pincer) |
| ACEA | **<20°** | **>40°** = overcoverage |
| Labrum | **Hypertrophied**, rim overload | Crushed/sheared |
| Surgery | **PAO** | Arthroscopy / surgical dislocation + osteoplasty |
| Arthroscopy alone | **Fails → instability** | Indicated |

| | **PAO** | **Salvage (Chiari / shelf)** |
|---|---|---|
| Joint | **Concentric, congruous** | **Incongruous / unreduced** |
| Coverage | **Hyaline cartilage** | **Fibrocartilage** (metaplasia) |

## How it's asked

**"Young woman, LCEA 16°, Tönnis 15°, labral tear, cartilage intact — best surgery?"** → **Bernese PAO**\
✗ Wrong options: surgical dislocation + osteoplasty · Salter · THA

**"Which statement about osteotomies is true?"** → **Bernese = multiple cuts in pubis, ilium, ischium**\
✗ Wrong options: Salter from AIIS (that is Pemberton)

**"Dysplasia, 18 yr, labral tear — true statement?"** → **PAO long-term survivorship > arthroscopy**\
✗ Wrong options: secondary instability after PAO · remodelling continues · Dega for retroversion

**"Reverse PAO — imaging that led to decision?"** → **LCEA 18° + positive posterior wall sign**

**"Hip arthroscopy with dysplasia vs without?"** → **greater failure rate**

**"Most common femoral abnormality in DDH?"** → **excessive anteversion + posterior greater trochanter**

**"Femoral concern on THA templating in DDH?"** → **excessive anteversion**

**"Crowe IV THA — clinical scenario most likely?"** → **over-lengthening the extremity** (TO)

**"Crowe IV THA — nerve supply of which muscle at risk?"** → **EHL** (TO)

**"Foot drop after THA with large lengthening — what would have prevented it?"** → **subtrochanteric femoral shortening osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior approach · ↑ offset · upsizing stem

**"Template shows 25% superolateral uncoverage — next step?"** → **accept and proceed as templated** (<30% OK)

**"Crowe IV with pseudoacetabulum — preop planning?"** → **subtrochanteric osteotomy to avoid foot drop**\
✗ Wrong options: cup in pseudoacetabulum · jumbo cup · MoM · cemented cup

**"Most common cause of THA <50 excluding osteonecrosis?"** → **DDH**

**"52-yr-old with dysplasia and secondary OA?"** → **THA**\
✗ Wrong options: PAO · hemiarthroplasty

**"Positive crossover sign indicates?"** → **anterior overcoverage, retroversion or posterior deficiency — any alone or combined** (TO)

**"Medial acetabular fossa medial to ilioischial line, head not?"** → **coxa profunda** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: protrusio

**"CE angle of Wiberg — true?"** → **<20° abnormal** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: only reliable <12 yr · Hilgenreiner–Perkin angle

**"Tönnis angle used in?"** → **hip dysplasia** (TO)

# Adult Limb Deformity & Correction

*Recon · 2 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & aetiology

**Causes** — childhood growth impairment, metabolic bone disease, severe OA, **trauma / malunion / nonunion**

**Demographics** — **no gender predilection** · young → trauma / childhood growth abnormality · upper limb rare (elbow, wrist malreduction)

**Pathoanatomy** — altered anatomic axis → **mechanical axis deviation (MAD)** → abnormal contact pressure + ligament strain → degeneration → worsening alignment

**Childhood causes** — **Blount's**, focal fibrocartilaginous dysplasia, proximal tibial physeal injury, **Cozen's phenomenon**, SED, femoral anteversion, **LLD**

**Medical causes** — renal osteodystrophy, **rickets** (vitamin D deficient, hypophosphataemic), OI, CP, neurofibromatosis

**Site rule** — adult **genu varum → mostly tibial** (CORA proximal tibia) · adult **genu valgum → mostly femoral** (CORA distal femur)

### Anatomy

**Proximal femur** — neck-shaft angle **130 ± 7°** · anteversion **10 ± 7°** · **GT tip level with head centre** (start of anatomic femoral axis)

**Femoral anatomic vs mechanical axis** — differ by **5–7°** · tibia: **anatomic = mechanical** if no deformity

**Tibial tubercle** — **anterolateral** · triangular shaft

**sMCL** — just posterior to medial epicondyle → **6 cm** below joint line · LCL — posterior to lateral epicondyle → fibular head

**JLCA >2°** — **soft-tissue (ligamentous) laxity**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain by deformity** — **varus → medial knee pain** · **valgus → lateral knee pain**

**Exam** — coronal/sagittal deformity · **femoral anteversion → IR >70°** · residual ↑ external tibial torsion · LLD · hip/knee/ankle ROM, laxity

**Intermalleolar distance >8 cm** — **valgus** (OB)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **weight-bearing full-length AP + lateral** · **patellae facing directly anterior** (rotation) · tube **10 ft** away at knee level · plus WB views of each segment

**CT** — **rotational malalignment** · axial cuts for femoral version

**Mechanical axis (limb)** — **centre of femoral head → centre of ankle**

**Anatomic axes** — **bisect medullary canal** (femur from GT tip, exits just medial to distal femoral midpoint)

**MAD** — **lateral to knee centre = valgus** · **medial = varus**

**LLD** — **top of femoral head → centre of ankle**, compare sides · femur/tibia measured separately

![Mechanical vs anatomic axes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422974-mechanical-alignment-corrected.jpg)

### Normal joint orientation angles

| Angle | Axis | Normal |
|---|---|---|
| **aMPFA** | Anatomic | **84°** (80–89) |
| **aLDFA** | Anatomic | **81°** (79–83) |
| **mLPFA** | Mechanical | **90°** (85–90) |
| **mLDFA** | Mechanical | **87°** (85–90) |
| **MPTA** | Both | **87°** (85–90) |
| **LDTA** | Both | **89°** (86–92) |
| **JLCA** | — | **0–2°** (femoral/tibial joint lines parallel) |
| **PDFA** (sagittal) | — | **79–83°** |
| **PPTA** (sagittal) | — | **77–84°** |

**Notation** — lowercase **a** = anatomic · **m** = mechanical

![Mechanical joint orientation angles](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422974-mechanical-angles-2.jpg)

### Deformity analysis – CORA

**CORA** — intersection of **proximal + distal mechanical axes** when deformity is purely angular

**Angulation + translation** — CORA **moves** from the axis intersection

**Multi-level deformity** — unrecognized intermediate segment → CORA may lie **outside bone**

**Osteotomy not at CORA** — induces **translation** in addition to angulation

**Proximity rule** — deformity **closer to joint → greater effect** on that joint's angulation

**Correction order** — **angulation → translation → length → rotation**

![CORA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422974-cora.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – varus vs valgus by angle

| | Varus (femoral source) | Varus (tibial source) | Valgus (femoral source) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Threshold | **mLDFA >90°** or **aLDFA >83°** | **MPTA <85°** | **mLDFA <85°** or **aLDFA <79°** · **MPTA >90°** also valgus |
| MAD | Medial | Medial | **Lateral** |

**OB trap values** — LDFA **88° = normal** · LDFA **91° = slight varus** · MAD **5–7° from femoral anatomic axis = normal**

## 5 Management

```
Adult limb deformity
├─ Mild / minimal deficit / unfit → bracing (KAFO), poorly tolerated
├─ Varus, CORA tibia (MPTA <85°) → valgus HTO (MOWHTO or LCWHTO)
├─ Varus, CORA femur (mLDFA >90°) → valgus DFO (lateral closing wedge)
├─ Valgus >12°, CORA femur → varus DFO (lateral opening / medial closing)
├─ LLD >2 cm → gradual lengthening ~1 mm/day
└─ Multicompartment OA → TKA ± extra-articular osteotomy
```

### Nonoperative

**Indications** — subtle deformity within normal parameters · minimal functional deficit · unfit for surgery

**KAFO** — AFO + metal uprights + mechanical knee joint + two thigh bands · bracing **poorly tolerated** · little data on progression

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Derotational femoral osteotomy** | No absolute · **excess femoral anteversion with <10° ER** · patellar maltracking/instability · gait (difficulty running) |
| **Valgus-producing HTO** | Varus MAD + **MPTA <85°**, CORA in tibia · **no arthritis in other 2 compartments** · young, not TKA candidate |
| **Valgus-producing DFO** | Varus MAD + **mLDFA >90° / aLDFA >83°**, CORA in femur · **joint-line obliquity after HTO** in combined deformity |
| **Varus-producing DFO** | Valgus MAD + **mLDFA <85° / aLDFA <79°** · valgus **>12°** · **no medial compartment arthritis** · **age <50** · isolated **lateral** OA · relative: genu valgum with ↑ Q angle, MCL stress |
| **Gradual lengthening** | LLD **>2 cm** (not absolute) |
| **TKA** | Deformity with **multicompartment** arthritis |

### Derotational femoral osteotomy

**Sites** — proximal (intertrochanteric/subtrochanteric, blade/locking plate — **also corrects flexion/extension + varus/valgus**) · diaphyseal (IM nail) · distal supracondylar (medial subvastus, locking plate)

**Diaphyseal IM technique** — derotate to **45° ER + 45° IR** with patella in line/slightly ER relative to ASIS

**Outcome** — **all forms improve outcomes** · ideal correction debated · may alter coronal alignment

**Complications** — undercorrection · overcorrection → retroversion + **out-toeing** (rare)

### Valgus-producing tibial osteotomy (HTO)

| | **Medial opening wedge** | **Lateral closing wedge** |
|---|---|---|
| Approach | Anteromedial | Anterolateral |
| Steps | Biplanar guide · hinge pin medial → lateral just below lateral plateau · protect patellar tendon · pack wedge | **Resect anterior fibular head** · cut proximal to tuberosity · **medial cortex = hinge** · staples/plate |
| Slope | **↑ tibial slope** | **↓ tibial slope** |
| Correction | May **lose correction** over time | — |

**HTO outcome** — **90–95% excellent at 5 yr → 50–70% at 10 yr** · **15–25% need TKA within 10 yr** · worse: **smokers, age >60**, other-compartment OA, obesity, prior failed arthroscopy, insufficient correction

### Distal femoral osteotomy – indications & outcomes

**Valgus-producing DFO** — **lateral closing wedge** · K-wires aimed at far medial cortex just above MFC · **hinge through medial cortex without perforating** → intact medial cortex = ↑ axial + rotational stability

**Varus-producing DFO** — ✓ **lateral opening wedge** (leave **1–2 cm medial hinge**, spacer + lateral plate) **or medial closing wedge** (metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction, medial locking plate)\
✗ Wrong options: lateral closing / medial opening (those make valgus)

**Candidate for lateral opening wedge DFO** — ✓ **young (35-yr) runner, isolated lateral joint-line pain, mechanical axis through fibular head**\
✗ Wrong options: PF/anterior pain · tricompartmental OA → TKA · prior MPFL/TTT anterior pain · varus/medial pain

**37-yr-old, 15° valgus, early lateral OA, failed NSAIDs + unloader brace** → ✓ **lateral opening wedge DFO**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral closing DFO + MOWHTO (varus) · HTO (varus) · TKA (too young)

**Survival** — ✓ **15-yr survival ~50%** (failure **48.5%**) · high mid/long-term deterioration + conversion to TKA

**Better outcome** — **higher preoperative function**\
✗ Wrong options: lower function

**TKA after varus DFO** — **conventional components** if bone stock adequate\
✗ Wrong options: stemmed/constrained in most

![Medial opening wedge HTO](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422974-medial-opening-wedge-tibial-osteotomy.jpg)

### Gradual limb lengthening

**Distraction osteogenesis** — **latency 5–7 days**, then **~1 mm/day**

**Devices** — external fixator (uniplanar, circular/hexapod) · **lengthening IM nail** ± ex-fix (for angular correction)

**Outcome** — LLD to **<1 cm** · can correct small diaphyseal/metaphyseal angulation

**Complications** — ex-fix: **pin-site infection**, scarring, cosmesis · IM: **poor regenerate**, joint contracture/dislocation, nail failure

### TKA with deformity

**Goal** — neutral mechanical alignment via distal femoral + proximal tibial cuts · **valgus cut angle 4–7°** (IM guide on AAF) · tibial IM = EM guides (AAT = MAT)

**Extra-articular deformity** — locate CORA · **CORA further from knee → easier** intra-articular correction · **severe or close-to-joint deformity → extra-articular osteotomy + TKA** · osteotomy better tolerated on **tibia** (equal effect on flexion/extension gaps)

**Constraint** — ↑ constraint / **stems** if collaterals compromised (laxity, prior osteotomy)

**Outcome** — consistent improvement · results **less reliable after prior extra-articular osteotomy**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Under/overcorrection** | Unfavourable compartment loading · **opening wedge** may **lose correction** |
| **Nonunion** | **~5%** · risk: **smoking, age >60, obesity, far-cortex hinge fracture** |
| **Patella baja** | Proximal tibial osteotomy · **supra-tubercle > infra-tubercle** · pain, harder future TKA |
| **Tendon injury** | **Quadriceps** → femoral osteotomy · **patellar tendon** → tibial osteotomy |
| **Infection** | **1–4%** · ↑ nonunion · risk: smoking, inpatient admission |
| **DFO-specific** | Lateral opening: quadriceps tendon injury, **intercondylar fracture (too distal)**, lateral hinge fracture (↓ stability) · closing: medial hinge fracture |

## X vs Y

| | **Varus-producing DFO** | **Valgus-producing DFO** |
|---|---|---|
| Treats | **Genu valgum**, lateral OA | Genu varum with femoral CORA |
| Wedges | **Lateral opening** or **medial closing** | **Lateral closing** (medial hinge) |
| Criteria | mLDFA **<85°**, valgus >12°, age <50 | mLDFA **>90°** |

| | **Genu varum (adult)** | **Genu valgum (adult)** |
|---|---|---|
| Usual CORA | **Proximal tibia** | **Distal femur** |
| Pain | Medial | Lateral |
| Osteotomy | HTO | DFO |

## How it's asked

**"Young man, 15° valgus, early lateral OA, failed brace — best surgery?"** → **lateral opening wedge DFO**\
✗ Wrong options: HTO · TKA

**"Varus-producing DFO — true?"** → **15-yr survival ~50%**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral closing/medial opening · age >50 medial OA · needs constrained TKA later

**"Lateral opening wedge DFO — which patient?"** → **35-yr-old runner, isolated lateral pain, axis through fibular head**

**"16-yr-old, intermalleolar distance 10 cm, lateral opening DFO planned — alignment?"** → **MAD lateral to knee centre**\
✗ Wrong options: LDFA 88° normal · LDFA 91° varus

**"Normal anatomic–mechanical femoral axis difference?"** → **5–7°**

**"Valgus HTO indication?"** → **MPTA <85°, CORA in tibia, other compartments spared**

**"Order of correcting deformity?"** → **angulation, translation, length, rotation**

**"Osteotomy away from CORA causes?"** → **translation**

**"Opening wedge HTO changes tibial slope?"** → **↑ slope** (closing → ↓)

**"Distraction osteogenesis protocol?"** → **5–7 day latency, 1 mm/day**

# Arthroplasty Preoperative Medical Optimization

*Recon · 3 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Five domains** — **medical optimization** · **preoperative counselling** · **VTE prophylaxis** · **anaesthesia plan** · **pain management plan**

**Goal** — identify modifiable **risk factors** for postoperative complications

### Genes & lab markers

**HbA1c** — screen hyperglycaemic patients · **HbA1c >6.7 → ↑ wound complications** · target roughly **<8** (varies by surgeon/hospital)

**Nicotine testing** — preoperative, to confirm cessation

**Toxicology screen** — failed screen → ↑ mortality, readmission, reoperation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Cardiac & vascular preoperative evaluation

**Noninvasive cardiac testing** — **≥3 risk factors in a sedentary patient**: CAD/MI · heart failure · prior CVA · diabetes mellitus or chronic renal disease

**No stress test** — if patient can do **moderate activity (climb a flight of stairs)**

**Typical stem** — ✓ **67-yr-old for TKA with MI + CVA + DM**\
✗ Wrong options: spine patient with hypercholesterolaemia/HTN/DVT · healthy drinker · hip fracture with CRF/cancer/obesity · 81-yr-old who climbs stairs

**PVD + ↓ pulses before TKA** — next step ✓ **ankle-brachial index** of affected limb → vascular surgeon decides revascularization vs clearance\
✗ Wrong options: angiography first · venous Dopplers · MRI popliteal fossa · radiographs for calcified plaque

## 5 Management

### Medical optimization – risk factors

| Risk factor | Threshold / action |
|---|---|
| **Diabetes** | **HbA1c >6.7** ↑ wound complications · target ~**<8** |
| **Obesity** | **BMI >40** used in studies (continuous risk, no clear cut-off) · **>5% weight loss** may ↓ risk · bariatric surgery may help · ↑ AKI, CV, wound, infection |
| **Cardiac stents** | Delay elective surgery if DAPT stopped within **30 days of BMS** / **12 months of DES** |
| **Clopidogrel** | Discuss with cardiologist · **stop 7 days preop** → ↓ bleeding/transfusion, no ↑ CV events · restart ASAP |
| **Transfusion** | Perioperative transfusion → ↑ complications |
| **Dialysis** | **10–20× ↑ complications** · ESRD may do better with **kidney transplant before arthroplasty** (weigh dialysis septicaemia vs immunosuppression) |
| **MRSA** | Screen + decolonize (**mupirocin** nasal / **chlorhexidine** wipes) · universal decolonization may be cost-effective |
| **Smoking** | ↑ complications + infection · formal cessation programme · **6 weeks cessation** · nicotine testing |
| **Illicit drugs** | **5× ↑ mortality** · ↑ infectious + non-infectious complications |
| **Inflammatory arthritis** | **Hold biologics** before surgery |

### Smoking cessation

**Minimum cessation before elective THA** — **6 weeks** (Orthobullets) · TO (FNB 2024) asked this with options 3/4/6/8 weeks — answer not provided by the source

### Preoperative counselling

**Expectations** — satisfaction driven more by **expectations** than function · patients **over-optimistic** · set expectations for pain, function, complications

**Social support** — affects **LOS, readmission, non-home discharge** · psychological distress worsens postoperative pain · absence of anxiety → lower pain, better function

### VTE prophylaxis

| Body | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| **ACCP** | **Mechanical compression + one agent**: warfarin, LMWH (enoxaparin), **aspirin**, factor Xa inhibitor (apixaban, rivaroxaban), fondaparinux, dabigatran |
| **AAOS** | **Pharmacologic, mechanical, or both** if no increased VTE risk |

**Aspirin** — favoured: oral, compliance, cost, **low bleeding**

### Anaesthesia

**Neuraxial (spinal)** — ↓ stress vs GA → ↓ surgical time, **infection**, CV events, **transfusion**, LOS

### Multimodal pain management

**Preoperative orals** — **NSAIDs (COX-2)** + **pregabalin** → ↓ opioid use, ↓ pain, ↑ ROM, **no ↑ bleeding**

**Postoperative** — continue above + judicious opioids · **opioid adverse events up to 50%** of post-arthroplasty complications

**Periarticular injection** — local anaesthetic + opioid + NSAID · TKA: **= femoral nerve block**, cheaper/safer/easier · THA: ↓ opioid, better pain + function

**Not effective** — ✓ **intra-articular pain catheter**\
✗ Wrong options: acetaminophen · opioids · gabapentin · NSAIDs (all valid components)

| Block | Joint | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Lumbar plexus / psoas, femoral, sciatic | THA | Good analgesia, may affect muscle function |
| **Femoral nerve** | TKA | **Quadriceps weakness** |
| **Adductor canal** | TKA | **Similar pain control to femoral block, no quad weakness** |
| Sciatic | TKA | Posterior knee pain · significant motor effect |

## X vs Y

| | **Bare-metal stent** | **Drug-eluting stent** |
|---|---|---|
| Delay elective arthroplasty if DAPT stopped within | **30 days** | **12 months** |

| | **Femoral nerve block** | **Adductor canal block** |
|---|---|---|
| Analgesia after TKA | Good | **Similar** |
| Quadriceps | **Weak** | **Spared** |

## How it's asked

**"Which patient needs preoperative noninvasive cardiac testing?"** → **sedentary, ≥3 of CAD/MI, CHF, CVA, DM/CKD**\
✗ Wrong options: able to climb stairs

**"PVD with ↓ pulses before TKA — next evaluation?"** → **ankle-brachial index**\
✗ Wrong options: angiography · venous Doppler

**"Not an effective part of a multimodal regimen?"** → **intra-articular pain catheter**

**"Smoking cessation before elective THA?"** → **6 weeks** (Orthobullets; TO answer not given)

**"HbA1c associated with wound complications?"** → **>6.7**

**"Delay elective surgery after DES?"** → **12 months** (BMS **30 days**)

**"Clopidogrel before TKA?"** → **stop 7 days preop**

**"Dialysis patient for THA — complication risk?"** → **10–20× ↑**

**"TKA block sparing the quadriceps?"** → **adductor canal**

# High Tibial Osteotomy

*Recon · 4 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Purpose** — correct angular deformity → unload one compartment → prevent/slow **unicompartmental OA**

**Commonest indication** — primary/secondary **medial knee arthrosis** with **varus** · isolated lateral OA much less common

**Combined with** — **cartilage restoration** procedures (better mechanical environment) · ligament reconstruction (ACL/PCL/PLC) when malaligned

### Anatomy – mechanical axis

**Mechanical axis** — centre of femoral head → centre of ankle · normally passes **just medial to medial tibial spine**

**Fujisawa point** (TO) — **62% of tibial plateau width measured from the medial edge** · slightly **lateral to lateral tibial spine** · = mechanical axis **3–5° valgus** · correction angle = lines from this point to femoral head + ankle centres · TO technique text also cites **62.5%**\
✗ Wrong options: 62% from lateral · 67%

![Fujisawa point](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140821-1.png)

### Normal tibial slope (OB)

**Posterior tibial slope** — **9–11° medial**, **6–8° lateral**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — medial (varus) or lateral (valgus) knee pain · localized

**Exam** — malalignment · **varus thrust** (LCL laxity, lateral opening in stance — predicts medial OA progression)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **standing hip-to-ankle alignment films** → mechanical axis · plus knee AP/lateral

**Stress radiographs** — narrow **lateral** compartment cartilage space → contraindicates valgus HTO

**Unloader brace test** — **medial unloader brace** = **therapeutic + diagnostic** · benefit → likely benefit from valgus HTO\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · NSAIDs · patellar/strap brace

### Varus types (TO)

| Type | Components | HTO |
|---|---|---|
| **Primary varus** | Bony varus, **no ligament instability** | **Best indication** |
| **Double varus** | Bony varus + **ACL** instability | Combined/staged ligament + HTO |
| **Triple varus** | Varus + **ACL + PCL** insufficiency + **PLC failure** · **varus thrust** | Combined/staged |

## 5 Management

```
Medial compartment OA + varus, young/active
├─ Failed nonop → trial medial unloader brace (predicts success)
├─ Isolated medial OA, ROM ok, BMI ok → valgus HTO
│      └─ overcorrect to 8–10° anatomic valgus (Fujisawa 62%)
├─ Smoker → lateral closing wedge (not opening wedge — nonunion)
└─ Contraindication present → UKA / TKA
```

### Indications & patient selection

**Ideal candidate** — **young, active <50 yr** (arthroplasty would wear out) · **good vascular status** · **non-obese** · pain/disability · **one compartment only** · compliant

**Overcorrection** — ✓ **overcorrect into valgus**: anatomic axis **8–10° valgus** (Orthobullets, OB) · mechanical **3–5°** valgus (TO) · least recurrence

**Return to impact sport** — **HTO allows faster return to sport/impact work** than UKA (activity levels equal) · running discouraged after UKA (PE wear, loosening)

**Typical stems** — 30–48-yr-old labourer/athlete, isolated medial OA, varus 5–9°, failed nonop → ✓ **medial opening wedge HTO**\
✗ Wrong options: UKA · TKA · arthroscopic debridement/microfracture · OCA · meniscal transplant · lateral opening DFO (for valgus) · unispacer

**HTO vs UKA with ACL tear** (TO) — 45-yr-old, ACL torn, lateral meniscus intact, grade 4 medial OA, 10° passively correctable varus → ✓ **HTO** · ACL deficiency **contraindicates UKA**, only relative for HTO (reconstruct simultaneously) · UKA preferred if varus <10°

**HTO vs UKA with thrust + lateral meniscal tear** (TO) — 48-yr-old marathoner, **varus thrust**, 8° varus, functional ACL, stable lateral meniscal tear, no lateral chondral loss → ✓ **medial opening wedge HTO**\
✗ Wrong options: fixed/mobile UKA · TKA

| Finding (TO) | UKA | HTO |
|---|---|---|
| **Varus thrust** | Relative/strong contraindication (loosening) | **Strong indication** — corrects cause |
| **Lateral meniscal tear** | **Absolute contraindication** | Relative (depends on extent + cartilage) |
| Goal | Resurfacing → pain relief | **Axis realignment + stability** |

**Note — varus thrust conflict** — Orthobullets/OB: **varus thrust = contraindication** to HTO · TO (2025 case): thrust **favours HTO over UKA** — read the stem (OB: thrust + lateral laxity → ✓ "contraindication")

### Contraindications

| Contraindication | Orthobullets / OB | TO |
|---|---|---|
| **Inflammatory arthritis** | ✓ (psoriatic — high failure) | ✓ |
| BMI | **>35** | **>30** |
| **Flexion contracture** | **>15°** (10° OK) | **>10°** (extension lag) |
| **Knee flexion** | **<90°** | **<90°** |
| Correction needed | **>20°** | — |
| **Varus deformity** | **>10°** (valgus HTO) | **>15°** · >10° relative only |
| **Lateral tibial subluxation** | **>1 cm** | **>1 cm** (✓ 2 cm subluxation) |
| Medial bone loss | **>2–3 mm** | Medial compartment bone loss |
| Lateral compartment | Narrow on stress films · **loss of lateral meniscus** · lateral OA | Lateral narrowing (bi/tricompartmental) |
| **PF arthritis** | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Ligament instability / varus thrust** | ✓ | **Relative** — (✗ Wrong options: cruciate insufficiency alone)|
| **Smoking** | ✓ (OB) · esp. opening wedge (nonunion) | High smoker |
| Age | Best <50 · **>60** worse | **Age >60 relative** |
| Others (OB) | Nonconcordant pain (PF pain + medial OA) · unwilling to accept cosmetic angular change | — |

**Not contraindications** — **ACL tear** (TO) · **varus 10°** (TO) · **flexion ROM 0–100°** (TO) · chondral lesion size · BMI 22–24 · age 38–42 · 10° flexion contracture · postmenopausal

### Varus-producing tibial osteotomy

**Use** — valgus knee + lateral OA · **less common than DFO** (creates **oblique joint line**) · deformity must be **<12°** · contraindicated: medial OA, loss of medial meniscus · **DFO better if lateral femoral condyle hypoplasia**

### Opening vs closing wedge

| | **Medial opening wedge** | **Lateral closing wedge** |
|---|---|---|
| Approach | Anteromedial · pes + sMCL release | Anterolateral · **fibular osteotomy / PTFJ disruption** |
| **Tibial slope** | **↑ slope** (anterior plate) + anterior tibial translation | **↓ or no change** + posterior translation |
| **Patellar height** | **↓ → patella baja** | **↑ → patella alta** (TO) |
| Stability / WB | Less stable · **longer NWB** · may lose correction | **More stable · earlier WB · faster rehab** |
| Graft | Defect **≥10 mm → graft** | **No graft** |
| Peroneal nerve | **Minimal risk** · avoids PTFJ | **↑ risk** (fibular osteotomy) |
| Nonunion / plate failure | **↑** · avoid in **smokers** | ↓ · choose in smokers, diabetics, steroids |
| Limb length | Lengthens | Shortens |
| Later TKA | Preserves tibial shape | Harder (stem impingement, lateral bone loss) |
| Favoured with (TO) | **PCL injury**, patella alta, MCL laxity, LLD | **Patella baja**, ACL reconstruction, nonunion risk |

**Exclusive to closing wedge** — ✓ **proximal tibiofibular joint disruption**\
✗ Wrong options: compartment syndrome · plate breakage · neurologic injury (seen in both)

**Smoker** → favours **lateral closing wedge**\
✗ Wrong options: age · BMI · meniscectomy history

**Lateral closing vs medial opening** — ✓ **shorter time to weight bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ baja · ↑ popliteal injury · less stable · ↓ peroneal risk

**Outcomes** — opening vs closing: both appropriate\
✗ Wrong options: "opening superior"

### Other osteotomies (TO)

| Osteotomy | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Focal dome** | Concavity proximal · centre at **CORA** · **least translation**, minimal shortening · for **large corrections (≥20°, 18–20 mm)** e.g. Blount, trauma · cut below tubercle → **no change in slope or patellar height** |
| **Hemicallotasis** | Gradual distraction, ex-fix · below tubercle · minimal change in slope/height |
| **Chevron** | Inverted-V · no bone loss/graft · technically complex |

![Lateral closing wedge HTO](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3135-closingwedge.jpg)

### Tibial slope & ligament instability

**Rule** — **↑ slope → anterior tibial translation** (stress ACL) · **↓ slope → protects ACL**

| Deficiency | Slope change in HTO |
|---|---|
| **ACL-deficient** | **Decrease** slope |
| **PCL / PLC-deficient** | **Increase** slope ✓ (✗ Wrong options: decrease · neutral) |

**ACL + varus + medial OA, young athlete (pain + instability)** → ✓ **HTO + ACL reconstruction at the same time**\
✗ Wrong options: either alone · vibration rehab

**Recurrent PCL + PLC (dial >10° asymmetry at 30° + 90°) + varus, 1A Lachman** → ✓ **PCL + PLC reconstruction + HTO increasing slope**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL reconstruction · PLC alone

**Native slope 14° ignored during HTO** → ✓ **positive Lachman** (anterior translation persists)\
✗ Wrong options: medial pseudolaxity · quadriceps active test/sag (PCL) · varus thrust

**Decrease slope if** — slope **>13°** + anterior translation **≥10 mm** vs contralateral · **1 mm anterior closure ≈ 2° slope decrease**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Recurrence of varus** | ✓ **Most common** (TO) · **60% failure at 3 yr** if undercorrected or overweight · prevented by overcorrection |
| **Patella baja** | **Most patients** after proximal tibial osteotomy · **opening wedge** (raises joint line) · scarring, tendon contracture, immobilization · ✓ **HTO most likely procedure** to cause baja (✗ Wrong options: ACL/PCL/MPFL recon · TKA) |
| **Patella alta** | **Lateral closing wedge** (TO) |
| **Loss of posterior slope** | Closing wedge |
| **Peroneal nerve palsy** | **Lateral** opening/closing wedges · **fibular osteotomy** · minimal in medial opening |
| **Popliteal artery** | Rare, devastating |
| **Nonunion / malunion** | ↑ opening wedge, smokers |
| Compartment syndrome | Any osteotomy |

**Prognosis** — varus-producing HTO **87% success at 10 yr** · valgus-producing **50–85% at 10 yr** (Orthobullets) · good results after later conversion to TKA

### Conversion to TKA

**Risk factors for TKA failure after HTO** — ✓ **BMI >35** · **male** · **young age** at TKA · laxity · preoperative malalignment\
✗ Wrong options: female · stiffness · advanced age

## X vs Y

| | **HTO** | **UKA** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **<50, high-impact**, labourer | Older, lower demand |
| ACL deficiency | Relative — reconstruct together | **Contraindication** |
| Varus | Up to ~10–15° (TO) | **<10°**, passively correctable |
| Return to impact sport | **Faster** | Discouraged (running) |

| | **Valgus HTO** | **Varus DFO** |
|---|---|---|
| Deformity | Varus, tibial CORA | Valgus >12°, femoral CORA |
| Compartment | Medial OA | Lateral OA |

## How it's asked

**"Young labourer, isolated medial OA, varus — best treatment?"** → **medial opening wedge HTO**\
✗ Wrong options: UKA · TKA · arthroscopy

**"Diagnostic + therapeutic test before HTO?"** → **medial unloader brace**

**"True of HTO?"** → **overcorrect into valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI irrelevant · contraindicated >40 · patella alta common · opening superior

**"Contraindication to HTO?"** → **inflammatory arthritis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ACL deficiency · varus >10° · age >60

**"Contraindication to HTO?"** → **lateral tibial subluxation 2 cm** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: varus 10° · ACL tear · ROM 0–100°

**"Postmenopausal smoker, BMI 22, isolated medial OA — contraindication?"** → **smoking**

**"Varus thrust, BMI 24, 8° flexion contracture — contraindication?"** → **varus thrust**

**"Psoriatic arthritis, 10° flexion contracture, 10° varus — contraindication?"** → **inflammatory arthropathy**

**"ACL tear, intact lateral meniscus, grade 4 medial OA, 10° correctable varus?"** → **HTO** (TO)

**"Marathoner, varus thrust, stable lateral meniscal tear, functional ACL?"** → **medial opening wedge HTO** (TO)

**"Fujisawa point?"** → **62% of plateau width from medial edge** (TO)

**"Complication exclusive to lateral closing wedge?"** → **proximal tibiofibular joint disruption**

**"Smoker — what favours lateral closing wedge?"** → **smoking history**

**"Lateral closing wedge vs medial opening — true?"** → **shorter time to weight bearing**

**"PCL-deficient knee — slope effect?"** → **increased slope ↑ stability**

**"Slope 14° not addressed — postop finding?"** → **positive Lachman**

**"Most common complication of HTO?"** → **recurrence of varus** (TO)

**"Which osteotomy can cause patella alta?"** → **lateral closing wedge** (TO)

**"Patella baja most likely after?"** → **HTO**

**"Risk factor for TKA failure after HTO?"** → **BMI >35**

**"33-yr-old, wants fastest return to impact activity?"** → **medial opening wedge HTO**\
✗ Wrong options: UKA · TKA · BMAC alone · varus DFO

# Hip Anatomy

*Recon · 5 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Osteology

**Joint** — ball-and-socket diarthrodial · stability from **bony architecture**, augmented by **labrum + capsule**

**Acetabulum** — 3 ossification centres: **ilium, ischium, pubis** · **posterosuperior** cartilage thickest (weight-bearing)

**Acetabular orientation** — **anteverted 15°** · **abducted 45°** (caudal tilt)

**Femoral neck** — **anteverted 15°** (relative to condyles) · **neck-shaft angle 125°**

**Femoral head centre** — level with **tip of greater trochanter**

### Acetabular quadrants – screw safety

**Construction** — line **ASIS → acetabular centre** (anterior/posterior) + perpendicular bisector (superior/inferior)

| Quadrant | Structures at risk | Screws |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior-superior** | **Superior gluteal nerve + vessels**, **sciatic nerve** | **Safest** |
| **Posterior-inferior** | **Inferior gluteal nerve**, **internal pudendal vessels** | Second safest |
| **Anterior-inferior** | **Obturator vessels** (and nerve) | Unsafe |
| **Anterior-superior** | **External iliac artery + vein** | Unsafe |

![Acetabular quadrant system](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12769-f001054f105-002-9780443069918.jpg)

### Capsule & ligaments

**Capsule** — anterior: **intertrochanteric line** · posterior: **intertrochanteric crest** · basicervical + intertrochanteric regions **extracapsular**

**Iliofemoral (Y ligament of Bigelow)** — **strongest** · AIIS → intertrochanteric line · **taut in full extension** · prevents **anterior dislocation / hyperextension**

**Ischiofemoral** — reinforces **posterior** capsule · **pubofemoral** — inferior/anterior

**Ligamentum teres** — cotyloid notch → fovea

**Transverse acetabular ligament** — **most inferior** labrum · landmark for **acetabular version** during THA reaming

### Femoral head blood supply

| Age | Main supply |
|---|---|
| **Birth–4 yr** | **MFCA + LFCA + ligamentum teres** |
| **4 yr–adult** | **Posterosuperior + posteroinferior retinacular vessels** from MFCA |
| **Adult** | **Medial femoral circumflex artery** ✓ (deep branch) |

**MFCA** — branch of **profunda femoris** · ✓ **major supply to femoral head** · **at risk in psoas tenotomy** · **avoid transecting quadratus femoris** in posterior approach (non-arthroplasty)\
✗ Wrong options: internal pudendal · IGA · SGA · LFCA

**Piriformis-start nails** — damage **posterosuperior retinacular vessels** → AVN

**Ligamentum teres artery** — **posterior division of obturator** · insignificant in adults

**Inferior gluteal artery** — internal iliac branch · exits greater sciatic notch **between piriformis and coccygeus**, runs **inferior to / along piriformis** · supplies **short ER + gluteus maximus** · **primary femoral head supply in up to 17%** → preserve in surgical dislocation, posterior dislocation, acetabular fracture · ✓ **found along border of piriformis tendon**

**Vessel-location distractors (OB)** — between pectineus + iliopsoas = **MFCA** · beneath TFL = **LFCA** portion · under gluteus medius = **superior gluteal** · between quadratus femoris + upper adductor magnus = **deep branch of MFCA**

![Pelvic vessels](../../MCQ/attachments/q212906-illa-pelvic-vessels.jpg)

### Vessels at risk

| Vessel | Origin | At risk |
|---|---|---|
| **External iliac → common femoral** | Aorta → external iliac | **Anterosuperior** quadrant screws |
| **LFCA ascending branch** | Profunda | **Direct anterior approach** |
| **MFCA** | Profunda | **Psoas tenotomy**, quadratus transection |
| **Obturator (posterior branch)** | Internal iliac | **Anteroinferior** screws + acetabular retractors · supplies TAL |
| **Superior gluteal** | Internal iliac | Supplies **abductors + TFL** |
| **Internal pudendal** | Internal iliac | Exits greater, **re-enters via lesser sciatic notch** |
| Femoral perforators | Femoral | Supply vastus lateralis |

**Corona mortis** — anastomosis between **inferior epigastric** (external iliac) and **obturator** vessels

![Corona mortis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12769-corona-mortis.jpg)

### Nerves

**Lumbosacral plexus** — **T12–S4** · **posterior to psoas**

| Sciatic division | Branches |
|---|---|
| **Posterior** | **Peroneal** · superior gluteal · inferior gluteal · nerve to piriformis · posterior femoral cutaneous |
| **Anterior** | **Tibial** · nerve to quadratus femoris · nerve to obturator internus · pudendal · coccygeus |

**Peroneal division** — **lateral** to tibial · runs deep to long head of biceps · **most commonly injured nerve in THA**

**Obturator** — medial compartment · all adductors **except hamstring part of adductor magnus**

**Femoral** — **between iliacus and psoas**

**LFCN** — **L2–L3** · deep to inguinal ligament near ASIS

**Genitofemoral** — **L1–L2** · femoral + genital branches · pierces iliopsoas, runs on its **anteromedial** surface

### Muscles by action

| Action | Muscles |
|---|---|
| **Flexors** | Iliopsoas, rectus femoris, sartorius, pectineus, iliacus |
| **Adductors** | Adductor magnus (adductor part), longus, brevis, minimus, gracilis |
| **External rotators** | Gluteus maximus, piriformis, obturator externus + internus, gemelli, quadratus femoris |
| **Abductors** | Gluteus medius, gluteus minimus, TFL |
| **Extensors** | Gluteus maximus, semitendinosus, semimembranosus, long head biceps, adductor magnus (hamstring part) |

### Approaches

**Five** — anterior **Smith-Petersen** · anterolateral **Watson-Jones** · medial · direct lateral **Hardinge** · posterior **Southern / Moore**

## 5 Management

### Surgical hip dislocation – capsulotomy (TO)

**Ganz safe dislocation** — ✓ **anterolateral + anteroinferior capsulotomy, hip dislocated anteriorly** (flexion + ER)\
✗ Wrong options: posterolateral/posteroinferior · dislocate posteriorly · anteromedial/anterosuperior

**Why** — first incision **anterolateral along neck axis** avoids **deep branch of MFCA** · anteroinferior limb kept **anterior to lesser trochanter** (main MFCA lies **just superior + posterior to LT**) · extended to rim, turned **posteriorly parallel to labrum** to retracted piriformis

**Preserve** — **IGA** along piriformis border (OB)

## X vs Y

| | **MFCA** | **LFCA** |
|---|---|---|
| Femoral head supply (adult) | **Major** | Minor |
| At risk | Posterior approach (quadratus), psoas tenotomy | **Ascending branch — direct anterior approach** |

## How it's asked

**"Main blood supply of femoral head in adult (femoral neck fracture)?"** → **medial femoral circumflex artery**

**"Surgical dislocation — inferior gluteal artery reliably found?"** → **along border of piriformis tendon**

**"Safe surgical dislocation — capsulotomy?"** → **anterolateral + anteroinferior, dislocate anteriorly** (TO)

**"Safest quadrant for acetabular screws?"** → **posterosuperior** (then posteroinferior)

**"Anterosuperior screw injures?"** → **external iliac vessels**

**"Anteroinferior screw / retractor injures?"** → **obturator vessels**

**"Ascending branch of LFCA at risk in?"** → **direct anterior approach**

**"Most commonly injured nerve in THA?"** → **peroneal division of sciatic**

**"Strongest hip ligament?"** → **iliofemoral (Y of Bigelow)**

**"Landmark for cup version?"** → **transverse acetabular ligament**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Arthrodesis

*Recon · 6 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Biomechanics

**Gait efficiency** — **↓ ~50%**

**Energy** — **↑ O₂ consumption** · **30% more energy** for ambulation

**Adjacent joints** — ↑ contralateral pelvic rotation · ↑ stress on **lumbar spine, ipsilateral knee, contralateral hip**

## 5 Management

```
Hip arthrodesis decision
├─ Young labourer, unilateral painful ankylosis (infection/trauma) → primary fusion
├─ Failed THA (most common) → salvage fusion
└─ Painful fused hip (back > knee > contralateral hip) → convert to THA
       └─ Abductors nonfunctional on EMG → constrained cup
```

### Operative criteria – primary arthrodesis

| Indications | Contraindications |
|---|---|
| **Salvage for failed THA** (most common) | **Active infection** |
| **Young active labourer, painful unilateral ankylosis** after infection or trauma | **LLD >2.0 cm** |
| Neuropathic arthropathy | **Bilateral** hip arthritis |
| Tumour resection | **Adjacent joint degeneration** — lumbar spine, contralateral hip, ipsilateral knee |
| Missed SCFE with severe arthritis, 18-yr-old (OB) | Severe osteoporosis |
| | **Contralateral THA** (THA failure **40%** with contralateral fusion) |

### Optimal fusion position

| Plane | Orthobullets / OB | TO |
|---|---|---|
| **Flexion** | **20–35°** | **20–30°** |
| **Adduction** | **0–5°** | **0–5°** |
| **External rotation** | **5–10°** | **0–15°** |

**Avoid abduction** — pelvic obliquity → ↑ back pain

**OB answer** — ✓ **5° ER, 5° adduction, 20° flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: any abduction · 0° flexion · 15° ER with 0° adduction

**TO answer** — ✓ **20–30° flexion, 0–5° adduction, 0–15° ER**\
✗ Wrong options: 10° flexion · abduction

### Technique

**Goals** — apposition of surfaces · **rigid internal fixation** · early mobilization

**Approach** — **lateral with trochanteric osteotomy** preferred · preserve **abductors** + **superior gluteal nerve** · anterior approach also popular

**Fixation** — **cobra plate** · anterior plating

![Hip arthrodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5034-hiparthrodesis.jpg)

### Conversion of fusion to THA

**Indications** — **severe low back pain (most common)** · ipsilateral knee pain + instability · contralateral hip pain

**Preop** — **EMG of gluteus medius** · **nonfunctional abductors → constrained acetabular component**

**Outcome** — depends on **abductor function** · weakness → prolonged rehab, **severe lurching gait**

## 6 Complications

**Low back pain** — improved by takedown, but depends on **abductor function**

**Ipsilateral knee** — degeneration + **laxity**

**Contralateral hip** — degeneration

**Adjacent joint degeneration** — **60%** · LBP + ipsilateral knee pain most common · within **25 yr**

## How it's asked

**"Ideal hip arthrodesis position?"** → **20–30° flexion, 0–5° adduction, 0–15° ER** (TO)

**"18-yr-old, missed SCFE, hip fusion — optimum position?"** → **5° ER, 5° adduction, 20° flexion**

**"Most common indication for hip arthrodesis?"** → **salvage of failed THA**

**"Most common reason to convert fusion to THA?"** → **low back pain**

**"Before converting fusion to THA?"** → **EMG of gluteus medius** (nonfunctional → constrained cup)

**"Gait efficiency after hip fusion?"** → **↓ ~50%**, energy ↑ 30%

**"Contraindication to hip fusion?"** → **ipsilateral knee / lumbar / contralateral hip arthritis, LLD >2 cm, active infection**

**"Position to avoid in hip fusion?"** → **abduction**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Biomechanics

*Recon · 7 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

**Joint reaction force (JRF)** — force within a joint in response to forces acting on it · hip: balances **body-weight moment vs abductor moment** → **level pelvis**

**Joint congruence** — fit of surfaces · ↑ congruence → ↑ contact area

**Instant centre of rotation** — point of rotation, shifts with translation · body **centre of gravity just anterior to S2**

**Friction** — **not a function of contact area** · lubrication ↓ friction · **human joint μ = 0.002–0.04** · **metal-on-PE TJA μ = 0.05–0.15**

### Degrees of freedom

**Hip** — ball-and-socket → **3 rotational DOF**: flexion–extension (sagittal), abduction–adduction (frontal), axial rotation (transverse) · **constrains all translations**\
✗ Wrong options: 2 DOF · translations · 6 DOF

### Free-body physics

| Quantity | Equation |
|---|---|
| Newton 1st | No net force → constant velocity |
| **Force (Newton 2nd)** | **F = ma** · 1 N = 1 kg × 1 m/s² |
| Newton 3rd | **F₂ = −F₁** |
| Vector | Magnitude + direction (scalar = magnitude only) |
| **Moment (torque)** | **Force (perpendicular) × distance** |
| Work | Force (parallel to displacement) × distance |
| Potential energy | **m × g × h** |
| Kinetic energy | **½ mv²** |

## 2 Mechanism

### Hip free-body analysis

**W** — body weight minus stance limb = **5/6 body weight**

**M** — abductor force · **R** — JRF = **3–6× body weight**

**Step 1 (moments)** — **A × My = B × W** · A = 5 cm (abductor arm), B = 12.5 cm (body-weight arm) → **My = 2.5 W**

**Step 2** — **Ry = My + W = 3.5 W**

**Step 3** — **R = Ry / cos 30° ≈ 4 W**

![Hip free-body diagram](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9064-hip-mechanics.jpg)

### Lever-arm ratio (TO)

**Normal ratio** — body-weight lever arm : abductor lever arm ≈ **2.5 : 1** → abductors ≈ **2.5 × BW** to keep pelvis level\
✗ Wrong options: 1:2 · 2:1 · 1:2.5

**FNB 2024** — "abductor lever arm ratio in single-leg stance" with options 0.5/1.5/2.5/3.5 — answer not given by source; matches **2.5** above

**Arthritic hip** — **abductor lever arm shortened** (head lost, neck short) · body-weight arm **unchanged** · ✓ **ratio ↑ (up to 4 : 1)**\
✗ Wrong options: abductor arm lengthened · body-weight arm lengthened · unchanged

**Surgical aim** — ratio toward **1 : 1** → total hip load ↓ **~30%** (theoretical)

## 5 Management

### Joint reactive force – cane, medialization, offset

| ↓ JRF (↑ A/B ratio) | ↑ JRF |
|---|---|
| **Medialize cup** (also inferior + anterior) → ↓ body-weight arm | **Valgus** neck-shaft angle (↓ shear) |
| **↑ femoral offset** → **lengthens abductor arm** | Lateralized / high hip centre |
| Long-stem prosthesis · **lateralize GT** | |
| **Varus** neck-shaft angle (↑ **shear**) | |
| **Lean over affected hip** (Trendelenburg lurch) | |
| **Cane in contralateral hand** | |
| Load carried in **ipsilateral** hand | |

**Offset question** — ✓ **↓ JRF; ↑ offset lengthens abductor moment arm**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ JRF · shortens arm · only hip centre matters

**Medialization worked example (OB)** — J = 1800 N; body-weight arm 100 → 50 mm → ✓ **J = 1200 N** · equilibrium: **(AT × A) − (5/6 BW × B) = 0**\
✗ Wrong options: 1000 · 1800 · 2200 · 3600

![Medialized acetabulum — free-body worked answer](../../MCQ/attachments/q2985-illa-hip-mechanics-ill-a.jpg)

**Contralateral cane** — ↓ hip JRF **up to ~50%** by ✓ **reducing abductor pull** · long moment arm from opposite side · needs upper-limb + trunk strength\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ flexor pull · moving centre of rotation · ↑ congruence · moving COG posterior to S2

![Cane in contralateral hand](../../MCQ/attachments/q3644-illa-cane.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Trendelenburg gait

**Cause** — ✓ **hip abductor weakness**\
✗ Wrong options: adductors · flexors · extensors · knee extensors

**Pattern** — ✓ **pelvis on swing side drops → ↑ adduction of affected hip in stance**\
✗ Wrong options: swing side elevates · ↓ adduction · circumduction

**Compensation (lurch)** — trunk leans **over affected hip** → COG closer → ↓ moment arm → ↓ abductor demand + ↓ JRF

![Trendelenburg gait](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9064-trendelenburg.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Varus neck-shaft** | **Valgus neck-shaft** |
|---|---|---|
| JRF | **↓** | **↑** |
| Shear across joint | **↑** | **↓** |

| | **Medialize cup** | **↑ Femoral offset** |
|---|---|---|
| Lever arm changed | **Body-weight arm ↓** | **Abductor arm ↑** |
| JRF | ↓ | ↓ |

## How it's asked

**"Arthritic hip — true?"** → **ratio of body-weight lever arm to abductor arm ↑** (TO)

**"Ratio of body-weight lever arm to abductor arm?"** → **2.5 : 1** (TO)

**"JRF after medializing cup (1800 N, arm 100 → 50 mm)?"** → **1200 N**

**"Contralateral cane reduces JRF by?"** → **reducing abductor pull**

**"High-offset stem — effect on JRF?"** → **↓ — lengthens abductor moment arm**

**"Trendelenburg gait compensates for weak?"** → **hip abductors**

**"Trendelenburg gait pattern?"** → **swing-side pelvis drops, ↑ adduction of stance hip**

**"Hip degrees of freedom?"** → **3 — flexion–extension, abduction–adduction, axial rotation**

**"JRF in single-leg stance?"** → **~3–6 × BW** (worked example ≈ 4 W)

**"Coefficient of friction, human joint vs metal-on-PE?"** → **0.002–0.04 vs 0.05–0.15**
