# Hip Hemiarthroplasty Periprosthetic Fracture

*Recon · 8 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1–4%** of hemiarthroplasties · **uncemented 1–7.4%** vs **cemented 0.2–1.3%**

**Risk factors** — ↑ age · **female** · **osteoporosis** · **uncemented stem** (RCTs + registries) · **undersized** component · **slip-taper stem** in cemented hemi

**Associated** — stress shielding, peri-implant osteopenia, infection, **osteolysis**, subsidence

**Mechanism** — **ground-level fall**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — fall · **start-up pain → antecedent loosening** · progressive pain, constitutional symptoms, wound problems → suspect **PJI**

**Exam** — pain, cannot bear weight · **shortened, externally rotated** limb · pain on log roll · check prior **scars**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **AP + lateral femur** (pattern, displacement, implant position) · **AP pelvis** if revising

**CT** — implant stability, fracture morphology · **best for cement-mantle integrity**

**Stable stem signs** — proximal cement mantle intact · **no subsidence** · always **test stem stability intraoperatively**

### Vancouver classification & treatment

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **AG** | Greater trochanter | ORIF if **>2 cm displaced** (abductor escape) · else nonop |
| **AL** | Lesser trochanter | **Nonoperative** |
| **B1** | Around stem, **well fixed** | **ORIF** |
| **B2** | Around stem, **loose**, good bone stock | **Revision** (diaphyseal-engaging) ± ORIF |
| **B3** | Around stem, loose, **poor bone stock** | **Proximal femoral replacement** |
| **C** | **Distal** to stem | **ORIF** |

![Vancouver classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob423186-vancouver-classification-illustration.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Hemiarthroplasty periprosthetic fracture
├─ Trochanteric: AL or AG <2 cm → protected WB, no active abduction 6–12 wk
│      └─ AG >2 cm → ORIF (claw plate, cables, TBW)
├─ Stem well fixed (B1) or distal (C) → ORIF (locking plate)
├─ Stem loose, good bone (B2) → revise to diaphyseal-engaging stem ± ORIF
└─ Stem loose, poor bone (B3) → proximal femoral replacement
```

### Treatment by stem stability

**Nonoperative (AG <2 cm, AL)** — protected WB · early walker WB in elderly · **restrict active abduction 6–12 weeks** (AG)

**ORIF (B1, C)** — as soon as medically optimized · lateral subvastus / **MIPO** · **polyaxial locking plate** · proximal fixation: cables, cerclage, screws · **drilling into cement ↑ pull-out strength but may compromise mantle** · allograft struts controversial · **15% complications**

**B2 ORIF alone (controversial)** — select **elderly, low-demand** · favourable: **simple pattern, anatomic reduction, no prior loosening, intact bone–cement interface**

**B2 revision** — ✓ **uncemented diaphyseal-engaging stem bypassing fracture by 2 cortical diameters** ± ORIF · monoblock cylindrical or **modular fluted tapered** · ± trochanteric bolt/claw · **up to 25% complications** (revision or ORIF) · revision → ↑ blood loss

**Long cemented stem (controversial)** — cement trapped in fracture → impaired healing · cement-in-cement may equal tapered stems in selected patients

**Metaphyseal-engaging components** — controversial in fracture setting

**B3** — resect fracture · cemented/uncemented **PFR** · reattach trochanter (suture/cable/wire)

![Vancouver B2 around a loose hemiarthroplasty stem](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob423186-hemivancb2.jpg)

**OB answer pattern** — B2 (6–8 wk post hemi, loose stem): ✓ **fracture fixation + diaphyseal-engaging revision**\
✗ Wrong options: protected WB (Vancouver A) · cerclage alone (B1) · locking plate ± strut (B1/C) · PFR (B3) · reuse original stem · **proximally coated stem** (needs press-fit in fractured zone) · bed rest

**B1 (5 yr post hemi, fracture at stem tip, proximal mantle intact)** → ✓ **ORIF alone**\
✗ Wrong options: long cemented revision (cemented stems not implant of choice in revision) · long uncemented ± ORIF (for B2) · PFR (salvage)

## 6 Complications

**List** — infection · dislocation · refracture · **stem subsidence** · implant failure · **trochanteric escape**

**Mortality** — **~25% at 1 yr**, up to **50% at 2 yr**

**Prognostic** — **early mobilization** critical · **surgical delay → ↑ mortality**

## X vs Y

| | **Cemented hemi** | **Uncemented hemi** |
|---|---|---|
| Periprosthetic fracture rate | **0.2–1.3%** | **1–7.4%** |
| Special risk | **Slip-taper** stem | Undersized stem |

| | **B1** | **B2** | **B3** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stem | Fixed | Loose | Loose |
| Bone stock | — | Good | **Poor** |
| Treatment | **ORIF** | **Diaphyseal revision ± ORIF** | **PFR** |

## How it's asked

**"Hemi 6 weeks ago, fall, loose stem — optimal treatment?"** → **fracture fixation + revision with diaphyseal-engaging stem** (B2)

**"Hemi 8 weeks, fall — best approach?"** → **ORIF + diaphyseal-engaging stem**\
✗ Wrong options: original stem · proximally coated stem

**"Hemi 5 years, fracture at stem tip, mantle intact proximally?"** → **ORIF alone** (B1)

**"Vancouver AG — ORIF threshold?"** → **>2 cm displacement**

**"Revision stem must bypass fracture by?"** → **2 cortical diameters**

**"Risk factor for hemi periprosthetic fracture?"** → **uncemented stem** (cemented: slip-taper)

**"Best imaging for cement mantle?"** → **CT**

**"Mortality at 1 year?"** → **~25%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Osteoarthritis

*Recon · 9 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence (symptomatic)** — **hip 88 / 100,000 / yr** · knee 240 / 100,000 / yr

**Modifiable risks** — **articular trauma** · muscle weakness · heavy physical work · high-impact sport

**Non-modifiable** — **female** > male · ↑ age · genetics · **DDH, SCFE, Perthes**

### Cartilage pathophysiology

| Feature | **Osteoarthritis** | **Ageing** |
|---|---|---|
| **Water content** | **↑** (matrix disruption) ✓ | ↓ |
| Proteoglycan | **↓** quantity | ↓ content + size |
| Collagen | Disorganized · **↓ in severe OA** · ↓ cross-linking | **Normal** quantity · ↑ stiffness, ↑ cross-linking |
| **Chondrocyte activity** | **↑** (proliferation) | Unchanged |
| **Subchondral bone** | **↑ thickness** | Unchanged |
| Chondroitin : GAG | — | ↓ |

**Moderate OA cartilage** → ✓ **↑ water**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ subchondral thickness · ↓ chondrocyte activity · ↑ proteoglycan · ↑ collagen

**Synovium** — early mild inflammation → middle **hypervascular** → late **thick + vascular**

**Subchondral bone** — remodelling → lytic lesions with **sclerotic edges** (unlike RA cysts) → **cysts** late

![OA vs ageing cartilage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5005-osteoarthritis-vs-aging.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**MMPs (degrade matrix)** — **stromelysin**, plasmin, **aggrecanase-1 (ADAMTS-4)** · zinc co-factor · stromelysin secretion **induced by PDGF**, **inhibited by TGF-β**

**TIMPs** — inhibit MMPs · **MMP–TIMP imbalance** in OA

**Cytokines (from synoviocytes, ↑ MMPs)** — **IL-1** (catabolic), IL-6, **TNF-α**

**Cartilage degeneration implicated** — ✓ **stromelysin**\
✗ Wrong options: TGF-β (anabolic) · aggrecan (compressive proteoglycan) · osteoprotegerin (RANKL decoy) · IGF-1 (anabolic)

**Inheritance** — **non-Mendelian** · candidate genes: **vitamin D receptor**, **oestrogen receptor 1**, IL-1, IL-4, matrilin-3, BMP-2, BMP-5

**Histology** — **loss of superficial chondrocytes** · **tidemark replication + breakdown** · fissuring · eburnation

### Rapidly progressive OA (RPOA)

**Causes** — **steroid injections** (high dose **>80 mg**, multiple) · cytokine/immune · autoimmune · **subchondral insufficiency fracture**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — function-limiting · **groin** commonest · referred to lateral hip, **anterior knee**, thigh, buttock, low back · night/rest pain · stiffness · mechanical symptoms

**Exam** — gait, LLD, scars · **flexion contracture >5°** · **flexion <90–100°** · **↓ internal rotation** · **SLR negative**

**Intra-articular signs (OB)** — **Stinchfield** (pain with resisted hip flexion) · **C-sign** (groin cupping)

### Hip pain referred to knee

**Concomitant hip pathology** — **2–29%** of knee-pain patients

**Suspect hip when** — disability **disproportionate** to knee findings · fails cortisone/PT · **no improvement after knee surgery** · Trendelenburg gait

**Next step** — ✓ **hip radiographs** (✗ Wrong options: ESR/CRP first · more PT · revision TKA · pain specialist) · benign knee exam + pain on hip motion → assess for ✓ **hip OA** (✗ Wrong options: meniscus · occult fracture · septic knee · LLD)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **standing AP pelvis** + **AP and lateral hip** · optional **false-profile** (dysplasia)

**OA signs** — joint-space narrowing · osteophytes · subchondral sclerosis · subchondral cysts

**Pelvic obliquity** — spinal deformity → LLD issues

**Acetabular retroversion (crossover)** — makes cup positioning harder

### Tönnis classification

| Grade | Findings |
|---|---|
| **0** | Normal |
| **1** | Sclerosis of head + acetabulum · slight JSN · slight lipping |
| **2** | **Small cysts** · **moderate JSN** · moderate loss of head sphericity |
| **3** | **Large cysts** · obliterated / severe JSN · severe head deformity vs AVN |

![Tönnis grade 2](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5005-tonnis-3.jpg)

### Protrusio acetabuli (TO)

**Definition** — ✓ **femoral head migrates past ilioischial (Köhler) line** · **CE angle of Wiberg >40°**\
✗ Wrong options: iliopectineal line · CEA >25° · ↓ CEA

**Köhler line** — most lateral pelvic brim → most lateral obturator foramen

| Unilateral causes | Bilateral causes |
|---|---|
| OA, septic + **TB** arthritis, AVN, **central fracture-dislocation**, fibrous dysplasia, **THA** (migrating prosthesis) | **RA**, **osteomalacia**, **ankylosing spondylitis**, **Paget**, renal osteodystrophy, rickets, osteoporosis, **Marfan** |

**Otto pelvis (arthrokatadysis)** — **primary** protrusio · **bilateral** · **younger women** · early pain + stiffness · ✓ **not caused by osteomalacia** (that is secondary)

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic hip OA
├─ First line → NSAIDs + weight loss/exercise (BMI >25) + contralateral cane
├─ Short-term relief → IA corticosteroid (also diagnostic)
├─ Young, dysplasia, concentric, mild–moderate OA → PAO ± femoral osteotomy
├─ Tönnis 2–3 → no arthroscopy (high conversion)
└─ End-stage, >50 yr → THA (≥3 months after ipsilateral injection)
```

### Nonoperative management

| Recommendation | Modality |
|---|---|
| **First line** | **NSAIDs** · **weight loss (BMI >25), activity modification, exercise/PT** · walking stick in **contralateral** hand |
| **Strongly supported (2013 AAOS CPG)** | **IA corticosteroid** short-term (therapeutic + diagnostic) · weight loss · low-impact exercise · NSAIDs |
| Controversial | Acupuncture · viscosupplement · glucosamine/chondroitin |
| **Against** | **Opioids incl. tramadol** · **IA hyaluronic acid** (strong against, 2017 CPG) · needle/arthroscopic lavage |

**Steroid mechanism** — ✓ **binds nuclear receptors → interrupts inflammatory cascade via mRNA**\
✗ Wrong options: hypothalamic antipyretic (acetaminophen) · cannabinoid receptor · synovial viscosity (HA) · Na⁺ channel block (lidocaine)

**OB note** — 2019 OB lists **tramadol** among supported options; Orthobullets lists tramadol as **recommended against** — read the stem

### Contraindication to joint preservation (Tönnis 2)

**Arthroscopy** — controversial · degenerative labral tears with little radiographic OA · ✓ **Tönnis ≥2 → high conversion to THA** · also **age >50**, obesity

**Not contraindications** — prior steroid injections · age >45 · large cam / 2B subspine · acetabular dysplasia (OB) · degenerative labral tear · alpha angle 52° · male sex · ligamentum teres tear

**PAO ± femoral osteotomy** — **symptomatic dysplasia**, adolescent/young adult, **concentric** hip, mild–moderate OA · mixed results, may delay THA

**Femoral head resection** — pathological lesions · painful subluxation

**Resurfacing** — **young (<50), active, male** · or severe proximal femoral deformity · ↓ use (MoM concerns)

### THA indications

**Indication** — end-stage/severe symptomatic OA · **preferred >50 yr** + advanced change · most predictable pain relief + function

**Typical stem** — 67-yr-old golfer, end-stage OA, failed NSAIDs/PT → ✓ **cementless THA**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid (transient + delays surgery 3 months) · HA · resurfacing · cemented hemi (acetabular disease)

**Hip–spine syndrome** — 1 week relief after IA injection + Stinchfield + C-sign → ✓ **recommend THA**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat injection · PT · lumbar fusion · osteochondroplasty (no cam/pincer)

**Injection timing** — wait **≥3 months** after ipsilateral IA injection (↑ PJI)

**Contraindications (TO)** — **absolute**: active infection (hip or elsewhere), unstable medical illness · **relative**: **morbid obesity**, **severe dementia**, tobacco, severe osteoporosis, untreated skin disease (psoriasis), **abductor insufficiency**

**Not a contraindication** — ✓ **asymptomatic bacteriuria** (TO) · HbA1c unreliable for predicting infection · Charnley: THA possible with chronic low-grade infection of **opposite** hip

### Combined hip & knee deformity – sequence (TO)

**Flexion deformity of hip + knee** — ✓ **hip first, then knee**\
✗ Wrong options: knee first · simultaneous · knee then hip 3 weeks later

**Reasons** — knee FFD ↓ after hip FFD corrected · knee easier with mobile hip · referred hip pain resolves · knee aligned to **new hip centre** · faster rehab

### Protrusio acetabuli – reaming (TO)

**Precaution** — ✓ **peripheral reaming only**\
✗ Wrong options: one size larger midway · one size smaller throughout · horizontal reaming

**Technique** — ream **periphery first** with larger reamers (1–2 sizes below final cup), **preserve subchondral bone** · medial floor: curette geodes, drill sclerotic bone with K-wires · **morselized graft** impacted on floor (none at rim) · cup **1–2 mm larger** than last reamer · ≥**50% host-bone contact**

![Protrusio reaming technique](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17099994-1.png)

## X vs Y

| | **Coxa profunda** | **Protrusio acetabuli** |
|---|---|---|
| Medial to ilioischial line | **Acetabular fossa** only | **Femoral head** |
| CE angle | — | **>40°** |

| | **OA subchondral cyst** | **RA cyst** |
|---|---|---|
| Margin | **Sclerotic** | Non-sclerotic (periarticular erosion) |

## How it's asked

**"Characteristic cartilage change in moderate OA?"** → **↑ water content**

**"Implicated in cartilage degeneration?"** → **stromelysin**\
✗ Wrong options: TGF-β · IGF-1 · aggrecan · OPG

**"Mechanism of drug strongly recommended for short-term hip OA pain (2013 CPG)?"** → **corticosteroid — nuclear receptor, mRNA change**

**"Contraindication for THR — all except?"** → **asymptomatic bacteriuria** (TO)

**"67-yr-old golfer, end-stage OA, tournament in 5 months?"** → **cementless THA**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection (delays surgery 3 months)

**"Brief relief from hip injection, Stinchfield + C-sign — next?"** → **recommend THA**

**"What precludes arthroscopy for joint preservation?"** → **Tönnis grade 2**

**"Persistent knee pain after TKA, Trendelenburg gait — next?"** → **hip X-rays**

**"Benign knee exam, pain with hip motion — imaging looks for?"** → **hip OA**

**"Flexion deformity of hip and knee — sequence?"** → **hip first, then knee** (TO)

**"Protrusio acetabuli defined by?"** → **femoral head past ilioischial line** (TO)

**"Reaming precaution in protrusio?"** → **peripheral reaming only** (TO)

**"Not true about Otto pelvis?"** → **osteomalacia** (it is bilateral, primary, young women) (TO)

**"Steroid dose linked to RPOA?"** → **>80 mg**, multiple injections

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Osteonecrosis

*Recon · 10 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **20,000 / yr** (US) · **10% of all THAs**

**Demographics** — **male > female** · age **35–50**

**Bilateral** — **80%** · **multifocal** (≥3 joints) **3%** → image contralateral hip

### Etiology & risk factors

| Direct causes | Indirect causes |
|---|---|
| **Irradiation** | **Alcoholism** |
| **Trauma** (MFCA injury) | **Hypercoagulable states** |
| **Haematologic** — leukaemia, lymphoma | **Steroids** (endogenous / exogenous) |
| **Dysbaric** — Caisson disease, "the bends" | **SLE** |
| **Marrow replacement** — **Gaucher** | Transplant |
| **Sickle cell** | Viruses — CMV, hepatitis, **HIV**, rubella, rubeola, varicella |
| | **Protease inhibitors** · **idiopathic** |

**Also (OB)** — **antiphospholipid syndrome** ✓ (20% asymptomatic ON without steroids) · antiseizure drugs · **collagen II mutation** (idiopathic ON)\
✗ Wrong options: collagen I disease · haemochromatosis · achondroplasia · Paget

### Pathophysiology

**Idiopathic final common pathway** — **intravascular coagulation**: microcirculation coagulation → **venous thrombosis** → retrograde arterial occlusion → **intraosseous hypertension** → ↓ flow → AVN → chondral fracture + collapse

**Trauma** — injury to **medial femoral circumflex artery**

| Injury | AVN rate |
|---|---|
| **Femoral head fracture** | **75–100%** |
| **Basicervical fracture** | **50%** |
| **Hip dislocation** | **2–40%** (**2–10% if reduced <6 h**) |
| Femoral neck fracture (OB) | **10–45%** (even non-displaced) |
| Intertrochanteric | Rare |

**↑ risk** — greater displacement, poor reduction · **↓ risk** — quick reduction, capsular decompression of haematoma

### Paediatric hip fracture AVN (TO)

**Best predictors** — ✓ **fracture type + patient age** (meta-analysis, 360 cases)\
✗ Wrong options: open reduction + rigid fixation · surgery <36 h + capsular decompression · displacement + associated injuries

| Delbet type | Level | AVN |
|---|---|---|
| **IA** | Transphyseal **with dislocation** | **~100% (highest)** |
| IB | Transphyseal, no dislocation | High |
| **II** | Transcervical | Intracapsular |
| **III** | Cervicotrochanteric | Intracapsular |
| **IV** | Intertrochanteric | **Extracapsular — lowest** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — insidious **anterior hip/groin** pain · worse with **stairs, inclines, impact**

**Exam** — normal early · late like OA (↓ **IR**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **AP + frog-lateral** hip · **AP + lateral of contralateral hip**

**MRI** — **sensitivity 99%, specificity 99%** (best test) · order when X-rays negative but suspicion persists · **double-line sign**: **T1 dark band**, **T2 bright inner line** (marrow oedema / granulation) · serpentine low-signal border with central fat-like signal · wedge-shaped subchondral T1 low signal

**Double-line sign meaning (TO)** — **active transition zone** dead bone ↔ granulation tissue = ongoing, often failing repair\
✗ Wrong options: "repair complete, lesion stable"

**Bone marrow oedema on MRI** — ✓ **best predictor of worsening pain + collapse**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid aetiology · effusion · age <30 · Kerboul 180°

**Histology** — **wedge-shaped infarct** with head collapse\
✗ Wrong options: OA · Perthes (no wedge) · TB (more cartilage destruction)

**Bone scan** — cold spot early (Ficat 0–1)

![Osteonecrosis — wedge-shaped infarct](../../MCQ/attachments/q6013-a-31.jpg)

### Steinberg classification (modified Ficat)

| Stage | Radiograph | MRI / bone scan |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | Normal | Normal |
| **I** | Normal | **Abnormal** |
| **II** | **Cystic / sclerotic** change | Abnormal |
| **III** | **Crescent sign** (subchondral collapse) | Abnormal |
| **IV** | **Flattening** of femoral head | Abnormal |
| **V** | **Joint narrowing** | Abnormal |
| **VI** | **Advanced degenerative** change | Abnormal |

![Steinberg III — crescent sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5006-ficat-iii.jpg)

### Ficat–Arlet staging (TO)

| Stage | Radiograph |
|---|---|
| **0** | None (bone scan ↓ uptake) |
| **1** | Normal (bone scan **cold spot**) |
| **2A** | Diffuse/localized osteoporosis, cyst, sclerosis |
| **2B** | **Crescent sign — subchondral collapse with maintained sphericity** ✓ |
| **3** | **Loss of sphericity** |
| **4** | Joint-space narrowing, arthritis |

**Note** — crescent sign = **Steinberg III** (Orthobullets) = **Ficat 2B** (TO) · OB uses Ficat III for crescent, IV flattening, V acetabular change — read which system the stem uses

### Prognosis of collapse – lesion size, Kerboul angle

**Most important factor** — ✓ **lesion size** (>⅓ of head)\
✗ Wrong options: age · sex · BMI/activity · alcohol

**Modified Kerboul combined necrotic angle** — **arc on mid-coronal + mid-sagittal MRI** added (e.g. 126° + 94° = **220°**)

| Combined angle | Orthobullets / OB risk | TO grade |
|---|---|---|
| **<160°** | Low | **Grade 1** — low; CD works |
| **160–190°** | Low | **Grade 2** — moderate |
| **190–240°** | **Moderate** (✓ 220° = moderate) | **Grade 3 — high** |
| **>240°** | **High** | **Grade 4 — very high**, preservation often contraindicated |

**TO high-risk thresholds** — Kerboul **>200°** · **combined necrotic index >33 → >80% collapse** untreated · lesions **>30% volume** — bisphosphonates don't prevent collapse

**Sickle cell** — asymptomatic MRI ON → **>75% progress to collapse** (vs **33%** non-sickle) · screen contralateral hip · some treat both

![Modified Kerboul angle](../../MCQ/attachments/q216630-illb-modified-kerboul.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Hip osteonecrosis
├─ Pre-collapse (Steinberg 0–II), small → bisphosphonate ± core decompression
│      └─ High-risk large lesion → CD + BMAC or vascularized fibula
├─ Small lesion <15%, rotatable → rotational osteotomy
├─ Young, pre- or early collapse, reversible cause → vascularized fibula
└─ Crescent sign / collapse / >40 yr large lesion / chronic steroids → THA
```

### Medical treatment – bisphosphonates

**Indication** — **pre-collapse** (Ficat/Steinberg 0–II) · **alendronate** prevented collapse with subchondral lucency · later RCTs (zoledronate) — **no benefit** in medium/large lesions

**Answer** — ✓ **bisphosphonate** ↓ collapse\
✗ Wrong options: cyclic PTH · RANKL/RANK therapy · SERM

**Mechanism** — ✓ **↓ osteoclast resorption by flattening ruffled border** + ↑ osteoclast apoptosis\
✗ Wrong options: vitamin D · denosumab (RANKL antibody, no benefit) · teriparatide · COX inhibition

**Risks** — atypical femur fracture · ONJ · gastric ulcer · oesophagitis

### Core decompression ± grafting

**Indication** — **early, pre-collapse** · **reversible** aetiology · not after collapse

**Technique** — traditional **8–10 mm** core through subchondral necrosis · alternative **3.2 mm pin passed 2–3×** · relieves **intraosseous hypertension** → ↓ pain · stimulates angiogenesis · augment with BMP / **BMAC**

**High-risk pre-collapse (TO)** — 28-yr-old, double-line sign, Kerboul 210°, CNI 45 → ✓ **core decompression alone insufficient → vascularized fibula or BMAC**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated multiple small-diameter CD · bisphosphonates + protected WB · THA (still young + pre-collapse)

### Rotational osteotomy

**Indication** — **small lesions (<15%)** rotatable off weight-bearing zone · intertrochanteric

**Direction** — **medial** disease → **varus** rotational · **anterolateral** disease → **valgus flexion** osteotomy

**Outcome** — **60–90%** success (Japan) · distorts head → harder THA · not after collapse

### Head-preserving grafting

**Techniques** — ✓ **core track**, **trapdoor**, **light bulb** · **"tramtrack"** — not a technique (TO)

| Technique | Route | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Core track** | Standard core | Wide debridement, simple, no dislocation, low complications |
| **Trapdoor (Mont)** | **Articular surface** flap via **surgical dislocation** | Direct view · cartilage damage, iatrogenic ON risk |
| **Light bulb (Merle d'Aubigné)** | **2 × 2 cm window at head–neck junction** | Allograft packed · **↑ neck fracture risk** |

### Vascularized free fibula

**Indication** — **pre-collapse and collapsed** AVN in **young** patient · reversible aetiology preferred

**Technique** — large core removes necrosis · fibular strut under subchondral bone (prevents/tamps collapse)

**Outcome** — **80% success at 5–10 yr** (some centres) · **less predictable >40 yr**

**Donor complications** — sensory deficit · motor weakness · **FHL contracture** · **tibial stress fracture**

**Tibial stress fracture after graft** — X-ray normal → ✓ **MRI** (or bone scan) → protected WB\
✗ Wrong options: compartment pressures · CT (inferior) · US · biopsy

### Surgical hip dislocation (TO)

**Artery to preserve** — ✓ **deep branch of MFCA**\
✗ Wrong options: deep/ascending LFCA · descending MFCA

**Deep MFCA course** — inferior border of **obturator externus**, between **pectineus (medial) and iliopsoas (lateral)** → intertrochanteric crest → terminal branches under reflected capsule, **posterosuperior neck** → enter bone **2–4 mm distal to head–cartilage junction** · **intact obturator externus protects it**

**Ganz** — ✓ **posterior (Kocher–Langenbeck/Gibson) approach → trochanteric flip osteotomy (≤1.5 cm, 5 mm medius cuff + piriformis left on bed) → anterior arthrotomy (Z-capsulotomy) → anterior dislocation** · capsulotomy stays **anterior to lesser trochanter**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior arthrotomy · posterior dislocation

### THA – outcomes & complications

**Indications** — **crescent sign or worse** (any age) ± acetabular DJD · **irreversible** cause (chronic steroids) · **>40 yr with large lesion** · most reliable pain relief + return of function

**Typical answers** — ✓ **27-yr-old with subchondral collapse** (✗ Wrong options: IT fracture → nail · non-displaced neck → fixation · compression stress fracture → NWB/screws · young displaced neck → ORIF) · 70-yr-old, chronic steroids, bilateral collapse → ✓ **THA** (✗ Wrong options: fibular graft >40 · osteotomy (collapse) · CD · resurfacing (poor bone)) · 68-yr-old, large MRI necrosis → ✓ **THA** (✗ Wrong options: ORIF · biopsy · resection · arthrodesis)

**Technique** — **cementless cup + stem** · **cemented THA → higher failure** in AVN · **hemiarthroplasty abandoned** (poor in young)

**Sickle cell THA** — ✓ **uncemented MoP THA** best · most common intraop complication ✓ **femoral canal perforation (4.9–18.2%)** — marrow hyperplasia + patchy sclerosis → **ream over guidewire**\
✗ Wrong options: fracture distal to implant · pelvic discontinuity · fat embolism arrest · sciatic injury

**Risk vs OA patients** — ✓ **higher post-op dislocation** · **canal perforation** · medial wall breach · **higher medical + surgical complications** · poor bone + soft-tissue laxity (steroids)

**Young AVN vs elderly OA** — ✓ **↑ PE wear + osteolysis**\
✗ Wrong options: sciatic palsy · ↑ longevity · ↓ ROM · ↓ revision

### Resurfacing contraindication

**Indication** — advanced DJD with **small isolated** focus · needs good bone

**Contraindicated** — **chronic steroids** / underlying disease (poor bone) · **renal disease** (metal ions) · **large necrotic segment**

**Higher failure** — ✓ **osteonecrosis of femoral head** · best results: **young men <55 with OA**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI 32 · acetabular changes (acetabulum resurfaced too) · age <55 · male

**Outcome** — acetabular erosion + pain at medium term

### Other options

**Hip arthrodesis** — only very young **labour-intensive** worker

### Sequelae after femoral neck fracture fixation

**AVN collapse after ORIF (72-yr-old)** → ✓ **THA**\
✗ Wrong options: Girdlestone · hardware removal ± revision ORIF

**Hemiarthroplasty for post-traumatic AVN, groin pain, normal labs, aspirate <500 cells** → ✓ **acetabular protrusio** (erosion) → convert to THA · THA gives better function than hemi for AVN\
✗ Wrong options: infection · radiculopathy · impingement · psoas tendinitis

## X vs Y

| | **Hip AVN** | **ITOH** | **Hip OA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| MRI | **Double-line**, wedge/serpentine | **Diffuse oedema**, no band | Joint-based change |
| Course | Progressive collapse | Self-limited | Progressive |

| | **Steinberg III** | **Ficat 2B (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Sign | **Crescent** | **Crescent, sphericity kept** |

## How it's asked

**"Tibial pain 6 months after free fibula, X-ray negative — confirm?"** → **MRI**

**"Wedge-shaped infarct with head collapse on histology?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Serpentine low-signal T1 band with central fat signal?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Associated with ON of large joints?"** → **antiphospholipid syndrome**

**"28-yr-old, Kerboul 210°, necrotic index 45, no collapse?"** → **CD alone insufficient — vascularized fibula or BMAC** (TO)

**"Sickle cell, asymptomatic MRI ON — % collapsing?"** → **>75%**

**"Kerboul 126° + 94° — angle and risk?"** → **220°, moderate**

**"Most important factor in contralateral asymptomatic hip?"** → **lesion size**

**"MRI finding most linked to pain progression?"** → **bone marrow oedema**

**"Pre-collapse with subchondral lucency — drug shown to ↓ collapse?"** → **bisphosphonate**

**"Bisphosphonate mechanism?"** → **flattens osteoclast ruffled border**

**"41-yr-old steroid AVN THA vs 80-yr-old father — risk?"** → **↑ PE wear + osteolysis**

**"Sickle cell AVN with collapse — best intervention?"** → **uncemented MoP THA**

**"Most common intraop complication of THA in sickle cell?"** → **femoral canal perforation**

**"THA for AVN vs OA — complication to consider?"** → **higher dislocation risk**

**"MoM resurfacing — higher failure because of?"** → **osteonecrosis of head**

**"Best predictor of AVN after paediatric hip fracture?"** → **fracture type + age** (TO)

**"Hemi for AVN 5 yr ago, groin pain, normal aspirate?"** → **acetabular protrusio**

**"Subchondral collapse with maintained sphericity — Ficat–Arlet stage?"** → **2B** (TO)

**"Artery preserved in safe surgical dislocation?"** → **deep branch of MFCA** (TO)

**"True about safe dislocation?"** → **anterior dislocation + anterior arthrotomy** (TO)

**"Not a bone-grafting technique in AVN?"** → **tramtrack** (TO)

# Hip Physical Exam - Adult

*Recon · 11 / 65*

## 3 Presentation

### Inspection

**Skin** — discoloration, wounds, deformity

**Bone** — length vs contralateral · rotation (IR/ER) · flexion contracture

**Gait** — stride, foot rotation, pelvic rotation, stance phase

**Antalgic gait** — **shortened stance phase** on painful side

**Trendelenburg gait** — **abductor weakness** → stance on affected hip → **contralateral hip drops**

### Palpation

| Site | Pain suggests |
|---|---|
| **Greater trochanter** | Bursitis, tendinitis, infection, fracture · **snapping ITB** over GT |
| **ASIS** | **Sartorius** avulsion |
| **Ischial tuberosity** | **Hamstring** avulsion / tendinopathy |
| **Iliac crest** | Oblique avulsion / **hip pointer** |

### Neurovascular

| Motor | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Hip adduction | **Obturator** |
| Thigh abduction | **Superior gluteal** |
| Hip flexion | **Femoral** |
| Hip extension | **Inferior gluteal** |

| Sensory area | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Proximal anteromedial thigh | **Genitofemoral** |
| Inferomedial thigh | **Obturator** |
| Lateral thigh | **LFCN** |
| Anteromedial thigh | **Femoral** |
| Posterior thigh | **Posterior femoral cutaneous** |

**Pulse** — femoral · **reflexes** — none at hip

### Range of motion

| Motion | Normal |
|---|---|
| Flexion | **120–135°** |
| Extension | **20–30°** |
| Abduction | **40–50°** |
| Adduction | **20–30°** |
| Internal rotation | **30°** |
| External rotation | **50°** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Special tests

| Test | Technique | Positive → |
|---|---|---|
| **FADIR** | Flex 90°, **adduct + IR** | Groin pain → **labral tear / FAI** |
| **FABER (Patrick)** | Flex 90°, **abduct + ER** | Hip/back pain, ↓ ROM → intra-articular, **iliopsoas**, **SI joint** (posterior pain) |
| **Log roll** | Passive max IR/ER supine | Click → **labral tear** · ↑ total ROM → **capsular laxity** |
| **Thomas** | Supine, fully flex one hip | Contralateral thigh lifts → **fixed flexion deformity** |
| **Modified Thomas** (TO) | Same at table edge, leg hangs | Measures peak hip extension angle in all |
| **Ober** | Lateral, affected side up, hip slightly extended, abduct then drop | Cannot adduct → **tight ITB** |
| **Stinchfield** | Supine, knee straight, resist hip flexion at **30–45°** | Pain → **intra-articular** pathology |
| **Staheli** (TO) | **Prone** at table edge, hand on pelvis, extend thigh until pelvis rises | **Hip flexion contracture** · measure thigh–horizontal angle |
| **Ely** (TO) | Prone, flex knee to buttock | Pelvis/hip flexes → **short rectus femoris** |

**Staheli conflict (TO)** — one version: ✓ **psoas contracture** (psoas mainly causes hip flexion contracture) · FNB 2020 version without psoas option: ✓ **rectus femoris contracture** — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: quadriceps · hamstring · gastrocnemius

![Thomas test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5037-thomas-test.jpg)

### Clinical tests – Allis, Trendelenburg (TO)

**Galeazzi / Allis test** — supine, **hips 45°, knees 90°**, malleoli together · normal knees level · affected knee **behind** → **femur short** · affected knee **lower** → **tibia short**

**Allis not done for** — ✓ **polio of lower limb**\
✗ Wrong options: DDH · Perthes · SCFE

**Trendelenburg test — opposite (non-tested) limb** — ✓ **foot neutral off the ground, hip slight extension** (flexion may activate iliopsoas)\
✗ Wrong options: plantigrade/hip neutral · hip 30–45° flexion

**Positive sign** — stance on weak side → **opposite pelvis dips** · patient **lurches to the weak (same) side** · bilateral = **waddling gait**

| Abductor mechanism defect (TO) | Causes |
|---|---|
| **Fulcrum** (hip joint) | Dislocation, advanced **TB/septic** hip, **DDH**, Perthes, advanced AVN |
| **Lever arm** (head/neck) | Head/neck fracture, **coxa vara**, trochanteric fracture |
| **Power** (abductors) | **Polio**, **superior gluteal nerve** injury, TFL/ITB palsy, **L5 radiculopathy** |

![Trendelenburg test](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140203-1.png)

### Key radiology findings – MRI for occult fracture

**Occult femoral neck fracture within 24 h** — ✓ **MRI** (NICE 2011) · **CT** second line if MRI contraindicated / not available in 24 h (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PET-CT · repeat X-ray

**Fracture signal** — ✓ **dark on T1, bright on T2** (free water/haemorrhage)\
✗ Wrong options: bright both · dark both · gadolinium

## How it's asked

**"Investigation of choice for occult femoral neck fracture within 24 h?"** → **MRI** (TO)

**"Limited MRI for occult hip fracture — signal?"** → **dark T1, bright T2**

**"Allis test is not done for?"** → **polio** (TO)

**"Trendelenburg test — position of the limb not being tested?"** → **foot off ground, hip slight extension** (TO)

**"Staheli test is done for?"** → **psoas (hip flexion) contracture**; if psoas absent → **rectus femoris** (TO)

**"Thomas test detects?"** → **fixed flexion deformity**

**"Ober test detects?"** → **tight ITB**

**"Stinchfield positive means?"** → **intra-articular pathology**

**"FADIR positive suggests?"** → **labral tear / FAI**

**"Antalgic gait?"** → **short stance phase** on painful side

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Resurfacing

*Recon · 12 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### History & design

**Early failures** — large head on **polyethylene** → ↑ **volumetric wear** → osteolysis

**Modern (FDA 2006, Birmingham — McMinn)** — **metal-on-metal** + **large femoral head** · popular in young (less femoral resection) · cemented femoral cap

![Birmingham hip resurfacing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5032-birmingham-hip-prosthesis.jpg)

### Advantages & disadvantages

| Advantages | Disadvantages |
|---|---|
| **Preserves proximal femoral bone** ✓ (only proven advantage) | **No modularity** — cannot adjust **length or offset** |
| **Better stability** vs 22–32 mm THA · **dislocation <1%** (0.75% at 3 yr) | **Larger exposure** / soft-tissue release |
| Restores biomechanics · **lower LLD risk** · physiologic stress transfer | **Femoral neck fracture** (unique) |
| Easier revision than IM THA | **↑ metal ions**, metallosis, pseudotumour |
| Suits **proximal femoral deformity** | ↑ AVN of head · ↑ reoperation |

**vs THA** — ✓ **preservation of proximal femoral bone**\
✗ Wrong options: better compliance · more sport · more mobility · better pain relief

**vs large-head MoM THA** — ✓ **femoral bone preservation**\
✗ Wrong options: lower loosening · acetabular preservation · lower reoperation · lower wear (**same bearing**)

**Disadvantages EXCEPT** — ✓ **higher dislocation rate** (it is lower) · true disadvantages: ↑ periprosthetic fracture, ↑ metal ions, ↑ osteonecrosis, larger incision

## 5 Management

### Indications & contraindications

**Indications (controversial)** — advanced arthritis + **good proximal femoral bone** · best in **young, large males with OA** · proximal femoral deformity making THA hard

**TO indications** — ✓ **active men <60 with OA** (or post-traumatic) · male labourers who squat · prerequisite: near-normal proximal femur, **¾ of head intact** after contouring\
✗ Wrong options: osteopenia · AVN >30% head · malunited trochanter

| Absolute | Relative |
|---|---|
| **Femoral head/neck bone-stock deficiency** — **cysts** (1.5 cm) | **Coxa vara** (↑ neck fracture) |
| Small / bone-deficient **acetabulum** | **Significant LLD** (cannot correct) |
| | **Women of child-bearing age** (ions cross placenta; ↑ complications) |
| | **Renal failure** (cannot excrete ions) |
| | **Osteoporosis** (TO) · **ON >20–30% of head** (TO) |

**Contraindications EXCEPT** — ✓ **age <50**\
✗ Wrong options: head cysts · acetabular dysplasia · coxa vara · neck bone deficiency

**Osteonecrosis — conflict** — OB: **not a contraindication** if proximal bone stock preserved · OB (large collapse): **higher failure** → THA safer · TO: ON **>20–30%** head = contraindication

**38-yr-old woman, AVN with mild collapse — true?** → ✓ **female sex → ↑ complications** · Canadian series: **96.4% 5-yr survival**, failures = neck fracture + femoral loosening\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ ions = well-functioning · ON absolute contraindication · ↑ LLD · <50% success (success **>90%**)

**Outcomes** — **79–98%** success · better in young large males with **OA** than dysplasia/ON · some products withdrawn · recent RCTs: few differences vs THA

### Tönnis grading (TO)

**Used for** — ✓ **arthritis grading for hip-preservation surgery** · grade 0 none · 1 sclerosis, minimal JSN · 2 small cysts, moderate JSN · 3 large cysts, obliteration, head deformity\
✗ Wrong options: infection · FAI grading · resurfacing

## 6 Complications

### Femoral neck fracture – early reoperation

**Incidence** — **0–4%** (~1%) · **> THA**

**Timing** — **most common early complication** (first 3 yr) · ✓ **most common cause of revision <20 weeks / first 6 months** · unique vs THA → ✓ **transcervical neck fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: abductor rupture · dislocation · HO · stiffness · loosening · infection · pseudotumour

**Mechanism** — **osteonecrosis** of retained head · vertical line neck → lesser trochanter · groin pain

**Risk factors** — **femoral neck notching** (superior) · **varus positioning** ✓ · **osteoporotic** bone · large preexisting **AVN / cystic bone loss** · **incomplete seating** · neck impingement from malaligned cup (e.g. **excessive retroversion**) · **female** · older age · obesity\
✗ Wrong options: sclerotic

**Not a risk factor** — ✓ **excessive acetabular inclination** (EXCEPT question)

**Prevention** — femoral component in **slight valgus** (~10° relative valgus ↑ load to fracture) · avoid notching

**Treatment** — **convert to primary THA** · **cerclage above lesser trochanter** before stem insertion

![Femoral neck notching](../../MCQ/attachments/q215-illb-noching.jpg)

### Metal ions, pseudotumour & ARMD

**Metallosis** — ↑ **cobalt, chromium**, Co:Cr ratio in blood + urine · mostly **edge loading** · cup in **less abduction** than standard THA

**Causes of metallosis** — ✓ **edge loading** · malposition · third-body wear · impingement · cobalt sensitivity\
✗ Wrong options: activity-related wear · psoas tendinitis · PJI · stress fracture

**Mimics infection** — synovial WBC falsely ↑ by metal debris/corrosion → ✓ **manual cell count** next · also MARS MRI, ion levels, repeat aspiration · PJI + metallosis can coexist\
✗ Wrong options: 2-stage revision · isolated cup/stem revision · arthrodesis

**Pseudotumour** — solid/fluid periprosthetic mass (TO) · pain, swelling, DVT, abductor destruction · risk: ✓ **women ≤40**, **small components**, **dysplasia** · may be asymptomatic · symptomatic → **revision to non-MoM** bearing\
✗ Wrong options: large components · men ≥40 · primary OA

**Pseudotumour revision** — **poor prognosis** ✓ · soft-tissue/nerve destruction, high re-revision\
✗ Wrong options: neck fracture · ON · femoral loosening (revise easily to stem)

**Revision with pseudotumour, abductors intact, cup well placed** → ✓ **revise femoral component to stem + non-MoM bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: I&D + spacer · PE liner exchange (no PE) · MoM THA · constrained liner

**ALVAL** — **aseptic lymphocyte-dominant** vasculitis-associated lesion\
✗ Wrong options: "macrophage-dominant"

![Pseudotumour around resurfacing](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17907825-2.jpg)

### Other complications

**Complications EXCEPT** — ✓ **polyethylene debris** (MoM — no PE) · true: DVT, neck fracture, aseptic loosening, dislocation

**Aseptic loosening** — early loosening of **cemented femoral component** → convert to THA

**Heterotopic ossification** — **↑ vs THA** (wider exposure)

**Hemi-resurfacing** — acetabulum not resurfaced → **acetabular erosion / groin pain**

## X vs Y

| | **Hip resurfacing (MoM)** | **THA (CoP / MoP)** |
|---|---|---|
| Femoral bone | **Preserved** | Resected |
| Dislocation | **<1%** | Higher |
| Early reoperation | **Neck fracture** | Dislocation / infection |
| Unique risks | Neck fracture, **metal ions, pseudotumour, ALVAL** | PE wear (MoP) |
| Length/offset | **Fixed** | Adjustable |
| Best patient | **Young large male, OA** | Older, women, poor bone |

## How it's asked

**"Indication for hip resurfacing?"** → **active man <60 with OA** (TO)

**"Contraindications EXCEPT?"** → **age <50**

**"Disadvantages of resurfacing EXCEPT?"** → **higher dislocation rate**

**"Advantage vs THA?"** → **proximal femoral bone preservation**

**"Advantage vs large-head MoM THA?"** → **femoral bone preservation**

**"38-yr-old woman with AVN considering resurfacing — true?"** → **female sex ↑ complications**

**"Most common cause of revision <20 weeks?"** → **periprosthetic (femoral neck) fracture**

**"Neck fracture 2 months after resurfacing — risk factor?"** → **varus positioning**

**"Uniquely at risk vs father's CoP THA?"** → **transcervical femoral neck fracture**

**"Neck fracture risk EXCEPT?"** → **excessive acetabular inclination**

**"Masses around trochanter on MRI after resurfacing?"** → **pseudotumour** (TO)

**"Complications EXCEPT?"** → **polyethylene debris**

**"Poor prognosis after revising failed resurfacing?"** → **pseudotumour**

**"High risk of pseudotumour?"** → **women ≤40**

**"Synovial WBC 15,000, ↑ Co/Cr — next step?"** → **manual cell count**

**"Runner with ↑ Co/Cr after BHR — cause?"** → **edge loading**

**"Pseudotumour, abductors intact — surgery?"** → **revise femoral component, non-MoM bearing**

**"Tönnis grading is used for?"** → **arthritis grading before hip preservation** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Idiopathic Transient Osteoporosis of the Hip (ITOH)

*Recon · 13 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare · **men > women (3 : 1)** · rare in Asians

**Two groups** — **middle-aged men (40–55)** (OB: sixth decade) · **women in 3rd trimester of pregnancy** · also non-pregnant women (TO)

**Laterality** — **unilateral** · may **recur**

### Pathogenesis

**Mechanism** — local **hyperaemia** + impaired **venous return** → **marrow oedema** → ↑ intramedullary pressure

**Synonyms** — bone marrow oedema syndrome · regional migratory osteoporosis · migratory osteolysis

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — progressive **atraumatic hip/groin pain** over weeks · sudden onset in some (TO) · may not bear weight · pain with WB + rest

**Exam** — local tenderness · ✓ **hip ROM often normal** (preserved except at extremes) · antalgic gait

### Genes & lab markers

**ESR** — **elevated** (Orthobullets) · OB stem: WBC, ESR, aspiration **normal** → still ITOH

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs (AP + lateral)** — lag clinical signs **4–8 wk** · **diffuse osteopenia of head + neck** · subchondral cortical loss · effusion · **joint space always preserved** · may be normal early

**MRI (modality of choice)** — **diffuse marrow oedema of head + neck** · **T1 ↓ signal** (loss of fatty marrow) · **T2 / STIR ↑ signal** · effusion · **no focal subchondral defect**, no double line

**Bone scan** — **homogeneous ↑ uptake** of head + neck · **precedes X-ray change**

**Mimic** — often mistaken for **metastasis** (no focal mass on MRI) · early subchondral bone formation favours ITOH (not seen in ON)

![ITOH — T2 marrow oedema](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9036-pic3-moved.jpg)

### Diagnosis of exclusion

**Exclude** — femoral neck **stress fracture** · **infection** · **malignancy** · **AVN**

### ITOH vs avascular necrosis

| | **ITOH** | **AVN** |
|---|---|---|
| Sex | **Male 3:1** | **Equal** (ITOH table) · male > female (AVN note) |
| Age | **3rd-trimester women, middle-aged men** | **20–40** (ITOH table) · 35–50 (AVN note) |
| Laterality | **Unilateral** | **Bilateral >50%** (80% per AVN note) |
| Risk factors | **Pregnancy** | **Alcohol, steroids** |
| Symptoms | Acute, improves with PWB | Insidious, progressive |
| Exam | Preserved except extremes | **Marked ↓ ROM, esp. IR** |
| Radiographs | **Diffuse osteopenia** head + neck at 4–6 wk | **Localized sclerosis, crescent, collapse** |
| Bone scan | **Homogeneous** ↑ head + neck | **Localized** |
| MRI | **Diffuse oedema, no focal/subchondral defect** · ↓ T1, ↑ T2 | **Focal anterosuperior subchondral defect**, double line · ↓ T1 + T2 band |
| Treatment | **Protected WB, symptomatic** | Early surgery |
| Prognosis | **Self-limiting** | Progressive |

![ITOH — bone scan](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9036-bone-scan-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Diagnosis & protected weight bearing

**First line** — ✓ **protected / partial / toe-touch weight bearing** + supportive care + **NSAIDs**, mild analgesics → prevents **stress fracture**

**Natural history** — **resolves spontaneously in 6–8 months**

**Avoid** — core decompression · vascularized fibula · intraosseous steroid (that is for simple bone cysts) · oral steroids · hemiarthroplasty · THA · PAO · biopsy · pinning · resection/megaprosthesis (malignancy) · radiation · antibiotics (osteomyelitis)

## X vs Y

| | **ITOH** | **Femoral neck stress fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| MRI | **Diffuse** head + neck oedema | **Fracture line** with oedema |
| Treatment | Protected WB | NWB / fixation by site |

## How it's asked

**"37-yr-old woman, sudden groin pain, MRI marrow oedema, no collapse?"** → **toe-touch WB + supportive care** (TO)

**"58-yr-old man, diffuse STIR signal through head and neck?"** → **protected WB + NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression · fibula · hemi/THA

**"Normal labs + aspirate, diffuse ↓ T1 / ↑ T2?"** → **protected WB + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · pinning

**"63-yr-old woman, normal X-ray/CT, marrow replaced on T1?"** → **partial WB + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: megaprosthesis · radiation · antibiotics · core decompression

**"Pregnant (30 weeks), groin pain, ITOH — true?"** → **hip ROM often normal**\
✗ Wrong options: bilateral · needs surgery after birth · focal subchondral defects · late joint degeneration

**"Time to resolution?"** → **6–8 months**

**"Imaging of choice?"** → **MRI**

**"Joint space in ITOH?"** → **always preserved**

# Knee Biomechanics

*Recon · 14 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Joint type (TO)

**Knee** — ✓ **ginglymus (hinge)** synovial joint · two joints: **tibiofemoral + patellofemoral**\
✗ Wrong options: condyloid · saddle · pivot

| Synovial type | Example |
|---|---|
| Ball & socket | Hip, glenohumeral |
| **Hinge (ginglymus)** | **Elbow, knee, IP joints** |
| Saddle | Thumb CMC |
| Plane | ACJ |
| Pivot | Atlantoaxial, proximal radioulnar |
| Condyloid (ellipsoid) | MCP, wrist |

**Fibrous** — sutures, gomphoses, syndesmoses (middle radioulnar/tibiofibular) · **cartilaginous** — synchondroses (physis, 1st rib), symphyses (pubis, disc)

### Patellofemoral mechanics

**Function** — transmits quadriceps tension to patellar tendon · **↑ extensor lever arm** · **patellectomy ↓ extension force 30%**

**Lever arm (TO)** — ✓ **greatest at 20–30° flexion → quadriceps force needed is minimum** · force ↑ markedly in **last 20° of extension** (less patella in trochlea)

**Motion** — sliding · patella moves **7 cm caudally** in full flexion · **maximum PF contact at 45° flexion**

| PFJRF | Orthobullets | TO |
|---|---|---|
| Walking | — | **0.5–1 × BW** |
| Vertical jump | — | **2.4–4.6 × BW** |
| Stairs | **Descending 2–3 × BW** | **3.5–6 × BW** (descent > ascent) |
| Running | — | **4.5–7 × BW** |
| Squat | **up to 7 × BW** | **7 ×**; **deep squat up to 20 × BW** |

**TO ascending order** — ✓ **walking < jumping < stair ascent < stair descent < running < deep squat** · PFJRF **↑ with ↑ flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: more going up stairs · less with more flexion

![Extensor lever arm vs flexion angle](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17747143-2.png)

### Patellar stability

| Restraint to lateral translation | Share |
|---|---|
| **MPFL** — primary at **20° flexion** | **60%** |
| Medial patellomeniscal ligament | **13%** |
| Medial retinaculum | **10%** |
| Dynamic | Quadriceps |

### Q angle

**Definition** — ASIS → centre of patella → tibial tuberosity

**Normal** — Orthobullets: extension **male 13°, female 18°**, flexion **8°** · TO: **male 14°, female 17°** (standing)

**↑ Q angle (TO)** — ✓ **↑ femoral anteversion** · **external tibial torsion** · **laterally displaced tubercle** · **genu valgum** · ↑ lateral pull → PF instability\
✗ Wrong options: medialized tubercle · internal tibial torsion · genu varum

![Q angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9065-q-angle.jpg)

### Tibiofemoral mechanics

**TFJRF** — **3 × BW walking** · **4 × BW climbing**

**ROM** — **3° hyperextension → 155° flexion** · limited by thigh–calf contact · **normal gait needs 0–70°**

## 2 Mechanism

### Knee kinematics – rollback, rotation

**Instant centre of rotation** — contact point · **polycentric**

**Medial pivot** — medial femoral contact **relatively stationary** · lateral contact **moves posteriorly** with flexion (**rollback**) → avoids impingement, ↑ flexion · ✓ **medial condyle translates much less than lateral**\
✗ Wrong options: equal · lateral less

**Beyond 120°** — **both** condyles roll back

**Tibial rotation with flexion** — ✓ **tibia internally rotates** · initial IR in first **~20°**, maintained, then **more IR past 90°** · **tibia ER with extension**\
✗ Wrong options: femur IR

**Paradoxical motion** — ✓ **contact point moves anteriorly during flexion** (ACL-deficient CR TKA fails to roll back)

### Screw-home mechanism

**Orthobullets** — **tibia ER 5° in last 15° of extension** · cause: **medial tibial plateau longer** than lateral · locks knee → ↓ quadriceps work standing

**TO** — **open chain** (foot in air): tibia **ER ~10° in last 30°** of extension · **closed chain** (foot planted): **femur IR ~10°** · factors: **medial femoral condyle longer** (30° lateral curve → 10–15° tibial ER in last 20°) + quadriceps lateral pull tensions ACL

**Unlocking** — **popliteus** · open chain: ✓ **tibia IR** to allow flexion · closed chain: ✓ **femur ER** during flexion

**TO answers** — ✓ "tibia must IR slightly to flex in open chain" · ✓ "with flexion normal tibia rotates internally" · ✓ "foot plantigrade — femur ER during flexion"\
✗ Wrong options: tibia IR in last 30° of extension · "brought by cruciates" (mainly bony anatomy)

![Screw-home mechanism](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9065-screw-home-mechanism.jpg)

### Ligament restraints

| Load | Primary restraint |
|---|---|
| **Varus** | **LCL** |
| **Valgus** | **Superficial MCL** |
| **Anterior translation** | **ACL** — AM bundle **tight in flexion**, PL bundle **tight in extension** · also axial rotation |
| **Posterior translation** | **PCL** — AL bundle **tight in flexion**, PM **tight in extension** |
| **External rotation** | **Posterolateral corner** |
| **Internal rotation** | **ALL + Kaplan fibres** (TO) |

**ACL** — origin **posteromedial lateral femoral condyle** (semicircular) → insertion **anterior to / between tibial eminences**

**PCL** — origin **anterolateral medial femoral condyle** → **tibial sulcus** below articular surface

**Kaplan fibres (TO)** — deep ITB → distal femur · proximal bundle **53.6 mm**, distal **31.4 mm** above lateral epicondyle (distal near **superior lateral genicular artery**) · ✓ **restrain internal rotation** · sectioning ↑ IR at **15–90°** (esp. 60–90°) vs ALL (15–30°) · **co-dominant with ALL** in anterolateral stability · control pivot shift\
✗ Wrong options: ER · anteromedial stability · recurvatum

### Injury mechanism – valgus collision (TO)

**Foot planted, hit on lateral distal leg** → ✓ **forced flexion + valgus → ACL tear**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated valgus MCL · varus LCL

**ACL** — **non-contact ~70%** · flexion-valgus + femoral adduction + tibial ER + ankle eversion (cutting) · stiff landing (<20° flexion) · deceleration · hyperextension · risk: poor core, weak hip abduction, ↑ valgus, heel-strike landing

**MCL** — **direct blow to lateral knee (valgus)** — commonest

**PCL** — **dashboard** (posterior force on flexed tibia) · **fall on flexed knee with foot plantarflexed** · hyperflexion · hyperextension (± ACL)

### Loading & rehabilitation forces

**Landing (basketball)** — ✓ **females → greater total valgus knee loading**

**Anterior tibial translation** — ✓ **terminal non-weight-bearing (open-chain) extension 60° → 0°** → avoid early after ACLR\
✗ Wrong options: WB extension/flexion · NWB flexion

### Terminal extension – VMO activity (TO)

**Open chain, terminal extension (15–20°) + tibia medially rotated** → ✓ **VMO maximal**\
✗ Wrong options: VL · VI · RF

**Closed chain** — **squats with external rotation** → max VMO

## How it's asked

**"Knee joint is an example of?"** → **ginglymus** (TO)

**"True about screw-home mechanism?"** → **tibia must internally rotate to flex in open chain** (TO)

**"Screw home — with flexion the normal tibia?"** → **rotates internally** (TO)

**"Foot plantigrade — during flexion?"** → **femur rotates externally** (TO)

**"Normal tibiofemoral kinematics?"** → **tibia internally rotates with flexion**

**"Paradoxical motion?"** → **contact point moves anteriorly with flexion**

**"Tibial rotation through flexion?"** → **initial IR, maintained, more IR in deep flexion**

**"Medial vs lateral condyle translation?"** → **medial translates much less**

**"Kaplan fibres restrain?"** → **internal rotation** (TO)

**"Gender and landing?"** → **females — greater valgus loading**

**"Exercise causing anterior tibial translation?"** → **terminal NWB knee extension**

**"Foot planted, hit on lateral distal leg?"** → **flexion + valgus → ACL tear** (TO)

**"Patellofemoral — at 20–30° flexion?"** → **lever arm greatest, quadriceps force minimum** (TO)

**"Q angle increased by?"** → **femoral anteversion** (TO)

**"Ascending PFJRF?"** → **walking < jumping < stair ascent < descent < running < deep squat** (TO)

**"Terminal extension + tibial IR — max activity?"** → **VMO** (TO)

**"Primary restraint to lateral patellar translation at 20°?"** → **MPFL (60%)**

**"Patellectomy reduces extension force by?"** → **30%**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Knee Osteoarthritis

*Recon · 15 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence (symptomatic)** — **knee 240 / 100,000 / yr** · hip 88 / 100,000

**Modifiable** — **articular trauma** · occupation (repetitive bending) · muscle weakness · **large body mass** · **metabolic syndrome**

**Non-modifiable** — **female** > male · age · genetics · race

**Metabolic syndrome** — **central obesity**, **dyslipidaemia** (↑ TG, ↑ LDL), **hypertension**, **↑ fasting glucose** · ✓ **PVD is NOT part** · strong link to **knee** (not hip) OA · **BMI most predictive** · no CRP link

### Disparities in arthroplasty

**Race** — ✓ **African-American males have lower TJA rates than white or Hispanic males** · Hispanics < whites · not explained by symptom perception

### Pathophysiology

**Cartilage** — **↑ water**, ↑ **permeability**, ↓ proteoglycan (GAG), disorganized + ↓ collagen, ↑ **chondrocyte activity** incl. ✓ **↑ aggrecanase production**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ GAG · ↓ permeability · ↓ IL-6 · ↓ water movement

**Synovium** — mild → hypervascular → thick + vascular

**Bone** — lytic lesions with **sclerotic edges** (vs RA) · cysts late · subchondral marrow oedema with **~97% ↑ intraosseous pressure**

**Osteophytes** — pathologic **endochondral ossification** via ✓ **Indian hedgehog (Ihh)** · Ihh inactive in normal adult cartilage\
✗ Wrong options: PPARγ (adipocytes) · RANKL · OPG · sclerostin (Wnt)

**Histology** — loss of superficial chondrocytes · **tidemark** replication + breakdown · fissuring · eburnation

### Genes & lab markers

**MMPs** — stromelysin, plasmin, **aggrecanase-1 (ADAMTS-4)** · **TIMPs** inhibit · **MMP/TIMP imbalance**

**Cytokines** — **IL-1** (catabolic), IL-6, **TNF-α**

**Genetics** — **non-Mendelian** · vitamin D receptor, oestrogen receptor 1, IL-1, IL-4, matrilin-3, BMP-2/5

**DMOADs (TO)** — monoclonal antibodies against **ADAMTS (aggrecanase, ADAMTS-5 > 4)**, **nerve growth factor (NGF)**, **VEGF** · ✓ **incorrect: "target fibroblast growth factor"** · anti-NGF: **tanezumab, fasinumab**, fulranumab (stopped 2016) · IV + SC routes (not oral) · AE: **abnormal peripheral sensation** (paraesthesia)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — function-limiting pain (walking distance) · night/rest pain · activity swelling · stiffness · instability, locking, catching

**Exam** — habitus · **limb alignment** · effusion · **flexion contracture >5°** · **flexion <110°** · ligaments

### Biomechanics – adduction moment & gait

**Knee adduction moment** — surrogate of **medial tibiofemoral contact force** · ✓ **↑ in medial compartment OA** (varus — GRF passes medial to knee) · large peak → ↑ pain + progression · ↓ by **offloader braces** · ↑ flexion moment also ↑ medial load

**Not linked to ↑ adductor moment** — cervical myelopathy · hip OA (slow, leans forward, pelvis drops) · ankle OA (↑ double support) · scoliosis

**Antalgic gait in knee OA** — knee held in **slight flexion** (quadriceps avoidance, ↓ capsular tension) · **toe walking** · ↓ stride · ↓ stance time · ✓ **not seen: walking predominantly on the heel**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **weight-bearing** AP · optional **sunrise** + **PA 30° flexion (Rosenberg)**

**Findings** — JSN · osteophytes · eburnation · subchondral sclerosis · cysts · pattern (medial, lateral, PF)

![Rosenberg / notch view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12287-knotch.jpg)

### Kellgren & Lawrence (AP weight-bearing)

| Grade | Findings |
|---|---|
| **0** | No JSN or reactive change |
| **1** | **Doubtful** JSN + possible osteophytic lipping |
| **2** | **Definite osteophytes** + possible JSN |
| **3** | **Moderate multiple osteophytes + definite JSN** + some sclerosis + possible bone-end deformity |
| **4** | **Large osteophytes + marked JSN + severe sclerosis + definite deformity** |

**Original** — coal miners, **1952** · grade ≤2 none/possible OA, >2 definite

![Kellgren–Lawrence grade 3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12287-kellgren-3.jpg)

### Loose bodies & radiographic signs (TO)

**Most common source of loose body in knee** — ✓ **osteophytes (OA)** · young → **OCD** · multiple → **synovial chondromatosis**\
✗ Wrong options: damaged menisci

**Grand piano sign** — ✓ **knee**: anterior femoral resection surface in TKA → marker of **correct femoral rotation**

![Grand piano sign](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17105903-1.png)

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic knee OA
├─ All → exercise + education + oral/topical NSAIDs (strong)
├─ BMI >25 → weight loss · medial OA → unloader brace (moderate)
├─ Persisting → IA steroid (moderate) · RFA (genicular) · PRP > HA
├─ Young, unicompartmental + malalignment → HTO (varus) / DFO (valgus)
└─ Failed nonop, end-stage → TKA (UKA if isolated compartment)
```

### AAOS guidelines – nonoperative management

| Strength | 2021/2022 CPG (OB) |
|---|---|
| **Strong FOR** | **Exercise** (supervised, self-managed, aquatic) · **patient education** · **oral + topical NSAIDs** · **oral acetaminophen** |
| **Moderate FOR** | **Weight loss** (BMI >25) · **IA corticosteroid** · **unloader brace** · cane |
| **Limited FOR** | **PRP** · acupuncture, massage · supplements (turmeric, ginger, glucosamine, chondroitin, vitamin D) · **denervation (RFA)** |
| **Moderate AGAINST** | **IA hyaluronic acid** (NNT 17) · **arthroscopic lavage/debridement** |
| **Strong AGAINST** | **Oral narcotics incl. tramadol** · **lateral wedge insoles** |

| Modality | Older CPG positions |
|---|---|
| HA | 2009 inconclusive → **2013 strong against** (weakest evidence) → 2021 moderate against |
| IA steroid | 2013 **inconclusive** (for/against) → 2021 moderate for |
| Tramadol | 2013 **recommended** → 2021 **strong against** |
| 2008 CPG | **Low-impact aerobic exercise "A"** · glucosamine/chondroitin "A" against · arthroscopic debridement without mechanical symptoms "A" against · **lateral heel wedge "B" against** · valgus brace inconclusive |
| Orthobullets list | Acupuncture strong against · glucosamine/chondroitin strong against · viscosupplement moderate against · needle lavage moderate against · lateral wedge moderate against · **arthroscopic debridement/lavage strong against** · meniscal debridement inconclusive |
| Acetaminophen | Max dose **3000 mg/24 h** (was 4000) |

**Not recommended (TO)** — ✓ **HA injection**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs · weight loss · self-management + exercise

**Exercise** — supervised + home best · **benefit lost 6 months after stopping**

**Medial OA brace** — ✓ **medial unloader (valgus)** for correctable varus\
✗ Wrong options: varus brace · Cho-Pat (patellar tendon) · ACL brace · sleeve

### Injections & orthobiologics

**IA steroid** — moderate for · short-term relief · **avoid repeat soon** (last 3 weeks ago, 10-day relief → discuss TKA)

**HA** — rooster-comb preparations → **contraindicated with chicken/egg allergy** ✓ · also joint/skin infection · no anti-inflammatory effect

**PRP** — ✓ **better clinical outcomes than HA** (**level 1** RCTs, meta-analyses) · best in **KL 2–3** (not KL 4) · **leukocyte-poor > leukocyte-rich** (more effective, safer) · injection site irrelevant · AAOS **limited** (downgraded)

**BMAC** — ✓ **highest IL-1 receptor antagonist** concentration (> LP-PRP, LR-PRP, HA)

**Genicular RFA** — ablates **superior lateral, superior medial, inferior medial** genicular nerves · ✓ **superior to corticosteroid** (RCTs) and to HA · safe · OK in **diabetes**, renal, CV, GI risk

**Glucosamine** — ✓ **substrate for proteoglycan** (chondroitin sulfate + hyaluronate)\
✗ Wrong options: COX-2 · viscosity · pain mediator · anti-apoptosis

### Arthroscopy

**Lavage/debridement vs placebo** — ✓ **no significant benefit** in chronic OA (1 wk–2 yr) · only for **mechanical** symptoms (meniscal tear, loose body)

### Surgical options – osteotomy, TKA

**HTO** — young, **medial** unicompartmental OA · AAOS limited evidence · ideal: **thin, active, stable, <60** → ✓ **outcomes approach TKA**\
✗ Wrong options: HTO faster recovery · more complete relief · better in old

**DFO** — 25-yr-old, **valgus + lateral JSN** → ✓ **varus-producing DFO** · most delay TKA ≥10 yr\
✗ Wrong options: varus HTO (joint-line obliquity) · lateral UKA · TKA · meniscectomy

**UKA** — isolated unicompartmental disease · **TKA has lower revision rates than UKA** · not tricompartmental

**TKA** — symptomatic OA + failed nonop · 70-yr-old with meniscal tear + tricompartmental change → ✓ **TKA** (✗ Wrong options: meniscectomy · HTO · UKA) · 62-yr-old, diffuse pain, no mechanical symptoms → ✓ **arthroplasty** (✗ Wrong options: PRP · meniscectomy · chondroplasty · allopurinol)

**TKA technique facts** — **CR = PS outcomes** · **patellar resurfacing**: no difference in pain/function, **↓ reoperation** · **drains not recommended** · **sensor-balancing device**: ✓ **no added benefit** in ROM/PROMs at 2 yr, ↑ time + cost

## X vs Y

| | **OA cartilage** | **Ageing cartilage** |
|---|---|---|
| Water | **↑** | ↓ |
| Chondrocyte activity / aggrecanases | **↑** | Unchanged |
| Collagen | ↓, disorganized | Normal |

| | **PRP** | **HA** | **BMAC** |
|---|---|---|---|
| AAOS 2021 | Limited for | **Moderate against** | — |
| Evidence | **Level 1 > HA** | Rooster comb (egg allergy) | **Highest IL-1ra** |

## How it's asked

**"Not recommended by AAOS for knee OA?"** → **HA injection** (TO)

**"Weakest evidence (2013 CPG)?"** → **IA hyaluronic acid**

**"Arthroscopic lavage vs placebo?"** → **no significant benefit**

**"48-yr-old, early medial OA, normal X-ray — initial treatment?"** → **low-impact aerobic exercise**

**"61-yr-old, BMI 31, early medial OA?"** → **weight loss + low-impact aerobic exercise**

**"Least evidence (2021)?"** → **lateral wedge insoles**

**"Strong evidence (2021)?"** → **supervised exercise programme**\
✗ Wrong options: tramadol · HA

**"Most strongly recommended (5° varus, moderate–severe OA)?"** → **NSAIDs**

**"BMI 38, failed glucosamine + home exercise — strongly recommended next?"** → **NSAIDs**

**"Recommended and strongly supported (2022)?"** → **exercise**

**"Recommended AGAINST (2021)?"** → **arthroscopic lavage + debridement**

**"AAOS on HA?"** → **moderate recommendation against**

**"Incorrect about DMOADs?"** → **target fibroblast growth factor** (TO)

**"Glucosamine works as?"** → **substrate for proteoglycan**

**"Systemic reaction to HA — also allergic to?"** → **chicken or eggs**

**"Highest IL-1ra?"** → **BMAC**

**"Highest level of evidence PRP > HA?"** → **level 1**

**"RFA — true?"** → **superior to corticosteroid**

**"PRP vs HA — true?"** → **PRP better clinical outcomes**

**"Metabolic syndrome EXCEPT?"** → **PVD**

# Ligaments of the Knee

*Recon · 16 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Ligament function overview

| Ligament | Primary | Secondary |
|---|---|---|
| **ACL** | Resists **anterolateral** tibial displacement | **Varus** at 0° (full extension) |
| **PCL** | Resists **posterior** tibial displacement, esp. **90°** | **Varus** at 0° |
| **LCL** | Resists **varus at 30°** | Posterolateral rotation at flexion **<~50°** |
| **PLC / popliteofibular** | Resists **posterolateral rotation** | Varus + posterior displacement |
| **MCL** | Resists **valgus** | Axial rotation (with ACL) |

### Rotational stabilizers (TO)

| Rotation | Main stabilizer |
|---|---|
| **Internal rotation 60–90°** | Anterolateral structures — **ITB** most significant |
| **Internal rotation near full extension** | ✓ **Posterior oblique ligament** (✗ Wrong options: LCL · popliteus/PFL · ACL+MCL) |
| **External rotation 60–90°** | **PLC — LCL, popliteus, popliteofibular** · ✓ **LCL = primary stabilizer to ER** (TO) (✗ Wrong options: ACL · PCL · MCL) |

### Layered anatomy

| Lateral layer | Contents |
|---|---|
| **1** | **ITB, biceps femoris** |
| *between 1 and 2* | ***Common peroneal nerve*** |
| **2** | Patellar (lateral) retinaculum |
| **3 superficial** | **LCL**, fabellofibular ligament, **ALL** |
| *between superficial and deep 3* | ***Lateral geniculate artery*** |
| **3 deep** | Arcuate ligament, coronary ligament, **popliteus tendon, popliteofibular ligament**, capsule |

| Medial layer | Contents |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Sartorius** + fascia (medial retinaculum) |
| *between 1 and 2* | ***Gracilis, semitendinosus, saphenous nerve*** |
| **2** | **Superficial MCL**, **MPFL**, **semimembranosus**, POL |
| **3** | **Deep MCL**, capsule, coronary ligament |

**Medial quadruple complex (TO)** — **MCL, semimembranosus, pes anserinus (ST, gracilis, sartorius), POL** · ✓ **not medial meniscus**

**Not attached to medial femoral condyle (TO)** — ✓ **popliteus** (lateral condyle, popliteal notch → posterior tibia above soleal line) · MFC attachments: MCL, MPFL, adductor magnus, PCL

**ITB (TO)** — **moves forward in extension, backward in flexion**, **tense in both** · in flexion **ITB, popliteus, LCL cross**; **ITB + biceps stay parallel**\
✗ Wrong options: lax in flexion · ITB/LCL parallel

### ACL

**Anatomy** — **extrasynovial, intracapsular** · origin **lateral femoral condyle** (medial wall, behind **resident's ridge**) · PL origin **posterior + distal** to AM · insertion broad, **anterior to / between intercondylar eminences** · **33 × 11 mm**

**AM bundle** — **tight in flexion**, loose in extension (Orthobullets/OB) · TO: **tight in both** flexion and extension · fibres parallel in extension, ER in flexion · **longer** · tibial attachment **anterior** to PL

**PL bundle** — **tight in extension**, loose in flexion · **prevents pivot shift** + IR near extension · shorter · isolated PL cut → ✓ **↑ translation + rotation at 30°**

**Blood supply** — **middle geniculate artery** · **innervation** — posterior articular branches of **tibial nerve** · mechanoreceptors (Ruffini, Pacini, GTO, free endings) → **proprioception**, quadriceps modulation

**Composition** — **90% type I, 10% type III** collagen

**Tensile strength** — **native ACL 2200 N < BPTB 3000 N < quadrupled hamstring 4000 N** · TO false statement: ✓ "native ACL > BPTB"

![ACL bundles in flexion and extension](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3001-acl-bundles-flexion-extension.jpg)

### PCL

**Anatomy** — **extrasynovial, intracapsular** · origin **medial femoral condyle** · insertion **tibial sulcus** · **38 × 13 mm** · strength **2500 N** · middle geniculate artery\
✗ Wrong options: intrasynovial

**AL bundle** — **shorter, thicker, stronger** · **tight in flexion** · double-bundle: tension **mid-flexion**

**PM bundle** — longer, thinner, weaker · **tight in extension** · tension in extension + high flexion (protects against **hyperextension**)

**Ridges** — **medial intercondylar ridge** = proximal border of femoral footprint · **medial bifurcate ridge** separates AL/PM

| | ACL | PCL |
|---|---|---|
| **Tight in flexion** | **AM** | **AL** |
| **Tight in extension** | **PL** | **PM** |

### Meniscofemoral ligaments

**Course** — **posterior horn lateral meniscus → medial femoral condyle** (lateral wall), into PCL substance

**Humphrey** — **anterior** to PCL · **Wrisberg** — **posterior** to PCL (more common) · one present in **71–100%**

**MRI** — sagittal: **small ovoid hypointense structures** just anterior (Humphrey) / posterior (Wrisberg) to PCL · coronal: hypointense band parallel to PCL · ✓ arrow = **ligament of Humphrey**\
✗ Wrong options: PCL · bucket-handle tear · ACL · intermeniscal ligament

![Meniscofemoral ligaments on MRI](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17122967-2.png)

### LCL

**Anatomy** — origin LFC **posterior + superior to popliteus** origin · runs **superficial to popliteus** · inserts **fibula anterior to popliteofibular ligament** · cord-like

**Biomechanics** — **tight in extension**, lax in flexion · **750 N**

### Posterolateral corner

**Function** — with PCL controls **external rotation + posterior translation**

**Structures** — **LCL (295 N)** · **popliteus (680 N)** · **popliteofibular ligament (229 N)** · lateral capsule · variable: **arcuate ligament**, ITB, fabellofibular ligament

**Dial test** — ✓ **↑ ER at 30° only → PLC** · ↑ at **30° + 90° → PLC + PCL**

**Posterior capsule** — closest structure ✓ **popliteal artery** · superficial → deep (lateral → medial): **tibial nerve → popliteal vein → popliteal artery** · CPN posterolateral · sural subcutaneous

### MCL

**Superficial MCL (tibial collateral)** — origin **3.2 mm proximal + 4.8 mm posterior** to medial epicondyle ✓ · inserts **periosteum of proximal tibia deep to pes**, ~6 cm below joint · layer 2, deep to gracilis + ST · **primary valgus restraint at all angles** · **57% at 5°, 78% at 25°** of medial stability · **4000 N**

**Deep MCL (medial capsular)** — separated by bursa · **meniscofemoral + meniscotibial** parts · attaches **medial meniscus** (coronary) · with posteromedial capsule = **secondary valgus restraint at full extension**

**Valgus opening 5 mm at 30°, other tests normal** → sMCL → ✓ origin **slightly posterior + proximal to medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: layer 1–2 · Gerdy's (ITB) · popliteal hiatus (popliteus) · Schöttle point (MPFL)

### Posteromedial corner – posterior oblique ligament

**Components** — **semimembranosus** insertion · **POL** · **oblique popliteal ligament** · posterior capsule · posterior horn medial meniscus

**POL** — **adductor tubercle** (just distal/posterior) → posteromedial tibia near articular margin · **posterior to MCL** · three arms (superficial, **central** — most important, capsular) · **tense near full extension**, lax with flexion

**Function** — ✓ **resists valgus + tibial internal rotation near full extension** · secondary restraint to posterior translation if PCL injured · mostly **nonoperative**; ↑ cruciate graft strain in multiligament\
✗ Wrong options: at 60° · ER · anterior translation (ACL) · varus/ER (PLC)

![Posteromedial corner — POL](../../MCQ/attachments/q218218-illa-posteromedial-corner-labeled.jpg)

### MPFL

**Function** — restrains **lateral patellar translation 0–30°** · low tension (**2–10 N**) · **isometric 0–90°**, slack >90° · fails at **200 N** (ACL **1725 N**)

**Anatomy** — **layer 2** · bundles: short oblique (superior pole), inferior straight · femoral: **distal to adductor tubercle, proximal to MCL** · **Schöttle's point** — **1.3 mm anterior** to posterior cortex line, **2.5 mm distal** to posterior origin of MFC, proximal to posterior Blumensaat line · patella: junction proximal–middle thirds of superomedial border

**Tears** — femoral > patellar · 2nd dislocation risk **13%**, 3rd after 2nd **50%**

### Anterolateral ligament

**Anatomy** — **layer 3** with LCL (TO ✓) · femoral **lateral epicondyle** → tibia **midway between Gerdy's tubercle and fibular head** · length **59 mm**, width 7 mm · attached to **lateral meniscus body** (meniscofemoral + meniscotibial) · **lateral inferior genicular vessels** between meniscus and ALL · **no ITB connection** · rotational stability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Avulsion fractures – Segond, arcuate

| Fracture | Structure | Associated |
|---|---|---|
| **Segond** | **ALL** (meniscotibial part), lateral tibial plateau | **ACL tear 75%** · **IR + varus** |
| **Arcuate sign** | **Arcuate complex** — proximal fibula (LCL/biceps on fragment) | **Cruciate injury ~90%** |
| **Reverse Segond** | **Deep MCL** capsular component (medial) | PCL |
| Gerdy's tubercle | ITB | — |

![Segond fracture](../../MCQ/attachments/q5960-illa-illus-a1.jpg)

### Rotatory instabilities (TO)

| Instability | Disrupted | Test |
|---|---|---|
| **Anteromedial** | Medial capsule, MCL, **POL**, ACL | Anterior drawer in 15° ER · valgus laxity at 30° |
| **Anterolateral (90°)** | ALL, ITB, ACL | Pivot shift |
| **Anterolateral (extension)** | ACL (primary) · ITB/Kaplan, lateral meniscus, ALL | **Jerk test, Slocum, MacIntosh pivot shift** |
| **Posterolateral** | LCL, arcuate, popliteus, lateral gastrocnemius ± PCL | **ER-recurvatum**, **reverse pivot shift** · varus thrust + hyperextension gait |
| **Posteromedial** | MCL, medial capsule, POL, PCL, posteromedial capsule, semimembranosus ± ACL | — |

## How it's asked

**"False about knee ligaments?"** → **native ACL stronger than BPTB** (TO)

**"Isolated PL bundle transection?"** → **↑ translation + rotation at 30°**

**"Bundle A anterior on tibia, tight in flexion?"** → **AM bundle; PL loose in flexion**

**"Main stabilizer of IR near full extension?"** → **POL** (TO)

**"Valgus laxity at 30°, injured structure — true?"** → **origin slightly posterior + proximal to medial epicondyle**

**"Resists internal tibial rotation in full extension?"** → **POL**

**"POL — true?"** → **static valgus restraint in full extension**

**"Structure A (posteromedial) — function?"** → **valgus + IR near full extension**

**"Primary stabilizer to ER?"** → **LCL** (TO)

**"Closest to posterior capsule?"** → **popliteal artery**

**"↑ ER at 30° only?"** → **posterolateral corner**

**"Arrow anterior to PCL on MRI?"** → **ligament of Humphrey** (TO)

**"Coronal T1 band from lateral meniscus to MFC?"** → **meniscofemoral ligament**

**"Two avulsions — player A and B?"** → **A = ALL (Segond), B = arcuate complex**

**"True about knee ligaments?"** → **ALL in layer 3 of lateral structures** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PCL intrasynovial · AL tight in extension · AM prevents pivot shift

**"Medial quadruple complex EXCEPT?"** → **medial meniscus** (TO)

**"True about ITB?"** → **forward in extension, backward in flexion** (TO)

**"Not attached to MFC?"** → **popliteus** (TO)

**"Nerve between lateral layers 1 and 2?"** → **common peroneal nerve**
