# Patellar Clunk Syndrome

*Recon · 17 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence** — **3.5%** (mean) · up to **18% (12–21%)** of **PS TKAs** (TO) · rarer with newer designs

**Timing** — average **12 months** (Orthobullets) · **7–11 months** (OB) · **3–12 months**, occasionally up to 3 yr (TO) · more with patellar resurfacing

**First described** — **Hozack 1989** (TO)

### Pathophysiology

**Lesion** — **fibrous nodule at superior pole of patella / posterior surface of distal quadriceps tendon** (suprapatellar, articular side)\
✗ Wrong options: inferior pole

**Mechanism** — nodule **entrapped in intercondylar box during flexion** → **jerked out during active extension** → painful clunk at **30–45°** (~40°)

**Cause** — trauma to quadriceps tendon at patellar resection · impingement of tendon on **sharp anterosuperior edge of the box** · abrupt transition ridge between box and trochlea

### Risk factors

| Category | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Preoperative** | **Patella baja** / short patellar tendon · **valgus** alignment · fibrosynovial proliferation at quadriceps insertion (resect it) · **previous knee surgery** / revision |
| **Patellar component** | **Small** button · placed **low (distal)** — exposes superior pole · **over-resection / thin button** (↓ quadriceps offset) · uncovered cut bone (resect lateral facet) · implant overhang at distal pole |
| **Femoral component** | **PS design** · **↑ posterior condylar offset** · **smaller (undersized) femur** · **flexed** femoral component (posterior IM entry point — anterior femoral bow) · **high intercondylar box ratio** (trochlea extends proximal + anterior) / **larger box** |
| **Other** | **Thick PE insert** (raises joint line → relative baja) · excessive distal femoral resection · **anterior tibial tray** · forced flexion |

**Intercondylar box ratio (ICBR)** — box height ÷ AP height of femoral component · **high ICBR** → earlier quadriceps contact → ↑ clunk · **low ICBR** favourable

**Prevention** — low-ICBR design · side-specific femur (7° oblique groove) · trochlear deepening + lengthening · exact sizing · avoid downsized/flexed femur (**anterior IM entry point**) · avoid anterior tibial tray · patellar button **as proximal as possible, not beyond superior border** · avoid over-resection/thin button · **neutral or posterior tibial tray**

![Patellar clunk — suprapatellar nodule](../../MCQ/attachments/q213959-illc-patellar-clunk-diagram.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — patellofemoral pain · **popping, catching** · may be painless

**Exam** — palpable **"pop"/"catch" as knee extends from flexion at ~40°** (30–45°)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Diagnosis** — mostly **clinical** · **ultrasound** shows suprapatellar fibrous tissue · **MRI** can confirm (TO)

### Takahashi classification (TO)

| Type | Finding |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Isolated suprapatellar fibrous nodule** (true PCS) |
| **II** | Impinging **hypertrophic synovitis**, no nodule |
| **III** | Nodule **+** generalized hypertrophic synovitis |

### Related entities

| Entity | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Patellar clunk** | Discrete nodule · **clunk on extension 30–45°** |
| **Patellofemoral crepitus** | Fibrosynovial hyperplasia **without discrete nodule** · grinding at **30–60°** with loading · 2 months–2 yr |
| **Patellar crunch** | Scar **around the patellar component** · crunching going **extension → flexion** |
| **Intra-articular fibrous band** | Transverse band prevents patellar seating |

## 5 Management

```
Patellar clunk after PS TKA
├─ Mild → observe / quadriceps exercises (20–60% improve, TO)
├─ Severe or failed nonop → arthroscopic nodule resection
└─ Failed arthroscopy / component problem → open excision ± patellar revision
```

### Causes & management

**Conflict** — OB: **nonsurgical management rarely successful → surgical debridement** best ✓ · TO: **conservative quadriceps exercises help 20–60%**, so "conservative has no role, arthroscopy for all" is ✓ **incorrect** · Orthobullets: observe mild, resect severe\
✗ Wrong options: rest/NSAIDs · patellectomy · patellar revision · lateral release/realignment

**Arthroscopic resection** — good outcomes, **very low recurrence**, ↑ Knee Society scores · recurrence rare after arthrotomy + button revision

**Open excision** — failed arthroscopy · planned component revision / realignment · limited lateral incision under local

### Component rotation & patellar maltracking

**Patellar pain/subluxation/dislocation** — ✓ **internal rotation of tibial component** (lateralizes tubercle) · also **IR of femoral component**\
✗ Wrong options: lateralized tibial tray (moves tubercle medially, helps) · lateralized femur (moves trochlea laterally, helps) · femoral ER

**Correct rotation** — femur **parallel to epicondylar axis** at 90° · tibial tray centred on **medial third of tibial tubercle**

## X vs Y

| | **Patellar clunk** | **CAM jump** | **Midflexion instability** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cause | Suprapatellar nodule in PS box | **Large flexion gap** in PS knee | Elevated joint line, multiradius design, MCL laxity |

## How it's asked

**"True about PCS?"** → **undersized femoral component ↑ risk** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: more common in CR · inferior pole · grinding in flexion

**"PCS occurs?"** → **superior pole, knee extending from flexion** (TO)

**"PCS results from?"** → **scar at superior pole impinging on femoral component during extension** (TO)

**"Incorrect statement on PCS?"** → **conservative treatment has no role** (TO)

**"PS TKA, ↑ posterior offset, small patellar button — complication?"** → **patellar clunk**\
✗ Wrong options: maltracking · midflexion instability · CAM jump

**"Best management (OB)?"** → **surgical debridement**

**"Clunk on extension after PS TKA — associated?"** → **larger femoral box**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior tibial tray · oversized button · proximal button · ↓ offset

**"Patellar pain/dislocation from component orientation?"** → **internal rotation of tibial component**

**"Angle of clunk?"** → **30–45° (~40°) on extension**

# Prosthetic Joint Infection

*Recon · 18 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

| Procedure | Orthobullets / OB | OB (alternative) |
|---|---|---|
| **Primary THA** | **0.3–1.3%** | 1.7% |
| **Primary TKA** | **1–2%** | 1–3% · 2.5% |
| **Revision THA** | **3–4%** | 3–5% |
| **Revision TKA** | **5–6%** (highest) | 5–10% |

**Order of risk** — ✓ **primary hip < primary knee < revision hip < revision knee** · knee higher (mobile, superficial) · most PJIs in first **1–2 yr** · aseptic revision ≪ revision for infection

**Early TKA revision (<2 yr)** — infection, instability, stiffness · ✓ **infection > stiffness** · late (>2 yr): **aseptic loosening**, infection (> instability) · PE wear now uncommon, occurs **late**

**Pathologic fracture salvage (PFR for metastasis)** — ✓ **deep infection = most common complication**

### Risk factors

| Category | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Preoperative** | **Active infection** (skin, deep, joint, septicaemia) · **previous surgery / prior infection** · active skin lesions (erysipelas, ulcer, folliculitis — contraindication until healed) |
| **Immunosuppressant drugs** | **Anti-TNF** (infliximab, etanercept, adalimumab, certolizumab, golimumab) · leflunomide · corticosteroids |
| **Diabetes** | **HbA1c >7** or **fructosamine ≥293 µmol/L** (Orthobullets) · OB: ✓ **HbA1c >8%** ↑ infection · fructosamine **≥292** |
| **Other medical** | CKD · acute liver failure · **malnutrition (albumin <3.5**, lymphocytes <800) · **HIV CD4 <400** · cancer/metastasis · depression |
| **Inflammatory arthropathy** | **RA** (seropositive men), psoriasis (controversial), AS |
| **Lifestyle** | **Morbid obesity (BMI >40)** · smoking · alcohol · IVDU · poor oral hygiene |
| **TO / OB extras** | **Male sex** · **BMI ≥30** · **allogeneic transfusion** · **hinged / constrained / PS** implant (vs CR) · indwelling catheter **>48 h** (UTI) · anticoagulation (haematoma) · trauma diagnosis |
| **Lower risk** | Uncemented TKA, **PFJ, UKA** vs cemented TKA · OA diagnosis · **age >65** (risk ↓ 1.2%/yr after 65 — Kaye) |

**Not a risk factor** — ✓ **asymptomatic bacteriuria** (TO) — no screening/treatment/delay needed · TO text elsewhere: a marker of risk but urine is not the source

**Not associated (TO)** — ✓ **age >65**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI ≥30 · transfusion · male

**RA THA (OB)** — ↑ **PJI** (e.g. 16,000 WBC, 88% PMN at 6 wk) · ↑ intraop fracture, revision, dislocation, wound infection · **no ↑ mortality or DVT**

**Stem-cell transplant** — arthroplasty ≥1 yr after (TO)

### Organisms

| Setting | Organism |
|---|---|
| **Acute (<4 wk)** | **S. aureus** ✓ (GPC in **clusters**) · β-haemolytic strep · gram-negatives |
| **Chronic / late (>3 mo) THA** | **S. epidermidis (CoNS)** ✓ · S. viridans · P. acnes |
| **Fungal** | **Candida** (C. albicans) |
| OB series, acute | S. aureus **43.8%**, CoNS **17.7%**, Enterococcus 6.3% |
| OB series, chronic | S. aureus **35.8%**, CoNS **21.2%**, Strep **11.6%** |

| Organism | Lab identity |
|---|---|
| **S. aureus** | **Catalase +, coagulase +**, GPC clusters · MRSA gene **mecA** |
| **S. epidermidis** | Catalase +, **coagulase −**, novobiocin-sensitive |
| **S. pyogenes** | **Catalase −**, β-haemolytic, bacitracin-sensitive |

![S. aureus — gram-positive cocci in clusters](../../MCQ/attachments/q211515-b-figure-b.jpg)

### Cutibacterium (Propionibacterium) acnes (TO)

**Organism** — **anaerobic gram-positive rod** (diphtheroid) · skin follicles · **shoulder**, men · ~**10%** of PJI (underestimated)

**Course** — ✓ **slow onset** · delayed (3–24 mo) / late · **CRP and WBC often normal**\
✗ Wrong options: high WBC

**Culture** — needs **anaerobic media, 7–15 days** (hold **≥14 days**) · sonication, molecular tests help\
✗ Wrong options: 48 h

**Antibiotics** — **penicillin G, ceftriaxone** first choice · **rifampin** active against biofilm · **resistant to metronidazole + fosfomycin** · aminoglycosides weak

### Biofilm & quorum

**Biofilm** — forms **within 4 weeks** · **15% cells, 85% polysaccharide (glycocalyx)** · adheres to implant, shields from immunity · infection invades **prosthesis–bone interface** · cannot be safely removed → **explant if >4 weeks**

**Antibiotics alone fail because** — ✓ **biofilm** · **sessile** bacteria more resistant than planktonic\
✗ Wrong options: resistance · polymicrobial · diabetes · planktonic bacteria

**Quorum** — ✓ **critical bacterial density needed to establish biofilm** → colony shifts **from replication to virulence/biofilm**\
✗ Wrong options: MIC · LD50 · empiric options index

### Prevention & prophylaxis

**Screening** — optimize risk factors · **nasal mupirocin** decolonization (MSSA/MRSA) · **no routine urine culture** unless UTI symptoms · **stop DMARDs 4–6 wk** · revision: **normal ESR/CRP off antibiotics** · reported penicillin/cephalosporin allergy → **allergy testing** (cephalosporins ↓ morbidity/mortality vs alternatives)

**Timing** — **within 30 min** of incision (Orthobullets) · **within 1 h** (OB ✓ cefazolin ↓ PJI) · **>10 min before tourniquet** · continue **24 h**

**Weight-based cefazolin** — **1 g <60 kg · 2 g 60–120 kg · 3 g >120 kg** (>120 kg often under-dosed)

**Aseptic revision** — ✓ **single weight-based dose + 3 more weight-based doses over 24 h** (q8h) (130 kg → 3 g)\
✗ Wrong options: 48 h (no benefit) · single dose · fixed 2 g

**Dilute povidone-iodine** — ✓ **cost-effective ↓ infection** · **0.35% for 3 min** · must be **rinsed out** (cytotoxic) · not an antibiotic (no resistance) · WHO/CDC/ICM support

**OR** — **vertical laminar airflow** · limit traffic · antiseptic skin wash · glove changes

**Antibiotic-loaded cement in primary TKA — conflict** — OB (2023): low-dose ALBC (1 g vanc + 1 g tobra / 40 g, <3%) → ✓ **↓ PJI** (no loosening, no AKI, ↑ cost) · OB (2021): routine ALBC in primary TKA **not shown to ↓ PJI** — read the stem

**No effect** — **staples vs sutures** · absorbable monofilament vs staples

**Dental prophylaxis** — depends on **host risk factors**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — bacteraemia · multiple surgeries · skin breach (IVDU, colonoscopy, dental work, ulcer, wound problem)

**Persistent pain + stiffness** — infection in **>90%** of such patients

**Acute** — swelling, tenderness, **drainage** · **chronic** — subtle, deteriorating function

**Sinus tract to joint** — **definite infection**

**Surgical site infection (with implant)** — within **1 year** of index surgery · 2 yr post-TKA sinus with pus → ✓ **"SSI" least accurate** term (✓ PJI, sinus, purulence, chronic)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification by timing

| System | Categories |
|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | **Acute <3–6 wk** (CDC **<90 days**) — joint space only, no biofilm · **Chronic >3–6 wk** (CDC >90 days) — biofilm, bone–implant interface |
| **OB (DAIR)** | **Early postop ≤30 days** · **acute haematogenous** >30 days with symptoms **<3–4 wk** · **chronic** >30 days with symptoms **>1 month** |
| **Fitzgerald (OB)** | **I** acute postop, radiographically silent · **II** 6–24 mo, indolent, new bone formation · **III** >2 yr, **haematogenous** |
| **TO (P. acnes)** | Early <3 mo · delayed 3–24 mo · late >24 mo |
| **Source** | **Direct** (sinus, dehiscence) · **haematogenous** (dental, gallbladder) |

### Tsukayama (modified Coventry, Segawa) – TO

| Type | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Positive intraoperative cultures** (≥2) | **IV antibiotics (6 wk)** |
| **2** | **Early postop** (<4 wk) | **Debridement, prosthesis salvage** |
| **3** | **Acute haematogenous** | **Debridement, prosthesis salvage** |
| **4** | **Late chronic** (>4 wk) | **Prosthesis removal** |

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **periosteal reaction** (lacey new bone, scalloping) · **patchy osteolysis** · **generalized resorption without wear** · transcortical sinus · loosening · TO: lucency **>2 mm** at bone–cement interface, component shift, cement fracture, motion on stress views · progressive lucency in **first year** → infection work-up

**Bone scan (Tc-99m)** — Orthobullets **99% sens, 30–40% spec** · TO **93% sens, 65% spec** (PPV 63%, NPV 94%) · hot for **years** after THA / at 6–7 months after TKA → not useful early · triple phase shows loosening, not sepsis

**Labelled WBC (In-111 / Tc-WBC)** — better to rule out · TO 2023: **Tc-99m WBC + bone scan = gold standard** for septic vs aseptic loosening (spec **86%**)

**FDG-PET** — **98% sens, 98% spec** (Orthobullets) · OB: no more accurate than ESR + CRP, no organism · normal scan excludes PJI

**MRI** — limited by metal (✓ TO "not sensitive due to metal") · MARS MRI knee **92% sens, 99% spec** · not first-line

**Septic vs aseptic loosening — conflict (TO)** — one TO item: ✓ **hip aspiration** (identifies + guides antibiotics; WBC scan can't definitively separate) · FNB 2023: ✓ **radiolabelled WBC scan** (aspiration sens 67%) — read the options/source

![Periprosthetic infection — radiograph](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5004-6a5afea96a483b9af7e3abd4f32d97df.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers – serum

| Marker | Facts |
|---|---|
| **CRP** | Peaks **2–3 d**, normal by **21 d** · threshold acute (<6 wk) **<100 mg/L**, chronic **<10 mg/L** · TO sens 94%, spec 74% · first-line with ESR |
| **ESR** | Peaks **5–7 d**, normal by **90 d** · chronic **<30 mm/h** · no acute cut-off · TO sens 91%, spec 72% |
| **IL-6** | Normal **<10 pg/mL** (TO <5) · peaks **8–12 h**, normal **48–72 h** · ✓ **highest correlation with PJI** · sens **100%**, spec **95%** (TO 95/87) · ✓ **most specific** (TO, vs PCT, CRP, IL-1) · false + **RA, MS, AIDS, Paget** |
| **Serum WBC** | Useless (sens **~20%**) |
| **D-dimer** | **>860 ng/mL** · outperforms ESR/CRP, good for indolent organisms |
| **Procalcitonin** | Spec **98%**, sens **33%** · good for septicaemia |
| **TNF-α** | High spec, low sens · unstable sample |
| **ICAM-1** | ✓ **Returns quickly to baseline** after clearance (✗ Wrong options: "stays elevated" (TO false statement)) |

**Not a correct constellation (TO)** — ✓ **ESR, CRP, WBC count, IL-1**

### Synovial fluid

| Setting | WBC / PMN cut-off |
|---|---|
| **Knee/hip, chronic** | **>3,000 cells/µL + >80% PMN** (Orthobullets) |
| **Knee, first 6 weeks after TKA** | **>10,000** ✓ (Parvizi 2018; Bedair 2011 used 27,800) |
| **MoM hip** | **>4,350 + >85%** · **manual count** (debris falsely ↑ automated) |
| **Hip antibiotic spacer** | **>1,166 + >64%** |
| TO best thresholds | Knee **1,630 + 60%** · hip **2,582 + 66%** → ✓ **2,500 cells/µL** "signifies infection" · early PJI often **>10,000 + >90%** |
| OB others | Chronic THA **3,966** · knee **>1,100** possible infection · knee PJI **>1,000 + >64%** |
| Native septic joint | **50,000** |

**Gram stain** — **specificity > sensitivity** · high false negatives in chronic PJI → **don't rely on it** (OB)

**Alpha-defensin** — ✓ **intra-articular antimicrobial peptide** (✗ Wrong options: serum · cytokine) · released by ✓ **neutrophils** (✗ Wrong options: macrophages · monocytes) · **100% sens, 98% spec** · ✓ **most sensitive + specific** marker (✗ Wrong options: frozen section · IL-6 · CRP · LE) · **>5.2 mg/L** · **unaffected by antibiotics** · accurate with blood · **false + in metallosis/ALTR** · ✓ **single most accurate predictor of chronic PJI**

**Leukocyte esterase strip** — sens **81%**, spec **97%** · cut-off **++** · blood → centrifuge · separates MoM failure from PJI

**Best combined accuracy (OB)** — ✓ **laboratory-based alpha-defensin + LE strips** · lab-based > lateral-flow alpha-defensin · synovial > serum > tissue tests

**Synovial CRP** — **>6.9 mg/L** · **high NPV, low PPV**\
✗ Wrong options: "high PPV"

### MSIS 2018 (Parvizi) criteria

**Major (either alone = infected)** — ✓ **sinus tract communicating with prosthesis** · **same pathogen from 2 separate cultures**\
✗ Wrong options: purulence alone (poly debris, MoM, gout mimic) · high WBC alone · CRP/ESR

| Minor — preoperative | Points |
|---|---|
| Serum **CRP >10 mg/L** or **D-dimer >860 ng/mL** | **2** |
| Serum **ESR >30 mm/h** | **1** |
| Synovial **WBC >3,000/µL** or **LE ++** | **3** |
| Synovial **alpha-defensin +** | **3** |
| Synovial **PMN >80%** | **2** |
| Synovial **CRP >6.9 mg/L** | **1** |
| **Score** | **≥6 infected · 2–5 inconclusive · 0–1 not infected** |

| Intraoperative (if 2–5 or dry tap) | Points |
|---|---|
| **Histology >5 PMN/hpf in 5 hpf (×400)** | **3** |
| **Purulence** | **3** |
| **Single positive culture** | **2** |
| **Combined pre + intraop** | **≥6 infected · 4–5 inconclusive · ≤3 not infected** |

**Performance** — **98% sens, 99.5% spec** · inconclusive → molecular tests (PCR, NGS)

**MSIS 2011** — major criteria **or 4 of 6 minor** (ESR + CRP, synovial WBC, PMN%, purulence, one culture, histology)

**Worked examples** — CRP 12 + ESR 45 + WBC 3,200 + PMN 78% → **6 points = infected** ✓ · WBC + alpha-defensin → **6** ✓ · WBC + PMN → 5 · D-dimer + synovial CRP → 3 · ESR + synovial CRP → 2

![MSIS 2018 criteria](../../MCQ/attachments/q218184-illa-the-2018-msis-criteria-for-prosthetic-joint-infect.jpg)

### Diagnostic workup – ESR/CRP then aspiration

```
Painful arthroplasty
├─ ESR + CRP normal → infection unlikely → observe / other causes
├─ ESR or CRP elevated → aspiration (cell count, diff, culture, crystals)
│      └─ On antibiotics / inconclusive → repeat after 2 wk off, or alpha-defensin
└─ Still equivocal → intraop frozen section ± sonication of implant
```

**First step** — ✓ **ESR + CRP** · AAOS: **strong recommendation to aspirate** if ESR and/or CRP abnormal\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · MRI · CT for rotation · revision · metal ions · IV antibiotics first

**Aspiration before revision** — elevated ESR with borderline CRP → ✓ **aspirate** · instability + ↑ ESR/CRP → ✓ **aspirate first**\
✗ Wrong options: Tc scan · FDG-PET · straight to revision + frozen section

**Normal ESR + CRP** — ✓ **observe** / no aspiration (e.g. eccentric head with normal labs)

**MoM hip with fever after URI** — ✓ **aspirate** · MoM pain with ↑ ESR/CRP + normal ions → ✓ **arthrocentesis** · ions **Co:Cr 1:1 → MoM debris**, **2:1 → trunnionosis**\
✗ Wrong options: MARS MRI · metal ions · empiric antibiotics

**Equivocal aspirate + recent antibiotics** — ✓ **repeat aspiration** (after 1–2 weeks off) · on antibiotics now → ✓ **repeat aspiration for alpha-defensin today**

**Equivocal (CRP 13, ESR 19, WBC 1,000, PMN 90%, α-defensin −, synovial CRP 4)** — ✓ **OR for histology** (frozen section)

**Wait 2 weeks off antibiotics** before repeat aspiration (Orthobullets)

**Intraop frozen section** — **>5 PMN/hpf × 5 hpf** · **sens 85%, spec 90–95%** · equivocal cases

**Intraop cultures** — same organism on repeat aspirations or **≥3 of 5** specimens · **false-positive 8%** · **tissue > swabs**

**Sonication (TO)** — ✓ **low-frequency (40 kHz) ultrasound of explanted implant** dislodges biofilm → ↑ culture sensitivity, even after antibiotics\
✗ Wrong options: high frequency · treatment of overuse injuries

**Hemiarthroplasty, 18 months, rapid acetabular erosion + linear lucency** → ✓ **infection**\
✗ Wrong options: cement technique · oversized head (would extrude)

## 5 Management

```
Confirmed PJI
├─ Acute postop (<3–4 wk) or acute haematogenous (symptoms <3–4 wk) → DAIR + modular exchange
│      (not if loose implant, flap needed, MRSA/fungal/mycobacteria)
├─ Chronic (>4 wk), fit host → two-stage exchange (gold standard)
│      └─ Low-virulence, healthy host, no sinus → one-stage (Europe)
├─ Positive intraop cultures at aseptic revision (≥2/5) → 6 wk IV antibiotics
└─ Unfit / refuses → suppression · salvage: resection, fusion, AKA
```

### Acute infection – DAIR & liner exchange

**Indications** — **acute postop <3–4 wk** · **acute haematogenous** (ideally **<48–72 h** of symptoms; <3 wk) · well-fixed implant

**Technique** — **modular exchange** (PE, femoral head — removes fibrin nidus) · synovectomy · **6–9 L irrigation** · culture-guided IV antibiotics **2–6 weeks** (TO)

**Success** — **50–55%** · implants removed if reinfection

**Improved success** — modular exchange · **within 7 days** of symptoms · **rifampin** for staph · **fluoroquinolone** for gram-negatives (FQ-sensitive E. coli ~**79%**)

**Predict failure** — **MRSA, VRE, Pseudomonas**, S. epidermidis (biofilm), **fungi**, ✓ **mycobacteria** · host: **age >80**, male, COPD, CRF, cirrhosis

**Absolute contraindications** — **loose implants** (stem subsidence) · **poor soft tissue / flap needed** (>85% failure) · compromised cement mantle · chronic PJI (even with 12+ wk antibiotics)

**Best DAIR candidate** — ✓ **TKA 2 yr ago, 2-day haematogenous E. coli** · ✓ **THA 3 wk ago, WBC 10,700 + 91% PMN**\
✗ Wrong options: 8 mo MRSA · loose stem · flap · 6 mo symptoms · 1 yr with 2–3 mo symptoms

**Head + liner exchange appropriate** — ✓ **infection 3 days after systemic infection**\
✗ Wrong options: 4 months · 8 weeks · osteolysis · Vancouver A

**Persistent wound drainage 10–14 days post-op** → ✓ **I&D ± PE exchange** in OR · drainage at 3 wk with benign aspirate → **surgical exploration + possible PE exchange** · drainage after prior HTO incision → **urgent debridement + cultures + labs**\
✗ Wrong options: oral/IV antibiotics alone · ointment · immobilizer · sutures in clinic · VAC · wait for cultures

**Acute haematogenous (48 h, RA, dental work, 55K WBC)** → ✓ **operate now**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for cultures · α-defensin · IV antibiotics only · IR drain

**Possible acute PJI 3 wk post-TKA (fall, diverticulitis, effusion)** → ✓ **aspirate first**\
✗ Wrong options: book DAIR directly · reassure

**Acute but MRSA + BMI 50 + persistent drainage (5 wk)** → ✓ **two-stage** (I&D <50% success; failed I&D worsens later two-stage)

**FNB 2024** — bilateral TKA, infected at 3 wk, low-virulence sensitive organism → options suppression / DAIR / one-stage / two-stage — answer not given by source (DAIR fits acute <3–4 wk)

### Chronic infection – two-stage exchange

**Indication** — ✓ **gold standard for infection >4 weeks** · medically fit · adequate bone stock · preferred for **fungal**

**Stage 1** — explant · debride bone–implant interface + soft tissue · **antibiotic spacer** · **IV antibiotics 4–6 weeks** (TO: 6 wk, bactericidal titre **1:8**) · excise sinus with elliptical incision

**Before stage 2** — benign exam, **normal ESR/CRP/WBC**, ✓ **2-week antibiotic holiday then aspiration** · OB case: 1-month holiday\
✗ Wrong options: aspirate at completion of IV · wait 6 months (spacer breaks) · spacer exchange

**Reimplantation** — tissue for culture + **frozen section** · acute inflammation → **re-debride, new spacer** · antibiotic cement if cementing · **cementless better in hip**

| Reimplantation timing | Success |
|---|---|
| **Early, <2 weeks** | **35%** |
| **Delayed, >6 weeks** | **70–90%** (Orthobullets) · **80%** (OB) |

**Cure rates** — two-stage **90–93%** (OB) · **89–100%** (TO) · one-stage **75–80%** (OB) · **75–100%** (Orthobullets) · **~89%** (TO)

**Prognosis** — ✓ **MRSA worse than MSSA** after two-stage (eradication + function) · **BMI ≥40** ↑ reinfection · bilateral simultaneous resection + reimplant → ✓ **successful at 2 yr, <20% revision**

**Typical answers** — late haematogenous after colonoscopy (6 wk) → ✓ **two-stage** · 5 wk after URI, 15K WBC, GPC → ✓ **resection + antibiotics** · MR-CoNS 8 months → ✓ **remove implants + spacer** · inflamed tissue + frozen section 20 WBC/hpf at re-revision, Gram −ve → ✓ **cultures, remove implants, spacer** · sinus tract → ✓ **labs, aspirate, two-stage** (✗ Wrong options: wait · oral antibiotics · DAIR · one-stage) · culture-negative with ↑ markers → still ✓ **two-stage** · MRSA chronic → ✓ **two-stage** (✗ Wrong options: fusion · suppression · I&D · AKA)

**Failed two-stage (persistent infection)** — ✓ **repeat debridement + spacer** · devitalized patella (lateral release kills **superior lateral genicular**) → sequestrum → consider **patellectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: fusion · AKA · suppression in healthy 62-yr-old · arthroscopic I&D

### One-stage exchange

**Indications** — **Europe**, infected THA · **no sinus**, healthy host + soft tissue, no prolonged antibiotics, no bone graft · **low-virulence, sensitive** organism · antibiotic cement

**Pros/cons** — cheaper, single surgery, early mobility vs **↑ persistent infection** · not fungal (rarely succeeds)

### Antibiotic spacers & cement

| | **Static spacer** | **Articulating spacer** |
|---|---|---|
| Antibiotic dose | **Higher** (not premade) | Lower |
| Use | **Flap coverage, extensive bone loss, ligament deficiency** (gross laxity) | Default |
| Pros | Wound healing (no motion) | ↓ reimplantation exposure time · **joint space + ROM kept** · ↓ **quadriceps shortening** · satisfaction |
| Reinfection + function | **Equivalent** | **Equivalent** ✓ (✗ Wrong options: "↓ infection recurrence") |

**Spacer pros** — ↓ dead space, stability, high local antibiotic · **cons** — allergy, resistant organisms, **heat-stable antibiotics only**

| Cement dose (per 40 g) | Orthobullets / OB | TO |
|---|---|---|
| **Low (prophylaxis)** | **≤2 g** (commercial ALBC; OB: 0.5 g tobra/genta) | **≤2 g (<5%)** keeps strength |
| **Treatment / retained hardware** | — | **2–3.6 g** |
| **Spacer (high)** | **≥3.6 g** · practical **3 g vanc + 3.6–4 g tobra** · OB: **min 3.6 g tobra + 1 g vanc** | **4–8 g** · ✓ **max 8 g** |
| **Max without systemic toxicity** | **12.5 g tobramycin · 10.5 g vancomycin** | — |
| **Strength compromised** | — | ✓ **>3.6–4 g** |

**Elution** — burst first **24 h**, rapid decline, low by **5 wk** · ✓ **↑ porosity → ↑ elution** · **hand mixing** for spacers, **vacuum** for prophylaxis · **combination (vanc + aminoglycoside) ↑ elution of both** · vancomycin added **after 30 s** of mixing → ↑ elution · extra monomer **1.5–2 ampoules** · NPWT: no difference\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ strength · ↑ density

**Cement chemistry (TO)** — **liquid antibiotics contraindicated** → ✓ **interfere with polymerization (soft mass)** · antibiotic voids ↓ ✓ **compressive strength** · extra powder ✓ **↑ dough time** · ✓ **polymyxin B / colistin** trapped (poor elution) · penicillins heat-labile · ✓ **fosfomycin ↑ (not ↓) companion elution**, thermostable, voids · amphotericin weakens cement most

**Local antibiotics** — aminoglycosides (gram-negatives, synergy vs GPC, low systemic toxicity) · **vancomycin** (GPC, excellent elution) · **thermostable** · high-dose aminoglycoside spacer → **AKI** risk

**Empiric IV after cultures** — **1st-generation cephalosporin** · **vancomycin** if true penicillin allergy, MRSA history, or unidentified organism · tailor to sensitivity

![Articulating antibiotic spacer](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5004-prostalac-dynamic-spacer.jpg)

### Fungal infection

**Features** — **Candida** (most common) · risk: immunosuppression, DM, CRF, IVDU · **indolent**, no fever, ESR/CRP minimally ↑

**Treatment** — ✓ **open I&D, two-stage exchange, antifungals 6–12 months** · **DAIR very high failure** ✓ · recurrence **20–25%** after two-stage · worse than bacterial\
✗ Wrong options: suppression · DAIR + suppression · resection + antimycobacterials · single-stage

**Fluconazole** — ✓ **oral = IV efficacy** · **heat-stable** (can go in cement) · **hepatotoxic** · **resistance rising** · no renal monitoring needed

### Positive intraoperative cultures at revision (TO)

**3/5 cultures CoNS after aseptic revision, afebrile, dry wound** → ✓ **IV antibiotics 6 weeks** (Tsukayama 1)\
✗ Wrong options: debridement + retention · one-stage · two-stage · observation

### Soft tissue coverage – gastrocnemius flap

**Large anteromedial knee defect with exposed implant** → **two-stage + flap at stage 1** · ✓ **medial gastrocnemius flap — medial sural artery**\
✗ Wrong options: free flap first · lateral gastrocnemius (lateral wounds, CPN risk) · soleus (middle/distal third) · fasciocutaneous (4–10 cm without exposed implant)

### Salvage options

| Option | Indications | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Chronic suppression** | Unfit, refuses, low-virulence organism sensitive to low-toxicity oral drug | **10–25%** eradication · **8–21%** complications |
| **Resection arthroplasty** | Poor bone/soft tissue · MDR recurrent · unfit · failed reimplantations · nonambulatory · **polyarticular RA, limited walking** (TO) | **TKA 50–89%** · **THA 60–100%** · short limb, poor function |
| **Arthrodesis** | Reimplantation not feasible (bone stock) · virulent recurrent · TO: young, high demand, single joint, deficient extensor, poor soft tissue | **71–95%** fusion + eradication |
| **Amputation** | Recalcitrant **TKA** infection, severe pain, soft-tissue/bone/vascular loss | **AKA** |

![Knee arthrodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5004-knee-arthrodesis.jpg)

### Special situations

**Infected pseudotumour (MoM, neutrophils on H&E, ↑ ESR/CRP)** → debride, explant, spacer + ✓ **systemic antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: radiation · chemotherapy

**MoM THA with 6,000 WBC + 92% PMN (manual count), 3 months** → ✓ **explant + spacer**\
✗ Wrong options: DAIR · CoP revision (that is for metallosis without PJI) · MRI

**Chronic MRSA enterococcus + global laxity + sinus** → ✓ **two-stage with static spacer**\
✗ Wrong options: dynamic spacer with laxity · flap for small sinus

## X vs Y

| | **Acute / haematogenous PJI** | **Chronic PJI** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | **<3–4 wk** post-op or symptoms <3–4 wk | **>4 wk** |
| Biofilm | No | **Yes** (glycocalyx, bone–implant interface) |
| Organism | **S. aureus** | **S. epidermidis / CoNS**, P. acnes |
| Synovial WBC | **>10,000** (early) | **>3,000 + >80%** |
| Treatment | **DAIR + modular exchange** | **Two-stage** |

| | **Static spacer** | **Articulating spacer** |
|---|---|---|
| Best when | Flap, bone loss, **laxity** | Standard |
| Infection control | Equal | Equal |

## How it's asked

**"Not associated with infection after TKA?"** → **asymptomatic bacteriuria** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: RA · hinged prosthesis · psoriasis

**"Not associated with infection after knee replacement?"** → **age >65** (TO)

**"THA infection risk — true?"** → **allogeneic transfusion ↑ risk**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI 43 safe · low albumin protective · staples better

**"Order of PJI risk?"** → **primary hip < primary knee < revision hip < revision knee**

**"Highest PJI risk?"** → **revision TKA**

**"Early vs late TKA revision causes?"** → **infection more common early than stiffness**

**"Would have reduced PJI risk?"** → **cefazolin within 1 h of incision**

**"130 kg aseptic revision prophylaxis?"** → **weight-based cefazolin + 3 weight-based doses over 24 h**

**"1 g vanc + 1 g tobra per 40 g in primary TKA?"** → **reduced infection rate**

**"HbA1c 8.1% — counsel?"** → **↑ infection risk (HbA1c >8%)**

**"Dilute povidone-iodine lavage?"** → **cost-effective infection reduction**

**"Septic vs aseptic loosening, THA at 8 yr?"** → **hip aspiration** (TO) · FNB 2023 version → **radiolabelled WBC scan**

**"Sonication refers to?"** → **low-frequency ultrasound of explanted prosthesis to aid diagnosis** (TO)

**"Correct statement on PJI biomarkers?"** → **MRI insensitive due to metal artefact** (TO)

**"Equivocal aspirate after recent 14-day antibiotics?"** → **repeat aspiration after 1 week**

**"Pain 7 months after TKA, normal X-ray — next?"** → **CRP, ESR, WBC**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan

**"Lacey periosteal new bone 15 months after THA — next?"** → **ESR, CRP, WBC**

**"Instability + raised ESR/CRP after TKA?"** → **aspirate**

**"RA, loose stem, ↑ ESR — next?"** → **hip aspiration for culture**

**"Painful THA, normal ESR + CRP?"** → **observation**

**"MoM hip, 6 months pain, ↑ ESR/CRP, normal ions?"** → **arthrocentesis**

**"Equivocal labs, α-defensin negative?"** → **OR for histology**

**"Aspirate 500 WBC while on oral antibiotics?"** → **repeat aspiration today for alpha-defensin**

**"Diabetic, 10 months pain, ↑ ESR/CRP?"** → **knee aspiration**

**"MoM THA, psoas fluid, ↑ ESR/CRP, normal ions?"** → **hip aspiration**

**"Most specific marker post-THR (TO)?"** → **IL-6**

**"Not true about markers?"** → **ICAM-1 stays elevated after clearance** (TO)

**"Not a correct constellation?"** → **ESR, CRP, WBC count, IL-1** (TO)

**"Cell count signifying PJI?"** → **2,500 cells/µL** (TO)

**"Alone sufficient for PJI (MSIS)?"** → **sinus tract communicating with prosthesis**

**"Serum marker with highest correlation?"** → **IL-6**

**"Alpha-defensin tests for?"** → **intra-articular antimicrobial peptide**

**"Most sensitive and specific marker?"** → **alpha-defensin**

**"Alpha-defensin released by?"** → **neutrophils**

**"Single most accurate predictor of chronic PJI?"** → **positive synovial alpha-defensin**

**"Best combined sensitivity + specificity?"** → **lab-based alpha-defensin + LE strips**

**"WBC 12,500 five weeks after TKA?"** → **above 10,000 cut-off — PJI likely**

**"Which labs meet 2018 criteria?"** → **CRP 12, ESR 45, WBC 3,200, PMN 78%**

**"Pair that alone suggests PJI?"** → **synovial WBC + positive alpha-defensin**

**"P. acnes PJI — true?"** → **slow onset of symptoms** (TO)

**"Late (>3 mo) THA infection organism?"** → **S. epidermidis**

**"Early THA infection organism?"** → **S. aureus**

**"MRSA vs MSSA after two-stage?"** → **MRSA poorer prognosis**

**"Why antibiotics alone fail?"** → **biofilm**

**"Quorum?"** → **critical bacterial density to establish biofilm**

**"Febrile, 65K WBC, chronic — organism and step?"** → **catalase +, coagulase + GPC; two-stage**

**"IV antibiotics after DAIR?"** → **2–6 weeks** (TO)

**"Predicts DAIR failure?"** → **mycobacteria**

**"Bilateral resection + spacers + antibiotics — outcome?"** → **successful reimplantation, <20% revision**

**"Articulating vs static spacer EXCEPT?"** → **decreased infection recurrence**

**"↑ spacer porosity leads to?"** → **↑ antibiotic elution**

**"Best antibiotic cement for spacer?"** → **≥3.6 g tobramycin + 1 g vancomycin per 40 g**

**"Why liquid antibiotics are contraindicated in PMMA?"** → **interfere with polymerization** (TO)

**"Threshold where strength compromised?"** → **>3.6–4 g per 40 g** (TO)

**"Property most hurt by voids?"** → **compressive strength** (TO)

**"Dough time with extra antibiotic powder?"** → **increases** (TO)

**"Max antibiotic in 40 g cement for spacer?"** → **8 g** (TO)

**"Antibiotic avoided — poor elution?"** → **polymyxin B** (TO)

**"Not a property of fosfomycin in cement?"** → **reduces elution of companion antibiotic** (TO)

**"Persistent infection after spacer + antibiotics?"** → **repeat debridement + new spacer**

**"After 6 weeks IV, normal CRP/ESR — next?"** → **2-week antibiotic holiday then aspiration**

**"Candida PJI in transplant patient?"** → **open I&D, two-stage, antifungals 6–12 months**

**"Fluconazole — true?"** → **oral = IV efficacy**

**"DAIR for Candida?"** → **very high failure rate**

**"Anteromedial defect with exposed tibial tray — flap?"** → **medial gastrocnemius — medial sural artery**

**"3/5 intraop cultures CoNS, afebrile, dry wound?"** → **6 weeks parenteral antibiotics** (TO)

**"Infected MoM pseudotumour — surgery plus?"** → **systemic antibiotics**

**"Persistent drainage 12 days after THA?"** → **open debridement + PE exchange + culture-guided antibiotics**

**"Drainage 14 days, culture pending?"** → **debride before cultures return**

**"Streptococcus 10 days after TKA?"** → **open I&D + PE exchange + IV antibiotics**

**"5 weeks, MRSA, BMI 50, drainage?"** → **two-stage with antibiotic spacer**

**"11 weeks, MRSA PCR, dry tap, drainage?"** → **debride, remove implants, spacer**

**"Methicillin-resistant enterococcus, gross laxity?"** → **two-stage with static spacer**

**"RA patient after THA (vs OA)?"** → **PJI risk ↑ (16,000 WBC, 88% PMN at 6 wk)**

# THA Acetabular Screw Fixation

*Recon · 19 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Acetabular quadrants & screw neurovascular risk

**Construction** — line 1: ✓ **ASIS → centre of acetabulum** (→ posterior fovea) · line 2: **perpendicular** at acetabular centre

| Quadrant | Zone | At risk |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior-superior** | **Target / safe zone** — ideal for screws | **Sciatic nerve**, **superior gluteal nerve + vessels** (✓ SGA — TO) |
| **Posterior-inferior** | **Caution zone** — screws **≤20 mm** | **Sciatic nerve**, **inferior gluteal nerve + vessels**, **internal pudendal nerve + vessels** |
| **Anterior-inferior** | **Danger zone** | **Obturator nerve, artery, vein** |
| **Anterior-superior** | **"Death zone"** (Orthobullets/TO) · "danger zone" (OB) | ✓ **External iliac artery + vein** (vein more medial → more at risk) |

**General rule** — posterior quadrants safe except **long posteroinferior** screws · safe areas **superior + posterior** · external iliac at risk with **anteromedial** screws

**High hip centre** — anterosuperior + anteroinferior structures shift into the **posterosuperior quadrant** · safest = **peripheral half of posterosuperior** quadrant · elevated revision centre → **sciatic** risk with posterosuperior screws

![Acetabular quadrant safe zones](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5029-safe-zones-3.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Screw injuries

**Foot drop + radicular pain after revision, screw in sciatic notch** → ✓ **posterior-superior quadrant** (long screw, high hip centre)

**Gluteus maximus lurch** (trunk extends at heel strike) after transacetabular screws → ✓ **posterior-inferior quadrant** → **inferior gluteal nerve** injury\
✗ Wrong options: posterior-superior (sciatic/SGN) · anterior-inferior (obturator) · anterior-superior (external iliac)

**Greatest vascular risk** → ✓ **anterior-superior**\
✗ Wrong options: superior · posterior-superior · posterior-inferior · superolateral

### Retractor placement & vascular injury

**External iliac vessels** — only **7 mm** from bone at **AIIS**, may lie on bone → **anterior retractor in anterosuperior quadrant** (✓) or anterosuperior screw

**Common femoral artery/vein** — separated from capsule only by **iliopsoas**, crosses hip near **AIIS** → anterior acetabular retractor (✓ position 2, anterosuperior) · esp. **anterolateral approach**

**Inferior retractor under transverse acetabular ligament** → **obturator** vessels/nerve

| Retractor position | Injury |
|---|---|
| **Anterior-superior** | **External iliac / femoral vessels** |
| **Anterior-inferior / directly inferior (under TAL)** | **Obturator** vessels + nerve |
| **Posterior-inferior** | Sciatic, inferior gluteal, internal pudendal |
| **Posterior-superior** | Relatively safe — superior gluteal, sciatic |

### Avoiding prosthesis impingement

**Proud anterior cup rim** → **iliopsoas impingement** · evaluate with **cross-table lateral** + **anaesthetic injection of iliopsoas sheath**

| Cause | Impingement type |
|---|---|
| **Medialized + raised cup** (↓ femoral offset) | **Bone-on-bone** |
| **Lateralized cup** | **Metal neck-on-metal cup** |
| **Head-to-neck ratio <2:1** (skirted heads, small heads) | Prosthetic impingement |

## How it's asked

**"Posterosuperior quadrant screw risks (TO)?"** → **superior gluteal artery**

**"Zone most risky for external iliac vessels?"** → **anterior-superior**

**"Line drawn to define quadrants?"** → **ASIS to centre of acetabulum (A and C)**

**"Foot drop, screw in sciatic notch after revision?"** → **posterior-superior quadrant**

**"Greatest risk of vascular injury?"** → **anterior superior**

**"Trunk extension at heel strike after transacetabular screws?"** → **posterior-inferior quadrant (inferior gluteal nerve)**

**"Posterior approach, bleeding from external iliac after retractor?"** → **anterior-superior**

**"Femoral artery bleeding from retractor?"** → **anterosuperior (position 2)**

**"Caution zone screw length?"** → **≤20 mm**

**"Obturator vessels at risk?"** → **anterior-inferior quadrant / retractor under TAL**

# THA Approaches

*Recon · 20 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Choice of approach

**Dictated by** — surgeon preference · prior incisions · obesity · dislocation risk · implant · deformity

**Standard** — direct anterior · anterolateral · direct lateral · posterolateral · **extensile** — trochanteric osteotomy · **MIS** variants

**Original Charnley THA** — ✓ **anterolateral** approach, supine, **greater trochanter osteotomy**, anterior dislocation (TO)

![Approaches to the hip](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12116-approaches-to-hip-1500.jpg)

### Intervals & nerves

| Approach | Superficial interval | Deep interval | Internervous? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Direct anterior (Smith-Petersen)** | **Sartorius (femoral) – TFL (superior gluteal)** | **Rectus femoris (femoral) – gluteus medius (SGN)** | ✓ **Femoral / superior gluteal** |
| **Anterolateral (Watson-Jones)** | **TFL – gluteus medius** (both SGN) | — | No — intermuscular only |
| **Direct lateral (Hardinge)** | **Splits gluteus medius + vastus lateralis** | — | No |
| **Posterolateral (Moore / Southern)** | **Splits gluteus maximus** (≤7 cm from GT tip — IGN/IGA) | Short ER + capsule | No |
| **Medial (Ludloff)** | Sartorius – adductor longus | Pectineus – iliopsoas | — |
| **Medial (Ferguson/Hoppenfeld)** | Gracilis – adductor longus | Adductor magnus – brevis (dual innervation) | Protect posterior obturator branch |

**Anterolateral approach (Smith-Petersen variant, TO)** — "7"-shaped incision · flap of **TFL, gluteus minimus, anterior medius**

**Somerville (TO)** — bikini incision · DDH open reduction · TFL–sartorius

**Harris (TO)** — U-incision + GT osteotomy · **Gibson** — anterior border of maximus, cut medius/minimus, dislocate **anteriorly** · **modified Gibson (Marcy–Fletcher)** — cut short ERs, dislocate **posteriorly** · **Osborn / Moore** — split maximus, cut short ERs, posterior capsule

## 5 Management

### Direct anterior – interval & LFCN injury

**Interval** — ✓ **TFL and sartorius** superficially · ✓ **internervous plane femoral + superior gluteal** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: sartorius–maximus · minimus–rectus · rectus–sartorius · TFL–rectus

**Bleeding in TFL–sartorius interval** — ✓ **ascending branch of LFCA** (~5 cm below hip) → ligate/coagulate\
✗ Wrong options: SGA · femoral · profunda · MFCA

**LFCN** — **L2–L3**, under inguinal ligament, pierces fascia **~1 cm distal to ASIS** near TFL–sartorius interval · ✓ **structure at greatest risk** (✗ Wrong options: femoral nerve/artery/vein (medial) · MFCA (posterior to neck)) · ✓ **most common complication** of anterior approach (81% altered thigh sensation, no functional loss) · ✓ **common, improves over time, no functional deficit** (✗ Wrong options: uncommon · intermediate femoral cutaneous)

**Fascial incision lateral, over TFL muscle belly** → ✓ **minimize LFCN injury**\
✗ Wrong options: protects femoral bundle · trajectory · PJI

**Advantages** — **↓ dislocation vs posterior** (~**0.5%**, regardless of capsule repair) · abductors intact · **no gait difference at 3 months** vs other approaches · unproven: less muscle damage/pain, faster recovery

**Disadvantages** — **steep learning curve (>100 cases)** · **wound infection ↑ in obese with large pannus** · **femoral nerve injury** from anterior retractor · femoral exposure difficult (special table) · **LFCN paraesthesia** · **↑ intraop fracture**

### Direct anterior – femoral complications

**Highest femoral-sided complication risk** — ✓ **direct anterior** (intraop **femur fracture, subsidence**, **femoral-sided revision for loosening**) · interval ✓ **femoral–superior gluteal plane** · superficial ✓ **TFL–sartorius**

**Distractors** — direct lateral = medius/vastus split → **Trendelenburg, HO** · posterior = maximus split → **dislocation** · anterolateral = TFL–medius → **limp**, lower dislocation

### Anterolateral (Watson-Jones)

**Interval** — ✓ **TFL (anterior) and gluteus medius (posterior)** (TO) · start midway ASIS–GT, expose SGN proximally · capsule incised along anterosuperior neck

**Pros/cons** — **lower dislocation** than posterior · **violates abductors → limp** · retractor through iliopsoas → **femoral nerve** risk, not instability

### Direct lateral (Hardinge) – abductor injury

**Detached from femur** — ✓ **vastus lateralis + gluteus medius (anterior ⅓–½) + gluteus minimus** (+ ligament of Bigelow)\
✗ Wrong options: psoas/sartorius · piriformis/obturator internus (posterior) · maximus/TFL · minimus/rectus

**Superior gluteal nerve** — runs between medius and minimus **4–6 cm above GT tip** · split ≤**4–5 cm** above GT (TO: **≤5 cm** from tip, or **≥2 cm** above acetabular lip — Frndak)

**Persistent limp + fatty atrophy of anterior abductors on CT** → ✓ **split >5 cm above GT tip** (SGN injury)\
✗ Wrong options: GT fracture · retractor (femoral nerve) · PT · offset

**Testing suspected SGN injury after extended incision (TO)** → ✓ **stand on operated limb** (Trendelenburg)\
✗ Wrong options: stand on other limb · ankle DF/PF

**Pros** — **lower dislocation** than posterior (OB: **lowest**) · anterior + posterior access without osteotomy · **cons** — abductor violation → **limp** · **HO common**

### Posterolateral (Moore / Southern)

**Most common** for primary + revision · abductors intact · excellent femur + acetabulum exposure · extensile

**Dislocation** — higher than anterior · ↓ with **capsule + short ER repair** · ✓ **Moore approach with capsulectomy = greatest dislocation risk** · overall dislocation **1–3%**\
✗ Wrong options: Smith-Petersen failed capsule repair · Watson-Jones with psoas penetration · Hardinge cup 40° · anterior with 15° anteversion

### Minimally invasive approach

**Mini-incision (≤10 cm)** — only proven advantage ✓ **better cosmesis**\
✗ Wrong options: pain · transfusion · LOS · analgesia

**Intraop femoral fracture during MIS** — ✓ **convert to extensile approach** to visualize + reduce · bypass by **2 cortical diameters** with long stem\
✗ Wrong options: limit WB alone · switch to cement · delay · autograft

**SuperPATH (TO)** — supercapsular percutaneously assisted · **modified posterolateral MIS** · interval ✓ **gluteus minimus–piriformis** · medius + minimus retracted anteriorly · **short ERs + ITB spared**, **no dislocation** · femoral canal prepared through **piriformis fossa** in situ · **accessory percutaneous portal for acetabulum** · ↓ LOS, blood loss, readmission\
✗ Wrong options: minimus–medius (the EXCEPT answer)

![SuperPATH interval](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733194-1.png)

### Extensile – trochanteric osteotomy

**Types** — standard · **trochanteric slide** · **extended trochanteric osteotomy (ETO)**

**Pros** — excellent acetabular exposure · component removal · difficult primary/revision

**Complications** — nonunion · HO · trochanteric bursitis · abductor weakness · ✓ **fracture of osteotomy fragment = most common** · **ETO needs a diaphyseal-engaging stem**

### Surgical hip dislocation (Ganz) – TO

**Preserve** — ✓ **deep branch of MFCA** (protected by intact **obturator externus**) · **posterior approach → trochanteric flip osteotomy → anterior (Z) arthrotomy → anterior dislocation** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: posterior arthrotomy/dislocation

## X vs Y

| | **Direct anterior** | **Anterolateral** | **Direct lateral** | **Posterior** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Abductors | Intact | **Violated** | **Violated** | Intact |
| Dislocation | **Low (~0.5%)** | Low | **Lowest** (OB) | **Highest** |
| Signature complication | **LFCN**, femoral fracture/loosening, obese wound | Limp | **Limp, HO, SGN** | **Dislocation**, sciatic |

## How it's asked

**"Internervous plane of direct anterior approach?"** → **femoral and superior gluteal** (TO)

**"Bleeding between TFL and sartorius?"** → **ascending branch of LFCA**

**"Anterior approach muscle plane?"** → **TFL and sartorius**

**"Greatest potential injury in anterior approach?"** → **LFCN**

**"Most common complication of Smith-Petersen THA?"** → **LFCN injury**

**"Superficial internervous plane of DAA?"** → **sartorius (femoral) & TFL (SGN)**

**"Why fascial incision over TFL belly?"** → **minimize LFCN injury**

**"Will my leg be numb?"** → **LFCN injury common, improves, no functional deficit**

**"Highest femoral-sided complication risk?"** → **direct anterior**

**"Interval of approach with most femoral complications?"** → **femoral–superior gluteal plane**

**"Superficial interval with ↑ femoral revision for loosening?"** → **TFL and sartorius**

**"Hardinge — muscles detached besides vastus lateralis?"** → **gluteus medius + minimus**

**"Limp + fatty abductors after modified Hardinge?"** → **medius split >5 cm above GT**

**"SuperPATH — all except?"** → **interval between gluteus minimus and medius** (TO)

**"True about approach in picture (TO)?"** → **SupraPATH (SuperPATH) approach** · explanation not provided by source\
✗ Wrong options: accessory portal for canal prep · fascia-lata-only no muscle violation · reaming through piriformis fossa portal without dislocation

**"Mini-incision advantage?"** → **better cosmesis**

**"Femoral fracture during MIS THA?"** → **convert to extensile approach**

**"Greatest dislocation risk?"** → **Moore approach with capsulectomy**

**"Approaches compared — true?"** → **no difference in gait at 3 months**

**"Incision 5 cm above GT, nerve injury suspected — test?"** → **stand on operated limb** (TO)

**"Original Charnley approach?"** → **anterolateral** (TO)

**"Watson-Jones plane?"** → **TFL and gluteus medius** (TO)

**"Most common ETO / trochanteric osteotomy complication?"** → **fracture of osteotomy fragment**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Aseptic Loosening

*Recon · 21 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Pathophysiology

**Definition** — **macrophage-induced inflammatory** bone loss → implant loosening **without infection**

**Sequence** — **prosthesis micromotion** → **particulate debris** → **macrophage-activated osteolysis** (RANK/RANKL pathway ↑)

**Debris source** — conventional PE (submicron particles) · proximal femoral lysis around cemented stem → mostly **cement debris** (OB)

**Epidemiology** — ✓ **most common cause of late THA revision**

**Early (≤6 wk) cementless stem subsidence** — lack of initial stability → ✓ **high BMI (e.g. 44)** or **undersized stem**\
✗ Wrong options: oversized · Dorr A · tapered stem · modular neck

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **groin** (cup), **thigh** (stem), knee · **activity related** · **start-up pain** (first steps) = loose femoral stem

**Exam** — minimal pain with ROM · pain with weight bearing · mild discomfort with SLR/rotation

### Genes & lab markers

**ESR + CRP normal** in aseptic loosening → rule out infection first; normal labs + loosening on X-ray → **no aspiration needed**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP + lateral · X-rays **insensitive**, **underestimate** osteolysis

**General** — **lucency >2 mm** at cement–bone or metal–bone interface · **progressive / continuous** radiolucent line · **component motion on stress views** · change in position on serial films · positive bone scan

**Femoral component** — **subsidence >1 cm** · **cement mantle fracture** · **debonding at lateral shoulder (Gruen zone 1)** · **pedestal** at stem tip · circumferential lucency · lateral cortical stress reaction

**Acetabular component** — migration / change in inclination · **PE liner wear → femoral head sits eccentric (superior)** in cup

![Aseptic loosening of THA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12304-asl1.jpg)

**Resurfacing-specific (Orthobullets)** — change in **peg–neck angle** · ↓ peg tip–lateral cortex distance (also head collapse) · **neck narrowing >10% / after 3 yr** (also impingement)

### Roentgen stereophotogrammetric analysis (RSA)

**Accuracy** — **2°, 0.5 mm**

**Finding** — ✓ **migration >1–2 mm in the first year → ↑ risk of loosening** (varus or retroversion) · both cemented and cementless stems migrate\
✗ Wrong options: cemented do not migrate · any early migration = failure · abandoned

### Cemented stem failure modes (Gruen) – TO

| Mode | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **I — Pistoning** ✓ | Stem subsides within cement (or stem + cement within bone) · **linear lucency around stem and cement** · **commonest** · poor cementing |
| **II — Medial midstem pivot** | Proximal stem medial, distal stem lateral · weak calcar support |
| **III — Calcar pivot (distal toggle)** | Distal tip toggles medial–lateral ("windshield wiper") · poor distal support |
| **IV — Bending cantilever (fatigue)** | Proximal support lost, distal fixed → **stem fracture** |

![Gruen failure modes of cemented stems](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356229-1.png)

### Cemented stem philosophies (TO)

| | **Force-closed / taper-slip** | **Shape-closed / composite beam** |
|---|---|---|
| Surface | **Polished** (Ra **<1 µm**) | **Rough** (Ra >1–2 µm), flanges, collar |
| Examples | **Exeter**, C-stem | **Charnley** |
| Subsidence | **Intended** (radial compression, no shear) · loosening if progressive **after 2 yr** or **>5 mm** total | **Not intended** · >**0.15 mm** after 2 yr → ↑ revision (**4% per 0.1 mm**) |
| Collar | **None** (would block subsidence) · use centralizer | Allowed (calcar load transfer) |
| Fracture | Slightly **↑ periprosthetic fracture** (hoop stress) | Anatomical stems fewer early fractures |

**Cement mantle** — **2–4 mm**, continuous · thin → fissures · **>5 mm** → micromotion, creep · **over-broaching** (stem smaller than rasp, ≥2 mm mantle) vs **line-to-line ("French paradox")** thin mantle · **retrograde gun filling** · 4th-generation: centralize stem · **Barrack–Mulroy** grades mantle quality · setting faster with heat, humidity, longer mixing, handling

## 5 Management

```
Painful THA, suspected loosening
├─ ESR/CRP abnormal → aspirate (exclude PJI)
├─ Stable implant, minimal symptoms → observe (frequent X-rays)
└─ Loose / painful osteolysis / thinning cortex → revise loose component(s)
       ├─ Loose stem → cementless (long) stem
       └─ Loose cup, fixed stem → isolated acetabular revision
```

### Revision indications

**Revise** — pain from aseptic loosening · pain + osteolysis · **extensive osteolysis that would compromise future revision** · pathological / impending fracture

**Observe** — stable implant with minimal symptoms · ↑ frequency of radiographs if wear/lucency

**Untreated loose osteolytic stem** — highest risk ✓ **periprosthetic femoral fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · pseudotumour (MoM) · acetabular fracture · dislocation

**Circumferential lucency + cement mantle fracture + thin cortex** → ✓ **revise to long-stem cementless** soon\
✗ Wrong options: observe · cane (use walker) · bisphosphonates (unproven, no effect on a loose stem) · long cemented stem

**Loose femoral stem, normal ESR/CRP, start-up thigh pain** → ✓ **cementless femoral revision**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · CT · MRI · cemented revision (high early failure)

**Acetabular osteolysis + loose cup, fixed stem** → ✓ **acetabular component revision**\
✗ Wrong options: head/liner exchange + grafting (loose cup) · 2-stage (not infected) · femoral · both

**Typical diagnoses** — hemiarthroplasty 5 yr with pedestal + large lucency, normal labs → ✓ **aseptic loosening** · cement mantle fracture + lateral shoulder debonding → ✓ **femoral loosening** (✗ Wrong options: Brooker I HO · Parkinsonism) · activity thigh pain + zone 1 debonding → ✓ **loose femoral component** (✗ Wrong options: PE synovitis · psoas · asymptomatic lysis · modulus mismatch)

## X vs Y

| | **Aseptic loosening** | **Septic loosening** |
|---|---|---|
| ESR / CRP | **Normal** | ↑ |
| Radiograph | Lucency, subsidence, pedestal | + periosteal reaction, patchy lysis, early lucency |
| Revision | **One-stage** of loose parts | **Two-stage** |

## How it's asked

**"Hemi 5 yr, thigh/knee pain, pedestal, normal labs?"** → **aseptic loosening**

**"RSA finding?"** → **migration >1–2 mm in first year ↑ loosening risk**

**"Cement debonding + mantle fracture?"** → **femoral loosening**

**"Zone 1 lucency after hybrid THA, activity pain?"** → **loose femoral component**

**"Loose osteolytic stem, refuses surgery — risk?"** → **periprosthetic femoral fracture**

**"Circumferential lucency + mantle fracture at 12 yr?"** → **revision to long-stem cementless**

**"Acetabular osteolysis, loose cup, stable stem?"** → **acetabular component revision**

**"Start-up thigh pain, continuous lucency, normal labs?"** → **femoral revision with cementless stem**

**"5 mm subsidence at 6 weeks — contributing factor?"** → **BMI 44** (undersizing)

**"Linear lucency around cemented stem — failure mode?"** → **pistoning** (TO)

**"Radiographic loosening threshold?"** → **lucency >2 mm**, subsidence >1 cm

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Dislocation

*Recon · 22 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1–3%** primary (OB: 0.5–10% primary, **up to 28% after revision**) \
✗ Wrong options: "5–10%"

**Timing** — **70% within first month** · 52% within 3 months · **majority within first year**, then constant lifelong rate · **late = >5 yr**

**Direction** — **75–90% posterior**

### Mechanism

| Dislocation | Position |
|---|---|
| **Posterior** (posterior approach) | ✓ **Flexion + internal rotation + adduction** — shoe tying, low seat/toilet, bending forward |
| **Anterior** (anterior approach) | **Extension + external rotation** |

### Risk factors

| Category | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Patient** | ✓ **Prior hip surgery (greatest; ~2×)** · **higher comorbidity burden** (Elixhauser/ASA) ✓ · **BMI <20 or >35** · neuromuscular/spastic (**Parkinson**), cognitive impairment · drug/alcohol abuse · female (OB) / gender conflicting (Orthobullets) · age >70 · inflammatory arthritis, AVN |
| **Spine** | ✓ **Prior lumbar(osacral) fusion / fixed spinopelvic alignment** |
| **Surgical** | **Posterior approach** (capsule + ER repair → near anterior rate) · component malposition (commonest cause of recurrent) · **↓ femoral offset** · ✓ **↓ head-to-neck ratio** |
| **Implant** | **MoP or MoM** bearings (vs CoP) · **cemented** components (vs uncemented) |
| **Late** | **PE wear (>2 mm)** — commonest late cause · progressive neuro decline · abductor insufficiency · loosening |

**Not risk factors** — male sex · cup 16° anteversion · 45° abduction · high offset (tightens abductors) · direct lateral approach (lowest) · MoM large head (lower dislocation)

**FNB 2024** — fold increase in dislocation after prior spinal fusion (options none/2/3/4×) — answer not given by source

### Spinopelvic mechanics

**Normal (TO)** — ✓ **pelvis retroverts when sitting upright**, anteverts during sit-to-stand · lumbar lordosis **↓ sitting**\
✗ Wrong options: pelvis anteverted sitting · lordosis ↑ sitting

**Sitting with mobile spine** — pelvis tilts posteriorly → **acetabular anteversion ↑** to accommodate femoral flexion · **sacral slope ↓**, pelvic tilt ↑

**Stiff spine** — **<10° change in sacral slope** standing → sitting · ✓ **lack of change (↓) in pelvic tilt** = fixed alignment → **anterior neck impingement → posterior dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ lordosis · ↓ sacral slope · ↑ anteversion · pelvic incidence (fixed parameter)

**Flatback** — standing anterior pelvic plane **−10°** or **PI–LL mismatch >10°** → posterior tilt → **anterior dislocation** in standing

**Management** — lateral **sitting + standing** spine–pelvis–hip films · ✓ **↑ cup anteversion** ± ↑ offset, **dual mobility**, navigation/robotics\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ abduction (vertical cup) · retroverted stem · ↓ offset · constrained liner primary

**Highest-risk history** — ✓ **lumbosacral fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: kyphoplasty · radiation · laminectomy · ablation

![Stiff spine — anterior impingement levering posterior dislocation](../../MCQ/attachments/q219559-illc-illustration-c.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP + cross-table lateral** · **CT** for version/malposition

**Malposition** — inclination **>60°** · anteversion **>20°** · **retroversion**

**Eccentric femoral head** — **PE wear** → impending dislocation

### Acetabular safe zone

| Source | Abduction | Anteversion |
|---|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | **40°** | **15°** |
| **Lewinnek (OB)** | **30–50°** | **5–25°** |
| OB other | 30–50° · 35–45° · 40° | 15–20° · 20° |
| Femoral stem | — | **10–15°** |

**Navigation shows 20° abduction / 20° anteversion** → ✓ **increase abduction by 20°, keep anteversion** (too adducted → flexion impingement, inferior dislocation)

### Cup position and dislocation direction (TO)

| Cup | Dislocation |
|---|---|
| **Excessive anteversion** | **Anterior** with **extension, adduction, ER** (✓ TO false statement: "flexion, adduction, IR") |
| **Retroversion** | **Posterior** with flexion, adduction, IR |
| **Vertical (excess inclination)** | **Superior** with adduction (± adduction contracture, inferior osteophytes) |
| **Horizontal** | **Posterior** with early flexion impingement |

## 5 Management

```
THA dislocation
├─ First early dislocation, components well placed → closed reduction + education
├─ Recurrent (≥2) + malposition → revise malpositioned component
├─ Recurrent, late + PE wear → revision (liner/head ± cup)
└─ Recurrent, well positioned + abductor/soft-tissue deficiency → dual mobility or constrained liner
```

### First dislocation management

**Closed reduction ± immobilization** — **two-thirds** of early dislocations · spica, abduction brace, knee immobilizer

**First dislocation at 4 weeks, components well placed** → ✓ **observation + education on precautions**\
✗ Wrong options: larger head · constrained · 3-month orthosis (only if cognitively impaired)

### Component malposition – acetabular revision

**Revision indication** — **≥2 dislocations** with malalignment, implant failure, or PE wear · malposition = **most common cause of recurrent dislocation** → correct first

**Answers** — vertical cup, 3 posterior dislocations → ✓ **revise acetabular component** (✗ Wrong options: spica · modular stem · brace · constrained liner on malposition) · 10° retroverted cup → ✓ **revise entire cup** to **15–20° anteversion, ~45° abduction** (✗ Wrong options: constrained · offset · 36 mm head · GT osteotomy) · 5° retroversion + 55° abduction → ✓ **acetabular revision** (✗ Wrong options: both · head-neck/length · brace) · underanteverted vertical cup, 3 dislocations bending forward → ✓ **revise cup + larger head/liner** (✗ Wrong options: head/liner exchange alone · PT · brace)

### Revision techniques to increase stability

| Technique | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Realign components** | Retroverted/vertical cup, short neck, low offset, retroverted stem |
| **Larger head** | ↑ head-neck ratio → ↑ ROM to impingement + jump distance |
| **Lateralized liner / extended offset stem** | ↑ offset → abductor tension |
| **Trochanteric advancement** | ↑ abductor tension + compression |
| **Elevated / lipped liner** | Rotate lip toward instability |
| **Dual mobility** | Larger effective head-neck ratio |
| **Constrained liner** | Mechanical capture |
| **Medializing cup** | ✓ **↓ stability** (shortens abductor arm, laxity) — the EXCEPT answer |

**Stem subsidence (cemented, 3 yr)** — effective **neck shortening** → ✓ **↓ abductor tension** → ↑ JRF + instability\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ excursion distance · ↑ arc · ↓ JRF · ↑ offset

**Posterior soft-tissue repair (TO)** — **single myocapsular sleeve (Yadav–Ashok)** = ✓ **capsule + short ERs sutured together to greater trochanter** · ↓ IR by 50%

### Late instability – PE wear & isolated liner exchange

**Late instability (>5 yr)** — ✓ **PE wear (eccentric head)** commonest · usually needs **surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: GT detachment · stem loosening · osteolysis · infection · HO

**Recurrent late dislocations + eccentric wear, normal labs** → ✓ **revision THA for PE wear**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · re-aspiration · observe · PT

**Isolated liner exchange** — stable, well-aligned implants with PE wear as **sole** cause · ✓ **most common complication = dislocation / instability**\
✗ Wrong options: osteolysis · shell–liner failure · PE fracture · sciatic injury · acetabular fracture

**Cementing a liner into a well-fixed shell** (broken locking tines) — **liner several sizes smaller** than shell (room for cement) ✓ · **score PE + smooth shell** · fully seat · avoid impingement · commonest complication still **dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: "closely match liner and shell size"

### Constrained liners

**Indications** — ✓ recurrent instability with **well-positioned components** · **abductor deficiency / unrepairable capsule** · **neuromuscular / cognitive** disease · no identifiable cause · failed larger head + trochanteric advancement · elderly low-demand

**Contraindications** — **component malposition** ✓ (revise first) · **PE wear alone** ✓ (EXCEPT answer) · pelvic discontinuity · infection

**Limited because of** — ✓ **high failure rates** (fatigue, **ring/liner dissociation**) · ↑ bone–implant stress · ↓ ROM, worse function · last resort

**Answers** — cup 20° anteversion + 40° abduction, recurrent → ✓ **constrained** (✗ Wrong options: discontinuity · infected · 15° retroverted stem · 10° retroverted/60° cup) · bariatric weight loss, failed big head + GT advancement, aligned → ✓ **constrained** (✗ Wrong options: resection · brace · capsular ablation · bipolar) · abductors absent, components well placed → indication ✓ **incompetent abductors** · post-MoM deficient abductors, Trendelenburg → ✓ **constrained liner** (✗ Wrong options: medialize cup · offset · large ceramic head)

**Broken constrained ring** → ✓ **↑ dislocation risk** · dislocated constrained + malposition + Parkinson → ✓ **acetabular revision with constrained implant**\
✗ Wrong options: closed or open reduction

![Broken constrained liner ring](../../MCQ/attachments/q1169-illa-broken-liner-xr-dislocation.jpg)

### Dual mobility & abductor deficiency

**Design** — small inner head captive in mobile PE liner, articulating with shell (Bousquet 1974, France) · Charnley low friction + McKee–Farrar ↑ head-neck ratio · ✓ **↑ arc of motion before impingement** + ↑ jump distance\
✗ Wrong options: locking ring (constrained) · ↓ jump distance · lipped liner · face-changing liner

**Unconstrained** — tripolar may add constraint ring · ✓ **"constrained design reduces dislocation" is NOT true** of DM (TO) · both articulations share centre · inner head dominates normal ROM, outer in high ROM

**Indications (TO)** — high instability risk: neuromuscular, cognitive, **ASA ≥3**, **>75 yr with prior hip surgery** · revision THA · THA after **femoral neck fracture** · tumour · ✓ **reduces instability in revision**\
✗ Wrong options: jumbo cup (↑ instability) · small head · capsular debridement · ↓ abductor tension

**Intraprosthetic dislocation (IPD)** — unique to DM · ✓ **wear of PE liner's retentive chamfer** · head articulates on metal shell → **acute shortening, limp**, metallosis · X-ray: **eccentric head** + **"bubble sign"** (liner in soft tissue) · can follow closed reduction attempt

![Dual mobility — bubble sign of intraprosthetic dislocation](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17099762-3.png)

### Salvage

**Conversion to hemiarthroplasty/bipolar** — soft-tissue deficiency · contraindicated if acetabular bone compromised · rarely used (DM) · groin pain, migration

**Resection arthroplasty** — options exhausted · bone + soft-tissue loss · **psychiatric secondary gain**

## X vs Y

| | **Dual mobility** | **Constrained liner** |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | ↑ ROM before impingement, ↑ jump distance | **Mechanical capture** (locking ring) |
| ROM | Large | **Restricted** |
| Failure | **Intraprosthetic dislocation** | **Ring/liner dissociation**, loosening |
| Use | High-risk primaries, revision | Last resort — abductor/soft-tissue deficiency |

| | **Early dislocation** | **Late dislocation (>5 yr)** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Malposition, soft tissue, noncompliance | **PE wear**, neuro decline, abductor loss |
| Treatment | Often closed reduction | Usually **surgery** |

## How it's asked

**"Normal pelvis in sitting (TO)?"** → **retroverted**

**"Broken ring (arrow) after revision — risk?"** → **dislocation**

**"Posterior approach — most dangerous position?"** → **flexion + internal rotation**

**"Higher dislocation risk?"** → **previous hip surgery**

**"Increases instability?"** → **reduced femoral head-to-neck ratio**

**"Lumbar fusion — spinopelvic change increasing risk?"** → **decreased (unchanged) pelvic tilt**

**"Dislocation after primary THA (2023) — true?"** → **higher comorbidity burden ↑ risk**

**"Stiff spine — adjust implants?"** → **increase cup anteversion**

**"Which history ↑ dislocation?"** → **lumbosacral fusion**

**"False about cup position (TO)?"** → **anteverted cup dislocates anteriorly in flexion, adduction, IR**

**"3 dislocations, vertical cup?"** → **revise acetabular component**

**"Retroverted cup 10°, 3 dislocations?"** → **revise entire acetabular component**

**"Unstable after reduction — EXCEPT increases stability?"** → **medializing the cup**

**"Navigation 20/20 — correct to safe zone?"** → **↑ abduction 20°, keep anteversion**

**"Five dislocations, retroverted cup?"** → **revise cup to 15° anteversion, 45° abduction**

**"Underanteverted cup, 3 dislocations, good abductors?"** → **revise cup + larger head/liner**

**"Cup 5° retroverted, 55° abduction?"** → **acetabular component revision**

**"Most common complication after liner revision?"** → **dislocation**

**"Liner exchange + grafting — disclose?"** → **postoperative instability**

**"5 late dislocations, eccentric wear, normal labs?"** → **revision for PE wear**

**"PE exchange with standard UHMWPE + grafting — ↑ risk?"** → **dislocation**

**"Late instability with eccentric head?"** → **wear**

**"Cemented liner into shell — most common complication?"** → **dislocation**

**"First instability at 15 yr?"** → **polyethylene wear**

**"Appropriate constrained liner use?"** → **recurrent dislocation with well-positioned cup (20°/40°)**

**"Constrained liner EXCEPT?"** → **PE wear**

**"Indication shown on revision radiographs?"** → **instability**

**"Post-MoM, deficient abductors, 4 dislocations?"** → **constrained liner**

**"Failed head size + GT advancement, aligned?"** → **constrained liner**

**"Cemented constrained liner dissociated — EXCEPT?"** → **closely matching liner and shell size**

**"Dislocated constrained implant + malposition, Parkinson?"** → **acetabular revision with constrained implant**

**"What limits constrained liners?"** → **high failure rates**

**"Avoid constrained liner because?"** → **component malposition**

**"Not true about dual mobility (TO)?"** → **constrained design reduces dislocations**

**"DM hip, shortening after fall?"** → **wear of retentive chamfer (IPD)** (TO)

**"Second-stage revision — reduce instability?"** → **dual mobility**

**"Constrained liner indication (abductors absent)?"** → **incompetent abductors**

**"DM with PFR — mechanism?"** → **↑ arc of motion before impingement**

**"Eccentric head + liner in soft tissue after reduction?"** → **intraprosthetic dislocation**

**"Cemented stem subsidence — effect?"** → **↓ abductor tension**

**"First dislocation, well-placed implants?"** → **observation + education**

**"Single myocapsular sleeve repair (TO)?"** → **capsule + ERs sutured together to GT**

# THA Iliopsoas Impingement

*Recon · 23 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Etiology

**Definition** — under-recognized cause of **recurrent groin pain** after THA (up to **4.3%** of painful THAs)

**Causes** — ✓ **malpositioned acetabular component with anterior overhang** (most common) · cup **too large**, **retroverted** or **lateral** · **excessively long screws** penetrating ilium · **retained cement** · LLD (Orthobullets lists; OB: not significant)

**Version trap** — anterior overhang from **retroversion** · (✗ Wrong options: excessive offset → **trochanteric bursitis**)\
✗ Wrong options: excessive anteversion

**Anatomy** — iliopsoas from lumbar transverse processes, passes **anterior to hip** → **lesser trochanter**

## 3 Presentation

**Onset** — average **20 months** after index THA\
✗ Wrong options: 2 weeks

**Pain** — **groin**, with **active hip flexion**: **ascending stairs** (not descending), getting into/out of car, rising from chair · **pain with passive extension**

**Exam** — subtle: slight limp, groin tenderness, rare palpable snap · ✓ **pain with resisted seated hip flexion or active straight-leg raise** · passive ROM/SLR painless

**Not iliopsoas** — **start-up groin pain** → cup loosening · start-up **thigh** pain → stem loosening · constant night pain + fevers → PJI

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP pelvis + AP/lateral hip · ✓ **cross-table lateral** shows **anterior cup prominence** (TO next step; ✗ Wrong options: MRI · PET · bursa aspiration)

**CT** — cup position, **anterior overhang**, excludes other causes · **MRI** — limited by artefact (shows tendon inflammation)

**Rule out** — infection (ESR, CRP, aspiration) + loosening first

### Diagnostic injection

**Image-guided local anaesthetic ± steroid into iliopsoas sheath** — ✓ **most reliable confirmation** · diagnostic **and** therapeutic\
✗ Wrong options: GT bursa injection · repeat aspiration · Ober test · knee films · bone scan · revision before diagnosis

![Anterior cup overhang causing iliopsoas impingement](../../MCQ/attachments/q3590-illa-tha-overhang-iliopsoas.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Groin pain with resisted hip flexion after THA
├─ Exclude infection + loosening → diagnostic iliopsoas sheath injection
├─ Cup well positioned (normal X-ray/CT) → arthroscopic iliopsoas tenotomy
└─ Excessive anterior overhang (>8 mm) → acetabular component revision
```

### Treatment – tendon release vs cup revision

**Nonoperative** — rest, NSAIDs, stretching, CT-guided steroid injection · **rarely sufficient** (relief in only **~50%**)

**Normal radiographs / well-placed cup** → ✓ **iliopsoas tenotomy** (arthroscopic)\
✗ Wrong options: indefinite activity modification · femoral/acetabular revision

**Anterior overhang** → ✓ **acetabular component revision** for permanent relief · **>8 mm prominence** → revision far better than tenotomy (Chalmers: persistent pain 50% nonop vs 24% operative)\
✗ Wrong options: tenotomy alone · lipped liner (worsens) · repeat injection · both components

**Arthroscopic tenotomy technique** — **extra-capsular** (at lesser trochanter, common after THA) or **trans-capsular** (between anterior labrum and zona orbicularis) · hip in slight flexion + ✓ **external rotation** → brings tendon toward surgeon, away from **femoral neurovascular bundle** · anterior portal ~**15 mm** from LFCN branches → shift portal lateral\
✗ Wrong options: IR (for deep gluteal space/sciatic) · MFCA (posterior)

## X vs Y

| | **Iliopsoas impingement** | **Trochanteric bursitis** | **Cup loosening** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | Groin, **active flexion / stairs up** | **Lateral**, side lying | Groin **start-up** |
| Cause | **Anterior cup overhang**, long screws | ↑ offset | Osteolysis, micromotion |
| Test | **Sheath injection** | Bursa injection | Serial X-rays |

## How it's asked

**"Groin pain 3 months post-THA, painful active SLR, normal labs/X-ray — next (TO)?"** → **cross-table lateral X-ray**

**"Most likely to determine cause of pain (stairs, passive extension)?"** → **local anaesthetic injection of iliopsoas sheath**

**"Resisted seated flexion pain, well-placed components — next?"** → **image-guided lidocaine injection into iliopsoas sheath**

**"Structure labelled 1 (groin pain with stairs up)?"** → **iliopsoas tendon**

**"MRI inflamed iliopsoas — cause?"** → **anterior overhang of acetabular component**

**"Stair-climbing groin pain, prominent cup anteriorly?"** → **iliopsoas impingement**

**"Offset ↓ 2 mm, length ↑ 3 mm, prominent cup — likely?"** → **iliopsoas tendonitis**

**"History + exam typical of psoas impingement?"** → **pain with passive extension + resisted flexion, onset ~20 months**

**"Injection relieved pain, normal X-rays — next?"** → **iliopsoas tendon release**

**"Arthroscopic tenotomy — hip position?"** → **external rotation (protect femoral neurovascular bundle)**

**"Recurrent pain with confirmed anterior overhang — permanent fix?"** → **acetabular component revision**
