# THA Implant Fixation

*Recon · 24 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Types of fixation

**Cement** — **PMMA**, acts as **grout** (interlock, no adhesion)

**Biologic (cementless)** — **ingrowth** into porous coating · **ongrowth** onto grit-blasted surface

**Collared stem** — transfers load to **calcar**, ↓ early subsidence, controls depth · cemented or cementless · **protective against early periprosthetic fracture**

**History** — cement: **Gluck 1891**, **Charnley 1950s** (dental cement) · "cement disease" 1980s → cementless · **AML (1983)** first microporous ingrowth stem → proximal coating (thigh pain, osteolysis)

**Trend** — **93% of US THAs cementless** (2012)

**Templating** — calibrated radiograph · goal: **limb length + femoral offset**

### Dorr classification

**Ratio** — canal diameter **10 cm below mid-lesser trochanter ÷ inner diameter at mid-lesser trochanter**

| Type | Ratio | Features | Stem |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** ("champagne flute") | **<0.5** | Thick cortices AP + lateral · thick posterior **fin** · young, heavy, male | **Uncemented** (flat taper) |
| **B** | **0.5–0.75** | **Posterior cortex thinning**, fin eroded, **"rat bites"** | Uncemented · flat taper may be **rotationally unstable** → metadiaphyseal/diaphyseal or cemented |
| **C** ("stovepipe") | **>0.75** | Thin, fuzzy cortices both views · elderly, thin, female | **Cemented** |

**TO EXCEPT (Dorr B)** — ✓ **"ratio <0.5"** is false (that is A)

![Dorr classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5003-dorr.jpg)

### Cemented stem types (Orthobullets)

| Type | Subtypes / examples |
|---|---|
| **1 — Taper-slip** | 1A anatomic (Olympia) · **1B double taper (Exeter, CPT)** · 1C triple taper (**C-stem**, CPCS) · traditional or line-to-line (Corail, Avenir, CMK) |
| **2 — Composite beam** | 2A anatomic (Lubinus SP) · 2B straight (Summit, Accolade C, Versys, Spectron) · 2C wedge (Müller) |

**Cementless stems** — A flat taper · B quadrangular taper · C fit-and-fill · D conical · E cylindrical · F calcar-guided short neck-preserving

**Modularity** — 1 head · 2 neck · 3 subtrochanteric · 4 metaphyseal sleeve

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria – cemented vs cementless

| Component | Cemented | Cementless |
|---|---|---|
| **Femur** | **Elderly / osteopenic** · **irradiated bone** · **Dorr C (stovepipe)** | Young, active males · old with good bone · **revision** (cemented revision stems fail more) |
| **Acetabulum** | Poor bone, complex revision · **fails more** (cement poor in **shear**) | **Preferred** (porous hemispheric) — except poor bone stock or irradiation |

**Elderly woman, minimize fracture + durable** → ✓ **hybrid (cementless cup + cemented stem)**\
✗ Wrong options: cemented cup (early loosening) · hemi · press-fit stem (fracture) · resurfacing

**Cemented vs cementless femur in elderly (registry)** → ✓ **lower early revision**, ↓ iatrogenic + early periprosthetic fracture, ↓ early aseptic loosening · LLD no different · intraop fracture **3.5% uncemented vs 0.4% cemented** · overall **3% vs 0.23%** (female, >65)

### Cemented fixation

**Generations**

| Generation | Technique |
|---|---|
| **1st** | Hand mixed, **finger packed**, no restrictor/preparation |
| **2nd** | **Cement restrictor**, **cement gun**, brush + dry canal |
| **3rd** | **Vacuum mixing** (↓ porosity), **pressurization**, **pulsatile lavage** |
| 4th (TO) | + **stem centralization** |

**Optimized by** — ↓ porosity (vacuum) · pressurization · **mantle >2 mm** (OB ~2 mm ideal; TO **2–4 mm**, >5 mm ↑ micromotion) · **stiff stem** · centralization · **clean dry bone** · **smooth stem, rounded corners** · **no mantle defects** (stem touching cortex) · neutral position\
✗ Wrong options: varus/valgus

**↑ Failure** — ✓ **PMMA-precoated stems** (extra interface)\
✗ Wrong options: collar–calcar contact · smooth corners · 2 mm mantle · stiff stem >115 GPa (stiffer improves)

**Osteopenia** — ✓ **stronger bone–cement interface** (porosity → deeper penetration) · young male dense bone → weaker interface · pressurization ↑ penetration + fracture resistance

### Barrack–Harris cement grading

| Grade | Appearance |
|---|---|
| **A** | Complete canal fill, **"white-out"** |
| **B** | Slight radiolucency (<50% of interface) |
| **C** | ✓ **Radiolucency >50%** or **incomplete mantle** |
| **D** | **Gross radiolucency** and/or **no cement around stem tip** |

### Bone cement properties (TO)

**Mechanics** — **strong in compression**, ✓ **weak in shear** (EXCEPT answer) · **E ≈ 2.2 GPa** · grout aiding load transfer

**Chemistry** — liquid **MMA monomer** + powder (PMMA–styrene co-polymer) · initiator **benzoyl peroxide** (powder) · accelerator **DmpT** (liquid) · stabilizer **hydroquinone** · radiopacifier **zirconium dioxide** / barium sulphate · ✓ **never 100% conversion** — **2–6% residual monomer** → 0.5% at 2–3 wk (EXCEPT answer) · glass transition **~70 °C** wet (above body temperature)

**Curing** — mixing → sticky → working → hardening · **heat, humidity, faster mixing ↓ working time** · pre-chilling prolongs it · antibiotics **<2 g** per pack keep strength

**Calcium phosphate cement** — no heat, osteoconductive · **not for load bearing** (EXCEPT answer) — weak, brittle, low cohesion in water

### Biologic fixation

| Parameter | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **Pore size** | **50–300 µm**, ideally 50–200 (Orthobullets) · **50–400 µm** (OB) |
| **Porosity** | **40–80%** (more → ingrowth but weaker, metal shearing) |
| **Gaps** | **<50 µm** |
| **Micromotion** | **<150 µm** (>150 → fibrous) · initial rigid fixation **<50 µm** |
| **Cortical contact** | Maximal |
| **Bone** | Viable — **irradiation ↑ aseptic loosening** |

**Press-fit** — implant slightly larger than reamed (no screws needed) · **line-to-line** — same size, screws often added

**Coatings** — **proximal porous** (less stress shielding) · **extensive porous** (more proximal shielding; revision) · **grit-blasted** ongrowth — always extensively coated (weaker fixation) · **hydroxyapatite** — osteoconductive adjunct · ✓ **shorter time to biologic fixation in animals**, no clinical advantage · needs **high crystallinity**, optimal (not thick) thickness, rough surface

### Radiographic signs of stable fixation

**Cementless stem** — **spot welds** (endosteal bone to porous surface) · no lines around porous part · **proximal stress shielding** (extensively coated) · no subsidence

**Cementless cup** — no migration · no progressive lines · intact screws

![Spot welds](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5003-spot-weld.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Aseptic loosening** — poor initial fixation, mechanical loss, particle osteolysis · cup → groin/buttock pain · stem → **thigh, start-up pain** · serial X-rays, bone scan · revise loose parts

**Stress shielding** — proximal bone loss around well-fixed stem · risk: ✓ **stiff stem (modulus mismatch; Co-Cr > Ti)** — most important · **large diameter** (biggest contributor to stiffness) · **extensive porous coating** · preoperative osteopenia · clinical significance unknown, no treatment

**Intraoperative fracture** — risk: **press-fit** · collar protective · **acetabular**: stable cup → add screws; unstable → remove, fix, reinsert with screws · **femoral**: stable stem → limit WB ± cerclage; unstable → remove, fix, stem bypassing by **2 cortical diameters**

## X vs Y

| | **Cemented femur** | **Cementless femur** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | Elderly, osteopenic, **Dorr C**, irradiated | Young, active, Dorr A/B, **revision** |
| Fracture | **↓** | ↑ (press-fit) |
| Early revision (elderly) | **↓** | ↑ |
| Failure mode | Debonding, mantle defects | Fibrous ingrowth if micromotion >150 µm |

## How it's asked

**"Optimal pore size?"** → **50–400 µm** (OB) · 50–300 µm (Orthobullets)

**"HA-coated vs porous stems in animals?"** → **shorter time to biologic fixation**

**"Barrack–Harris — incomplete mantle, >50% lucency?"** → **grade C** (TO)

**"Bone cement EXCEPT?"** → **strong in shear** (TO)

**"Bone cement EXCEPT?"** → **hardening = polymerization of 100% MMA** (TO)

**"Calcium phosphate vs acrylic — false?"** → **better in load-bearing areas** (TO)

**"↑ cemented femoral failure?"** → **PMMA-precoated stems**

**"Osteopenia and bone–cement interface?"** → **improved mechanical integrity**

**"78-yr-old woman — minimize fracture, durable?"** → **hybrid: cementless cup + cemented stem**

**"Cemented vs cementless stem in elderly?"** → **lower risk of early revision**

**"Dorr B — EXCEPT?"** → **cortical thickness ratio <0.5** (TO)

**"Most important stress-shielding factor?"** → **stem stiffness (modulus mismatch)**

**"Micromotion threshold for bone ingrowth?"** → **<150 µm** (gaps <50 µm)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Leg Length Discrepancy

*Recon · 25 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Litigation** — **2nd most common cause of THA litigation** (after **nerve injury**)

**Prevention** — **preoperative planning/templating** ↓ incidence

### Mechanism

| Cause | Effect |
|---|---|
| **ABDuction contracture** | Involved hemipelvis **lower** → apparent **LONG** leg |
| **ADDuction contracture** | Involved hemipelvis **higher** → apparent **SHORT** leg |
| **Weak abductors** | Sensation of **long leg without true LLD** · resolves **3–6 months** |
| Unilateral OA | Pre-op shortening (cartilage loss, contracture) → equalized leg **feels long** |

### What changes length

| Lengthens | Does NOT change length |
|---|---|
| **Longer femoral neck** | ✓ **↑ femoral offset** (moves femur laterally → abductor tension, stability) |
| **High (proximal) neck cut** | **Medialized cup** (tension/JRF only) |
| **Incomplete seating of cemented stem** | Cemented vs cementless stem (if done properly) |
| **Inferior cup** (graft above to seat lower) | — |
| — | **Short-neck** component → **shortens** |

![Neck length vs offset effect on limb length](../../MCQ/attachments/q995-illb-picture1.jpg)

### Offset restoration

**Decreased offset** — femur closer to pelvis → ✓ **bone-on-bone impingement** (GT on pelvis) → levering, instability · **↓ abductor moment, ↑ JRF**, abductor weakness, soft-tissue laxity\
✗ Wrong options: trochanteric bursitis

**Increased offset** — **trochanteric pain / bursitis** · ↑ stability, ↓ impingement

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **perceived LLD** despite anatomic equality

**True length** — **ASIS → medial malleolus**

**Apparent length** — includes contractures + pelvic obliquity · hard to measure

**Perceived LLD at 6 weeks, equal lengths on X-ray, normal motion/strength** → ✓ **perceived LLD**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion/adduction contracture (would make the operated leg feel short) · medialized cup · femoral malposition

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**AP pelvis** — compare limb lengths (inter-teardrop / ischial line to lesser trochanter) · ↑ neck length · **offset change does not alter length**

![Leg length discrepancy on AP pelvis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12115-lld.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
LLD after THA
├─ Perceived / apparent (contracture, weak abductors) → reassure, PT, wait 3–6 months
├─ True LLD persisting >6 months → shoe lift (most)
└─ Significant LLD affecting life >6–12 months → revision (rare; dislocation risk if shortening)
```

**Shoe lift** — adequate in most · **wait 6 months** for muscle relaxation

**Revision** — rare · significant LLD unresolved at **6–12 months** affecting quality of life · **shortening revision risks dislocation**

### Intraoperative factors

**Restore anatomic length** — templating · variable neck lengths · intraoperative length check · clinical + radiographic pre-op assessment · ✓ **variable-offset modular system does NOT restore length** (EXCEPT answer)

## How it's asked

**"Help restore limb length EXCEPT?"** → **modular system with variable femoral offset**

**"Lengthening techniques EXCEPT?"** → **increasing femoral neck offset**

**"Most likely to lengthen the leg?"** → **very proximal neck cut**\
✗ Wrong options: medialized cup · high-offset stem · short neck · cemented stem

**"Equal lengths on X-ray, operated leg feels long at 6 weeks?"** → **perceived LLD**

**"Failure to restore offset (TO)?"** → **bone-to-bone impingement**

**"Abduction contracture causes?"** → **apparent long leg**

**"When to treat LLD with shoe lift?"** → **after 6 months**

**"Second most common THA litigation cause?"** → **LLD** (first: nerve injury)

# THA Other Complications

*Recon · 26 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Scope

**This chapter** — **heterotopic ossification**, **squeaking**, **postoperative anaemia/transfusion**, stem fracture, medical complications, femoral nerve palsy, persistent pain · other THA complications have their own chapters

## 6 Complications

### Heterotopic ossification

**Frequency** — common **radiographic** finding (first year), rarely functional · **~15%** clinically significant stiffness (Brooker III/IV) · more after THA than TKA · 2–5% despite prophylaxis

**Biology** — mesenchymal cells in muscle/fascia → osteoblasts · starts **within 16 h** of insult, matures over >1 yr into lamellar bone

**Risk factors** — prolonged surgery · excessive soft-tissue handling · **hypertrophic OA** · ✓ **male** · ✓ **ankylosing spondylitis** · **prior HO** (other site or **contralateral hip**) · DISH · traumatic brain injury

| Brooker grade | Clinical significance |
|---|---|
| **I–II** | Usually asymptomatic, inconsequential (I not a pain source) |
| **III–IV** | Clinically significant stiffness, may need excision |

![Heterotopic ossification after THA (Brooker)](../../MCQ/attachments/q218204-illa-illustration-a.jpg)

### HO prophylaxis

| Method | Regimen |
|---|---|
| **NSAIDs** | **Indomethacin** classic (other NSAIDs equivalent) · **7–21 days** (≥10 days; longer no benefit) · not 2 days · not in renal insufficiency/warfarin |
| **Radiation** | **600–800 cGy** (700–800 as effective as higher) · Orthobullets: within **24–48 h post-op** · OB: **within 24 h pre-op** or **72 h post-op** · Iorio/Healy: **≤6 h pre-op** or **≤4 days post-op** · pre-op = post-op efficacy (73% vs 76%) |
| **Not effective** | Drains · **etidronate (EHDP)** — delays mineralization only · radiation 1 week before surgery · 1,500 cGy (unnecessary) |

**Answers** — prior HO + renal insufficiency + warfarin → ✓ **800 cGy pre-op on morning of surgery** (✗ Wrong options: indomethacin 3–6 wk · 400 cGy day 2 · no treatment) · ankylosing spondylitis → ✓ **pre-op radiation 700 cGy** (✗ Wrong options: sciatic monitoring · 1,500 cGy · CPM · soft collar) · contralateral HO → ✓ **radiation 8 h post-op** (✗ Wrong options: indomethacin 2 days · drains · EHDP · RT 1 week pre-op)

**Prophylaxis candidates (Purcell–Lachiewicz)** — bilateral hypertrophic OA · OA + lumbar **DISH** · **AS** · HO after contralateral hip surgery

### HO treatment

**Early Brooker III at 6 weeks, asymptomatic** → ✓ **observe, repeat X-ray in 6 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: indomethacin (too late) · RT (too late) · excision at 10 wk (immature, ↑ recurrence)

**Formed HO** — prophylaxis ineffective · ✓ **only surgical excision** eradicates

**Excision** — severe loss of motion · **wait ≥6 months** for maturation + capsule · confirm maturity: stable serial films, normal bone scan uptake, **normalized serum ALP** (non-specific) · **pre-op CT** for resection planning · **perioperative RT or NSAIDs** after excision ✓ (mature grade III/IV + severe limitation → **excision + post-op irradiation**) · instability after excision common → have **constrained liners** available · protect sciatic nerve

**Stiffness at 6 months with HO** → ✓ **first check ALP, ESR, CRP** (exclude PJI, assess maturity)\
✗ Wrong options: late indomethacin/RT · immediate excision · excision + constrained revision

### Squeaking

**Definition** — high-pitched audible sound with hip motion (e.g. extension from flexion on stairs)

**Incidence** — **CoC 0.5–10%** · **MoM 4–5%** · revision for squeaking **0.5%**

**Causes** — **impingement** · **edge loading** · **component malposition** · **loss of fluid-film lubrication** · **third-body particles** · **thin flexible titanium stem** · ✓ **NOT subclinical infection**

**Painless squeak, well-positioned implants** → ✓ **observe**\
✗ Wrong options: revise to PE · change cup abduction

**10-yr alumina CoC results** — ✓ **low osteolysis, squeaking and head fracture** (not eliminated)\
✗ Wrong options: same osteolysis as MoP · noisy hips ↑ osteolysis · fractures eliminated

![Edge loading causing squeaking](../../MCQ/attachments/q4756-illa-illus-a-superior-edge-loading.jpg)

### Metal wear & ARMD

**Pseudotumour risk (MoM)** — ✓ **edge loading** (most significant) · double heat treatment · low carbon content · **female**, **dysplasia**\
✗ Wrong options: obesity · age >65

**Large proximal femoral osteolysis, fixed MoM implants, normal ESR/CRP** → ✓ **adverse reaction to metal debris**\
✗ Wrong options: loosening · metastasis · PJI

### Stem fracture

**Mechanism** — ✓ **cantilever bending** fatigue: **loss of proximal (calcar) support + solid distal fixation** → **middle-third** fracture · modular fluted stems fracture at **modular junction** with poor proximal bone · body weight + cyclic stress\
✗ Wrong options: torsion · pistoning · subsidence

### Postoperative anaemia

**Best predictor of transfusion** — **low preoperative haemoglobin** · prevention **TXA** · transfuse at **Hb 7–8**

### Medical complications

**Intraoperative death** — fat + marrow embolism during femoral preparation/cementing · lung histology may show ✓ **methylmethacrylate cement in pulmonary capillaries**\
✗ Wrong options: thrombus PE · infarct · granuloma · amyloid

**Ileus** — ✓ **~1%** of TJA · **THA > TKA** · risk: **older age, male, prior abdominal surgery**

**Ogilvie syndrome** (acute colonic pseudo-obstruction) — elderly, male, narcotics, hip arthroplasty · distended non-tender abdomen POD 3 → ✓ **abdominal radiograph** first\
✗ Wrong options: occult blood · NG lavage · US · CT

### Femoral nerve palsy (TO)

**Presentation** — weak SLR, **cannot actively extend knee** next day

**Causes** — retractor **anterior to iliopsoas** · **anterior capsulotomy/capsulectomy** · femoral retraction · **extruded cement** (acetabular pressurization) · **iliacus haematoma**

**NOT a cause** — ✓ **lengthening >3 cm** → **sciatic (peroneal)** palsy instead

### Postoperative shortening (TO)

**Shortening on POD 1** — ✓ **low femoral neck-shaft angle** (↓ vertical offset)\
✗ Wrong options: +8 head (lengthens) · high NSA (lengthens) · undersized stem (subsides later with ambulation)

### Persistent pain workup

**No improvement after THA, groin → medial thigh → past knee, stairs/uphill, normal labs** → ✓ **image lumbar spine** (stenosis) · stenosis ↑ nerve palsy risk · operative order debated (spine first ↑ hip dislocation)\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · EMG first · bone scan · CT

### Osteolysis revision

**Failed cup with bone loss** → ✓ **cementless porous-coated acetabular revision** ± structural graft\
✗ Wrong options: bipolar · impaction grafting both sides · cemented jumbo cup · protrusio ring (Paprosky III only)

### Disparities

**Black non-Hispanic + Hispanic vs white non-Hispanic** — ↑ **LOS**, ↑ 30-day readmission, ↑ non-home discharge · ✓ **WNH shortest LOS**

## X vs Y

| | **HO prophylaxis** | **HO treatment** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | Peri-op (RT ±24–72 h, NSAIDs 7–21 d) | **≥6 months**, mature |
| Tool | Indomethacin / 600–800 cGy | **Excision** + RT/NSAID |

| | **Femoral nerve palsy** | **Sciatic (peroneal) palsy** |
|---|---|---|
| Deficit | Quadriceps (SLR, knee extension) | Foot drop, EHL |
| Cause | Anterior retractor, cement, iliacus haematoma | **Lengthening**, posterior approach, haematoma |

## How it's asked

**"Symptomatic mature HO 2 yr after THA?"** → **surgical excision + post-op irradiation**

**"Prior HO, renal insufficiency, warfarin — prophylaxis?"** → **800 cGy pre-op morning of surgery**

**"Brooker III at 6 weeks, asymptomatic?"** → **observe, repeat X-rays in 6 weeks**

**"AS patient for THA — consider?"** → **pre-op radiation 700 cGy**

**"Most effective to prevent contralateral HO?"** → **radiation 8 h post-op**

**"Stiff THA with HO at 6 months — next?"** → **ALP, ESR, CRP**

**"Squeaking contributors EXCEPT?"** → **subclinical infection**

**"Painless squeak, good position?"** → **observe**

**"10-yr alumina CoC results?"** → **low osteolysis, squeaking, head fracture**

**"Most significant pseudotumour risk with MoM?"** → **edge loading**

**"MoM, large femoral osteolysis, normal labs?"** → **adverse reaction to metal debris**

**"Stem fracture mechanism?"** → **cantilever bending**

**"Lung histology after intraop death?"** → **methylmethacrylate cement**

**"Ileus risk?"** → **~1% of TJA**

**"Distended abdomen POD 3, BM yesterday?"** → **abdominal radiograph (Ogilvie)**

**"Weak SLR next day — cannot cause it (TO)?"** → **lengthening >3 cm**

**"Shortening on POD 1 (TO)?"** → **low femoral neck-shaft angle**

**"No relief after THA, radiating thigh pain past knee?"** → **lumbar spine imaging**

**"Failed cup with bone loss at 12 yr?"** → **cementless acetabular revision**

**"Minorities vs WNH after TJA?"** → **WNH shorter LOS**

**"Best predictor of transfusion?"** → **low preoperative haemoglobin**

# THA Periprosthetic Fracture

*Recon · 27 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

| Type | Incidence |
|---|---|
| **Intraop femur — primary uncemented** | **3.5%** (OB: ~4%; 1–18% / 1.5–27.8% quoted) |
| **Intraop femur — cemented** | **0.4%** |
| Intraop femur — primary / revision THA | **0.1–5% / 3–21%** |
| **Intraop acetabulum** | **Cemented 0.2% · cementless 0.4%** |
| **Postoperative femur** | **0.1%** overall · **0.1–3%** primary cementless · most at **stem tip** |
| Overall (Abdel, 32,000 THAs) | **1.7%** · uncemented **3%** vs cemented **0.23%** · female, >65 |

**Prevention** — **pre-operative templating** · adequate exposure · care with cementless stems in poor bone (RA, osteoporosis) · low threshold to cement

### Risk factors

| Intraoperative femur | Postoperative femur |
|---|---|
| **Female** ✓ · **cementless / press-fit** · osteoporosis · revision · impaction grafting · technical error · **MIS** (controversial) · sclerotic bone (sickle cell) · metabolic bone disease, Paget | Poor bone · **cementless** stems · compromised bone stock · revision · **collarless** stems |

**Timing pattern** — **cementless** stems fracture **early (<6 months)** — wedge-tapered → proximal, cylindrical fully coated → distal split · **cemented** stems fracture **late (~5 yr)** at/below tip

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP + lateral of hip **and whole femur** · CT for detail

**Loose-stem clues** — **prodromal thigh/start-up pain** · **subsidence** vs earlier films · **cement-column disruption** · long spiral fracture to stem tip

**Stem stability** — ✓ **intraoperative assessment = gold standard** · up to **20% discrepancy** pre- vs intraop · **B1 vs B2 hard to separate on X-ray**

### Vancouver classification – postoperative

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **AG** | **Greater trochanter** · often **osteolysis** | **<2 cm** displaced → nonop, partial WB, limit abduction · **>2 cm** (TO: **>2.5 cm**) → **ORIF claw plate / cables / TBW** · address osteolysis |
| **AL** | **Lesser trochanter** | **Nonoperative** (ORIF if large medial cortex — TO) |
| **B1** | Around/just below stem, **well-fixed** | **ORIF** — locking plate + cerclage cables ± strut |
| **B2** | Around/below stem, **loose**, **good proximal bone** | ✓ **Revision to long porous-coated cementless (or tapered fluted) stem** bypassing **2 cortical diameters** + fixation of fragments |
| **B3** | Loose + **poor / comminuted proximal bone** | **PFR (elderly, low demand)** · **APC / proximal femoral allograft (young)** · tapered fluted stem + struts |
| **C** | **Well below** stem | **ORIF plate**, leave arthroplasty alone |

**TO** — "Vancouver type 2" → ✓ **shaft around stem**\
✗ Wrong options: GT · LT · distal

![Vancouver classification of postoperative fractures](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17099981-1.png)

### Vancouver classification – intraoperative

| Type | Location · subtype | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **A1** | Proximal metaphysis · **cortical perforation** | **Bone graft** (reamings) |
| **A2** | Proximal · **nondisplaced crack** (calcar) | ✓ **Cerclage before inserting stem** |
| **A3** | Proximal · **displaced unstable** | **Fully porous-coated or tapered fluted stem** |
| **B1** | Diaphyseal perforation (cement removal) | Fully porous stem bypassing **2 cortical diameters** ± strut |
| **B2** | Diaphyseal nondisplaced crack (hoop stress) | **Cerclage** if stable · fully porous stem if unstable ± strut |
| **B3** | Diaphyseal displaced | Fully porous stem bypassing ± strut |
| **C1** | Distal to tip, perforation | Morcellized graft, long stem, strut |
| **C2** | Distal nondisplaced | **Cerclage + strut** |
| **C3** | Distal displaced | **ORIF** |

![Intraoperative Vancouver classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5013-vancouver-intraoperative-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Postoperative periprosthetic femur fracture
├─ Trochanteric (A) → nonop if <2 cm · ORIF if >2 cm · treat osteolysis
├─ Around stem, stable (B1) or below stem (C) → ORIF (plate + cables)
├─ Around stem, loose, good bone (B2) → long cementless revision + fixation
└─ Loose + poor proximal bone (B3) → PFR (elderly) / APC (young)
```

### Intraoperative femoral (calcar) fracture

**Clue** — **change in resistance / audible change** · stem sinks further than final broach → ✓ **calcar fracture**

**Management** — ✓ **remove stem/broach → explore extent (intraop X-ray) → cerclage above lesser trochanter → reinsert SAME-size stem** · braided cables > wires · restores **"barrel hoop"** stability\
✗ Wrong options: continue insertion + cable · keep implant and cable · undersized stem (subsides) · convert to cement without cerclage (loses hoop stress) · delayed staged reconstruction · long stem without fixing calcar

**Same stem gives good outcome** — ✓ **female sex is a risk factor** · Berend: no revisions at 7.5 yr\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ subsidence/early revision · long stem better · press-fit/MIS not risk factors

**Weight bearing — conflict** — OB (Berend, 1349, 211416): ✓ **immediate full WB** after cerclage of minimal calcar fracture · OB (7026, 8433): **partial/protected WB 6 weeks** with 2-weekly X-rays — read the options

**Extends past lesser trochanter / displaced (A3)** → **fully porous-coated or tapered fluted** long stem · distal cracks found post-op → touch-down WB

**Mechanisms** — **proximal**: rasping, insertion, dimension mismatch · **mid**: exposure/preparation force · **distal**: straight-stem tip impacting **femoral bow**

### Intraoperative acetabular fracture

**Mechanism** — during **cup impaction** · ✓ **most common complication of press-fit cup** (TO) · risk: **under-reaming >2 mm** (most common cause), **elliptical/monoblock** cups, osteoporosis, cementless, dysplasia, radiation

**Stable cup** → ✓ **no change** or **add screws**, protected WB **8–12 wk** (OB: flat-foot 2 wk) · nondisplaced anterior column with stable cup → ✓ **multiple screws + brief WB restriction**\
✗ Wrong options: larger cup (propagates) · plating · cup-cage · 6 wk bed rest

**Unstable cup** → screws ± **jumbo cup** ± ORIF + revision · **posterior column displaced** → ✓ **remove cup, fix posterior column, antiprotrusio cage** (or cage-over-cup)\
✗ Wrong options: larger cup · graft · WB protection · cemented PE

### Vancouver A, B1 & C – ORIF

**B1** — ✓ **ORIF: lateral locking plate + proximal cables** · stable stem confirmed intraop\
✗ Wrong options: traction · revision · cemented stem

**B1 fixation details** — ✓ **locking screws proximally** (bicortical where possible; unicortical alone insufficient) · **2–3 cables** between LT and stem tip · **≥3 bicortical screws / 6–8 cortices distal** · strut allograft only for bone loss/migration\
✗ Wrong options: avoid cables · <3 distal screws

**B1 reoperation** — ✓ **nonunion** most common (B2 revision + ORIF → **infection**)

**Bridge plating (relative stability)** — ✓ **endochondral (secondary) healing**\
✗ Wrong options: intramembranous (needs compression)

**Ogden construct (TO)** — ✓ **periprosthetic femur fractures (Vancouver B)** · cables proximally + screws distally (now unicortical locking proximal, bicortical distal)

**Vancouver C** — ✓ **plate + proximal cables + distal screws** (✗ Wrong options: retrograde nail (no canal) · conventional plate · traction) · **interprosthetic** (THA + TKA) → ✓ **lateral locking plate spanning whole femur** (✗ Wrong options: nail · revision · strut alone · total femur)

**Below long revision stem, well fixed** → ✓ **ORIF plate + cables/wires**\
✗ Wrong options: strut + wires alone (no rotational control)

**FNB 2024** — mid-shaft fracture 3 months after THA, well-fixed stem → options nail / plating ± cerclage / traction / long-stem revision — answer not given (well-fixed stem → plating fits B1/C)

**AG >2 cm** → ✓ **ORIF trochanteric claw plate** · **AG with osteolysis + PE wear (eccentric head)** → ✓ **revision THA** (liner exchange, address lysis, fix GT)

![Trochanteric claw plate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5013-claw-plate.jpg)

### Vancouver B2 – long cementless revision stem

**Treatment** — ✓ **revise to long uncemented diaphyseal-engaging stem** (extensively porous-coated cylindrical or **modular tapered fluted titanium**) bypassing fracture by **≥2 cortical diameters** + **ORIF of fragments** (cables/plate)

**Why not cemented** — cement **extrudes into fracture → nonunion**, mid-term loosening, poorer results in active patients · proximally coated stem (no proximal fixation — ↑ loosening/nonunion) · ORIF alone (loose stem) · strut alone when bone stock adequate · traction · PFR (bone stock adequate) · antibiotic spacer (no PJI)

**Typical stems** — prodromal thigh pain + subsidence · cement column disruption · loose at 2 weeks with subsidence · "easily extractable" stem at surgery

**B2 ORIF alone (relative)** — low demand, **ASA ≥3**, **CCI ≥5**, anatomic reconstruction possible, no prior loosening/pain (González-Martín) · revision favoured: high demand, ASA <3, comminution / ≥2 zones, prior pain

**Loose cemented stem eroding lateral cortex (impending fracture)** → ✓ **cementless revision**\
✗ Wrong options: cemented ± strut · strut + cables · PFR

**Fracture around antibiotic spacer, infection cleared (WBC 500, α-defensin −)** → ✓ **reimplant uncemented diaphyseal-engaging stem + cables**\
✗ Wrong options: new spacer · traction + suppression · ORIF

**Normal ABI (0.9–1.3) with sluggish refill + B2** → ✓ **revision + ORIF**, serial exams\
✗ Wrong options: vascular consult/arteriogram · US · traction · exploration

![Vancouver B2 fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5013-shows-vancouver-b2-fx.jpg)

### Vancouver B3 – proximal femoral replacement

**Options** — **PFR / megaprosthesis / tumour prosthesis** (elderly, low demand, dementia, can't restrict WB → immediate WB) · **APC / proximal femoral allograft / impaction grafting** (young, restore bone) · diaphyseal cementless or tapered fluted stem + struts

**Not an option (TO)** — ✓ **long porous-coated stem with plate + cable** construct alone

**Typical answers** — 18 yr post-THA, loose cemented, poor bone → ✓ **PFR** (✗ Wrong options: NWB · ORIF · long cemented · fully porous + ORIF) · 88-yr-old with dementia → ✓ **PFR** (✗ Wrong options: proximally coated · ORIF · impaction grafting · strut) · Paget + coagulopathy → ✓ **PFR** (fast) · 91-yr-old minimally ambulatory with start-up pain → ✓ **PFR** · active 83-yr-old → ✓ **PFR** (✗ Wrong options: metaphyseal press-fit stem) · loose + progressive bone loss → **cementless tapered fluted modular stem + proximal strut**

**Loose stem + loose migrated cemented cup + poor bone (B3)** → ✓ **revise both components**

![Vancouver B3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5013-vancouver-b3.jpg)

### Fully porous-coated stem fracture

**Biggest risk** — ✓ **inadequate proximal bone stock** → distal ingrowth + no proximal support → **cantilever** fatigue mid-stem · also **stem <13.5 mm**, weight **>88 kg**, **young age (<60)**, ETO nonunion · revise via **ETO**, address proximal bone (graft/PFR)\
✗ Wrong options: older age · offset · exposure

## 6 Complications

### Associated neurovascular injury

**Loose stem, comminuted fracture, absent pulses, arteriogram occlusion** → ✓ **traction → vascular shunt → revision THA → definitive vascular repair**

**Cerclage cabling of proximal femur — bleeding** → ✓ **deep femoral (profunda) artery / perforators** (close to diaphysis, tethered by perforators) · **SFA** at risk **distally** (adductor canal) · **midshaft safest** · late pseudoaneurysm (swelling, falling Hct)

![Femoral arteries relative to cerclage zones](../../MCQ/attachments/q219150-illb-screenshot-2023-09-01-at-12-37-32-pm.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **B1** | **B2** | **B3** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stem | **Fixed** | **Loose** | **Loose** |
| Proximal bone | Good | **Good** | **Poor / comminuted** |
| Treatment | **ORIF** | **Long cementless stem + ORIF** | **PFR / APC** |
| Common reoperation | **Nonunion** | Infection | — |

## How it's asked

**"Nondisplaced calcar fracture inserting cementless stem — next?"** → **remove stem, cerclage, reinsert stem**

**"1 cm calcar crack after final broach?"** → **cerclage, insert standard press-fit stem, WBAT**

**"Calcar cabled, same stem reinserted — true?"** → **female sex is a risk factor**

**"Nondisplaced crack above LT with tapered wedge stem?"** → **cable above LT + partial WB 6 weeks**

**"Small anteromedial neck crack at final insertion?"** → **remove implant, cable, reinsert**

**"Displaced calcar fracture during insertion?"** → **intraoperative exploration of extent**

**"Implant sinks further than final broach?"** → **calcar fracture — remove, cable, insert same implant**

**"Interprosthetic fracture?"** → **plate + screws + cerclage cables**

**"Which fracture for locking plate + cerclage?"** → **Vancouver C**

**"Below cemented stem, mantle intact?"** → **plate with proximal cables + distal screws**

**"Below well-fixed long revision stem?"** → **ORIF plate, screws, cables**

**"High-speed MVA, well-fixed cementless stem?"** → **ORIF**

**"Stable THA + TKA, femur fracture between?"** → **Vancouver C; lateral locking plate**

**"Displaced GT fracture?"** → **AG; ORIF with claw plate**

**"B1 ORIF — most important for fixation?"** → **locking screws proximally**

**"B1, stem fixed intraop — classify and treat?"** → **B1; ORIF**

**"B1 ORIF — commonest reoperation?"** → **nonunion**

**"Bridge plate — healing mode in 6 weeks?"** → **endochondral ossification**

**"Loose stem, fracture around stem (TO)?"** → **B2; long-stem revision + plate**

**"82/F, B2 (TO)?"** → **revision with uncemented long stem**

**"Loose stem, sufficient bone?"** → **long cementless stem**

**"Loose stem at surgery, cup fixed?"** → **uncemented femoral revision bypassing by two cortices**

**"Loose stem day 1 home?"** → **long diaphyseal press-fit stem**

**"Femoral component easily extractable?"** → **B2; long porous-coated cementless stem + fixation**

**"Which fracture needs long uncemented stem?"** → **the B2 radiograph**

**"Loose stem 3 yr post-THA?"** → **extensively porous-coated stem**

**"Cement column disruption?"** → **cementless revision + adjuvant fixation**

**"GT fracture with osteolysis + eccentric head?"** → **revision THA**

**"Loose stem 53-yr-old?"** → **long porous-coated cylindrical stem**

**"Thigh pain for 3 yr, subsidence, fall?"** → **cementless stem bypassing ≥2 cortical diameters**

**"Loose stem + bone loss, prior thigh pain?"** → **cementless long tapered fluted modular stem + proximal strut**

**"Loose cemented stem + loose cemented cup?"** → **revise acetabular and femoral components**

**"10 weeks post-op, loose stem?"** → **revision to long fluted tapered uncemented stem**

**"Fracture around spacer, infection cleared?"** → **reimplant uncemented diaphyseal-engaging stem + cables**

**"B2 with normal labs?"** → **revision of femoral component + ORIF**

**"Loose stem — femoral component?"** → **uncemented long stem + ORIF**

**"Which needs revision + ORIF?"** → **Vancouver B2 (active 67-yr-old)**

**"Loose cemented stem eroding lateral cortex?"** → **cementless femoral revision**

**"Which radiograph — ORIF + diaphyseal stem?"** → **B2**

**"B2 with sluggish refill, ABI 1.1?"** → **revision + ORIF**

**"Golfer, fall — revision + ORIF for?"** → **B2**

**"2 weeks post-op, subsidence?"** → **B2; long cementless stem + fixation**

**"Which radiograph for long porous stem + fixation?"** → **B2**

**"B3 — not an option (TO)?"** → **long porous-coated stem with plate + cable**

**"B3, 18 yr post-THA?"** → **proximal femoral replacement**

**"88-yr-old with dementia, B3?"** → **PFR megaprosthesis**

**"Ambulatory 76-yr-old, loose cemented, poor bone?"** → **PFR**

**"Paget's + coagulopathy?"** → **PFR**

**"85-yr-old, THA 25 yr ago — classify and treat?"** → **B3; PFR**

**"91-yr-old minimally ambulatory?"** → **PFR**

**"Active 83-yr-old with B3?"** → **PFR**

**"Fractured fully porous-coated stem — biggest risk?"** → **inadequate proximal bone stock**

**"Absent pulses, loose comminuted fracture?"** → **traction, shunt, revision, vascular repair**

**"Bleeding while cabling proximal butterfly?"** → **profunda femoris (structure D)**

**"Vancouver type 2 involves (TO)?"** → **shaft around stem**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Postoperative Inpatient Management

*Recon · 28 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Team & phases

**Team** — surgeon ± hospitalist · PT · OT · case manager · nursing · patient + family

**Phases** — **pre-op teaching** → **inpatient acute care** → inpatient extended care (rehab/SNF) → outpatient/home

## 5 Management

### Pain management

| Phase | Measures |
|---|---|
| **Pre-op** | **NSAIDs + acetaminophen** · gabapentin/opioids (weaker data) |
| **Intra-op** | **Regional (spinal/epidural) preferred over GA** · **periarticular multimodal injection** |
| **Post-op** | **Multimodal oral therapy = gold standard** |

**Liposomal bupivacaine (FDA 2011)** — slow release, longer action, no systemic toxicity · ✓ **comparable to peripheral nerve blocks for opioid use** · not clinically superior to standard bupivacaine\
✗ Wrong options: same cost (expensive) · earlier ambulation · superior after 48 h (not even at 72 h) · shorter LOS vs fascia iliaca block

### Medical management

| Problem | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Hypertension** | >140/90 · check reversible causes (missed meds, pain, anxiety, constipation) · oral agents preferred |
| **Hypotension** | SBP <90 or drop ≥40 · ✓ **commonest cause: intravascular volume depletion** · hold antihypertensives, IV fluids ± CBC/BMP, ECG, troponin, CXR, CTPA |
| **Hypoxia** | ↓ cardiac output + O₂ uptake · **incentive spirometry for all** · consider ischaemia, COPD, CHF, **PE** · keep SpO₂ >90% · distress → ICU |
| **↓ Urine output** | Hypovolaemia, **urinary retention**, **AKI** · retention → straight cath, ↓ anticholinergics/opioids, **tamsulosin** · AKI → fix cause, euvolaemia, stop nephrotoxins |
| **Altered mental status** | Find cause · **reorient, normalize sleep–wake cycle** |
| **Chest pain** | Troponin + ECG if cardiac · aspirin + cardiology (ischaemia) · NSAIDs (pleuritic/wall) · PPI (dyspepsia) |
| **PONV** | Opioids, anaesthetics · antiemetics, fluids, electrolytes |
| **Ileus** | NG tube may be needed |
| **Fever** | **POD 1–2 cytokine fever** — observe if well · **after POD 3** → infectious (UTI, cellulitis, pneumonia, C. diff) or non-infectious (PE, withdrawal, transfusion/drug reaction) · antibiotics only for an identified source |

### Postoperative ileus & Ogilvie syndrome

**Ogilvie** — **acute colonic pseudo-obstruction** without mechanical cause · **laxatives may worsen** it

**Most severe complication** — ✓ **bowel ischaemia** (→ colectomy/laparotomy)\
✗ Wrong options: SMA syndrome · renal failure · cholangitis · appendicitis

**Treatment** — stop opioids · **NG tube** · IV fluids, electrolytes · **neostigmine** · failure → **colonoscopic decompression**

![Ogilvie colonic pseudo-obstruction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422739-an-x-ray-example-of-colonic-pseudo-obstruction-consistent-wi.jpg)

### Physical & occupational therapy

**Start day of surgery** — ↓ LOS, ↓ pain, ↑ function

**Exercises** — ankle pumps, knee bends, glute sets, quad sets, hip abduction, SLR → edge of bed → chair → walker → gait training

**Stairs** — **"up with the good, down with the bad"** (up: non-operated leg first · down: operated leg first)

**Walker** in immediate post-op period · OT: **raised toilet seat**, shower seat, raised chair

**Progression** — sitting → walker gait, out of bed → transfers, normal gait → independence

### Discharge criteria (home)

**Criteria** — **independent ambulation with aid 50–100 ft** · independent **transfers** · independent **ADLs** · **2 stairs with supervision** · **follows dislocation precautions** · adequate **home help**

### Extended care

**Earlier discharge to rehab** → **improved outcomes**

| | **SNF / subacute rehab** | **Inpatient rehab** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | Fail home criteria, don't qualify for IRF | Can do **3 h therapy/day** + (**>85 yr**, **BMI >50**, or **bilateral TJA**) |
| Cost | More cost-effective | Intensive, rarely used after TJA |

## How it's asked

**"Liposomal bupivacaine periarticular injection — true?"** → **comparable to nerve blocks for opioid consumption**

**"POD 4, distension, no BM 7 days — most severe complication?"** → **bowel ischaemia**

**"Most common cause of post-op hypotension?"** → **volume depletion**

**"Fever POD 1, patient well?"** → **observe (cytokine fever)**

**"Stairs after THA?"** → **up with the good, down with the bad**

**"Inpatient rehab eligibility?"** → **3 h/day therapy + age >85, BMI >50 or bilateral TJA**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Prosthesis Design

*Recon · 29 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Determinants

**THA biomechanics depend on** — prosthesis design · **bearing surface + lubrication** · fixation

**Components** — femoral (cemented, press-fit: tapered, extensively coated, modular) · acetabular (cemented PE or metal; press-fit metal; **modular dual mobility** — ↓ revision dislocation, risk of **liner malseating**) · bearings (PE, metal, ceramic)

### History

| Year | Milestone |
|---|---|
| **1891** | **Gluck** — ivory femoral head |
| 1940 | **Austin Moore** — first metallic hip (bolted proximal femoral replacement) |
| **1952** | **Austin Moore prosthesis** — large uncemented stem, **fenestrations for self-locking** (impetus for biologic fixation), no PE ✓ |
| **1960s** | **Charnley low-friction arthroplasty** — **small head** (↓ wear), metal stem, **PE cup**, **acrylic cement** · Teflon cup first · mentor Sir Harry Platt · manufacturer Thackray |
| Other | **San Baw** — ivory hips for neck nonunion |

**Charnley head** — ✓ **22.225 mm (7/8 inch)** (TO)

**Charnley all-PE cemented cup** — ✓ **20–30% failure at 35 yr** (88% intact at 20 yr, 78% at 35 yr; 77.3% at 21 yr)

![Austin Moore prosthesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5033-austin-moore-illustration.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Press-fit femoral stems

**Principle** — **biologic fixation** · **hoop stresses** give initial stability

| Type | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Tapered** (proximally coated) | Tri-Lock, M/L Taper |
| **Extensively coated** (diaphyseal) | **AML**, VerSys Full Coat |
| **Modular** (mix-and-match body + stem) | **S-ROM**, ZMR |

**Cementless stem classification (TO, Khanduja)** — **1** single wedge (proximal, flat AP/tapered ML, broach only) · **2** double wedge (proximal, ML + AP) · **3** double-wedged, fixation at **metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction** (3a/b/c) · **4** **extensively coated**, full-length fixation · **5** **modular** · **6** **anatomic** (posterior metaphyseal bow) · ✓ **not distally fixed = type 2** (types 3b, 3c, 4 are)

**Complications** — **intraop fracture** (lateral approach + under-reaming) · **loosening** in **irradiated bone** · **junctional (taper) corrosion** in modular necks → metallosis, pseudotumour

![Cementless stem types](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17099971-1.png)

### Cemented femoral stems

**Indications** — **irradiated bone** · **Dorr C** (poor proximal stock) · elderly · grout; limited remodelling

**Material** — **Co-Cr or stainless steel** (stiff → ↓ cement stress) · not **cemented titanium** (less stiff → micromotion, debonding, **crevice corrosion** — no longer recommended)

**Stem breakage** — smaller stems, poor tolerance of **cantilever bending**

**Desired features (TO)** — **high-modulus superalloy (Co-Cr)** · broad rounded edges · collar (depth control, ↓ calcar resorption) · **macrotexturing** (grooves) · **polished surface** (less debris if debonded; Exeter matte > polished loosening) · **no PMMA precoat** · **centralizer** · ✓ **NOT microtexturing / matte finish** (that is for uncemented ingrowth)

**Gruen failure modes** — **I pistoning** (linear lucency around stem + cement, commonest) · II medial midstem pivot · III calcar pivot · IV bending cantilever

**Force-closed vs shape-closed** — taper-slip polished collarless (**Exeter, C-stem**; subsidence intended, >5 mm or progressive after 2 yr = loose) vs composite beam rough ± collar (**Charnley**; subsidence >0.15 mm after 2 yr → ↑ revision) · mantle **2–4 mm** · line-to-line = "French paradox"

### Elderly Dorr C femur

**82-yr-old walker, Dorr C** → ✓ **cemented femoral stem**\
✗ Wrong options: cemented cup (shear loosening) · diaphyseal stem (save for revision) · 4.5 g antibiotic for prophylaxis (≤1 g; 4.5 g for spacers) · smallest stem for >1 cm mantle

### Dorr classification (TO)

**Classifies** — ✓ **proximal femur**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral stem · acetabulum

| Type | Ratio | Shape | Stem |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **<0.5** | **"Champagne flute"**, thick cortices, young men | Uncemented (either works) |
| **B** | **0.5–0.75** | Medial + posterior cortical loss, wider canal | Uncemented |
| **C** | **>0.75** | **"Stovepipe"**, thin cortices, elderly postmenopausal women | ✓ **Cemented** (most suited) |

### Bearing surfaces

| Bearing | Benefits | Disadvantages |
|---|---|---|
| **Metal-on-PE** | **Longest record**, **cheapest**, most modular | **↑ wear + osteolysis** · small head → impingement |
| **Metal-on-metal** | ↓ linear wear · particles **smaller + more numerous** (smaller total volume) · large head → ↑ ROM | Cost · **ions 5–10× normal** (serum + urine; peak **12–24 months** run-in) · **pseudotumour** · **type IV hypersensitivity** · no proven cancer link |
| **Ceramic-on-ceramic** | ✓ **Best wear** (alumina 4000× < CoCr-on-PE) · **lowest friction** · **inert** | Cost · **brittle — fracture** · **squeaking** · **stripe wear** · fewer neck lengths · 28 mm heads only zirconia |
| **Ceramic-on-PE** | **Standard of care** · ↓ PE wear vs MoP | **Zirconia phase transformation** (tetragonal → monoclinic: >8 yr, pressure, heat — 99 °C vs alumina 50 °C) |
| **Titanium-on-PE** | — | **High wear** — not recommended |

**MoM contraindications** — **pregnancy / child-bearing women** (ions cross placenta) · **renal disease** (renal excretion) · metal hypersensitivity

**Wear order (TO)** — **CoC (0.5–2.5 µm/yr) < MoM (2.5–5 µm/yr) < MoP** · ✓ **CoC lowest wear of any bearing**

### Ceramic properties

**Ceramic** — ✓ **non-ductile, high wettability, highest modulus, low fracture toughness** · inert, biocompatible · alumina + zirconia composites

**Modulus order** — ceramic > Co-Cr > stainless steel > titanium (OB) · TO: polymers < cement < cortical bone < **Ti** < SS < Co-Cr < ceramics

**MoM advantage over CoC** — ✓ **↑ fracture toughness**\
✗ Wrong options: wettability, hardness, roughness, friction

**CoC vs metal on HXLPE** — ✓ **lower volumetric wear**\
✗ Wrong options: jump distance/ROM (head size) · cost · stripe wear (unique to CoC)

**CoC complications (TO)** — **stripe wear** (head–rim line contact during subluxation, crescent line), **squeaking** (0.3–24.6%; onset 14–40 months; fundamental 400–7,500 Hz; friction from lost lubrication), **fracture**, impingement · ✓ **NOT high wear rate**

**Ceramic fracture risk** — **28 mm** > 32/36 mm heads · **short neck** · canted liner · malposition

**Ceramic generations** — 1st pure zirconia (roughening, fracture) · 2nd CaO/MgO (↓ grain) · 3rd **hot isostatic pressing** (ultrapure alumina, zirconia, yttrium) · 4th **strontium oxide platelets** deflect cracks

**After ceramic head fracture** — revise with **ceramic** head, not metal · metal head → retained ceramic fragments cause ✓ **massive third-body (abrasive) wear**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · pseudotumour · metallosis · psoas

![Ceramic stripe wear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5033-ceramic-stripe-wear-moved.jpg)

### Head size & wear

**Large head** — ✓ **↑ ROM** before impingement · ↑ head-neck ratio · **↑ jump distance (~½ head diameter)** · more stable · **↓ ceramic fracture** · **↓ neck-to-rim impingement** · ✓ **↑ volumetric wear**\
✗ Wrong options: "decreases ROM" · "↑ neck-rim impingement" (TO EXCEPT answers)

**Volumetric wear** — **V = πr²w** · ✓ **head size (radius²) most important** · ✓ relates to **total contact area** (TO) · main determinant of particle number

**22 mm vs 32 mm** — 22 mm: ✓ **higher linear wear (creep)**, ↓ volumetric wear, ↑ dislocation, ↓ ROM · 32 mm: ↑ volumetric wear, ↓ linear · **28 mm compromise**

**Linear wear** — penetration of head into cup · **>0.1 mm/yr → osteolysis** · conventional UHMWPE **0.1–0.2 mm/yr** · **RSA most accurate** wear measure

**Wear modes (TO)** — **adhesive** · **abrasive** ("cheese grater"; two- and three-body) · **third-body** (cement, bone, metal, PE, HA) · **surface fatigue/delamination** (TKA) · **fretting** (THA backside shell–liner) · corrosive · hip PE wear mainly **adhesive/abrasive**; TKA **delamination**

**Wear stages** — run-in → steady state → rapid failure

### MoM tribology

**Lubrication (TO)** — ✓ **elastohydrodynamic** in MoM · **boundary** in MoP · mixed lubrication (OB)

**Diametral clearance** — most important wear variable · ideal **100–200 µm** · too small → equatorial contact, seizing · too large → lost fluid film, rapid wear · ✓ TO false statement: "large clearance = better fluid film" · OB: ideal **radial mismatch ~50 µm**

**High carbon (>0.20%)** Co-Cr → ↓ wear · **double heat treatment** + **low carbon** → ↑ wear, pseudotumour

**Larger MoM bearings wear similarly** — ✓ **greater sliding speed draws in more fluid**\
✗ Wrong options: boundary lubrication · flexibility · carbides · smaller clearance

**Not affecting MoM wear** — ✓ **head-to-neck ratio**\
✗ Wrong options: radial mismatch, sphericity, surface finish, carbon content

**ALVAL** — delayed-type hypersensitivity, ~**0.3%** of MoM

### MoM systemic effects

**True** — ionically charged particles · ↑ **Co + Cr in serum + urine** · **higher frictional torque than CoC** · **no activity–ion correlation** · ions **renally excreted** (not liver) · cross **placenta** · ✓ **pseudotumours** · ↑ registry revision rates

**Not true / not shown** — ✓ **higher cancer risk** (EXCEPT answer) · sarcoma, carcinomas · ↑ dislocation · nephrotoxicity

**Observed more often** — ✓ **leukocyte chromosomal aberrations** (translocations, aneuploidy) — significance unknown · haematopoietic malignancy data mixed

**Hypersensitivity (type IV)** — T-cell mediated · **nickel > cobalt, chromium** · most cells are macrophages (lymphocytes **5%**) · cytokines → macrophage activation → MHC II, IL-2 → more T-cells

### Taper corrosion & modularity

**Taper (trunnion / neck–stem) corrosion in MoP** — ✓ **pseudotumour** (ALTR) · fretting + crevice corrosion (mechanically assisted) · affects **>⅓ of modular devices**\
✗ Wrong options: PE wear · dislocation · squeaking · sepsis

**Modular-neck ALTR findings** — X-ray usually normal; **medial calcar erosion** commonest · MRI **thickened capsule + fluid** · **↑ cobalt with near-normal chromium** (Cr precipitates as orthophosphate) · black bore of female taper

**Treatment** — ✓ **single-stage revision of liner, head, neck + stem to a MONOBLOCK stem**\
✗ Wrong options: revise stable cup · two-stage · retain stem if female taper corroded · modular revision stem

**Impingement ↓ ROM** — ✓ **skirted modular heads** · avoid skirts, maximize head-neck ratio, chamfered liners, **trapezoidal** (not circular) neck · cup 45–55° with 10–20° combined anteversion (wear/instability if >50°)

**Malseated modular dual-mobility liner** (Co-Cr inelastic, hard bone deforms shell; "4-quadrant test") → ✓ **↑ fretting corrosion**\
✗ Wrong options: dislocation · PJI · fracture · ↓ ROM

![Modes of taper corrosion](../../MCQ/attachments/q5744-illa-illus-a.jpg)

### Implant choice in nickel allergy

**Nickel** — in **Co-Cr and stainless steel**, not titanium or ceramic

**Answer** — ✓ **press-fit titanium stem + alumina ceramic head + cementless titanium cup** · TKA: **oxidized zirconium femur + titanium tibia**\
✗ Wrong options: cemented Ti (less stiff) · Co-Cr · titanium head (abrasive wear)

### Stem design, stress shielding & fracture risk

**Stress shielding** — proximal bone loss around a well-fixed stiff stem (**Hooke's law** — stiffer "spring" carries load) · **Wolff's law** bone loss

| ↑ Stress shielding | ↓ Stress shielding |
|---|---|
| **Large diameter** (stiffness ∝ **r⁴**; ≥16 mm; OB >18 mm) ← most important | Small diameter |
| **Co-Cr** (10× femur; alumina 19×) | ✓ **Titanium** (lower modulus) |
| **Solid, round** | Hollow, slotted, fluted, tapered, short |
| ✓ **Cylindrical distal-fitting / extensively coated / distally fixed** | **Tapered, proximally fixed** |
| **Cementless** | ✓ **Cemented** stems (TO true statement) |
| Osteoporosis | — |

**Fully coated Co-Cr stem → X-ray** — ✓ **proximal femur / trochanteric osteopenia** · late GT insufficiency fracture\
✗ Wrong options: distal · whole femur · acetabulum

**Grade III stress shielding with 16.5 mm stem — EXCEPT** → ✓ **titanium stem**\
✗ Wrong options: round · large diameter · solid

**Material to ↓ shielding** → ✓ **titanium** (highest fatigue strength among alloys) · flexible, short, hollow stems

**Osteolysis location** — extensively coated → **proximal** · patch-coated → **distal** (joint fluid reaches diaphysis) · HA fully coated → ↑ cortical shielding vs proximally porous

**FNB 2024** — stem diameter threshold for proximal resorption (11 / 12.5 / 13.5 / 14 mm) and "proximal stress shielding occurs in…" — answers not given by source (diaphyseal-fitting > tapered fits the rule above)

**Titanium vs Co-Cr (TO)** — Ti: ✓ **lower tensile strength + fatigue resistance (than Co-Cr)** · **lower modulus**, less stiff · **more biocompatible**, better osseointegration · Ti porous coat restricted to bulky proximal stem (notch sensitivity)\
✗ Wrong options: higher modulus · stiffer · less biocompatible

**Intraop femur fracture — highest risk** — ✓ **final insertion of uncemented stem via lateral approach in female >65** (medius directs broach medial/anterior → calcar stress)

### Cementless ingrowth – pore size & osseointegration

| Parameter | Values (source) |
|---|---|
| **Pore size** | **50–400 µm** (OB, TO) ✓ "<500 µm" · **100–400 µm** (TO) · 100–150 / 50–150 µm (TO list) · **50–300 µm**, ideally 50–200 (Orthobullets) |
| **Porosity** | **40–50%** (TO) · 40–80% (Orthobullets) |
| **Gap** | ✓ **<50 µm** (TO false: "<100 µm") |
| **Micromotion** | Minimal (<150 µm) |
| **Ongrowth roughness** | **3–5 µm**, grit blast / plasma spray |
| **Pore depth** | ↑ depth → ↑ fixation strength |

**Osseointegration timeline** — starts **4–12 weeks**, continues ✓ **up to 3 years**

| Stable (bony ingrowth) | Stable fibrous | Unstable |
|---|---|---|
| No subsidence, minimal lines, **spot welds**, distal cortical hypertrophy, proximal stress shielding | No progressive migration · **parallel radiopaque lines ~1 mm** from stem · no cortical hypertrophy | **Progressive subsidence** · **divergent** lines · pedestal / hypertrophy under collar + tip |

**Acetabular osseointegration signs** — no lucent lines · **superolateral buttress** · medial stress shielding · **radial trabeculae** · **inferomedial buttress**

### Impaction bone grafting & conversion after failed fixation (TO)

**Conversion after failed neck fixation (screw holes)** → ✓ **long uncemented stem + prophylactic cerclage**, bypass last hole by **2 cortical diameters**\
✗ Wrong options: cemented (leaks through holes)

**Impaction grafting (femur)** — Hastings & Parker 1975, Nijmegen/Exeter · **1–2 mm cancellous allograft chips** impacted → **cemented** stem in neo-canal · loads transmitted as **compression on graft** · **some subsidence expected** · indication: **Paprosky 3B in young**, type 4 · complications: dislocation **4.1%**, fracture **5.4%** · ✓ **incorrect: "stem must be tapered fluted, collarless, flexible titanium"** (no single required stem; polished collarless or collared used)

## X vs Y

| | **MoM** | **CoC** | **MoP / CoP** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wear | Low linear, tiny numerous particles | **Lowest** | Highest (MoP) |
| Unique problems | **Ions, ALVAL, pseudotumour** | **Fracture, stripe wear, squeaking** | Osteolysis |
| Toughness | **High** | **Low (brittle)** | — |
| Lubrication | Elastohydrodynamic | Fluid film (wettable) | Boundary |

| | **Titanium** | **Cobalt-chrome** |
|---|---|---|
| Modulus / stiffness | **Lower** | Higher |
| Stress shielding | **Less** | More |
| Cemented use | No (crevice corrosion) | Yes |
| Nickel | **None** | Present |

## How it's asked

**"Not associated with CoC (TO)?"** → **high wear rate**

**"Large head — NOT true (TO)?"** → **decreases ROM**

**"Not true about CoC (TO)?"** → **high wear rate**

**"Not true about MoM (TO)?"** → **large diametral clearance → better fluid film**

**"Predominant lubrication in MoM (TO)?"** → **elastohydrodynamic**

**"Large head NOT associated with (TO)?"** → **increased neck-to-rim impingement**

**"True about bearing wear (TO)?"** → **CoC lowest wear of any combination**

**"22 mm vs 32 mm head (TO)?"** → **higher linear wear**

**"Volumetric wear depends on (TO)?"** → **total contact area (head size)**

**"Charnley all-PE cup failure at 35 yr?"** → **20–30%**

**"Groin pain after ceramic head fracture revised to metal head?"** → **massive third-body wear**

**"Benefit of CoC vs metal-on-HXLPE?"** → **lower volumetric wear**

**"MoM vs MoP particles?"** → **smaller and more numerous**

**"MoM wear not affected by?"** → **head-to-neck ratio**

**"Advantage of MoM over CoC?"** → **increased fracture toughness**

**"Parkinson + mild renal insufficiency — bearing?"** → **large metal head on HXLPE**

**"Larger MoM wears similarly — why?"** → **greater sliding speed**

**"Non-ductile, wettable, high modulus, low toughness?"** → **ceramic**

**"MoM EXCEPT?"** → **higher cancer risk**

**"MoM patient worried — accurate?"** → **no correlation between activity and ion levels**

**"Higher frequency with MoM?"** → **leukocyte chromosomal aberrations**

**"Taper corrosion in MoP?"** → **pseudotumour**

**"Modular neck corrosion, stable components?"** → **revise liner, head, neck + stem to monoblock**

**"Decreases ROM to impingement?"** → **skirted modular head**

**"Malseated modular dual-mobility liner?"** → **↑ fretting corrosion**

**"Severe nickel allergy?"** → **press-fit Ti stem, alumina head, cementless Ti cup**

**"Fully coated Co-Cr stem — X-ray change (TO)?"** → **trochanteric / proximal femoral osteopenia**

**"Stress shielding EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **titanium stem**

**"More proximal stress shielding (TO)?"** → **cylindrical distal-fitting vs tapered**

**"Not distally fixed (TO)?"** → **type 2**

**"True about stress shielding (TO)?"** → **cemented less than uncemented**

**"Material to ↓ stress shielding (TO)?"** → **titanium**

**"↑ stress shielding?"** → **distally fixed components**

**"Highest intraop fracture risk?"** → **final insertion, uncemented stem, lateral approach, female >65**

**"Elderly Dorr C — PMMA use?"** → **cement the femoral stem**

**"Charnley head size (TO)?"** → **22.225 mm**

**"Austin Moore designer?"** → **Austin T. Moore**

**"MoM vs MoP outcome?"** → **pseudotumours**

**"Converting failed neck screws to THR (TO)?"** → **long uncemented stem + cerclage**

**"Impaction grafting — incorrect (TO)?"** → **stem must be tapered fluted flexible titanium**

**"Not desired in cemented stem (TO)?"** → **microtexturing / matte finish**

**"Linear lucency around cemented stem (TO)?"** → **pistoning**

**"Ti vs Co-Cr (TO)?"** → **Ti lower tensile strength + fatigue resistance**

**"Dorr classification is for (TO)?"** → **proximal femur**

**"Femur most suited for cementing (TO)?"** → **Dorr C**

**"Ideal pore size (TO)?"** → **<500 µm (50–400)**

**"Osseointegration continues until (TO)?"** → **up to 3 years**

**"Not true for ingrowth (TO)?"** → **gaps <100 µm**

**"True about cementless stem (TO)?"** → **optimal pore size <400 µm**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Pseudotumor (Metal on Metal Reactions)

*Recon · 30 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Pseudotumour = ALVAL** (aseptic lymphocyte-dominant vasculitis-associated lesion) — **mass-forming tissue reaction** to MoM wear · (semi-)solid or cystic periprosthetic mass **≥2 cm** not due to infection, tumour, bursa or scar (TO)

**Incidence** — **10–15%** of MoM THAs · resurfacing **0–39%** (OB) · ALVAL ~0.3% (TO) · **45–50% of MoM revisions** are for pseudotumour/ALTR · **~40% asymptomatic** · revision for symptomatic pseudotumour only **1.7–5.6%**\
✗ Wrong options: majority

**Seen with** — ✓ **MoM THA / resurfacing** (TO) · also **MoP with head–neck (trunnion) corrosion** → ALTR

### Risk factors

| Risk factors | Not risk factors |
|---|---|
| **↑ Cobalt + chromium** (Co **>5 µg/L** — TO) | ✓ **BMI >40** (TO EXCEPT) |
| **Female** | Metal allergy (TO) |
| **High cup inclination >55°** / ↑ anteversion → **edge loading** | Obesity |
| **Age <40** (TO) | — |
| Taper/trunnion corrosion even with good position | — |
| Large metal heads (some studies), low diametral clearance | — |

### Pathophysiology

**Two mechanisms** — **hypersensitivity to metal ions** · **local high wear debris** · ✓ pseudotumour associated with **↑ linear wear** of both components

**Hypersensitivity** — ✓ **type IV delayed (cell-mediated)** · metal ions form **haptens** with native proteins · ✓ **lymphocyte-mediated** in metal-allergic patients (jewellery reactions) (✗ Wrong options: macrophage-mediated · large/small particle wear) · ALVAL due to ✓ **hypersensitivity to metals** (✗ Wrong options: abrasion of shell · screws · stem corrosion)

**Cells — conflict** — Orthobullets/OB: macrophages + lymphocytes in all, **lymphocytes predominant** · high-wear cases → more macrophages, smaller lymphoid aggregates · TO: metal debris **cytotoxic to macrophages → macrophage depletion** → lymphocyte predominance · OB (DTH): effector = T-cell but **most participating cells are macrophages (lymphocytes ~5%)**\
✗ Wrong options: macrophage predominant

**Histology** — **dense perivascular lymphocytic infiltrate** (hallmark), T **and** B lymphocytes, lymphoid follicles/aggregates, histiocytes, giant cells, necrosis · ✓ dark areas = **T + B lymphocyte infiltrates** (✗ Wrong options: particle-laden macrophages (typical of MoP)) · MoP trunnion ALTR → ✓ **predominance of perivascular lymphocytes** (✗ Wrong options: neutrophils (PJI) · lipid-laden histiocytes/giant cells (PVNS))

**ALVAL true (TO)** — B lymphocytes · macrophage depletion · lymphoid aggregates · ✓ **false: "diagnosis is not made by macroscopic appearance"** (solid mass visible) · definitive diagnosis histological

## 3 Presentation

**History** — gradual or sudden groin pain · may be **asymptomatic** · mass causing discomfort

**Exam** — periprosthetic soft-tissue mass · **Trendelenburg gait** (abductor destruction) · groin pain with **flexion, IR, adduction** or rising from chair · NV usually normal

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP pelvis + AP/lateral hip · exclude fracture · **calcar erosion**

**MARS MRI** — ✓ **test that best correlates with prognosis** (TO) (✗ Wrong options: ESR/CRP/WBC · metal ions (don't correlate) · CT (bone loss only)) · ✓ **best test for painful MoM** (✗ Wrong options: CT · bone scan · ion levels alone) · findings: periarticular **fluid collection / solid mass**, synovial/capsular hypertrophy, **tendon avulsion**, bone oedema · **T1 ≈ bladder (transudate)** · **T2 hyperintense** (hypointense foci = necrosis/metal) · MAVRIC, SEMAC newer

**CT** — bony destruction, need for structural graft/augments

![MARS MRI pseudotumour](../../MCQ/attachments/q211284-illa-t2-pseudotumor.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**Metal ions (Co, Cr)** — peak **12–24 months** (run-in) then fall · **>7 ppb (µg/L)** → MARS MRI · ions renally excreted · **activity does not change levels** · Co:Cr ≈ **1:1** = MoM bearing, **high Co:Cr (2:1)** = trunnionosis\
✗ Wrong options: pseudotumour

**Aspiration** — distinguishes **metallosis** from PJI · **"dishwater" fluid** (grey, watery, hazy) · ✓ **manual cell count** (fibrinous debris falsely ↑ automated) · MoM PJI cut-off **>4,350 WBC** · ARMD → **lymphocytes >40%**; PJI → **PMN >70%** (e.g. WBC >3,000 with low PMN, high lymphocytes = ✓ **pseudotumour hypersensitivity**; ✗ Wrong options: bacterial PJI · large-particle PE disease)

**PJI vs ALTR** — ALTR raises ESR, CRP, synovial WBC and gives **false-positive α-defensin** → ✓ **thorough work-up: cultures, manual WBC, %PMN (80% cut-off)** · α-defensin remains valid in RA\
✗ Wrong options: α-defensin alone diagnoses PJI · ESR/CRP confirm · ALTR excludes PJI

## 5 Management

```
MoM THA follow-up
├─ Asymptomatic, ions <7 ppb, normal films → observe (serial X-ray + ions)
├─ Ions >7 ppb (even asymptomatic) → MARS MRI
├─ Symptomatic + normal ions + normal MRI → close follow-up 3–6 months
└─ Pain/↑ ions + pseudotumour (or progressive osteolysis) → revise to CoP
       └─ Abductor loss → constrained liner ± gluteus maximus transfer
```

### Observation

**Indication** — well-functioning, **low ions**, no pseudotumour · serial radiographs (wear, loosening)

**Close follow-up allowed** — ✓ **symptomatic, Co 6 / Cr 5, normal ESR/CRP, MARS MRI normal**\
✗ Wrong options: asymptomatic with ions >7 (needs MRI) · symptomatic with fluid (revise) · asymptomatic + fluid + progressive osteolysis (revise)

### Revision indications

**Revise** — ✓ **MARS MRI with periarticular fluid + abductor destruction** · painful MoM + ↑ ions + pseudotumour · delay → more soft-tissue damage\
✗ Wrong options: CRP 6 · HO · aspirate 900 WBC · cobalt 5 ppb alone

**Technique** — revise to **ceramic-on-polyethylene** · single- or two-stage (if infection concern) · lateral or anterior approach · **bimodular (modular-neck) stem + monoblock cup** → ✓ **revise both components to CoP + I&D**\
✗ Wrong options: head/liner exchange (monoblock cup) · larger Co-Cr head · I&D alone · observe

**Abductor deficiency** — **Trendelenburg gait** → ✓ **gluteus maximus transfer** (extensor → abductor) ± **constrained liner**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion contracture · medial migration (cup-cage) · psoas · ER weakness

**Dislocation after head/liner exchange for pseudotumour** → ✓ **abductor insufficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: offset · larger head · vertical cup · impingement

**Infected pseudotumour** — neutrophils on histology + ↑ ESR/CRP → debride, explant, spacer, **systemic antibiotics**

## 6 Complications

**Revision outcome** — bone loss, soft-tissue destruction, abductor deficiency → harder revision, worse function · **↑ dislocation + infection**

**After revision** — instability · deep infection · aseptic loosening · persistent psoas tendinitis · persistent metal reaction · **14–20% re-operated within 5 yr**

**Femoral nerve palsy** — rare (case reports) · decompress + revise to CoP

## X vs Y

| | **ALTR / pseudotumour** | **PJI** |
|---|---|---|
| Synovial cells | **Lymphocytes >40%** | **PMN >70–80%** |
| α-defensin | **False positive** possible | Positive |
| Fluid | "Dishwater" grey | Purulent |
| Count | **Manual** | Automated fine |

| | **MoM bearing wear** | **Trunnionosis** |
|---|---|---|
| Co:Cr | ~1:1 | **Co ≫ Cr (2:1)** |
| Bearing | MoM | MoP or MoM with modular taper |

## How it's asked

**"Painful MoM — test best correlating with prognosis (TO)?"** → **MARS MRI**

**"Pseudotumour risk factors EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **BMI >40**

**"Masses around resurfacing on MRI (TO)?"** → **pseudotumour formation**

**"38-yr-old woman, resurfacing, normal labs, mass on MRI?"** → **pseudotumour**

**"MoM aspirate WBC high but lymphocyte-predominant?"** → **pseudotumour hypersensitivity response**

**"Groin pain after resurfacing, X-ray normal — best test?"** → **MARS MRI**

**"Which MoM patient can just be followed?"** → **symptomatic, ions <7 ppb, normal MARS MRI**

**"Finding that mandates revision?"** → **MARS MRI fluid + abductor destruction**

**"ALTR + ↑ ESR/CRP + α-defensin + — true?"** → **needs cultures, manual WBC, %PMN**

**"ALVAL seen with (TO)?"** → **metal-on-metal THR**

**"ALVAL due to (TO)?"** → **hypersensitivity to metals**

**"ALVAL — all true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **diagnosis not made by macroscopic appearance**

**"Dark areas in MoM pseudocapsule histology?"** → **T- and B-lymphocyte infiltrates**

**"MoP with ↑ ions, ALTR — histology?"** → **perivascular lymphocyte predominance**

**"MoM + jewellery reactions — mechanism?"** → **lymphocyte-mediated hypersensitivity**

**"MoM property?"** → **ions excreted mainly by kidney**

**"Pseudotumour on MARS — true?"** → **associated with ↑ linear wear**

**"Bimodular stem, monoblock cup, pseudotumour, ↑ ions?"** → **revise both components to CoP + I&D**

**"Dislocation 3 weeks after head/liner exchange for pseudotumour?"** → **abductor insufficiency**

**"Gluteus maximus transfer planned — why?"** → **Trendelenburg gait (abductor deficiency)**

**"Ion level prompting MRI?"** → **>7 ppb**
