# THA Rehabilitation

*Recon · 31 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Phases

**Team** — surgeon, PT, OT, case manager, nursing, patient + family

**Phases** — **pre-op teaching** → **inpatient acute** → **inpatient extended (rehab/SNF)** → **outpatient**

## 5 Management

### Preoperative teaching

**Pre-op PT** — ✓ **not shown to improve post-op outcomes**

**Hip precautions** — most useful if taught **before** surgery · hips dislocate in the **direction of the approach** (if implants well positioned)

| Approach | Avoid |
|---|---|
| **Posterolateral** | **Flexion >90°**, **extreme IR**, **adduction past midline** |
| **Anterolateral** | **Extension**, **extreme ER**, adduction past midline |
| **Direct anterior (Smith-Petersen)** | **Bridging**, **extension**, **extreme ER**, adduction past midline |

**Told to avoid extension, adduction, ER** → ✓ **Smith-Petersen (anterior)**\
✗ Wrong options: Southern/Kocher-Langenbeck (posterior) · Stoppa · Pfannenstiel

![Posterior hip precautions](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5036-posterior-hip-precautions.jpg)

### Inpatient acute care

**Pain** — pre-op NSAIDs (± opioids) · **regional anaesthesia preferred over GA** · periarticular multimodal injection · **multimodal oral therapy = gold standard**

**PT progression** — sit upright → walker gait, out of bed → transfers, normal gait → independence

**Discharge home** — independent ambulation with aid · independent transfers · independent ADLs · stairs with supervision · home help

### Extended care

**Earlier discharge to rehab** → **improved outcomes** · same discharge criteria

### Outpatient – return to activity

**Sport** — **low-impact** preferred · **golf handicap barely changes after THA** (worsens after TKA) · **high-impact ↑ revision in patients <55**

**Driving** — **right THA 3–4 weeks** · **left THA <3–4 weeks** · ✓ **reaction time back to pre-op at 4–6 weeks** (OB answer: **4 weeks**) · influenced by side, lengthening, rehab, narcotics\
✗ Wrong options: 1–2 weeks · 2–3 months

**Work** — **within a month** if not manual

## How it's asked

**"When does driving reaction time return after THA?"** → **4 weeks (4–6 weeks)**

**"Avoid extension, adduction, ER — approach?"** → **Smith-Petersen**

**"Posterior approach precautions?"** → **no flexion >90°, no IR, no adduction past midline**

**"Does pre-op PT improve outcome?"** → **no**

**"High-impact sport after THA in <55 yr?"** → **↑ revision**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Revision

*Recon · 32 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Volume** — US revisions projected **>100% ↑ by 2030** · ~equal sex · age **65–70**

**Causes** — overall + after 2 yr: **mechanical failure** (aseptic loosening) · **early (<2 yr): infection, periprosthetic fracture**

**Indications** — wound problems · periprosthetic/implant fracture · instability · **PJI** · ALTR (MoM) · trunnionosis · osteolysis · aseptic loosening · LLD

**Charnley LFA (1962, 22 mm head, cemented)** — **11.8% revised at 30 yr** · ✓ **acetabular failure most common** reason (> deep infection > fracture; acetabular > femoral) · acetabular exchange = commonest Charnley revision

### Categories

| Category | Examples |
|---|---|
| **No component change** | Wound closure · **psoas release** · **HO excision** · ORIF fracture with stable implants |
| **Modular only** | **Head ± liner exchange** · titanium sleeve |
| **Non-modular** | **Cup** or **stem** exchange · both · spacer · Girdlestone |

### Sciatic nerve risk (TO)

**Nerve palsy rates** — primary OA **0.5%** · DDH **2.3%** · ✓ **revision 3.5% (highest)** · risk factors: DDH, post-traumatic, posterior approach, cementless, **revision**, female, lengthening

## 3 Presentation

| Symptom | Suggests |
|---|---|
| **Groin pain** | Acetabular problem |
| **Thigh pain** | Femoral stem |
| **Start-up pain** | Component loosening |
| **Night pain** | Infection |
| No improvement after surgery | Wrong diagnosis |

**Stem subsidence** — stems subside into **retroversion** → ✓ **limb externally rotated**\
✗ Wrong options: IR · flexion contracture

**Resisted hip flexion pain** — psoas impingement

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP pelvis · orthogonal hip views · **full-length femur** · prior/immediate post-op films · **Judet views** (columns, discontinuity)

**CT** — ✓ **osteolysis extent** (X-rays **underestimate**) · pre-op planning, discontinuity · **CT angiogram for Paprosky IIIB ("up and in")** — iliac vessel relation ✓

**Labs** — ESR, CRP, CBC · Co/Cr (trunnionosis, pseudotumour) · aspirate if labs suggest infection · ✓ **↑ ESR + CRP precludes single-stage revision without further work-up**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ cobalt · metallosis · high Co:Cr · pseudotumour

**Lysis around stable cup, normal labs** → ✓ **CT** (Judet) to plan · failed cup with bone loss → ✓ **CT** to plan and detect discontinuity\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · aspiration · ions · dynamic fluoroscopy

### AAOS acetabular bone loss

| Type | Defect |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Segmental** — rim or medial wall |
| **II** | **Cavitary** — volumetric loss |
| **III** | **Combined** |
| **IV** | **Pelvic discontinuity** |
| **V** | ✓ **Arthrodesis** (TO) |

### Paprosky acetabular bone loss

| Type | Description | Typical reconstruction |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Minimal deformity, **intact rim** | Hemispherical cup + screws |
| **IIA** | Superior (± medial) lysis, **intact superior rim** | Cup + screws |
| **IIB** | **Absent superior rim**, superolateral migration (<3 cm) | Cup + screws ± jumbo/augment |
| **IIC** | **Localized medial wall** destruction | Cup / jumbo + screws + morsellized graft |
| **IIIA** | Rim loss **10 → 2 o'clock**, **"up and out"** (>3 cm superolateral) | **Porous cup + metal augments** + graft |
| **IIIB** | Rim loss **9 → 5 o'clock**, **"up and in"** (superomedial), **Köhler line broken**, discontinuity | **Cup-cage**, **custom triflange**, structural graft |

**Type II vs III (TO)** — hip centre migration **<3 cm vs >3 cm** · II has supportive columns (≥50% host contact), III lacks them

![Paprosky acetabular classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5009-paprosky-acetabulum-classification.jpg)

### AAOS femoral bone loss

| Type | Defect |
|---|---|
| **I** | Segmental (shell) |
| **II** | Cavitary (endosteal, intact cortex) |
| **III** | Combined |
| **IV** | **Malalignment** |
| **V** | **Stenosis** (canal obliterated) |
| **VI** | **Discontinuity** (fracture/nonunion) |

### Paprosky femoral bone loss

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Minimal metaphyseal loss | **Primary components** |
| **II** | Extensive metaphyseal, **intact diaphysis** | **Extensively porous-coated long stem** |
| **IIIA** | Metadiaphyseal loss, **≥4 cm intact isthmus** | **Extensively porous-coated (cylindrical) stem** · modular tapered |
| **IIIB** | Metadiaphyseal loss, **<4 cm isthmus** | ✓ **Modular fluted tapered stem** · extensively coated (if canal **<19 mm**) · **impaction grafting** · APC |
| **IV** | **Non-supportive diaphysis** (no isthmus) | **Impaction grafting**, **APC**, **EPR / PFR**, long cemented |

**TO (FNB 2014)** — <4 cm diaphysis → ✓ **IIIB: extensively porous-coated tapered stem + impaction grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: IIIA · II · IV/APC

![Paprosky femoral classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5009-paprosky-femoral-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Revision THA planning
├─ Exclude infection (ESR/CRP → aspirate) → if PJI, staged
├─ Well-fixed, well-positioned cup + wear/lysis → head + liner exchange ± graft
├─ Acetabulum: ≥50% host support → porous cup/jumbo + screws ± augments
│      └─ Discontinuity / <50% → cup-cage, triflange, column plating
└─ Femur: Paprosky I → primary · II/IIIA → extensively coated · IIIB → tapered fluted · IV → PFR/APC
```

### Acetabular reconstruction

**Porous hemispherical / jumbo cup + screws** — needs **≥50% host bone** · cup **inferior + medial** (↓ JRF) · cavitary defects → particulate graft · ✓ **most reliable long-term** for Paprosky II · jumbo may compromise posterior column, dislocation\
✗ Wrong options: cemented all-PE (high failure) · cage · bilobed · bipolar in acetabulum (pain, erosion)

**Paprosky IIC (medial wall), ≥50% stock, no discontinuity, normal centre** → ✓ **jumbo cup + screws + morsellized graft** (most stable + cost-effective)\
✗ Wrong options: cage · triflange · spacer · augments

**Paprosky IIB** → ✓ **cementless hemispherical cup + screws**

**Paprosky IIIA (LLD 3.5 cm, Trendelenburg)** → ✓ **porous cup + metal augments + graft + screws** (unitize augment–cup with cement)\
✗ Wrong options: particulate graft alone (IIA/IIB) · triflange (IIIB) · cemented cup (shear failure) · 2-stage

**Augments** — Paprosky **IIB–C, IIIA–B** · survivorship **94–100% (2 yr), 92–100% (5 yr), 92% (10 yr)**

**Reconstruction / antiprotrusio cage + structural graft** — rim **<⅔**, <50% stock · PE cemented into cage · **allograft failure commonest complication** · without cage **40–60% failure** · major bone loss + medial wall penetration → ✓ **reinforcement cage + graft**\
✗ Wrong options: jumbo

**Non-contained bicolumn defects >50% of weight-bearing area** → ✓ **ilioischial reconstruction ring + structural corticocancellous graft** (restores stock)\
✗ Wrong options: morsellized graft + cup · bilobed · roof ring

**Impaction grafting (acetabulum)** — contained cavitary defects + cemented PE · not for discontinuity

**Cemented cup** — cement liner into a well-fixed shell · **revision cemented cups fail** (shear)

### Pelvic discontinuity & triflange

**Definition** — separation of **superior ilium from inferior ischiopubic** segment · AAOS IV / Paprosky IIIB · acute pain, cannot bear weight (e.g. RA) → ✓ **pelvic discontinuity**

**Principles** — reconnect ilium + ischium · bleeding bone contact with ingrowth surfaces · graft defects · stable construct until ingrowth

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Column plating + hemispherical cup** | **Acute** discontinuity, minimal bone loss, healing potential |
| **Cup-cage** | Chronic discontinuity, **Gross IV/V** segmental defects · ✓ displaced transverse periprosthetic acetabular fracture with medial migration · ✓ Paprosky IIIB + structural graft |
| **Custom triflange (CTAC)** | Massive bone loss + **chronic discontinuity** · **irradiated** bone · iliac → pubic → ischial flanges, screws ± posterior column plate · ✓ **most common complication: dislocation** |
| **Distraction** | Discontinuity with shell ± augments |
| Bulk allograft + cemented cup | **~50% failure** (resorption, infection) |
| Independent cage alone | **50–60% failure** |

**Triflange chosen because of** — ✓ **pelvic discontinuity** (✗ Wrong options: dislocations · IPD · loosening · Paprosky 2B) · triflange needs posterior/extensile exposure (✗ Wrong options: anterior approach for ischial/iliac flanges) · not a bilobed cup

**Paprosky IIIB with discontinuity** → ✓ **custom triflange** or **cup-cage** (✗ Wrong options: cemented cup · multihole cup) · chronic discontinuity after infection cleared (6 months, normal labs) → ✓ **triflange + femoral revision** (✗ Wrong options: new spacer · bulk allograft · cage alone · Girdlestone)

![Antiprotrusio cage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5009-radiographic-example-of-an-antiprotrusio-cage.jpg)

### Osteolysis & isolated liner exchange

**Retain shell + liner exchange + graft lysis** — ✓ **well-designed, well-fixed modular cup** with good locking mechanism and record · lysis via screw holes/dome hole; loosening late\
✗ Wrong options: non-modular · instability · radiolucency · migration

**Indications** — eccentric PE wear with stable components · acute infection · trunnionosis

**Typical answers** — eccentric head + proximal femoral lysis, fixed components → ✓ **synovectomy + liner exchange** (✗ Wrong options: stem/cup revision) · massive cup lysis, ceramic-on-conventional PE, fixed cup → ✓ **liner exchange + grafting + switch head to METAL** (✗ Wrong options: new ceramic head on used taper (fracture; metal sleeve if ceramic wanted)) · lysis around one screw → ✓ **liner exchange + remove screw + debride ± graft** (Beaulé: no lesion recurrence) · retroacetabular lysis with clicking, normal labs → ✓ **head + liner exchange + retroacetabular graft**

**Osteolytic lesions** — may shrink/heal without grafting once wear source removed · grafting optional/controversial

**Complication** — ✓ **dislocation** (11–25%) · use larger head · damaged locking mechanism → cement liner into shell (2–4 mm mantle)

**Ischial lysis with dissociated dome-hole eliminator** → ✓ **wear-induced osteolysis**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · metastasis · corrosion

### Femoral revision

**Primary components** — Paprosky I (no unexpected bone loss)

**Extensively porous-coated long stem** (or porous/grit combo) or **modular tapered** — Paprosky **II, IIIA** (IIIB modular fluted tapered) · **95% survival at 10 yr** · bypass most distal defect by **2 cortical diameters** · cavitary → particulate graft · cortical defects → struts/plates · ✓ superior to cemented revision

**Typical answers** — loose hemi with proximal loss → ✓ **cementless diaphyseal press-fit stem** (✗ Wrong options: bone scan · tumour prosthesis · metaphyseal cement) · IIIA with fracture → ✓ **fully coated cementless + cables + graft** (✗ Wrong options: ORIF · modular · total femur) · start-up pain + subsidence, no defects → ✓ **uncemented long fully porous stem** (✗ Wrong options: cemented · APC · PFR · spacer) · deficient proximal femur with >4 cm isthmus → ✓ **diaphyseal-engaging extensively coated** (✗ Wrong options: standard cemented · proximally coated · PFR · APC) · <4 cm isthmus (IIIB) → ✓ **modular fluted tapered stem** (✗ Wrong options: struts alone · impaction + cementless (impaction needs **cemented** stem) · metaphyseal tapered) · early loosening at 3 months → ✓ **non-modular tapered fluted Ti stem**

**Failed ingrowth signs** — reactive line around porous part · **hypertrophy under collar** · **pedestal** at tip (no stress shielding) → ✓ **revise femoral component**

**Symptomatic loose osteolytic stem** → ✓ **protected WB + urgent (not emergent) revision** when cleared\
✗ Wrong options: reassure · alendronate

**Impaction grafting (femur)** — ectatic canal, thin cortices · **Paprosky IIIB, IV** · morsellized fresh-frozen allograft + **smooth tapered cemented stem** · **subsidence** commonest complication

**APC** — Paprosky **IIIB, IV** · allograft canal slightly larger than host · cement stem into allograft, **press-fit 4–6 cm into host**

**EPR / PFR** — massive loss, **non-supportive diaphysis** · elderly/sedentary · frozen section of osteotomy bone to exclude tumour · distal fixation cemented / compressive osseointegration / press-fit

**Cemented revision stems** — irradiated bone, severe osteopenia, Dorr C · **high failure** · remove old cement, neocortex, sclerotic bone

### Proximal femoral replacement

**Indications** — ✓ **<4 cm diaphyseal cortex** · extensive metadiaphyseal loss with <4 cm isthmus · **Paprosky IV** · **failed multiple osteosyntheses of proximal femoral nonunion** · elderly/sedentary · ✓ **EXCEPT: age <50**

**Pre-op film indicating PFR** — ✓ **Paprosky IV** (no isthmus) (II → extensively coated, IIIA → diaphyseal/modular tapered)

### Extended trochanteric osteotomy

**Use** — remove well-fixed stem / **difficult cement mantle** (✓ ETO when only half the cement can be removed) · **12–14 cm** including vastus ridge · enables diaphyseal-engaging stem\
✗ Wrong options: short metaphyseal stem · impaction on loose mantle · cement-in-cement on loose mantle · spacer

**Biomechanics** — ✓ **reduces torque-to-failure to <50% of intact femur** → guide rehab restrictions

**Proximal migration** — ✓ **plate + cables**\
✗ Wrong options: abduct >10–15° when fixing · <10 cm osteotomy (↑ migration) · distal prophylactic cable (prevents propagation only) · strut (junctional failure)

**Complications** — nonunion, intraop fracture, proximal migration, infection, subsidence

**Fully coated stem fracture after ETO** — ✓ **cantilever bending after distal ingrowth**\
✗ Wrong options: poor distal fixation · anteversion · inadequate bypass · excessive diameter (thin stems fail)

### Abductor deficiency & instability after revision

**Gluteus maximus transfer** (anterior part + TFL to GT) for irreparable abductors ✓ (label A) · Hardinge most associated with post-op Trendelenburg

**Hemi in demented 91-yr-old dislocating** → contributor ✓ **dementia** → convert to THA + constrained liner

**MoM resurfacing with abducted cup, clunking** → ✓ **revise to conventional THA**\
✗ Wrong options: cup-only revision (leaves MoM)

**Loose stem + well-fixed well-positioned cup, 10 dislocations, unknown implant, high-risk host** → ✓ **revise stem + cement constrained liner into existing shell** (shell fixed, positioned, locking broken/unknown, room for 2–4 mm mantle)\
✗ Wrong options: retrovert stem · remove all · Girdlestone

### Conversion of hip arthrodesis

**Indications** — **low back + knee pain** · ✓ **gluteus medius function predicts ambulatory outcome** (pre-op **EMG**)\
✗ Wrong options: >95% survival at 20 yr (97% at 8 yr; others <70% at 10 yr) · time limit (success 30 yr after fusion) · complications = primary (higher) · nerve palsy = primary (higher)

**Technique** — GT osteotomy · neck osteotomy + reaming under fluoroscopy · revision cup/stem ± **dual mobility or constrained** · glut max transfer if abductors deficient · improves knee + back pain

### THA in ankylosing spondylitis (TO)

**Features** — ↑ blood loss, ↑ infection (steroids), intraop fracture, dislocation, **HO** · **stuck-sitting, posterior pelvic tilt, sacral slope change <10°** → **↓ cup anteversion** (and inclination) to prevent posterior impingement + **anterior dislocation** · dual mobility · release tight capsule/soft tissue · cement if Dorr C · ✓ **false: "difficult reaming"** (canal wide, cortex thin — perforation/fracture risk) · hip before knee if equal · spine first if both operative

## 6 Complications

**Revision vs primary** — lower survival · ↑ **dislocation** (even simple), infection, nerve palsy, fracture, abductor deficiency, DVT, LLD

## X vs Y

| | **Paprosky IIIA (acetabulum)** | **Paprosky IIIB (acetabulum)** |
|---|---|---|
| Direction | **Up and out** | **Up and in** |
| Rim loss | 10–2 o'clock | 9–5 o'clock |
| Köhler line | Intact | **Broken** |
| Discontinuity | Uncommon | **Common** — CT angiogram |
| Reconstruction | Cup + **augments** | **Cup-cage / triflange** |

| | **Paprosky IIIA femur** | **Paprosky IIIB femur** |
|---|---|---|
| Isthmus | **≥4 cm** | **<4 cm** |
| Stem | **Extensively porous-coated cylindrical** | **Modular fluted tapered** (or coated if canal <19 mm) |

## How it's asked

# THA Sciatic Nerve Palsy

*Recon · 33 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **0–3%** (OB ~**1%**; TO 0.6–3.7%, revision **7.6%**) · by diagnosis (TO): **primary OA 0.5%**, **DDH 2.3%** (5.8% sciatic), **revision 3.5%** (3–8%)

**Litigation** — ✓ **nerve injury = most common cause of THA malpractice litigation (38%)** · LLD second (26%) · dislocation not reported · TO: suits arise mainly from **failure to diagnose/manage** the palsy

### Nerves involved

**Sciatic** — **~80%** (TO >90%) of THA nerve injuries · ✓ **peroneal division almost always** · peroneal: lateral, superficial, tightly packed fascicles, less connective tissue, tethered\
✗ Wrong options: tibial (isolated only ~1%)

**Anatomy** — sciatic nerve **closest to acetabulum at the ischium** · care with **posterior acetabular retractor when hip flexed**

| Approach (TO) | Nerve at risk |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | **LFCN** (15–81%) > femoral > sciatic |
| **Anterolateral / direct lateral** | **Superior gluteal** |
| **Posterior** | **Sciatic (peroneal)** |

**Other nerves (TO)** — **femoral** (2nd commonest, 2.3% of injuries, ~0.8% of THAs) — anterior retractors, hyperextension, **iliacus haematoma** · **obturator** (rare) — **cement extrusion** through medial wall, anteroinferior screws → **groin pain, medial thigh paraesthesia, adductor weakness** · **superior gluteal** — medius split **>5 cm** above GT → Trendelenburg, often recovers

### Causes

**Mechanisms** — **direct trauma** (retractor, laceration) · **stretch** (lengthening, lateralization, dislocation manoeuvres) · **compression — haematoma**, tight dressings · **cement heat** · **unrecognized lumbar lateral recess stenosis** ("double crush") · positioning (CPN in lateral position) · **unknown ~40%** (TO ≥50%)

### Risk factors

| ✓ Risk factors | Not risk factors |
|---|---|
| **DDH** ✓ (congenitally short neurovascular structures → over-lengthening) | **Obesity / BMI** |
| **Revision** ✓ (highest) | **Rheumatoid arthritis** |
| **Female** | **Osteonecrosis / AVN** |
| **Limb lengthening** | Male sex |
| **Post-traumatic arthritis** | Anterolateral approach |
| **Posterior approach** | Posterosuperior quadrant screws (safe zone) |
| **Cementless femoral fixation** | DMARDs, chronic analgesics, low pre-albumin |
| **Surgeon rating case as difficult** ✓ (Eggli — only correlating factor) | Coxa vara (no exploration needed) |
| **Pre-existing lumbar stenosis / spine surgery** | — |
| **Operative time** ✓ · younger age · smoking · uncontrolled DM · VTE history · experienced surgeon, later case | — |

**Lengthening thresholds** — Orthobullets **>4 cm** · OB keep lengthening **<3 cm** · TO **>3.8 cm** peroneal at risk, **>4 cm** complete sciatic palsy

**Sciatic exploration** — not routine · advisable with distorted anatomy: ER deformity, **protrusio**, short head/neck, **revision**, **DDH** · ✓ **not needed in coxa vara** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — numbness, paraesthesia in sciatic distribution · **foot drop** (no dorsiflexion, no EHL, weak eversion) · "slapping gait"

**Prognosis by deficit** — **worst: complete motor + sensory**\
✗ Wrong options: motor with intact sensation

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CT / ultrasound** — suspected **haematoma**

**EMG** — later, to confirm level + guide prognosis · MRI of hip limited by artefact\
✗ Wrong options: as immediate step

**Foot drop with back pain, stable Hb, no swelling (POD 2)** → ✓ **lumbar lateral recess stenosis (double crush)** → lumbar MRI\
✗ Wrong options: positioning · haematoma · infection · VTE

**Foot drop after therapeutic enoxaparin for PE + transfusions** → ✓ **haematoma compressing sciatic nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: mechanical · stenosis · embolic infarct · anaemia

## 5 Management

```
New sciatic palsy after THA
├─ Intraop (wake-up test / neuromonitoring) → extend hip, ↓ lengthening (subtrochanteric shortening)
├─ Immediate post-op → hip extension + knee flexion; loosen dressings
│      └─ Haematoma (drop in Hb, drainage, anticoagulated) → reverse + urgent evacuation
└─ Persistent foot drop → AFO; tendon transfer (PTT) if AFO fails
```

### Prevention – femoral shortening & nerve monitoring

**DDH** — pre-op planning · lengthening **<3 cm** · ✓ **subtrochanteric (femoral) shortening osteotomy** · **downsize components** · **intraop wake-up test** · **neurophysiologic monitoring**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior approach · modular components · larger head · acetabular osteotomy

**Wake-up test shows no dorsiflexion with equal lengths in dysplastic hip** → ✓ **shortening subtrochanteric osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG · continue + observe · spacer · neurology

**Intraop MEP/EMG alert** → ✓ **extend the hip**\
✗ Wrong options: traction · flex hip (impinges on retractor) · extend knee · irrigation

![Subtrochanteric shortening osteotomy](../../MCQ/attachments/q1247-illa-fig-1-illustration-of-modi-fi-ed-oblique-subtrocha.jpg)

### Postoperative haematoma & other causes

**Immediate palsy** — ✓ **hip extension + knee flexion** (slackens nerve) · then search for reversible cause (axial imaging for haematoma)\
✗ Wrong options: hip flexion + knee extension · flat bed NWB · EMG first · knee X-ray

**Haematoma** (warfarin INR 6, Hct drop, saturated dressing, complete palsy) → ✓ **reverse INR + urgent surgical evacuation (I&D)**\
✗ Wrong options: VAC + monitor · transfuse + observe · switch to enoxaparin · IVC filter + exploration

### Prognosis & management of foot drop

**Recovery** — ✓ only **35–40% regain full strength after complete palsy** → **60–65% never fully recover** (OB: 30–40%; TO: best series ~50%) · maximal recovery takes **~2 years**

**Persistent foot drop** — ✓ **AFO first line** (restricts plantarflexion in swing) · continue AFO at 4 weeks–3 months\
✗ Wrong options: spine MRI · ankle fusion · revision THA · sural grafting · early tendon transfer · neurology first

**AFO not tolerated, chronic** → ✓ **posterior tibial tendon transfer through interosseous membrane to lateral cuneiform** (donor needs **≥4/5**, loses 1 grade)\
✗ Wrong options: ankle fusion · split TA transfer (TA 0/5) · peroneus longus (weak) · FHL + TAL

### Retractor injury

**DAA, foot drop + dorsal foot numbness after retractor slip** → ✓ **posterior acetabular retractor (4)** · deep trochanteric retractor also risks sciatic · position 1 → superior gluteal · 2 → **femoral** (≈2 cm from anterior lip) · 3 → **obturator**

### Obturator nerve injury (TO)

**Persistent groin pain, no foot drop, no plantarflexion weakness, no Trendelenburg** → ✓ **obturator nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: femoral (anterolateral thigh) · sciatic (foot drop) · superior gluteal (Trendelenburg)

## X vs Y

| Nerve | Deficit after THA | Typical cause |
|---|---|---|
| **Sciatic (peroneal)** | **Foot drop**, dorsal foot numbness | Lengthening, posterior retractor, haematoma |
| **Femoral** | Quadriceps weakness, anterior thigh numbness | Anterior retractor, iliacus haematoma, cement |
| **Obturator** | **Groin pain**, adductor weakness | **Cement extrusion**, anteroinferior screw |
| **Superior gluteal** | **Trendelenburg** | Medius split >5 cm |
| **LFCN** | Lateral thigh numbness | Direct anterior approach |

## How it's asked

**"Sciatic exploration not indicated (TO)?"** → **THR in coxa vara**

**"Highest sciatic risk (TO)?"** → **revision surgery**

**"Motor palsy risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **obesity**

**"Sciatic palsy risk factors EXCEPT?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis**

**"Associated with palsy EXCEPT?"** → **AVN of femoral head**

**"↑ sciatic injury in primary THA?"** → **DDH**

**"Most commonly injured nerve?"** → **peroneal branch of sciatic**

**"Risk factor for peripheral nerve injury?"** → **operative time**

**"Wake-up test — no dorsiflexion?"** → **shortening subtrochanteric osteotomy**

**"Neuromonitoring alert — reduce tension?"** → **extend the hip**

**"Foot drop after DDH THA — prevent?"** → **femoral shortening osteotomy**

**"INR 6, Hct drop, complete sciatic palsy?"** → **reverse INR + urgent evacuation**

**"Foot drop POD 2, no drainage, normal anticoagulation?"** → **hip extension + knee flexion**

**"Back pain, foot drop, stable Hb?"** → **lumbar lateral recess stenosis**

**"Enoxaparin for PE, transfusion, new foot drop?"** → **haematoma**

**"Complete peroneal palsy — % never recovering full strength?"** → **60–65%**

**"Foot drop 2 yr, AFO intolerant?"** → **PTT transfer to lateral cuneiform**

**"Slapping gait at 3 months?"** → **continue AFO**

**"Slapping gait at 4 weeks?"** → **AFO**

**"Nerve injury after THA — true?"** → **most common cause of malpractice litigation**

**"DAA retractor slip → foot drop?"** → **posterior acetabular retractor**

**"Groin pain, no foot drop/Trendelenburg (TO)?"** → **obturator nerve**

# THA Stability Techniques

*Recon · 34 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Four determinants of stability

**1** — **component design** · **2** — **component position** · **3** — **soft-tissue tensioning** · **4** — **soft-tissue function**

### Component design – head size & head-neck ratio

**Head-neck ratio** — head diameter ÷ neck diameter · ✓ **larger ratio → greater arc of motion before impingement** (primary arc) · ✓ **larger head = most closely linked to ↑ ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: approach · extended offset · anteversion >18° · abduction <35°

**Skirts** — extend neck length but ✓ **↓ head-neck ratio → ↑ impingement + dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: large ratio · 15° stem/cup anteversion · 50° abduction are safe

**Jump distance** — translation before dislocation (~½ head diameter) · larger heads seat deeper → ↑ jump distance → ↑ stability

**36 vs 28 mm head in revision** — ✓ **delays neck–socket impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: compensates abductor deficiency · ↓ volumetric wear (↑) · ↓ trunnion stress (↑) · compensates vertical cup

**Large head will NOT compensate for** — **abductor deficiency** or **vertical cup** · ✓ **high inclination negates the benefit of a larger head** (dislocates with minimal translation)

**Primary arc ↑ by** — ✓ **larger head**\
✗ Wrong options: collar · hooded liner · offset (affects JRF not arc) · more anteversion

![Head-neck ratio and impingement-free arc](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5007-head-neck-ratio.jpg)

### Neck-shaft angle

| Change vs native | Leg length | Offset |
|---|---|---|
| **↑ NSA (valgus)** | **↑** | **↓** |
| **↓ NSA (varus)** | **↓** | **↑** |

**Longer leg + less offset post-op** → ✓ **increased prosthetic neck-shaft angle**\
✗ Wrong options: medialized cup (↓ offset only) · standard offset stem (offset only) · longer neck (↑ both) · constrained liner

**Trendelenburg + 3 dislocations + already long leg** → ✓ **more varus (high-offset) stem** — ↑ offset without length\
✗ Wrong options: skirt · longer neck · valgus stem · medialized cup

### Acetabular liners

**Elevated (lipped) rim** — placed posteriorly ↑ stability · ↓ revision for instability

**Lateralized (offset) liner** — +4–10 mm acetabular offset (+ small lengthening) · ↑ tension · ✓ **↑ acetabular component loosening** (eccentric load, torsion at liner–shell + bone–implant interfaces, ↑ JRF + wear) — only liner type linked to early loosening

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Component position

| Component | Target |
|---|---|
| **Cup anteversion** | **5–25°** (Lewinnek 15 ± 10) |
| **Cup abduction (inclination)** | **30–50°** (Lewinnek 40 ± 10) |
| **Stem anteversion** | **10–15°** (hard to adjust in cementless) |
| **Combined anteversion** | **37°** (stiff spine: 35–40°) |

**Approach adjustments** — **posterior → more anteversion** · **anterior → less anteversion**

| Malposition | Result |
|---|---|
| **Excessive retroversion** | **Posterior** dislocation |
| **Excessive anteversion** | **Anterior** dislocation |
| **Excessive abduction (vertical, high theta)** | **Posterosuperior** dislocation · **eccentric PE wear**, late instability |
| **Excessive adduction (horizontal)** | **Flexion impingement**, **inferior** dislocation |

**Cup medialization** — ↓ body-weight moment arm → ↓ JRF ✓ (needs ↑ femoral offset to keep abductor tension) · not possible with resurfacing

**AIIS hypertrophy** — impingement + instability in straight flexion → resect · ✓ **direct head of rectus femoris** arises from AIIS (reflected head — groove above acetabular brim; sartorius — ASIS)

### Spinopelvic mobility & cup position

**Normal standing → sitting** — pelvis tilts posteriorly **20–35°** (15–20°) · femur flexes **55–70°** · sacral slope change **10–30°** (11–29°) · pelvic-femoral angle change **51–69°** · sitting anteinclination **41–63°** · standing PFA 165–195° · sitting CSI 162–198°

**Stiff spine** — ✓ **Δ sacral slope <10°** → **hip hypermobility** → ↑ dislocation · every 1° ↓ spinopelvic motion → 0.9° ↑ femoral motion

| Phenotype | Pelvis | Impingement → dislocation |
|---|---|---|
| **Stuck sitting** (APP tilted back 10–13°) | Fixed posterior tilt | **Posterior impingement → ANTERIOR dislocation** ✓ (even via posterior approach, if combined anteversion excessive) |
| **Stuck standing** (APP neutral) | Fails to tilt back when sitting | **Anterior impingement → POSTERIOR dislocation** |

**Stiff-spine targets** — Lum: inclination **45–50°**, anteversion **20–25°**, combined **35–40°** · McKnight (stuck sitting): **less inclination (35–40°) + less anteversion (15–20°)** · large head / **dual mobility**, restore offset (high-offset stem), navigation/robotics

![Stuck-sitting pelvis — anterior instability](../../MCQ/attachments/q218995-illc-screenshot-2023-05-02-at-10-14-32-pm.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Femoral offset & abductor tension

**Femoral offset** — ✓ **perpendicular distance from head centre to femoral shaft axis** (line 4) · **horizontal offset (label B)** most affects JRF (neck length also ↑ offset but lengthens leg; vertical height = length only)

| ↑ Offset | ↓ Offset |
|---|---|
| ↓ impingement · **↓ JRF** · ↑ soft-tissue tension **without ↑ length** · ↑ stability · ↓ wear, ↓ cup strain, ↓ limp | **Instability** · **abductor weakness** · **gluteus medius lurch / Trendelenburg** · ✓ **↑ osseous (bony) impingement** · ↑ JRF |
| Risk: lateral prominence → **trochanteric bursitis** | ↓ bursitis risk |

**High-offset stem** — ✓ **↑ soft-tissue tension** (✗ Wrong options: ↑ JRF · ↑ length · ↑ cup loosening (that is lateralized liner) · ↑ dislocation) · ✓ **improves stability by ↑ abductor tension** (✗ Wrong options: shorter neck · low offset · larger head · medialized cup)

**Exacerbates Trendelenburg** — ✓ **decreasing femoral offset**\
✗ Wrong options: extended offset neck · bigger head · longer neck · inferior cup

**↓ JRF** — ✓ **medialize cup** or ↑ offset\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ NSA · ↓ offset · ↑ femoral anteversion · head-neck ratio (stability only)

**Ways to ↑ offset** — longer neck · **↓ NSA** · medialize neck + lengthen · **trochanteric advancement** · lateralized liner

![Femoral offset measurement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5007-offset-measurement.jpg)

### Soft-tissue function

| System | Conditions impairing stability |
|---|---|
| **Central nervous** | Stroke, cerebellar disease, **dementia**, MS, **Parkinson**, myelopathy, delirium, **alcoholism** |
| **Peripheral nervous** | **Spinal stenosis** (medius = **L5**), neuropathy, radiculopathy, paresis |
| **Local tissue** | Trauma, myoligamentous disruption, deconditioning, ageing, irradiation, osteolysis, collagen disease, myopathy, malignancy, infection |

**Abductor integrity** — **gluteus medius** main abductor · chronic insufficiency → **gluteus maximus ± TFL transfer**

### Constrained & lipped liners

**Constrained indications** — absent abductors · recurrent dislocation with **well-positioned** implants · failed unconstrained solutions · lower threshold in elderly, low-demand, neurologically impaired

**Contraindication** — ✓ **poorly positioned cup** · young patient with large ROM (relative) · risks: loosening, ↓ ROM, mechanism failure\
✗ Wrong options: well-positioned cup in elderly · absent abductors · cage · central neurologic decline

### Recurrent instability revision

**Vertical (high theta) cup with recurrent dislocation** → ✓ **uncemented acetabular revision**\
✗ Wrong options: lipped liner · bigger head/thinner liner · cemented cup (↑ revision) · constrained (early failure on malposition)

**Posterior capsular insufficiency + irreparable medius tear (trunnionosis ALTR)** → ✓ **split gluteus maximus transfer** (one limb to anterior capsule / minimus, one to GT / medius; ± TFL — Whiteside)\
✗ Wrong options: constrained liner alone · PFR + direct repair · medialize cup (worsens) · high offset + dual mobility alone

**Dislocation rates** — primary **0.5–6%** · revision **2–20%** · patient risks: Parkinson, dementia, spasticity, alcoholism, previous surgery, ON, obesity, hip fracture

## X vs Y

| | **Larger head** | **↑ Offset** | **Cup medialization** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main effect | ↑ arc + jump distance | ↑ abductor tension, ↓ JRF | ↓ body-weight arm, ↓ JRF |
| Leg length | — | **No change** | — |
| Fixes abductor deficiency? | **No** | Partly | **No (worsens tension)** |

| | **Stuck sitting** | **Stuck standing** |
|---|---|---|
| Dislocation | **Anterior** | **Posterior** |
| Cup | Less anteversion + inclination | More anteversion |

## How it's asked

**"Increases ROM before impingement?"** → **larger femoral head-to-neck ratio**

**"CoC impingement — increase primary arc?"** → **larger femoral head**

**"Most likely to ↑ dislocation?"** → **skirted femoral head**

**"Impingement in straight flexion from labelled structure — muscle origin?"** → **direct head of rectus femoris (AIIS)**

**"36 mm vs 28 mm head in revision?"** → **delays neck–socket impingement**

**"High cup inclination negates benefit of?"** → **larger femoral head**

**"Template with reduced offset leads to?"** → **↑ osseous impingement**

**"Most closely related to ↑ post-op ROM?"** → **larger femoral head**

**"Worsens Trendelenburg?"** → **decreasing femoral offset**

**"↑ abductor tension for stability?"** → **high-offset femoral component**

**"Line representing femoral offset?"** → **head centre to femoral shaft axis (line 4)**

**"Adjust to ↓ JRF most?"** → **horizontal offset (B)**

**"High-offset stem leads to?"** → **↑ soft-tissue tension**

**"↓ JRF during THA?"** → **medialize the cup**

**"Trendelenburg, 3 dislocations, long leg — implant change?"** → **more varus femoral stem**

**"Longer leg + less offset post-op — cause?"** → **↑ prosthetic neck-shaft angle**

**"Stuck sitting — risk if ignored (posterolateral approach)?"** → **anterior dislocation**

**"Parameter indicating hypermobile hip?"** → **Δ sacral slope <10°**

**"Contraindication to constrained liner?"** → **poorly positioned cup**

**"Liner linked to early cup loosening?"** → **lateralized (offset) liner**

**"Recurrent dislocation, vertical cup?"** → **uncemented acetabular revision**

**"Capsular insufficiency + irreparable medius tear?"** → **gluteus maximus split transfer**

# THA Templating

*Recon · 35 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — anticipating **implant size + position** pre-op · reproduces hip biomechanics · minimizes **LLD**

**Accuracy** — **52–98%** within ± one size · improves with experience

**Order** — radiographs → patient data on template → landmarks → **LLD** → ✓ **acetabulum first** (sets new centre of rotation) → femur

**Tip** — aim for sizes in the **middle range** of the system · otherwise consider a different system

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Film | Requirement |
|---|---|
| **AP pelvis** | Centred over **pubic symphysis** |
| **AP hip** | **10–15° internal rotation** (Orthobullets) · **15–20° IR** (TO) — matches native anteversion · neck parallel to cassette |
| **Frog lateral hip** | — |
| **Magnification** | **20%** standard (tube 1 m, film 5 cm below table) · ↑ in obese, ↓ in slim · use magnification markers |

**Position of legs for templating** — ✓ **15° IR** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 30° IR · 15° ER · 30° ER

**External rotation on film** — **falsely ↓ offset** · **valgus-appearing neck** · **falsely ↓ canal diameter**

**Secondary checks** — **pelvic obliquity** (spinal deformity → LLD issues) · **acetabular retroversion** (harder cup positioning)

**Pelvic sagittal tilt (TO)** — sacrococcygeal joint to top of symphysis · neutral **32 mm women (8–50)** · **47 mm men (15–72)** · ↑ when pelvis tilts forward · standing film with symphysis in line with sacrum (TO)

### Radiographic landmarks

| Side | Landmarks |
|---|---|
| **Femur** | Medullary canal · GT · LT · **saddle point** — most distal junction of superior neck and GT |
| **Acetabulum** | Roof · **teardrop** — superimposed distal medial wall + tips of anterior/posterior horns · lies behind **TAL**, at superior border of obturator foramen (TO) |
| **Pelvis** | **Ischial tuberosities** (LLD) |

![Teardrop landmark](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12124-teardrop.jpg)

### Limb length discrepancy

**Step 1** — horizontal line across **ischial tuberosities** (extend past medial femoral cortices) · alternative **inter-teardrop line** (may be **more accurate**)

**Step 2** — mark **proximal-most point of both lesser trochanters**

**Step 3** — LLD = difference in distance from the reference line to each LT

![LLD — ischial line to lesser trochanters](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12124-limb-length.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Template the acetabulum

**Abduction** — roughly **40°**

**Medial border** — approximates **ilioischial line**, close to **teardrop** · medial cup → ✓ **↓ JRF** + **↓ abductor force** to keep pelvis level\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ abductor force · ↑ leg length (set by cup + stem together) · ↑ / no change in JRF

**Inferior border** — at **inferior teardrop line**

**Then** — mark **acetabular centre of rotation (COR)**

**Acetabular offset (TO)** — acetabular COR to vertical line through teardrop · also: centre of femoral head to **true floor of acetabulum**

### Template the femur

**Size** — fills **medullary canal**

**Insertion depth** — set to equalize limb length

**Neck cut** — **posterior approach → lesser trochanter** reference · **anterior approach → saddle point**

**Femoral offset** — ✓ **femoral head COR to line bisecting long axis of femur** (TO) · ↑ offset → ↑ stability, ↓ JRF, ↓ impingement · ↓ offset → instability, abductor weakness, **gluteus medius lurch**

**Restore offset by** — stem with more/less offset · different **neck-shaft angle** · change **neck length**

### Templating effects on leg length & offset

**Rule** — acetabular COR is **fixed** · femoral COR moves to meet it intra-op

| Femoral COR relative to cup COR | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Superior** | **Lengthens** limb |
| **Inferior** | ✓ **Shortens** limb (directly inferior → shortens, **no offset change**) |
| **Medial** | **↑ offset** |
| **Lateral** | **↓ offset** |
| **Overlapping** | No change in length or offset |

| Cup COR vs femoral COR (TO table) | Cup medial to femur | Equal | Cup lateral to femur |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Cup superior** | Short + ↓ offset | Shortened | Short + ↑ offset |
| **Equal** | **↓ offset** | No change | ↑ offset |
| **Cup inferior** | Long + ↓ offset | Lengthened | Long + ↑ offset |

**EXCEPT trap (TO)** — "femoral COR **inferior** to cup COR **lengthens**" = ✓ **false** · true statements: AP in 15° IR at 20% magnification · inferior → shortens · cup COR medial to femoral COR → ↓ offset

**↑ offset with same leg length** — ✓ femoral COR **medial** to cup COR at the **same level** (Figure E)

![Template positions — length and offset](../../MCQ/attachments/q5454-illb-table.jpg)

### Large femoral heads

**Associated with** — ✓ **acceptable wear with modern bearings** · ↓ dislocation · ↑ ROM before impingement · available in all bearing materials\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ dislocation · ↓ ROM · fewer materials · ↑ impingement

### Acetabular anteversion measurement (TO)

| Film | Method |
|---|---|
| **AP radiograph** | **Liaw** (sin⁻¹ tan β) · **Lewinnek** (arcsin D1/D2 — short/long axis of ellipse) · **Widmer** (short axis / total length) · **Hassan** · **Ackland** |
| **Cross-table lateral** | ✓ **Woo and Morrey** — measured directly · distinguishes anteversion vs retroversion |

**NOT an AP method** — ✓ **Woo and Morrey** (TO)

## X vs Y

| | **Femoral offset** | **Acetabular offset** |
|---|---|---|
| Definition | Head COR → femoral shaft axis | Acetabular COR → teardrop vertical (or true floor) |
| ↓ when | Leg externally rotated on film | Cup medialized |

| | **Posterior approach** | **Anterior approach** |
|---|---|---|
| Neck-cut reference | **Lesser trochanter** | **Saddle point** |

## How it's asked

**"Templating truths — EXCEPT?"** → **femoral COR inferior to cup COR lengthens the limb** (TO)

**"Femoral offset definition?"** → **femoral head COR to line bisecting femoral long axis** (TO)

**"Leg position while templating?"** → **15° internal rotation** (TO)

**"Template that ↑ offset, same leg length?"** → **Figure E — femoral COR medial, same level**

**"Femoral COR directly inferior to cup COR?"** → **decreases limb length**

**"Femoral COR medial to cup COR at same level?"** → **length same, offset increased**

**"Cup moved medially (A → B)?"** → **decreases joint reactive force**

**"Large heads associated with?"** → **acceptable wear with modern bearings**

**"Not an AP-radiograph anteversion method?"** → **Woo and Morrey** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# THA Trunnionosis

*Recon · 36 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **mechanically assisted crevice corrosion (MACC)** · metal ion release from corrosion at the **femoral head–neck Morse taper** → hip pain + **adverse local tissue reaction (ALTR)**

**Occurs with any bearing** — ✓ **metal-on-polyethylene** as well as MoM · relatively rare · lucency between head and cup = PE liner → not MoM\
✗ Wrong options: only in MoM

**Modularity** — lets surgeon tune length + offset · extra junction = corrosion site · **dual-modular (modular neck) stems** → two junctions (head–neck + neck–stem) → **fretting corrosion** most common failure → revise to **monoblock stem**

### Risk factors

| Factor | ↑ Corrosion | ↓ Corrosion |
|---|---|---|
| **Head size** | ✓ **Larger CoCr heads** (↑ torque on trunnion) | Smaller heads |
| **Trunnion geometry** | ✓ **Shorter, thinner** taper (flexible, ↓ interference fit, ↑ micromotion, edge loading) | **Longer, wider, more rigid** taper |
| **Materials** | ✓ **Mixed alloy** taper/bore (CoCr head on Ti stem) | Similar alloy couple · **ceramic head** |
| **Head length** | ✓ **Long (high-length) heads** | Low-length heads |
| **Stem** | **High-offset** stems (↑ fretting) · low-rigidity stems | — |
| **Assembly** | **Impaction <4,000 N** · **wet taper** · impaction off-axis | **4,000 N**, dry, in line with taper |
| **Patient** | **High BMI**, ↑ cyclical loading | — |
| **Bearing** | MoM (ions contribute to taper wear) | — |

**Cemented vs cementless fixation** — unrelated to trunnionosis

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism – fretting & crevice corrosion

| Type | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Galvanic** | Electrochemical electron transfer between **dissimilar metals** in conductive medium (CoCr head on Ti stem) |
| **Fretting** ✓ | **Micromotion** between two materials → wear + ion release · disrupts **passivation (oxide) layer** · ✓ **micromotion at head–neck junction → fretting** · commonest in modular necks |
| **Crevice / pitting** | Physicochemical metal–environment interaction · low-O₂ area anodic, O₂-rich cathodic · in fatigue cracks · **316L stainless steel most prone**, **titanium least** |
| **MACC** | Fretting + crevice combined → passivation layer breaks, O₂-rich fluid enters crevices, acidic layer, ↑ ions → catastrophic failure |

**Self-passivation** — Ti and CoCr form adherent oxide coat → **protective**\
✗ Wrong options: cause of symptoms

**Adhesive wear** — PE adheres to bearing → osteolysis\
✗ Wrong options: a corrosion type in trunnionosis

**Tissue response** — **ALVAL / pseudotumour** = **lymphocyte**-mediated (type IV) · same as MoM · **macrophages** → PE debris osteolysis · neutrophils → infection

![Trunnion wear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422821-trunnion-wear.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **pain + limp** commonest · delayed groin/buttock/thigh pain · "clunking"

**Exam** — palpable pseudocyst if ALTR large · **abductor weakness / Trendelenburg** (attritional abductor destruction by ARMD) — not primary avulsion

**Catastrophic failure** — sudden pop, inability to walk → **head–neck dissociation** / stem fracture at trunnion · not dislocation from failed precautions

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — AP + lateral · ✓ **may be normal** · osteolysis (calcar, GT) late · **Judet / CT** if advanced osteolysis

**MARS MRI** — **periarticular pseudotumour**, fluid collection, capsular thickening, soft-tissue destruction · **US** — fluid collection adjacent to joint

![Pseudotumour on MARS MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422821-pseudotumor.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**Rule out infection first** — ESR, CRP, WBC · aspiration with ✓ **manual cell count** (metal debris **falsely ↑ automated count**)\
✗ Wrong options: automated count confirms

**Metal ions** — ✓ **cobalt ↑ much more than chromium** · no pathognomonic level

| | **Co : Cr ratio** |
|---|---|
| **Trunnionosis** | **≥2:1** (Orthobullets) · **~5:1** / Co **4–5×** Cr (OB explanations) |
| **MoM bearing wear** | **~1:1** (1–2:1) |

**Cobalt threshold for MACC work-up** — **>1 ng/mL (ppb)** (Orthobullets; OB: MoP with ions >1.0 µg/L) · **≥1.6 ng/mL** suggested (OB) — read the options

**Elevated serum cobalt** — ✓ expected with CoCr head + fluid collection\
✗ Wrong options: pedestal (loosening) · macrophages (PE) · HO · LLD

## 5 Management

```
Suspected trunnionosis
├─ Asymptomatic → observe
├─ Infection not excluded → manual cell count, aspiration
└─ Painful, infection excluded → single-stage revision
       ├─ Stem + cup well fixed → ceramic head + Ti sleeve + new PE liner
       └─ Taper grossly damaged / stem failed → revise stem + ceramic head + new liner
```

### Revision – ceramic head with titanium sleeve

**Indication** — painful THA + risk factors + **Co elevated > Cr**, no infection

**Technique** — **frozen sections + cultures** · **extensive synovectomy / debridement** (remove metal debris, devitalised tissue → prevents recurrence) · inspect + **clean taper** · retain vs revise stem

**Head** — ✓ **ceramic head with titanium sleeve** · **sleeve prevents ceramic fracture** on damaged taper\
✗ Wrong options: CoCr → CoCr (recurrent ALTR) · CoCr + sleeve

**Liner** — ✓ **always exchange** (embedded metal debris)

**Lowest recurrence** — ✓ **ceramic-on-polyethylene**

**Prevention** — ✓ **ceramic femoral head** eliminates CoCr–Ti interaction\
✗ Wrong options: larger head · cemented fixation · diaphyseal stem

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| Large CoCr head, black fluid, Co 12/Cr 2, manual WBC 1,500 | ✓ **ceramic head + Ti sleeve + new PE** (✗ Wrong options: revise both components · spacer · bursa injection) |
| Intra-op corroded trunnion, well-fixed parts | ✓ **ceramic head + Ti sleeve** (✗ Wrong options: I&D only (recurrence) · 2-stage · IV antibiotics · cup revision) |
| Mild trunnion damage, 44 mm CoCr head | ✓ **clean trunnion + ceramic head + Ti sleeve + liner exchange** (✗ Wrong options: remove all components) |
| Catastrophic stem/trunnion failure, cup fixed | ✓ **revise femoral component + ceramic head + new liner** (✗ Wrong options: observe · ions first · sleeve only · revise cup) |
| Modular-neck stem with ALTR | **Revise entire femoral component to monoblock** |

![Corroded trunnion at revision](../../MCQ/attachments/q212928-illa-trunnionosis-example.jpg)

### Trunnionosis vs other causes

| | **Trunnionosis** | **MoM wear** | **Aseptic loosening** | **PJI** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ions | **Co ≫ Cr** | Co ≈ Cr | Normal | Normal |
| Cells | **Lymphocytes** | Lymphocytes | **Macrophages** | **Neutrophils** |
| Imaging | Pseudotumour, may be normal X-ray | Pseudotumour | Subsidence, lucency, start-up pain | Variable |
| Revision | **1-stage** head + liner | Bearing revision | Loose component | **2-stage** |

## How it's asked

**"Head–neck dissociation at 5 yr, infection excluded — true statement?"** → **micromotion and/or electrochemical transfer of metals**

**"↑ taper corrosion associated with?"** → **mixed alloy taper and bore**

**"Sudden pop, Co 15 / Cr 3, PE liner — cause?"** → **mechanically assisted crevice corrosion**

**"CoCr head, Trendelenburg, MARS fluid, Co ≫ Cr — root cause?"** → **corrosion at head–neck Morse taper**

**"Modular-neck stem, Co 6.1, Cr 2.3, normal ESR — aetiology?"** → **micromotion at head–neck or stem–neck junction**

**"Micromotion at head–neck — which corrosion?"** → **fretting corrosion**

**"Retrieved corroded stem — design feature contributing most?"** → **shorter trunnion mating surface**

**"Why switch to ceramic heads — true about trunnionosis?"** → **elevated serum Co:Cr ratio**

**"44 mm CoCr on UHMWPE, fluid collection — likely present?"** → **elevated serum cobalt**

**"Cell mediating ALVAL in trunnionosis?"** → **lymphocyte**

**"Black fluid, manual WBC 1,500, Co 12 / Cr 2 — next step?"** → **ceramic head + titanium sleeve + new PE liner**

**"Corroded trunnion, well-fixed parts — best option?"** → **ceramic femoral head with titanium sleeve**

**"Calcar osteolysis, Co 4.2 — what would have reduced risk?"** → **ceramic femoral head**

**"Mild trunnion damage at revision — perform?"** → **clean trunnion + ceramic head + Ti sleeve + liner exchange**

**"Catastrophic femoral failure, cup well fixed?"** → **revise femoral component + ceramic head + new liner**
