# THA Vascular Injury & Bleeding

*Recon · 37 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — vascular injury **0.1–0.2%** · rare but devastating

**Risk factors** — **acetabular screws** · prior vascular bypass / dysvascular limb · **revision** · **intrapelvic cup** and femoral cerclage wiring

**Mechanisms** — screw puncture/laceration · **errant retractor** · compression by **anti-protrusio cages** · direct laceration during approach

### Vessels at risk

| Vessel | Location / threat |
|---|---|
| **Obturator a. + v.** | Distal to **transverse acetabular ligament (TAL)** · along quadrilateral surface, exits at superolateral obturator foramen · **inferior retractor under TAL** · **anterior-inferior screw** |
| **External iliac a. + v.** | **Anterior-superior quadrant** ("zone of death") · ~**7 mm** from bone at ASIS (may lie directly on bone) |
| **Common femoral a. + v. (+ nerve)** | Superficial to iliopsoas · only **anterior capsule + iliopsoas** separate from hip · **anterior rim retractor** |
| **Medial femoral circumflex a.** | Under **quadratus femoris** / gluteus maximus tendon · posterior approach |
| **Ascending branch of LFCA** | Between **TFL and sartorius** · **direct anterior approach** |
| **Superior gluteal a.** | Posterior-superior quadrant (safe zone, still possible) |
| **Inferior gluteal + internal pudendal** | Posterior-inferior quadrant |

### Acetabular quadrants

| Quadrant | At risk | Zone |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior-superior** | Superior gluteal vessels, sciatic n. | **Safe** (best bone) |
| **Posterior-inferior** | Inferior gluteal + internal pudendal vessels, sciatic n. | Safe-ish |
| **Anterior-inferior** | **Obturator** vessels + nerve | Danger |
| **Anterior-superior** | **External iliac** vessels | **Danger ("death")** |

![Obturator artery and TAL](../../MCQ/attachments/q5594-illa-obturator.jpg)

### Obturator artery injury

**Retractor deep to TAL → sudden massive bleed** → ✓ **obturator artery** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: external iliac a./v. · MFCA

**Greatest risk to obturator a.** → ✓ **inferior retractor under TAL** (into obturator foramen)\
✗ Wrong options: posterior wall retractor (sciatic n.) · posterior-superior screw (sciatic n. + superior gluteal a., safe zone) · anterior-superior screw (external iliac) · anterior wall retractor (external iliac/femoral)

**Uncontrollable obturator bleed** — **ligate internal iliac** artery reported

### Sources of hematoma (TO)

**Most common source** — ✓ **branches of obturator vessels** (near ligamentum teres, TAL, inferior acetabular osteophytes) (TO)

**Others** — **first perforating branch of profunda femoris** (deep to gluteus maximus insertion) · femoral vessel branches near anterior capsule · superior + inferior gluteal branches (TO)

### Anterior retractor – femoral neurovascular bundle (TO)

**Hohmann/cobra on anterior acetabular rim** — between rim and **psoas tendon** · over psoas → ✓ **femoral artery + nerve** at risk (TO) · risk ↑ the **more inferior** the retractor · safest near **ASIS level** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: psoas tendon · external iliac · obturator

### Nerve injury link (TO)

**Sciatic** — **80%** of THA nerve injuries (femoral **20%**) · **compression** commonest mechanism · closest to acetabulum at **ischium** · **peroneal division** most affected · errant retractor commonest intra-op cause (TO)

**Nerve injury risk factors (TO)** — **female** · post-traumatic arthritis · **revision** · **DDH** · **lengthening >3 cm**

## 3 Presentation

**Signs** — hemodynamic instability · **pulsatile intra-op bleeding** · **abdominal distension** · loss of limb pulses (palpation/Doppler)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Radiograph** — **extra-osseous acetabular screw**

**Angiography** — stable patient with high suspicion of uncontrolled bleed · delineates vessel · allows **immediate embolization**

## 5 Management

```
Vascular injury during THA
├─ Hemodynamically stable → angiography + endovascular embolization
└─ Unstable + pulsatile bleeding → emergent retroperitoneal exploration
       └─ Prompt hemostasis + vascular surgery consult
```

**Outcome with treatment** — mortality **9%** · amputation **12%** · permanent ischaemic disability **17%**

### Transfusion predictors

**Best predictor of transfusion** — ✓ **low pre-op hemoglobin**\
✗ Wrong options: shorter height · lower weight · previous contralateral THA · female gender

**Other risks** — **RA** · advanced age · longer operative time · no clear link with BMI, gender, prophylactic anticoagulation

**Anemia symptoms** — syncope, dyspnea, chest pain · ↓ SpO₂, tachycardia, tachypnea, delayed capillary refill, pallor · check CBC, coagulation, iron

**Prevention** — pre-op **erythropoietin** · topical/IV **tranexamic acid** · hypotensive regional anaesthesia

### Transfusion criteria

| Patient | Transfuse if |
|---|---|
| **No ischaemic cardiovascular disease** | **Hb <7** + symptomatic anemia |
| **Ischaemic CVD (MI, CHF)** | **Hb <8** + symptomatic anemia |

**Transfusion complications** — ✓ **↑ PJI** · transfusion reactions

## X vs Y

| Retractor / screw | Structure at risk |
|---|---|
| **Inferior retractor under TAL** | **Obturator a.** |
| **Anterior rim retractor over psoas** | **Femoral a. + n.** |
| **Posterior wall retractor** | **Sciatic n.** |
| **Anterior-superior screw** | **External iliac a./v.** |
| **Anterior-inferior screw** | **Obturator** a./v./n. |
| **Posterior-superior screw** | Sciatic n., superior gluteal a. (safe zone) |

## How it's asked

**"Best predictor of post-op transfusion?"** → **lower preoperative hemoglobin**

**"Massive bleed after retractor deep to TAL?"** → **obturator artery** (TO)

**"Most common source of hematoma in THR?"** → **branches of obturator vessels** (TO)

**"Manoeuvre placing obturator artery at greatest risk?"** → **inferior retractor under the TAL**

**"Hohmann on anterior acetabular rim — structure at risk?"** → **femoral artery and nerve** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Approaches

*Recon · 38 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Approach dictated by** — surgeon preference · **prior incisions** · degree of deformity · **patella baja** · obesity

**Simple primary** — medial parapatellar · midvastus · subvastus · minimally invasive

**Complex primary / revision** — medial parapatellar · **quadriceps snip** · **V-Y turndown** · **tibial tubercle osteotomy (TTO)**

**Outcome** — ✓ **no midterm/long-term difference** in function between medial parapatellar, midvastus, subvastus · similar **blood loss** · best long-term practice → ✓ **approach the surgeon is most comfortable with** · subvastus had higher mean ROM at 6 mo only (meta-analysis, 60 RCTs)\
✗ Wrong options: midvastus · mini-medial parapatellar · subvastus · standard MPP

### Prior incisions

**Skin blood supply** — mainly from **medial side** (saphenous a. + medial genicular perforators) → **lateral flap** at greatest necrosis risk

**Multiple longitudinal incisions** — ✓ use the **most lateral** (TO) (or most recently healed)\
✗ Wrong options: medial · transverse · new midline

**Dual-plated plateau fracture with medial + lateral scars** — ✓ **lateral one** (TO) · preserve medial flap (perforators) · avoid excess lateral subcutaneous dissection (TO)

**Single previous scar** — reuse if suitable · **transverse scar** → cross at **right angles** (perpendicular), avoid acute angles

**Skin bridge** — **≥5 cm** if old scar cannot be used (TO) · exact length controversial (Orthobullets)

**Old transverse incision years earlier** — standard anterior longitudinal incision usually fine (TO)

**Post-traumatic (plateau ORIF) TKA** — risk of TKA **5× controls (7.3% at 10 yr)** · remove only hardware that interferes with prosthesis · stiff knees → snip, V-Y, TTO, lateral release · bone loss → cement, graft, metal wedges · computer assistance helpful (TO)

## 5 Management

### Primary approaches

| Approach | Technique | Advantages | Disadvantages / relative CI |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Medial parapatellar** | Straight midline incision, arthrotomy medial to patella | Familiar · **excellent exposure** · easy patellar eversion | Medial capsular repair failure · lateral patellar subluxation · indirect lateral retinaculum access · **patellar circulation risk if lateral release added** |
| **Lateral parapatellar** | Lateral arthrotomy | **Fixed valgus** · preserves patellar blood supply · prevents lateral subluxation · direct lateral access | Technically demanding · **difficult medial patellar eversion** · may need TTO |
| **Midvastus** | Through **VMO muscle belly** · spares VMO insertion on quad tendon | Possibly faster rehab · better patellar tracking | Less extensile · hard in obese, flexion contracture · ✓ **partial VMO denervation** · CI: ROM <80°, obese, hypertrophic OA, previous HTO |
| **Subvastus** | **Vastus medialis belly lifted off intermuscular septum** (incision not through muscle) | **Patellar vascularity preserved** · extensor mechanism intact · minimal lateral release · accelerated rehab | ✓ **Least extensile** · CI: **revision**, large quads, previous HTO, obese, previous parapatellar arthrotomy |
| **Minimally invasive / quad-sparing** | Special instruments | — | Technically demanding · **↑ component malposition** · longer tourniquet time |

![Medial parapatellar, quad-sparing, subvastus, midvastus](../../MCQ/attachments/q219773-illa-median-para.jpg)

### Vastus approaches & denervation

**Greatest quadriceps denervation potential** — ✓ **midvastus** (abnormal EMG · blunt interval dissection recovers better than sharp)\
✗ Wrong options: subvastus · quad sparing · MPP · TTO

**Subvastus EXCEPT (TO)** — ✓ **"spares quad tendon, carried through belly of VMO"** = false (that is **midvastus**) · true: VM belly lifted off septum · accelerated rehab · not for revision (TO)

**Picture with incision not through muscle substance** → ✓ **subvastus** (TO)

![Subvastus — incision below VM, not through muscle](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17106215-2.jpg)

### Minimally invasive & quadriceps-sparing approaches

**Outcomes** — ✓ **no clinically significant improvement in PROs**, gait or quad strength\
✗ Wrong options: quickest rehab · normal gait sooner · shorter tourniquet / less pain (longer tourniquet) · no malalignment risk

**Higher component malposition vs MPP** — ✓ **quadriceps sparing**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral parapatellar · midvastus · snip · V-Y

**Mini-subvastus** — preserves all four quadriceps attachments to patella

**Convert to standard parapatellar when** — ✓ **jig alignment difficult / incision too small** · **patellar tendon starts peeling off tubercle**\
✗ Wrong options: subvastus · TTO · two-incision · ligament release

### Lateral parapatellar approach for valgus knee (TO)

**Approach of choice** — **fixed valgus** · lateral release integral to approach · preserves **medial** patellar blood supply · medial displacement of extensor mechanism internally rotates tibia → brings **posterolateral corner** into view · optimises PF tracking · tubercle can be medialised (TO)

**Medial arthrotomy + lateral release** — significant impairment of extensor mechanism blood supply (TO)

**EXCEPT (TO)** — ✓ **"more patellar necrosis than medial arthrotomy"** = false · true: less maltracking · difficult exposure (patellar eversion, may need TTO) · **fixed varus = contraindication** (TO)

### Extensile exposure in revision

**First steps** — extend medial release posteriorly (tibial ER → lateralises extensor mechanism) · ✓ **scar debridement recreating medial + lateral gutters + remove polyethylene** · scar under extensor mechanism commonest cause of failure to evert patella · need **~110° flexion** for safe implant removal

**Stepwise** — debridement → **quad snip** → **TTO (preferred)** or quad turndown → collateral release + hinge = **last bail-out**

**Lateral release alone** — for lateral patellar subluxation after implants · does not improve intra-articular exposure

| | **Quadriceps snip** | **V-Y turndown** | **Tibial tubercle osteotomy** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Technique | Oblique cut from **apex of quad tendon at 45° into vastus lateralis** | MPP arthrotomy + diverging cut down **vastus lateralis tendon** toward lateral retinaculum (inverted V, closed as Y) | **6–10 cm** fragment cut **medial to lateral** (lateral bony hinge) · screws/wires |
| Exposure | Least extensile of three | **Excellent** (ankylosed knee) · lengthens quad tendon | **Excellent** · best for **well-fixed cemented tibial stem / cone** |
| Post-op | ✓ **No change** — **WBAT + ROM as tolerated**, same as MPP (TO) | **Immobilize in extension** until extensor heals | ROM limited **4–6 wk** · some limit WB |
| Problems | Minimal long-term · early quad weakness, no extensor lag | ✓ **Extensor lag** · quad weakness · **patellar devascularisation** (inferior geniculate) → necrosis/fracture | Tubercle avulsion fracture · **non-union (low — union ~100%)** · wound problems |

**Snip + proximal lateral release joined** = **quadriceps turndown** → extensor lag, patellar blood supply loss, worse function

**TTO needed for** — ✓ **well-fixed tibial cone + cemented stem**\
✗ Wrong options: maltracking · metaphyseal bone loss (cones/stems) · extensor lag (extensor reconstruction) · elevated joint line (distal femoral augments)

**V-Y vs TTO** — ✓ **V-Y → more extensor lag** · V-Y → higher patellar fracture risk · PROMs similar · TTO low non-union · V-Y starts ROM later

**Patella baja + exposure difficulty — which needs rehab change?** → ✓ **V-Y turndown**\
✗ Wrong options: MPP · lateral parapatellar · midvastus · quad snip (all standard rehab)

**Most difficult TKR (TO, FNB 2024)** — options Insall–Salvati <0.7, Caton–Deschamps <0.7, bipartite patella · answer **not provided by the source** · patella baja complicates exposure (Orthobullets)

![V-Y turndown](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5031-vy.jpg)

### Bilateral TKA

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Simultaneous** | Two surgeons, both knees at the same time |
| **Sequential** | One surgeon, one knee then the other, **one anaesthetic** |
| **Staged** | Separate anaesthetics · **3 days to 1 year** apart |

**Antibiotic-loaded bone cement** — ↓ deep infection in **revision** · primary use controversial (theoretical ↓ strength) · not shown to ↑ aseptic loosening

## X vs Y

| | **Midvastus** | **Subvastus** |
|---|---|---|
| Plane | **Through** VMO belly | **Under** VM, off intermuscular septum |
| Risk | **Denervation** | **Least extensile** |
| Revision | Avoid if previous HTO, ROM <80° | **Contraindicated** |

| | **Medial parapatellar** | **Lateral parapatellar** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | Default, all knees | **Fixed valgus** |
| Patellar eversion | Easy | **Difficult** (may need TTO) |
| Patellar blood supply | Risked if lateral release added | **Preserved** |

## How it's asked

**"Quad-sparing MIS, jig alignment difficult — next step?"** → **convert to standard parapatellar arthrotomy**

**"Approach with higher component malposition?"** → **quadriceps sparing**

**"True about MIS quad-sparing approaches?"** → **no clinically significant improvement in PROs**

**"Approaches A, B, C (MPP, midvastus, subvastus) — accurate?"** → **similar blood loss and midterm function**

**"Best long-term practice for a new surgeon?"** → **the approach the surgeon is most comfortable with**

**"Subvastus — EXCEPT?"** → **spares quad tendon, carried through belly of VMO** (TO)

**"Picture of approach (incision not through muscle)?"** → **subvastus** (TO)

**"Greatest quadriceps denervation potential?"** → **midvastus**

**"Rehab after Q-snip?"** → **same as midline parapatellar** (TO)

**"Quad snip in revision — therapy regimen?"** → **no restriction in ROM or weight bearing**

**"TTO likely needed because?"** → **well-fixed tibial cone and cemented stem**

**"Quad snip in revision for aseptic loosening — instructions?"** → **WBAT, ROM as tolerated**

**"Stage-2 revision, stiff, cannot evert patella — next exposure step?"** → **scar debridement recreating gutters + remove polyethylene**

**"V-Y turndown (A) vs TTO (B)?"** → **A more likely to have extensor lag**

**"Two longitudinal + one transverse scar — which incision?"** → **lateral** (TO)

**"Prior dual plating, medial + lateral scars — incision?"** → **lateral one** (TO)

**"Patella baja, exposure needed — which technique changes rehab?"** → **V-Y turndown (Figure F)**

**"Lateral parapatellar for valgus — EXCEPT?"** → **more patellar necrosis than medial arthrotomy** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Aseptic Loosening

*Recon · 39 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **macrophage-induced inflammatory** bone loss → implant loosening **without infection**

**Epidemiology** — ✓ **most common mode of long-term (>2 yr) TKA failure** · HXLPE has ↓ wear failures but loosening persists

**Steps** — **particulate debris formation** → **macrophage-activated osteolysis** (RANKL → osteoclasts) → **prosthesis micromotion** → **debris dissemination**

### Osteolysis & prevention

**Factors affecting PE wear rate** — **sterilization method** (gamma in air ↑) · manufacturing (conventional vs cross-linked) · **shelf life** (prolonged ↑) · third-body debris · **backside wear** (modular insert on metal tray) · femoral counterface roughness · **alignment + stability** · **activity level**

**Malalignment** — asymmetric loading → early loosening · ✓ **varus more than valgus**

| ↓ Osteolysis | ↑ Osteolysis |
|---|---|
| ✓ **All-polyethylene tibial component** (no backside wear) | Metal tray + **modular PE insert** (backside wear) |
| **HXLPE** | Avoiding HXLPE |
| Congruent PE | **↓ congruency** → sliding → adhesive wear |
| Less constraint | **↑ constraint** (VVC) → ↑ stress at articulation |

![All-polyethylene tibial component](../../MCQ/attachments/q215497-illa-all-polyethylene-tibial-component.jpg)

### Early cement debonding

**Weak link** — ✓ **implant–cement interface** (more common than bone–cement)

**↑ Risk** — ✓ **ROM + ligament stability testing before cement fully cures** (↓ tibial tray pull-out strength) → tibial tray loosening with **varus collapse**

**Prevention (✗ Wrong options: as risk factors)** — **fresh gloves** before handling implants (lipid) · **suction + dry bone surface** · cement on **both implant undersurface and bone** · **roughened** tray undersurface (✗ Wrong options: smooth polished) · let cement fully cure

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — painless early · later pain localised around loose component · **weight-bearing / activity related**

**Exam** — minimal pain with ROM · ↑ pain with weight bearing · effusion possible

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP** | **Tibial osteolysis** readily seen · **femoral osteolysis hard to see** (posterior condyles hidden by component) |
| **Lateral** | Routine |
| **Oblique** | ✓ **Best for femoral osteolysis** |
| **CT / MRI** | Size large lesions for pre-op planning |

**Findings** — radiolucency with **sclerotic border**, especially **>2 mm** · implant position change (**varus/valgus tibial subsidence**) · **progressive widening** of cement–bone / bone–prosthesis interface · **cement cracking / fragmentation**

![Femoral and tibial osteolysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12754-latos.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**ESR + CRP normal** · always exclude **PJI** first

**Osteolysis vs others** — effusion, near-normal ESR, normal CRP, lytic lesions, no loosening → ✓ **osteolysis**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · aseptic loosening (no loosening signs) · PE dislodgement (locking mechanism intact) · crystal (doesn't explain lysis)

## 5 Management

```
Painful TKA, suspected loosening
├─ Rule out PJI (ESR, CRP, aspirate)
├─ Stable implant, minimal symptoms → observe
└─ Pain + loosening / osteolysis → revise all loose components
       ├─ Defect >10 mm, young → bone graft
       ├─ Defect >10 mm, elderly low demand → metal wedges/augments
       └─ Small defect → cement
```

### Revision indications

**Revise** — pain due to aseptic loosening · pain + osteolysis · **extensive osteolysis that would compromise future revision**

**Observe** — stable implant, minimal symptoms

| Defect | Fill |
|---|---|
| **>10 mm, younger** | **Bone graft** (preserves bone stock) |
| **>10 mm, elderly / low activity** | **Metal wedges / augments** |
| **Small** | **Bone cement** (heat → thermal necrosis → can cause loosening) |

## X vs Y

| | **Osteolysis** | **Aseptic loosening** | **PJI** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Implant | Fixed | **Loose / subsided** | Variable |
| Labs | Normal | Normal | ↑ ESR, CRP |
| Treatment | Observe or liner exchange + graft | **Revise loose parts** | Two-stage / DAIR |

## How it's asked

**"7 yr TKA, effusion, CRP normal, lytic lesions, no loosening?"** → **osteolysis**

**"Reduces osteolysis risk?"** → **all-polyethylene tibial component**

**"Tibial tray varus collapse at 3 yr — technical risk?"** → **ROM/stability testing before cement fully cures**

**"Best view for femoral osteolysis?"** → **oblique** (AP misses posterior condyles)

**"Most common weak link in cemented TKA?"** → **implant–cement interface**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Axial Alignment

*Recon · 40 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Relevance** — pre-op planning · component insertion · ligament balancing · prosthesis selection

### Normal anatomy

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| **Distal femur** (anatomic axis vs joint line) | **~9° valgus** |
| **Femoral anatomic vs mechanical axis** | **5–7° valgus** |
| **Proximal tibia** (anatomic axis vs joint line) | **2–3° varus** (tibial joint line ~3° varus to mechanical axis) |
| **Q angle** | **11 ± 7°** |

### Mechanical axis

**Limb mechanical axis** — ✓ **vertical line from femoral head centre through centre of knee to centre of ankle** · re-established at TKA\
✗ Wrong options: 5–7° valgus between shafts (anatomic axis) · 3° varus between shafts (varus knee) · passes 1.5 cm lateral (valgus knee) · 1.5 cm medial (varus knee)

![Mechanical vs anatomic axes](../../MCQ/attachments/q870-illa-mechanical-alignment-corrected.jpg)

### Technical goals

**Goals** — restore **mechanical alignment (0°)** · restore **joint line** (lets preserved ligaments e.g. PCL function) · **balanced flexion + extension gaps** · maintain **normal Q angle**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **Standing AP + lateral knee** | Joint-space narrowing · collateral insufficiency (**lateral gapping in varus**, **medial gapping in valgus**) · femorotibial subluxation · bone defects |
| **Standing full-length AP + lateral** | Accurate **valgus cut angle** when **femoral/tibial deformity** or **very tall / short** stature |
| **Flexion + extension laterals** | — |
| **Sunrise** | Patellofemoral |

## 5 Management

### Femoral alignment

**Anatomic axis (AAF)** — bisects femoral canal · sets **IM guide entry point** · IM guide runs down AAF

**Mechanical axis (MAF)** — femoral head centre → point where AAF meets **intercondylar notch** · neutral → even medial/lateral condylar load

**Valgus cut angle (VCA)** — AAF–MAF difference (**~5–7°**) · distal cut perpendicular to MAF · jig usually **6°** from AAF · **tall → VCA <5°** · **short → VCA >7°** · measure on full-length standing AP

### Tibial alignment

**Anatomic axis (AAT)** — bisects canal · IM/EM guide parallel to it

**Mechanical axis** — centre of proximal tibia → **centre of talus** · tibial cut **perpendicular to mechanical axis**

**Usually** — AAT = mechanical axis → **IM jig** fine · **tibial deformity** (axes differ) → ✓ **extramedullary guide**

### Patellofemoral alignment

**Abnormal patellar tracking** — ✓ **most common complication of TKA** (not most serious)

**Most important variable** — preserve **normal Q angle (11 ± 7°)** · Q = ASIS–patella centre vs patella centre–tibial tuberosity

**↑ Q angle errors** — **IR of femoral component** · **medialised femoral component** · **IR of tibial component** · **lateral placement of patellar button** → lateral subluxation, pain, wear, dislocation

### Joint line preservation

**Goal** — implant thickness = bone + cartilage removed · fill defects so joint line not jeopardised

| | **Elevated joint line** (>8 mm → motion problems) | **Lowered joint line** |
|---|---|---|
| Effects | **Mid-flexion instability** · PF tracking problems · **pseudo patella baja** | **Lack of full extension** · **flexion instability** |

**Valgus knee** — never elevate joint line until balanced to full extension

### Deformity & risk (peroneal palsy)

**Valgus vs varus TKA** — valgus ↑ ✓ **peroneal nerve palsy** (lateral lengthening → traction) · also instability, **recurrent valgus**, wound breakdown, patellar maltracking · lateral contracture + medial laxity · lateral condyle / plateau deficiency → hard to find epicondylar axis → **femoral IR** → maltracking\
✗ Wrong options: recurrent varus · ↑ revision · PJI · longer stay

**67 yo, old ACL tear, varus with medial tibial deficiency + subluxation** → ✓ **TKA**\
✗ Wrong options: HTO (older) · UKA (ACL deficient) · interpositional (0/12 excellent) · closing wedge

**UKA criteria** — limited unicompartmental disease · fixed **≤10° varus or ≤5° valgus** · **intact ACL** · no ML subluxation

### Extra-articular deformity (McPherson rule) (TO)

**Principle** — the **closer** a coronal extra-articular deformity is to the knee, the **greater** knee malalignment (TO)

**McPherson rule** — ✓ deformity within **distal ¼ femur or proximal ¼ tibia** and **≥20°** → **concomitant osteotomy + TKA** (TO) · closing wedge osteotomy + **long diaphyseal-fit stem** (no extra fixation) · bone cuts alone → excessive resection threatening collaterals or incompetent ligaments (TO)

**Traps (TO)** — ✗ Wrong options: farther deformity → greater malalignment · farther deformity needs bigger intra-articular cut · deformity within distal/proximal ¼ easier to correct with cuts

### Robotic-assisted TKA

**Greatest positioning precision** — ✓ **robotic assistance** (↓ outliers)\
✗ Wrong options: spinal anaesthesia · TXA · IM / EM guides

**RA-TKA literature** — ✓ **better implant position + limb alignment, no difference in long-term outcomes** · no consistent difference in complications, revision, survivorship · surgical time normal after **15–20 case** learning curve · dissatisfaction after TKA **5–20%**\
✗ Wrong options: longer time for experienced · ↑ longevity

### Patient-specific instrumentation (TO)

**PSI** — 3D model from imaging → custom cutting jigs → sizing, resection, rotation, slope · ↓ steps → **less operative time** (TO)

**Uses (TO)** — **extra-articular deformity** · **retained femoral hardware** (IM guides impossible)

**EXCEPT (TO)** — ✓ **"fewer outliers than conventional TKR"** = false · PSI equal or **less accurate**, more outliers · uses **measured resection** for rotation, ignores soft tissue → femoral rotation errors (TO)

## X vs Y

| | **Robotic / navigation** | **PSI** |
|---|---|---|
| Outliers | **↓** | Same or **↑** |
| Long-term outcome | No difference | — |
| Use without IM canal | ✓ | ✓ |

| | **IM tibial guide** | **EM tibial guide** |
|---|---|---|
| When | Anatomic = mechanical axis | **Tibial deformity** |

## How it's asked

**"Mechanical axis of a normal limb?"** → **vertical line femoral head → centre of knee → centre of ankle**

**"67 yo, old ACL tear, varus with subluxation?"** → **total knee arthroplasty**

**"Valgus knee vs varus knee — ↑ risk of?"** → **peroneal nerve palsy**

**"Greatest implant positioning precision?"** → **robotic assistance**

**"RA-TKA vs conventional — true?"** → **better positioning/alignment, no difference in long-term outcomes**

**"McPherson rule?"** → **deformity in distal ¼ femur / proximal ¼ tibia ≥20° → concomitant osteotomy + TKR** (TO)

**"Patient-specific TKR — EXCEPT?"** → **fewer outliers than conventional TKR** (TO)

**"Most common complication of TKA?"** → **abnormal patellar tracking**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Coronal Plane Balancing

*Recon · 41 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Principle** — **concave side = tight → release** · **convex side = stretched → tighten** (fill gap with thicker poly) · always test in **flexion and extension**

**Epidemiology** — **>85%** of TKA patients neutral or varus · valgus = more complex

**Kinematic alignment** — implants in patient's constitutional varus/valgus · outcomes ≈ neutral mechanical alignment

![Coronal balancing — concave vs convex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5015-coronal-balancing.jpg)

### Posterior oblique ligament function

| Structure | Primary role | Secondary |
|---|---|---|
| **POL** | ✓ **Resists internal rotation** | Valgus, external rotation |
| **Superficial MCL** | **Valgus** | Valgus + IR combined |
| **Deep MCL** | Released in standard approach | — |
| **LCL** | **Varus** | — |
| **LCL + posterolateral release** | → **external rotation laxity** | — |

\
✗ Wrong options: POL resists flexion

## 5 Management

### Medial release sequence (varus knee)

| Step | Orthobullets | TO (FNB 2022) |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Deep MCL** release to mid-coronal plane of tibia | Deep MCL + posteromedial capsule from tibia + osteophytes |
| **2** | **Medial osteophytes** | **POL** |
| **3** | **Posteromedial corner (POL)** | **Semimembranosus** (+ transect PCL if preserved) |
| **4** | **Medial tibial reduction osteotomy** | **Pie-crust superficial MCL** + release **pes anserinus** |
| **5** | **PCL release / substitution** if imbalance persists | Strip periosteum + sMCL off tibia distally **4–5 cm** |
| **6** | **Semimembranosus** (esp. with flexion contracture) | — |
| **7** | **Pie-crust superficial MCL** (**18-gauge needle**) | — |
| **8** | Complete **sMCL / pes** release — rarely needed · destabilises medial flexion gap → **constrained prosthesis** | — |

**Differential sMCL release** — **posterior (oblique) portion tight in extension** · **anterior portion tight in flexion**

**Extension vs flexion space (TO)** — posterior structures (**POL → posterior capsule → semimembranosus**) affect ✓ **extension** space · **anterior half of sMCL + pes anserinus** affect **flexion** space (TO)

**Tight medial in extension, flexion balanced (FFD 15°)** → ✓ **release POL** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: sMCL · pes · PCL

**Pie-crusting** — ✓ **soft-tissue balancing in TKR** (TO) · multiple stabs with blade/needle **parallel to joint line** on contracted side · titrated lengthening of varus or valgus sleeve\
✗ Wrong options: syndesmosis · deltoid · bone cuts

**Lateral side (varus knee)** — fill gap to tension stretched lateral ligaments · **poly >15 mm needed → constrained** (avoid joint-line elevation)

**Blount's varus 25° with thrust** → ✓ **constrained implants may be needed** (posteromedial bone loss + extensive posteromedial releases)\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal palsy common (that is valgus) · arthrodesis · lateral reefing (attenuates) · TKA then osteotomy

### Valgus knee (lateral release, popliteus, peroneal nerve)

**Valgus pathology** — ✓ **lateral femoral condyle hypoplasia** · tight lateral retinaculum, LCL, posterolateral corner, ITB · **attenuated MCL** · **lateral patellar tracking** · **tibial external rotation** relative to femur\
✗ Wrong options: tibial IR · medial tracking · tight MCL

| Order | Lateral release (Orthobullets) | Lateral release (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Osteophytes** | Lateral osteophytes + posterolateral capsule |
| **2** | **Posterolateral capsule** | **PCL always released** |
| **3** | **ITB** if **tight in extension** (pie-crust or off Gerdy's) | Pie-crust ITB (extension) at joint line or at Gerdy's |
| **4** | **Popliteus** if **tight in flexion** (anterior part of insertion) | Strip LCL + popliteus (flexion) off LFC |
| **5** | **LCL** (some release first if tight in flexion + extension) | Lateral gastrocnemius + posterolateral capsule off femur |

| Tight | Release |
|---|---|
| **Extension only** | **Iliotibial band** |
| **Flexion only** | ✓ **Popliteus** (anterior portion) → ↑ **lateral flexion space** (tibia max IR in flexion → popliteus tightest) |
| **Flexion + extension** | **LCL** |
| **LCL + popliteus both released** | Flexion gap unstable → **constrained prosthesis** |

**Balanced in extension, lateral tight in flexion** → ✓ **release anterior popliteus**\
✗ Wrong options: pie-crust ITB (extension) · PCL recession (medial flexion) · LCL (instability → constraint) · semimembranosus (medial extension)

**Medial side (valgus)** — fill until MCL taut · poly **>15 mm** (OB: >14 mm) → **constrained** · CCK / CPS for longstanding deformity

**Femoral rotation trap** — hypoplastic LFC + **posterior referencing** → ✓ **femoral component IR** → PF maltracking + asymmetric flexion gap · check against **epicondylar + AP (Whiteside) axes**\
✗ Wrong options: angulation (IM guide controls) · exposure (easier in valgus) · flexion · tibial rotation

**Severe valgus (≈45°) — IM femoral entry point (TO, FNB 2024)** — options more lateral / medial / superior / standard · answer not provided for this item · another TO explanation (FNB 2023): entry point in a valgus knee is usually ✓ **more medial** than standard (TO)

### Femoral component rotation

**Posterior condylar axis** — normally **3° IR** relative to TEA / tibial joint line → cutting block has **3° ER** built in

**Valgus with hypoplastic LFC** — posterior condylar angle **>3°** (e.g. **9°**) → standard 3° guide leaves **6° IR** → ✓ **add 6° more ER**\
✗ Wrong options: 0° guide → 9° IR · upsize · IR 6° vs TEA · rotate tibia

![Posterior condylar angle vs TEA](../../MCQ/attachments/q5729-illa-aslkhdakj.jpg)

### Peroneal nerve palsy

**Highest risk** — correction of **valgus + flexion contracture**

| Risk factor | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Valgus >12°** | Stretch beyond tolerance |
| **Flexion contracture >10°** | Stretch → ischaemia |
| **Lumbar laminectomy** | **Double crush** |
| **Epidural anaesthesia** | ↓ proprioception → unprotected compression |
| ✗ Wrong option: **female gender** | **Not a risk factor** |

**Palsy in recovery** — remove dressing, **flex knee** · observe **3 months** · then NCS / exploration · explore at **posterior border of biceps femoris**

**Severe valgus (>15°) + FFD** → ✓ **prophylactic CPN release** — fascial septum between **peroneus longus + EDL** (anterior/lateral compartments, below knee)\
✗ Wrong options: MCL pie-crust (varus) · less distal femur (resect more for FFD) · medial opening HTO (worsens valgus) · CR implant (needs more constraint)

### Flexion contracture

| Order | Posterior release |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Posterior femoral + tibial osteophytes** |
| **2** | **Posterior capsule** (stripped short distance from condyles) |
| **3** | **Additional distal femoral resection** (raises joint line → mid-flexion instability; try soft tissue first) |
| **4** | **Gastrocnemius origins** (medial + lateral) |

**Releases at 90° flexion** — popliteal artery falls posteriorly · do not remove **more tibia** for contracture → changes joint line → **patella alta**

### Extra-articular deformity & constraint

**>20° coronal deformity** — ✗ Wrong options: correctable with cuts + balancing → ✓ **extra-articular osteotomy** (staged or concomitant)

**Femoral varus 28° after old distal femur fracture** → ✓ **distal femoral lateral closing-wedge osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: cuts + balancing · medial closing wedge (worsens varus) · HTO (wrong bone) · hinged TKA

![Femoral extra-articular deformity correction](../../MCQ/attachments/q210157-illa-screen-shot-2018-04-07-at-10-41-50-pm.jpg)

### Kinematic alignment

**Varus knee, kinematic** → ✓ **varus tibial cuts + valgus femoral cuts** · equal aseptic loosening, satisfaction equal/better, ROM equal/greater, same reoperation\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ loosening · ↓ satisfaction · ↓ ROM · ↑ reoperation

## X vs Y

| | **Varus knee** | **Valgus knee** |
|---|---|---|
| Tight side | **Medial** | **Lateral** |
| Bone deficiency | Posteromedial **tibia** (tibia-driven) | **Lateral femoral condyle** (femur-driven) |
| Key error | — | **Femoral IR** with posterior referencing |
| Nerve risk | Low | **Peroneal palsy** |
| Release for flexion tightness | Anterior sMCL, pes | **Popliteus** |
| Release for extension tightness | POL, posterior capsule, semimembranosus | **ITB** |

## How it's asked

**"Peroneal palsy risk factors — EXCEPT?"** → **female gender**

**"Isolated popliteus release appropriate when?"** → **valgus knee tight in flexion**

**"Severe valgus — pathology encountered?"** → **lateral femoral hypoplasia**

**"Valgus knee, IM femoral guide, posterior referencing — watch for?"** → **femoral component rotation**

**"Popliteus release — effect?"** → **increases lateral flexion space**

**"Balanced in extension, lateral tight in flexion?"** → **release anterior popliteus**

**"Valgus + 15° FFD — consider?"** → **release fascial septum between peroneus longus and EDL**

**"3° ER guide off posterior condyles left IR femur — prevention?"** → **add 6° more ER**

**"28° femoral varus deformity?"** → **distal femoral lateral closing-wedge osteotomy**

**"Blount's varus TKA — true?"** → **constrained implants may be needed**

**"Kinematic alignment in varus knee?"** → **varus tibial + valgus femoral cuts**

**"POL primary function?"** → **resists internal rotation**

**"Soft-tissue balancing — true?"** → **tight extension space: release posterior capsule then semimembranosus** (TO)

**"Pie-crusting used for?"** → **soft-tissue balancing in TKR** (TO)

**"FFD 15°, medial tight in extension, flexion balanced — release?"** → **POL** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Extensor Mechanism Rupture

*Recon · 42 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — periprosthetic **quadriceps tendon** rupture, **patellar fracture** or **patellar tendon** rupture · arguably the most devastating TKA complication

### Epidemiology

| Injury | Incidence |
|---|---|
| **Overall EM failure** | **0.1–2.5%** (↑ in revision) |
| **Patellar tendon rupture** | **0.17%** · ✓ **most common type** · ↑ **females** |
| **Quadriceps rupture** | **0.1%** (OB: ~1%) |
| **Patellar fracture** | **0.05%** unresurfaced · **0.2–21%** resurfaced · ↑ **men** |

**Age** — mean 65–75

### Risk factors

| Category | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Multiply operated knee** | **3–4 prior surgeries** (TKA, revision, realignment, HTO) |
| **Systemic** | **Renal disease**, **DM**, **RA**, PVD, obesity, chronic steroids |
| **Drugs** | **Testosterone replacement** · **fluoroquinolones** |
| **Technical** | Stiff knee + forced **patellar eversion** · **residual patella <12 mm** · **lateral release** (superior lateral geniculate a.) · **V-Y turndown** · **component malrotation** (IR femur/tibia), **lateralised button** · **joint-line elevation** (max acceptable **4 mm**) |
| **Implant** | **Single-peg** > tri-peg patella fracture risk · **thick anterior femoral flange** |

### Anatomy & blood supply

**Quadriceps tendon** — rectus femoris superficial (continues over patella), VM + VL deep, VI deepest · **watershed 1–2 cm proximal to superior pole** = commonest rupture site

**Patellar tendon** — inferior pole → tubercle · **4–6 mm thick, 5 cm long**

**Supply** — **geniculate anastomotic ring** (SM, SL, IM, IL) · **inferior geniculates + recurrent anterior tibial a.** → patellar tendon · ring disruption → **patellar AVN → stress fracture**

**PF forces** — walking **0.5× BW** · stairs **3.1× BW** · squatting **7× BW** · patella ↑ quad mechanical advantage **30–50%**

## 2 Mechanism

**Iatrogenic** — **tendon avulsion** from tubercle in forced flexion of stiff knee · excess lateral release · V-Y turndown · **MUA**

**Traumatic** — direct blow (patella fracture) · eccentric load on weak EM · fall · hyperflexion

## 3 Presentation

**Key sign** — ✓ **inability to straight-leg raise / active extension** · passive ROM preserved · extensor lag · palpable defect · effusion · pop

**Patellar height** — **patella alta** = **patellar tendon** rupture · **patella baja** = **quadriceps** rupture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray (AP, lateral, Merchant)** | **Insall–Salvati >1.2 → alta (PT rupture)** · **<0.8 → baja (QT rupture)** · normal 0.8–1.2 (lateral, 30° flexion) · posterior tibial subluxation · avulsions · compare old films for loosening, joint line |
| **CT** | **Component rotation** · patellar fragmentation + bone stock · loosening |
| **MRI (MARS)** | ✓ **Modality of choice** for extent of tendon tear (partial vs complete) · **early patellar AVN** |
| **US** | Bedside continuity, dynamic gap · no metal artefact, cheap |

### Genes & lab markers

**Always exclude PJI before reconstruction** — ESR, CRP, aspiration (cell count, Gram, culture) · alpha-defensin / leukocyte esterase · BMP, CBC, **HbA1c** for optimisation

### Ortiguera & Berry (periprosthetic patellar fracture)

| Type | EM | Component | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Intact** | **Well fixed** | **Non-op** (good–excellent) |
| **II** | **Disrupted** | Any | **ORIF** (adequate bone) ± EM reconstruction |
| **IIIa** | Intact | **Loose**, bone **≥10 mm** | ORIF + **revise patellar component** |
| **IIIb** | Intact | **Loose**, bone **<10 mm** | **Patellectomy** or component removal (patelloplasty) |

**Criteria** — EM integrity · patellar component fixation · bone stock

### Differential

| Condition | Distinguisher |
|---|---|
| **PJI** | ↑ ESR/CRP, positive aspirate |
| **Component loosening** | Pain, **no extensor lag** |
| **Patellar clunk** | Painful clunk **30–60°** flexion → extension · no lag · arthroscopic debridement |
| **Maltracking** | Tilt/subluxation on Merchant · **J-sign** · extension intact |
| **Arthrofibrosis** | ↓ passive **and** active ROM, no lag |

## 5 Management

```
EM disruption after TKA
├─ Partial, active extension kept / lag <20° undisplaced → immobilizer 6 wk
├─ Acute (<2 wk) quad tendon, intact patella → primary repair (± augment)
├─ Complete / chronic (>6 wk) / patellar tendon → reconstruction
│      (fresh-frozen allograft or synthetic mesh, tensioned in full extension)
└─ Multiple failed reconstructions ± infection → knee arthrodesis
```

### Nonoperative management indications

**Immobilizer 6 wk** — **partial rupture with active extension** · **type I** patellar fracture · non-displaced fracture with intact EM · **lag <20°** · active PJI / unfit · low-demand elderly

**Technique** — extension splint/cylinder cast · **drop-lock brace** if no intention of healing (only for those **unfit for reconstruction**) · WBAT in brace · X-ray every 4–6 wk · ROM after 6–8 wk · ~**69%** of patellar fractures managed conservatively

**Partial quadriceps rupture, weak active extension** → immobilizer 4–6 wk → ✓ **minor lag with acceptable flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ flexion · re-rupture · 20° lag · early failure

**Appropriate non-op** → ✓ **chronic displaced patella nonunion with only 5° lag**\
✗ Wrong options: acute PT rupture + dehiscence (I&D, poly exchange, repair) · QT rupture with 45° lag · PT rupture 30° lag (reconstruct) · partial QT + coronal instability (address instability)

### Primary repair

**Indication** — acute **<2 wk**, intact patella + component, good tissue · **quad tendon repair <2 wk may equal allograft**

**Avoid** — **chronic >6 wk** · poor bone stock · PJI · **patellar tendon** (largely abandoned)

| Repair | Complication rate |
|---|---|
| **Patellar tendon** | **63%** |
| **Quadriceps tendon** | **25%** |

**Technique** — #5 non-absorbable, anchors/tunnels · **allograft augmentation** advised · knee in **full extension** · cast 6–8 wk

**Why fails** — approach + dissection destroy **inferior genicular** pedicles to the tendon

### Reconstruction – allograft vs synthetic mesh

**Standard of care** for tendinous disruption — ✓ **reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair (high re-rupture/lag)

| Option | Key points |
|---|---|
| **Achilles allograft (calcaneal block)** | PT rupture with intact patella mobile within **3–4 cm** of joint line · chronic retracted QT · block in tibial trough |
| **Whole EM allograft** | **Deficient/absent patella**, failed prior reconstruction · needs **5 cm** tibial bone + **5 cm** quad tendon · allograft patella **not resurfaced** |
| **Synthetic (Marlex polypropylene, LARS)** | PT insufficiency with intact patella/component/QT · **immunocompromised** · tunnelled lateral to host tendon, distal end in tibial trough with screw/cement, covered by host flaps |
| **Medial gastrocnemius flap** | EM rupture + **poor anterior soft-tissue cover** · ↑ vascularity |
| **Semitendinosus autograft** | Option |

**Allograft rules** — ✓ **fresh-frozen** (✗ Wrong options: freeze-dried (↑ failure)) · ✓ **maximal tension in full extension** (✗ Wrong options: minimal tension → lag)

| Mesh vs allograft | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Cost** | ✓ **Mesh lower** |
| **Failure / revision / infection / function** | ✓ **Equivalent** (OB219493, OB217844 — 10–20° lag either) |
| **Extensor lag + walking aid** | **Mesh smaller lag + fewer walking aids** (OB219933) · equivalent (other OB) — read the options |
| **Disease transmission / availability** | Mesh: none / readily available |
| **Wound complications** | Not shown lower with mesh |

![Mesh reconstruction of the extensor mechanism](../../MCQ/attachments/q220113-a-extensor-mesh-recon.jpg)

**Chronic PT rupture, 60° lag, patella alta** → ✓ **bone-tendon allograft reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: hamstring-augmented repair · therapy · arthrodesis · orthotics

**Chronic QT rupture 4 mo, active patient** → ✓ **EM reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: repair via tunnels/anchors · hinged revision (fails without EM)

**QT rupture (baja), 30° lag, infection excluded** → ✓ **mesh reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: drop-lock + PT · lower joint line (for true baja) · Krackow repair · spacer

### Component malrotation & revision

**60° lag, failed PT repair, femur IR 9°, tibia IR 12°** → ✓ **revise femoral + tibial components + EM allograft tensioned tightly in full extension** · CT for rotation pre-op\
✗ Wrong options: PT · brace · poly exchange + hamstring · poly exchange + allograft

### Postoperative protocol

**After mesh/allograft reconstruction** — ✓ **long-leg cast in full extension 6–12 wk**, then gradual ROM in hinged brace · Orthobullets: cylinder cast **6–8 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: splint in 15° flexion · brace 2–4 wk · 4 months (stiffness) · immobilizer 4 wk

### ORIF of patella

**Indication** — type II with adequate bone · type IIIa (revise component) · screws or tension band · complications: AVN, **nonunion (high)**, wire migration to popliteal fossa, infection

### Knee arthrodesis salvage

**Indication** — ✓ **recurrent EM failure after multiple reconstructions + recurrent infection** · active PJI precluding reimplantation · massive bone loss (or AKA)

**Technique** — **long IM nail** preferred · ex-fix if infected/bone loss · position **0–15° flexion, 5–7° valgus**, neutral rotation

**Not arthrodesis** — failed DAIR → **2-stage** · loose distal femoral periprosthetic fracture in elderly → **DFR** · aseptic loosening + metaphyseal loss → **cones/sleeves** · flexion instability → **revision with posterior femoral augments**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / note |
|---|---|
| **Extensor lag >30°** | **Most common**, **45%** |
| **Re-rupture** | **25%** · ↑ repair alone, ↑ freeze-dried |
| **Infection** | **23%** after reconstruction → 2-stage |
| **Stiffness** | ROM **75–90°** after 6–8 wk immobilisation |
| **Instability / walking aid** | Common |
| **Patellar AVN** | Lateral release, V-Y |

**Prognosis** — functional success **50–68%** at 3–5 yr · **54%** 5-yr implant survival · good: acute <2 wk, fresh-frozen graft, intact component, good bone, no infection

## X vs Y

| | **Patellar tendon rupture** | **Quadriceps tendon rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Incidence | **Commonest** (0.17%), ♀ | 0.1% |
| Patellar height | **Alta** (I-S >1.2) | **Baja** (I-S <0.8) |
| Primary repair | **63%** complications — abandoned | 25% · OK if <2 wk |
| Reconstruction | Achilles / whole EM / mesh | Achilles / whole EM / mesh |

## How it's asked

**"Pop at PT 3 mo, 60° lag, alta — treatment?"** → **bone-tendon allograft reconstruction**

**"Chronic QT rupture after fall, active male — best long-term?"** → **extensor mechanism reconstruction**

**"Mesh vs allograft — what is decreased?"** → **cost**

**"PT rupture after hyperflexion — true about treatment?"** → **synthetic mesh equivalent to allograft**

**"Baja, 30° lag, negative aspirate — treatment?"** → **tendon reconstruction with interwoven mesh**

**"Allograft vs synthetic — true?"** → **failure rates equivalent**

**"Mesh vs allograft, 40° lag with alta — true?"** → **mesh has lower cost**

**"Polypropylene graft — true?"** → **less cost than allograft**

**"Post-op protocol after mesh reconstruction?"** → **long-leg cast in full extension 6–12 wk, then gradual ROM**

**"Partial QT tear, immobilizer 6 wk — outcome?"** → **minor extension lag with acceptable flexion**

**"Which scenario suits non-op?"** → **chronic displaced patella nonunion with 5° lag**

**"60° lag, failed PT repair, IR components?"** → **revise both components + EM allograft tensioned in full extension**

**"Knee arthrodesis optimal in?"** → **recurrent EM disruption with recurrent PJI**

# TKA Heterotopic Ossification

*Recon · 43 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — bone in soft tissues (not periosteum) after surgery, with or without a precipitating event · distinct from **myositis ossificans** (post-traumatic muscle injury)

**Incidence** — **1–42%** (Orthobullets) · **15–50%** (OB) — read the options · **<1%** clinically relevant · much **less** frequent / problematic than after THA

### Risk factors

| ✓ Risk factor | Not a risk factor |
|---|---|
| **Hypertrophic arthrosis** (pre-op osteophytes, often post-traumatic) | ✓ **Incorrect component sizing** |
| **Male gender** | **Valgus deformity** |
| **Obesity** | — |
| **History of trauma** | — |
| **Periosteal stripping** of anterior femur | — |
| Post-op **effusion / hematoma** | — |
| Post-op **forced manipulation** | — |

## 3 Presentation

**Usually asymptomatic** · rarely: ↓ motion, pain, **quadriceps snapping**, **PF instability**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**AP + lateral** — ossification along **anterior distal femur** and in **quadriceps mechanism** · visible **3–4 wk** · matures **1–2 yr**

### Furia & Pellegrini classification

| Type | Location | Size |
|---|---|---|
| **IA** | Island in **suprapatellar** soft tissue | **≤5 cm** |
| **IB** | Island in suprapatellar soft tissue | **>5 cm** |
| **IIA** | **Contiguous with anterior distal femur** | **≤5 cm** |
| **IIB** | Contiguous with anterior distal femur | **>5 cm** |

![Furia & Pellegrini HO classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12750-furia-and-pellegrini-classification-system-of-tka-ho.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Observe** — most cases (rarely affects outcome)

**Excise** — **quadriceps snapping** · **PF tracking difficulty** · **PF instability**

**Prophylaxis** — **radiation** + **NSAIDs** used in high-risk **THA** · no evidence in TKA

## How it's asked

**"HO risk factors — EXCEPT?"** → **incorrect sizing of femoral/tibial components**

**"Indication for HO excision after TKA?"** → **quadriceps snapping / PF tracking problems / PF instability**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA in Patella Baja (Prior HTO)

*Recon · 44 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — TKA in a knee with patella lowered relative to its normal position (patella infera), classically after **HTO**

### Causes of acquired baja

**Proximal tibial osteotomy** — ✓ **most frequent complication of both lateral closing-wedge and medial opening-wedge HTO** · patellar tendon **shortening** during osteotomy + post-op **scarring** · ↑ with post-op immobilisation · patellar height ↓ 89% (Insall–Salvati) / 76.3% (Blackburne–Peel) after HTO\
✗ Wrong options: fracture · peroneal palsy · compartment syndrome · infection

**Others** — **tibial tubercle slide/transfer** · proximal tibial trauma · **joint-line elevation at TKA** · **ACL reconstruction**

| Prior procedure | Converting to TKA → |
|---|---|
| **Medial opening-wedge HTO** | ✓ **Patella baja** |
| **Lateral closing-wedge HTO** | **Baja** · leg **shortening** |
| **Lateral distal femoral (closing) osteotomy** | **Patella alta** |
| **Medial UKA / PF arthroplasty** | not associated |

**Other HTO-to-TKA problems** — proximal tibial surface shifted relative to canal · retained hardware · old incisions · scar · **valgus** more frequent after HTO\
✗ Wrong options: severe varus · collateral instability · alta

### Pseudo-baja from joint line elevation

**Mechanism** — elevated joint line with normal tendon length = "equivalent" of baja

**Insall–Salvati 0.95 → 0.76 after TKA + lateral release** → ✓ **excessive distal femoral resection + lateral release**\
✗ Wrong options: notching · pre-op baja · tendon released off tubercle · excess tibial resection (lowers joint line)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — ✓ **anterior knee pain** (commonest after lateral closing-wedge HTO) · **stiffness**\
✗ Wrong options: lengthening · quad weakness (pre-existing) · infection (0–4%)\
✗ Wrong options: laxity

**Exam** — **mechanical block to flexion** (patella impinges on tibia) · ↓ patellar glides · pain with compression

**Closing-wedge HTO, burning anterior pain at 9 mo, ↓ patellar glides** → ✓ **patella infera**\
✗ Wrong options: alta · recurrent medial overload · nonunion

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, lateral, **lateral at 30° flexion** · patella sits distal to trochlea

| Index | Baja | Normal | Alta |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Insall–Salvati** (tendon ÷ patella length) | ✓ **<0.8** (e.g. **0.7**) | **0.8–1.2** (~1:1) | >1.2 |
| **Caton–Deschamps** | — | — | **1.4** → alta |
| **Blackburne–Peel** | — | **0.6** normal | — |

**Distractors** — TT-TG **22 mm** → instability · patellar translation **>20 mm** abnormal (↓ after HTO scarring)

![Patellar height measurements](../../MCQ/attachments/q212052-illc-patella-measurements.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Non-op** — activity modification, PT · mild symptoms, younger

**TKA** — severe impingement, older with OA

### Techniques for baja at TKA

| Severity | Technique |
|---|---|
| **Mild** | **Patellar button placed superiorly** (smaller dome) + trim inferior patellar bone |
| **Moderate** | ✓ **Lower joint line** — **distal femoral augments** + **lower (more) proximal tibial cut** · may need revision system |
| **Moderate** | **Tibial tubercle transfer cephalad** — difficult near cemented tray · unpredictable healing, extensor lag |
| **Severe** | **Patellectomy** (partial to **10–12 mm**) + **cruciate-substituting** design |

**Avoid** — anything that **raises joint line** (thicker poly, under-resecting tibia) · **inferior** button · **caudad** tubercle transfer\
✗ Wrong options: downsizing femur (no effect)

**HTO + baja — intra-op technique** → ✓ **add distal femur augments**

## X vs Y

| | **True baja** | **Pseudo-baja** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Short / scarred **patellar tendon** (HTO, tubercle surgery, ACLR) | **Elevated joint line** (excess distal femoral cut + thick poly) |
| Insall–Salvati | **<0.8** | Normal tendon ratio, patella low relative to joint line |
| Fix | Lower joint line, superior button, tubercle transfer, patellectomy | Restore joint line |

## How it's asked

**"HTO 10 yr ago, TKA planned — likely complicating factor?"** → **patella baja**

**"Baja commonly encountered converting which procedure?"** → **medial proximal tibial opening-wedge osteotomy**

**"Most common complaint after lateral closing-wedge HTO?"** → **anterior knee pain**

**"HTO 5 yr ago, converting to TKA — patellar problem?"** → **patella baja**

**"Most frequent complication of closing and opening HTO?"** → **patella baja**

**"Closing-wedge HTO, anterior burning pain at 9 mo?"** → **patella infera (baja)**

**"Opening HTO 15 yr ago — consistent finding?"** → **Insall–Salvati 0.7**

**"I-S 0.95 → 0.76 after TKA + lateral release?"** → **excessive distal femoral resection + lateral release**

**"HTO with baja — intra-op technique?"** → **add distal femur augments**
