# TKA Instability

*Recon · 45 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — common cause of **early** TKA failure · **10–20%** of revisions · causes: ligament dysfunction, component malposition, poly wear

**Types** — **extension (varus-valgus)** · **flexion (AP)** · **mid-flexion** · **genu recurvatum** · **global, multiply operated**

### Valgus TKA instability (TO)

**Most common complication of valgus TKR** — ✓ **tibiofemoral instability** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal palsy · maltracking · recurrent valgus (second)

| Valgus TKR complication (TO) | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Tibiofemoral instability** | **2–70%** |
| **Recurrent valgus** | **4–38%** |
| Stiffness needing MUA | 1–20% |
| Wound problems | 4–13% |
| Patellar stress fracture / ON | 1–12% |
| Patellar tracking | 2–10% |
| **Peroneal palsy** | **1–4%** |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — prior operations · original implant · connective tissue / inflammatory disease · DM, **Charcot** · trauma

**Exam** — **varus/valgus thrust** in gait · laxity through ROM · ✓ **flexion instability test = posterior sag at 90°** · strength · extensor competence · patellar tracking

**Flexion instability symptoms** — instability **without frank giving way**, worst **descending stairs** · **recurrent (often bloody) sterile effusions** · diffuse **peripatellar + pes anserinus** tenderness (anterior soft-tissue strain) · quad weakness

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Assesses |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing AP** | Joint-line symmetry |
| **Full-length AP** | Mechanical alignment |
| **Lateral** | **Tibial slope**, **tibial subluxation**, recurvatum |
| **CT** | **Component rotation** |

| Instability | Radiographic cause |
|---|---|
| **Extension** | **Excess distal femoral resection** · oversized femoral component |
| **Flexion** | **Over-resected posterior condyles** · **undersized femur** · **↑ tibial slope** |
| **Mid-flexion** | **Anterior or proximal femoral component** (joint-line change) |

### Genes & lab markers

**Rule out infection** — CBC, ESR, CRP + **aspiration** (cell count, culture) · bloody aspirate with negative markers fits flexion instability

## 5 Management

### Extension (varus-valgus) instability

| Type | Cause | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Symmetric** | **Excess distal femoral resection** → flexion/extension mismatch | **Distal femoral augments** · **upsizing poly fails** (tightens both gaps) |
| **Asymmetric** (commoner) | Uncorrected coronal deformity / ligament imbalance | Release contracted side · ligament insufficient → **VVC (varus-valgus constrained)** |

### MCL injury & constraint

**MCL injury causes** — medial retractors · subperiosteal elevation (tibial sleeve avulsion) · **saw** during tibial / posterior condylar cut

**Intra-op midsubstance MCL transection** → ✓ **suture repair + CR or PS implant + hinged brace 6 wk** · alternative **unlinked constrained (CCK)** ± repair\
✗ Wrong options: CR/PS without repair · PS + immobilizer · hinge (not for simple collateral rupture)

**Hinge indications** — moderate–severe instability · **absent collateral(s)** · severe bone loss · large deformities

**Charcot knee (diabetic)** — excellent ROM, **ligamentous laxity** + bone loss → ✓ **semiconstrained prosthesis prevents instability** · augments for bone loss\
✗ Wrong options: stiffness (not a problem)

![Unlinked constrained (CCK) implant](../../MCQ/attachments/q5430-illa-fig-d-cck.jpg)

### Flexion instability & gap mismatch

**Definition** — **flexion gap > extension gap** · PS knee can **"jump the post"** → dislocation

**Femoral cam jump (TO)** — **specific to PS knees** · ✓ **loose flexion gap** · varus/valgus stress in flexion opens gap → cam rotates in front of post · **popliteus over-release** may cause · closed reduction: **90° flexion + anterior drawer** (clunk) · definitive: **revision** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: tight flexion gap

**Loose flexion gap, normal extension gap (PS)** → ✓ **posterior knee dislocation** (cam locked behind/in front of post)\
✗ Wrong options: MUA · hyperextension (loose extension gap) · anterior dislocation · patella fracture (<13 mm patella)

**Recurrent dislocation of PS mobile-bearing revision** → ✓ **loose flexion gap** (undersized / anteriorised femur, excess distal augmentation, collateral + posterior capsule insufficiency)\
✗ Wrong options: loose extension gap (V/V instability) · malrotation (patellar/rotational) · design

**Popliteus injury** — popliteus = **flexion stabiliser** · cut while resecting **posterolateral femoral condyle** → flexion laxity → PS dislocation\
✗ Wrong options: too little slope · posterior femoral translation · over-recessed PCL (PCL usually removed in PS) · joint line

![PS knee — cam jumped post](../../MCQ/attachments/q214242-illa-jumped-post.jpg)

### Femoral cuts & flexion gap

| Cut | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | Does not affect flexion gap · under-resect → oversize → **PF stuffing** · over-resect → **notching** · good cut = "grand piano" |
| **Posterior condylar** | ✓ **Sets flexion gap** · over-resection → **flexion instability** (line 2) |
| **Anterior / posterior chamfers** | Fit only |
| **Distal femoral** | **Sets extension gap** · = component thickness · extra for flexion contracture |
| **Tibial slope** | ↑ slope → **↑ flexion gap** (line 5) |

**Flexion instability in PS knee** — **anterior tibial translation at 90°** pathognomonic · no posterior subluxation (cam-post)

**Flexion instability causes (OB)** — ↑ posterior femoral resection · **under-resected distal femur** (joint line distalised) · **excess tibial slope** · PCL incompetence (CR)

| Cause | Fix |
|---|---|
| **Over-resected posterior condyles** | **Posterior femoral augments** |
| **Undersized femur** | **Upsize femoral component** |
| **Excess tibial slope** | **↓ slope** + consider **PS** |
| **PCL insufficiency after CR** | **Convert to PS** |

**Flexion instability revision (to PS)** → ✓ **additional distal femoral resection + posterior femoral augments + upsize poly**\
✗ Wrong options: downsize femur + distal augments + thinner poly · ER tibial tray + thicker poly · isolated thicker poly (over-tightens extension) · IR tray + ↑ slope

**Non-op** — quad strengthening, bracing · revision for flexion instability shows **least improvement** of any revision indication

**CR knee, instability on stairs, effusions, posterior tibial subluxation** → ✓ **PCL insufficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: patellar tendon rupture (no active extension) · joint line · ACL (sacrificed) · loosening

![Femoral cutting block — posterior condylar cut](../../MCQ/attachments/q5554-illa-illus-a.jpg)

### Mid-flexion instability

**Features** — poorly understood · **joint-line modification** (elevation) · instability/malrotation between **45–90°** · contributors: sagittal femoral design, **anterior MCL attenuation**, tibiofemoral geometry

**Joint-line elevation threshold (TO, FNB 2024)** — options >2 / >4 / >6 / >8 mm · **answer not provided by the source** · Orthobullets: elevation **>8 mm** → motion problems incl. mid-flexion instability (TKA axial alignment)

**Treatment** — usually **full revision** · restore joint line · equalise gaps

### Genu recurvatum

**Associations** — **poliomyelitis** (quad weakness → walks locked in hyperextension, ankle equinus) · **RA** · **Charcot** · fixed valgus + ITB contracture

**Treatment** — **long-stemmed PS or VVC** · **rotating hinge = salvage** (residual hyperextension → early failure)

### Global, multiply-operated instability

**Features** — flexion + extension + V/V laxity · bone loss · recurvatum gait

**Treatment** — **VVC minimum** · usually **hinge** ± augments, sleeves, cones · **endoprosthesis** if severe bone loss

### Component malrotation

**Valgus knee, femur IR 8°, tibia IR 4°, dislocated patella + coronal instability** → ✓ **revise femoral + tibial components** into ER\
✗ Wrong options: PT · brace · thicker liner · lateral release

## X vs Y

| | **Flexion instability** | **Extension (V/V) instability** | **Mid-flexion** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gap | Flexion > extension | Extension loose | 45–90° |
| Cause | Posterior condyle over-resection, small femur, ↑ slope, PCL loss | Excess distal cut (symmetric) · imbalance (asymmetric) | Joint-line elevation |
| Fix | Posterior augments, upsize femur, ↓ slope, PS | Distal augments · release · VVC | Revision, restore joint line |
| Thicker poly alone | No (over-tightens extension) | No (symmetric) | — |

## How it's asked

**"Femoral cam jump — EXCEPT?"** → **associated with tight flexion gap** (TO)

**"CR TKA, instability on stairs, posterior tibial subluxation?"** → **PCL insufficiency**

**"PS TKA, anterior translation at 90° — over-resection at?"** → **posterior femoral condylar cut (line 2)**

**"PS mobile-bearing revision, recurrent dislocation?"** → **loose flexion gap**

**"Loose flexion gap, normal extension gap (PS) — risk?"** → **posterior knee dislocation**

**"Medial + lateral laxity at 90°, bloody negative aspirate — which cut?"** → **excess posterior tibial slope (line 5)**

**"Flexion instability revision to PS — additional strategy?"** → **more distal femoral resection + posterior augments + thicker poly**

**"Intra-op MCL transection, coronal instability?"** → **suture repair + PS implant + hinged brace**

**"Charcot knee — semiconstrained prosthesis prevents?"** → **instability**

**"PS knee dislocation on lateral X-ray — cause?"** → **popliteus injury cutting posterolateral condyle**

**"IR femur 8°, tibia 4°, dislocated patella?"** → **revise femoral and tibial components**

**"Most common complication of valgus TKR?"** → **tibiofemoral instability** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Kinematic Alignment

*Recon · 46 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — TKA that restores the patient's **pre-arthritic limb alignment + joint lines** by **resurfacing** distal femur and proximal tibia · ✓ **"ligament-sparing"** — bone cuts, not releases, recreate native laxity

**Origin (TO)** — **Howell and Hull (2008)**, "custom-fit positioning" · driven by **~20%** dissatisfaction after mechanical alignment (TO)

**Individualised approaches (TO)** — ✓ **kinematic** + **functional** · fixed approaches = **mechanical** + **anatomical**\
✗ Wrong options: anatomical as individualised

### Anatomic rationale

**HKA axis** (varus / neutral / valgus) and **joint-line obliquity** (varus / neutral / valgus) are **independent** → **9 combinations**

**Only ~15%** of knees have 180° HKA with joint line perpendicular → MA alters **~85%** of patients

### Native alignment (TO / OB)

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| **Distal femur** | **0–4° valgus** (to mechanical axis) · **~9° valgus** to vertical |
| **Femoral anatomic vs mechanical axis** | **4–6°** valgus (OB) · **6°** (TO) |
| **Proximal tibia** | **1–5° varus** · ~3° varus to midline |
| **Overall limb** | Slight **varus ~3°** |
| **Joint line** | **~3° varus** (flexion + extension) |
| **Tibial slope** | **1–9°** posterior |

### Foundational principles

**Arthritis** — minimal **femoral** bone wear · minimal ligament contracture/attenuation · PCL usually intact · varus/valgus deformity = **cartilage loss + tibial wear**

**Balance** — laxity least in **extension** · in flexion **lateral more lax than medial** · avoid joint-line elevation/depression (baja/alta)

### The three kinematic axes of the femur

| Axis | Description |
|---|---|
| **Flexion–extension (cylindrical)** | Transverse axis across distal femur about which tibia flexes · **oriented differently from TEA** (Orthobullets) · **same as TEA** (TO) — read the options |
| **Internal–external rotation** | Longitudinal axis in **medial compartment** (**medial pivot**) · tibial IR in flexion → lateral condyle **roll-back** |
| **Patellar tracking** | Isometric axis across **medial condyle** · analogous to **Schöttle point** (MPFL) |

**All three** — parallel or perpendicular to the native femoral joint line → resurfacing femur restores them

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### CPAK classification & constitutional alignment (TO)

**Constitutional alignment** — ✓ **arithmetic HKA (aHKA)** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: mechanical HKA (changed by arthritis) · LDFA · MPTA alone

| Formula (TO) | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **aHKA = MPTA − LDFA** | **Negative = varus**, positive = valgus · unaffected by joint-space narrowing/subluxation · ignores JLCA |
| **JLO = MPTA + LDFA** | **180° = neutral** · **>180° apex proximal** · **<180° apex distal** |
| **mHKA** | Angle between femoral + tibial mechanical axes |
| **mLDFA** | Lateral angle, femoral mechanical axis vs distal femoral joint line |
| **mMPTA** | Medial angle, tibial mechanical axis vs proximal tibial joint line |

| Boundaries (TO) | Neutral | Varus / apex distal | Valgus / apex proximal |
|---|---|---|---|
| **aHKA** | **0 ± 2°** | **< −2°** | **> +2°** |
| **JLO** | **180 ± 3°** | **<177°** (apex distal) | **>183°** (apex proximal) |

| CPAK (TO) | aHKA | JLO | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Varus | Apex distal | KA better from outset |
| **II** | **Neutral** | **Apex distal** | ✓ **Commonest (~40%)** → **anatomic alignment** |
| **III** | Valgus | Apex distal | Constitutional valgus |
| **IV** | Varus | Neutral | KA better from outset |
| **V** | **Neutral** | **Neutral** | ✓ **Best balanced by mechanical alignment** · only **~15%** |
| **VI** | Valgus | Neutral | Constitutional valgus |
| **VII–IX** | Varus / neutral / valgus | Apex proximal | — |

**CPAK I / IV under MA** — need varus recuts or extensive **MCL release** (TO)

**CPAK III / VI** — lateral femoral + tibial deficiency, femoral/tibial ER deformity, lateral contracture, later MCL attenuation (TO)

![CPAK matrix](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733104-4.png)

## 5 Management

### Surgical technique

**Femoral target** — ✓ **femoral component condyle thickness − 1 mm (saw kerf) − 2 mm (cartilage loss)**

**Tibial balancing** — after femoral resurfacing, adjust tibial **varus/valgus + slope** · no soft-tissue releases · no PCL sacrifice/recession · TO: only **tibial cut** used to balance (plus osteophytes, posterior capsule off femur, tibial ML shift)

| Gap | Shape |
|---|---|
| **Extension** | **Rectangular** (equal, negligible V/V laxity) |
| **Flexion** | **Trapezoidal** (lateral progressively looser) |

**Patella** — resect component thickness − 1 mm kerf − **4 mm** cartilage · leave **≥13 mm** bone

### Distal femoral cut angle

**LDFA 81°, anatomic–mechanical difference 6°** → femur in **9° valgus** at joint line → ✓ **9° valgus** to cut parallel to native joint line (KA) · MA would use **6°** · KA femoral component **2–4° more valgus**, tibia more **varus**\
✗ Wrong options: 0°, 3°, 6° valgus, 3° varus

### Component positioning goals

| Target | **Mechanical alignment** | **Kinematic alignment** |
|---|---|---|
| Femoral flexion | Similar | Similar |
| **Distal femoral cut** | ⊥ femoral mechanical axis | ∥ distal articular surface (after wear correction) |
| **Posterior femoral cut** | **3° ER** relative to posterior condyles | **Neutral (0°)** relative to posterior condyles |
| **Tibial cut** | ⊥ tibial mechanical axis | ∥ articular surface (after wear correction) |
| **Tibial slope** | **2–7°** posterior | **∥ medial plateau** |
| **Tibial axial rotation** | **Aligned with tibial tubercle** | **∥ long axis of lateral plateau** |
| Balancing | Ligament releases | Bone cuts (tibia) |

**NOT a KA goal (TO)** — ✓ **tibial rotation aligned with tubercle** (that is MA)

### Methods of performing KA

| Method | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| **Caliper-verified** | Any instruments/implant · cuts within **±0.5 mm** of target | Sagittal femoral flexion hard with IM rod · **tibial recuts** common · sizing hard pre-op |
| **Patient-specific guides** | Detailed planning · few trays | Recuts still needed · advanced imaging · closed vendor |
| **Navigation** | Planning + intra-op adjust, avoid recuts | Imaging · closed vendor · cost |
| **Robotics** | Planning + intra-op adjust | Imaging · closed vendor · equipment burden · cost |

### KA versus MA

**Varus knees** — varus tibial cuts / residual varus ✓ **not associated with bony or hardware failure**

**Valgus knees (KA view)** — **no hypoplastic LFC** · natural valgus joint line with comparable condyles · lateral cartilage + tibial wear (contrast: conventional teaching = LFC hypoplasia)

**Rotation** — **ER of femur/tibia not needed** for tracking · many native trochleae are **IR relative to TEA** · tracking from correct HKA, JLO, joint-line height + tubercle IR in flexion

**Implant** — **medial-pivot** design needed for normal kinematics · concern about shear on **PS cam-post**

**Outcomes (OB)** — loosening, reoperation equal · satisfaction + ROM equal or better

**Controversies (TO)** — needs PSI/technology · ACL + menisci sacrificed · non-weight-bearing kinematics differ · no limits on outliers → restrict to mild deformity · few long-term data

### Related techniques

| Technique | Principle |
|---|---|
| **Restricted KA** | Limits on component + HKA varus/valgus · safe zone (TO): **LDFA + MPTA 86–93°**, final **mHKA −5° varus to +4° valgus** · minor releases rather than exceed limits |
| **Functional alignment** | Adjust femoral + tibial cuts in all 3 planes for symmetric/asymmetric gaps · no releases · e.g. tibia up to **3° varus** for tight medial extension · **femoral IR** for tight lateral flexion · limb within **0 ± 3°** |
| **Inverse KA** | Predetermined tibial cut → femur gap-balanced · no releases |
| **Anatomical (Hungerford 1982)** | Tibia **3° varus** + femur extra 3° valgus (**7–10°** total) · for **CR** · risks asymmetric poly wear, LCL/popliteus damage (TO) |
| **Mechanical (Freeman & Swanson 1973)** | Both components ⊥ mechanical axis · releases balance gaps · varus knee: more lateral tibia + more distal/posterior MFC removed → MCL tight · **15–20%** remain symptomatic (TO) |

## X vs Y

| | **Mechanical** | **Kinematic** |
|---|---|---|
| Target | Neutral 0° HKA, horizontal joint line | Patient's pre-arthritic HKA + JLO |
| Balancing | **Soft-tissue release** | **Bone cuts** (tibia) |
| Femoral rotation | 3° ER to posterior condyles / TEA | 0° to posterior condyles |
| Best CPAK | **V** | I, III, IV, VI |
| Implant | Any | Medial pivot preferred |

## How it's asked

**"LDFA 81°, cut parallel to native joint line — guide setting?"** → **9° of valgus**

**"CPAK type best balanced by MA?"** → **CPAK V** (TO)

**"Constitutional alignment best defined by?"** → **arithmetic HKA** (TO)

**"Commonest CPAK type?"** → **neutral aHKA + apex distal JLO (type II)** (TO)

**"Individualised approach to alignment?"** → **kinematic alignment** (TO)

**"NOT a KA positioning goal?"** → **tibial rotation aligned with tibial tubercle** (TO)

**"Kinematic vs mechanical in varus knee — result?"** → **varus tibial + valgus femoral cuts**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Metal Hypersensitivity

*Recon · 47 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — allergic reaction to metallic TKA components → **persistent pain + stiffness** · **rare**

### Type IV hypersensitivity

**Mechanism** — ✓ **Type IV delayed-type, T-cell (cell-mediated)** hypersensitivity

**Allergen** — **nickel** (in **cobalt-chromium** alloys) · metal ions/wear lodge in tissue, **bind proteins** → initiate reaction · may initiate **early loosening**

| Gell–Coombs | Mediator | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | IgE | Anaphylaxis, bee sting |
| **II** | IgG (antibody, cytotoxic) | Haemolytic anaemia, Goodpasture, Graves |
| **III** | Immune complex | SLE, serum sickness, PAN |
| ✓ **IV** | **T cells, delayed** | **Metal allergy** |
| **V** | IgG stimulatory | — |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — dermatitis, eczema, atopy · **intolerance to cheap jewellery**

**Symptoms** — **vague persistent pain** · **persistent effusion / swelling** · **stiffness** · document ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Note |
|---|---|
| **Patch test** | Limited evidence of direct link |
| **Lymphocyte transformation test (LTT)** | — |
| **T-lymphocyte-rich immunohistopathology** | Synovium |
| **Infection work-up** | ✓ **Must be negative** (alpha-defensin, synovial fluid) |

**Diagnostic criteria (controversial)** — **positive patch test + positive immunohistology + relief after exchange** (strict) · or **diagnosis of exclusion** after infection + aseptic loosening ruled out

![Metal hypersensitivity synovial histology](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12753-metal-hypersensitivity-synovial-histology.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Implant exchange** — persistent symptoms affecting quality of life · **hypoallergenic femoral component** (e.g. nickel-free) + **all-polyethylene tibia** if possible · chronic inflammatory **synovitis** at surgery

## How it's asked

**"Jewellery intolerance — Gell–Coombs type of metal hypersensitivity?"** → **IV (delayed type)**

**"Painful TKA, negative infection work-up, jewellery reaction — pathophysiology?"** → **type IV delayed T-cell mediated**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Other Complications

*Recon · 48 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Covered elsewhere** — neurovascular (vascular injury, peroneal nerve) · extensor mechanism (patellar loosening, clunk, maltracking, EM rupture) · PJI · periprosthetic fracture · metal hypersensitivity · wounds · tibiofemoral instability · stiffness

### Popliteus snapping

**Presentation** — **posterolateral** pain, popping, **palpable snapping at posterolateral joint line** · no effusion, normal gait · ~6 mo after TKA

**Cause** — ✓ **popliteal snapping syndrome** · tendon subluxes over a **retained lateral femoral condyle osteophyte**

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: **patellar clunk** (PS knees, patella jumps flexion → extension, anterior) · tight PCL · retained PMMA · tight lateral retinaculum

### Infrapatellar saphenous neuroma (TO)

**Nerve** — ✓ **infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve (IPBSN)** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: superomedial, inferomedial, inferolateral genicular

**Anatomy (TO)** — saphenous n. exits **subsartorial canal** → **infrapatellar branch** (fat pad + anteromedial knee) + **descending branch** (anteromedial leg/foot to 1st MT)

**Causes (TO)** — **midline/parapatellar TKA approach** · more often ACL reconstruction, arthroscopy, tibial nailing

**Features (TO)** — sharp **medial** knee pain · ↓ ROM · medial tenderness · **Tinel +**

**Treatment (TO)** — conservative → **neuroma excision ± nerve graft**

![Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17105292-1.png)

## X vs Y

| | **Popliteus snapping** | **Patellar clunk** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Posterolateral** joint line | **Anterior** (suprapatellar nodule) |
| Cause | **Retained LFC osteophyte** | Fibrous nodule at quad tendon–patella junction |
| Implant | Any | **PS** knees |

## How it's asked

**"Posterolateral snapping 6 mo after TKA, no effusion?"** → **popliteal snapping syndrome**

**"Nerve causing painful neuroma after TKR?"** → **infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Patellar Maltracking

*Recon · 49 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Epidemiology** — common cause of dissatisfaction · ✓ **most common reason for secondary surgery after TKA** · historically main cause of revision

**Principle** — anything that **↑ Q angle** → lateral pull → subluxation/dislocation

### Causes of lateral maltracking (TO)

| ✓ Causes (↑ Q angle) | Not a cause |
|---|---|
| **Femoral component IR** (missed **hypoplastic LFC** in valgus knee) | ✓ **Medial placement / translation of patellar button** (TO) — this is the correct position |
| **Medial shift of femoral component** (moves trochlea medially) | Excess femoral ER (helps tracking; may loosen medial flexion gap) |
| **Tibial component IR** | Joint-line elevation (→ mid-flexion instability) |
| **Lateral placement / centralisation of patellar button** | Femoral component in extension (→ notching) |
| **Flexed femoral component** | KA vs MA per se (debated) |
| **Residual valgus** | — |
| **Asymmetric patellar resection / thickness** | — |
| Pre-op: excess femoral anteversion, tibial IR · design · extensor imbalance · anterior femoral component · patellar height | — |

### Rules for tracking

**Femur** — **lateralise** · avoid IR · ER helps

**Tibia** — **lateralise** · avoid IR · centre of tray on **medial ⅓ of tibial tubercle** · slight **ER** → net medialisation of tubercle

**Patella** — ✓ **medialise button** (replicates median eminence) — the only implant placed medially · total thickness ~**21–22 mm** native/composite

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — subluxation sensation · frank dislocation · **peripatellar / anterolateral pain** · **limited flexion** · stiffness

**Sign** — ✓ **J-sign** (subluxed in extension, reduces as it engages trochlea in flexion)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Assesses |
|---|---|
| **AP** | Femoral component placement, Q angle |
| **Lateral** | Femoral size + rotation |
| **Tangential (Merchant)** | **Patellar subluxation**, button placement, resection angle, **tilt** |
| **CT** | ✓ **Best for rotational malalignment** |

**Findings** — lateral subluxation · ↑ Q angle · anterior femoral component · asymmetric resection · lateral button · lateral patellar osteophyte

**Most important test for multifactorial subluxation** → ✓ **axial CT from supracondylar femur to below tibial tubercle**\
✗ Wrong options: long-leg AP · fluoroscopy · bone scan · MRI (artefact)

![Merchant view — subluxed patellar component](../../MCQ/attachments/q5971-c-06c.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Rule** — address the **cause** · component malalignment first

### Component internal rotation – CT evaluation & revision

**Subluxed patella on Merchant, femoral IR** → ✓ **revision with ER of femoral component + lateral release** (± tibial revision if IR, soft-tissue realignment)\
✗ Wrong options: brace · tantalum patella lateralised · more tibial IR · femoral IR

**CR TKA, anterolateral pain, J-sign, normal X-ray + labs, CT → revision** → ✓ **internal rotation of components**\
✗ Wrong options: joint-line elevation · infection · osteolysis · clunk (PS knees)

**Tray centred medial to medial ⅓ of tubercle** → ✓ **patellar maltracking** (IR tray)\
✗ Wrong options: flexion instability (gaps balanced) · osteolysis (HXLPE) · fracture (22 mm thick) · clunk (PS)

### Intra-op lateral subluxation despite lateral release

✓ **Slight ER of tibial component** (medialises tubercle)\
✗ Wrong options: femoral IR · anterior tibial translation · fixed vs mobile bearing (no difference) · thicker button (overstuffs)

### Hypoplastic lateral condyle

**Valgus knee, patella tracks laterally on trials** → ✓ **failure to recognise hypoplastic LFC** → femoral IR with posterior referencing\
✗ Wrong options: KA · raised joint line · excess ER · femur in extension

### Patellofemoral arthroplasty failure

**Most common early failure** — ✓ **patellar instability / maltracking** (malalignment, soft tissue, malposition) · **late** failure = **tibiofemoral OA progression (~25%)** · loosening **<1%** cemented, higher cementless

## X vs Y

| Error | Effect on Q angle / tracking |
|---|---|
| **Femur IR** / **femur medialised** | ↑ Q → lateral maltracking |
| **Tibia IR** | ↑ Q → lateral maltracking |
| **Button lateral / central** | ↑ Q |
| **Button medial** | ✓ ↓ Q (correct) |
| **Femur ER / lateralised** | ✓ Better tracking |

## How it's asked

**"Subluxed patellar component 3 mo post-op — best course?"** → **revision with lateral release + femoral ER**

**"Intra-op lateral subluxation despite lateral release?"** → **slight ER of tibial component**

**"Multifactorial patellar subluxation — most important test?"** → **axial CT supracondylar femur to below tibial tubercle**

**"CR TKA, J-sign, CT → revision — cause?"** → **internal rotation of components**

**"Tray medial to medial ⅓ of tubercle — complication?"** → **patellar maltracking**

**"Valgus knee, patella tracks laterally on trials — error?"** → **failure to recognise hypoplastic LFC**

**"Most common early failure of PF arthroplasty?"** → **patellar instability/maltracking**

**"Lateral subluxation after TKR — cannot be the reason?"** → **medial placement of patellar component** (TO)

**"Lateral maltracking caused by all EXCEPT?"** → **medial translation of patellar component** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Patellar Prosthesis Loosening

*Recon · 50 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **0.6–5%** (OB: 0.6–4.8%) · ↑ with **metal-backed** patellae

**Mechanism** — **maltracking** or **PF overstuffing** → high shear (↑ PFJRF at high flexion) · **subclinical infection**

**Risk factors** — **metal backing** · obesity · **lateral release** · **joint-line elevation** · **flexion >100°** · poor bone stock · **asymmetric resection** · **small pegs** (inadequate fixation) · **secondary osteonecrosis** + osteolysis

### Patellar blood supply & AVN

**Supply** — **anastomotic ring** · ✓ **medial-sided vessels contribute more** than lateral

| Step | Vascularity disrupted |
|---|---|
| **Medial arthrotomy** | **0–35%** |
| **Fat pad excision** | **0–16%** (anterior tibial recurrent a. + inferior ring, not medial/lateral vessels) |
| **Lateral release** | **53–75%** (superior + inferior lateral geniculates) |
| ✓ **Medial arthrotomy + lateral release** | **50–100%** (OB option) · **up to 75%** (Orthobullets/OB text) |
| **Lateral meniscectomy** | Does not disrupt ring |

**Patellar ON incidence** — up to **33% at 1 yr**, **10% at 10 yr** · direct link unproven

**Protect** — leave **≥15 mm medial cuff** · lateral release **distal to superolateral border**, **proximal to lateral joint line**, **≥15 mm lateral** to patella · care converting **lateral UKA → TKA** (prior lateral arthrotomy)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — persistent knee pain · swelling · pain with flexion · **no extensor lag** (vs EM rupture) · may see **subcutaneous component** through capsular defect

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral** | **Loss of PF radiolucent space** — patellar bone touching femoral component · dissociated component |
| **Tangential** | Dissociated component superficial to patella · **thinning of polyethylene** |

**Aspirate 700 WBC, 55% PMN, no lag, component migrated subcutaneously** → ✓ **implant loosening**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · EM rupture (no lag) · fracture (would give haemarthrosis) · overstuffing (cause not diagnosis)

![Loose patellar component migrated subcutaneously](../../MCQ/attachments/q216613-illa-illus-a.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Revise patellar component** · if revision fails → **resect component** (patelloplasty)

## How it's asked

**"10 yr TKA, swelling, no lag, low WBC, component migrated — diagnosis?"** → **implant (patellar component) loosening**

**"Patellar blood supply — accurate statement?"** → **medial arthrotomy + lateral release can disrupt 50–100%**

**"Which side contributes more to patellar supply?"** → **medial**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Patellofemoral Alignment

*Recon · 51 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Key fact** — ✓ **abnormal patellar tracking = most common complication of TKA** · PF complications (subluxation, clunk, wear/loosening, fracture) occur in ~**30%**

**Most important variable** — **preserve normal Q angle** (ASIS → patella centre vs patella centre → tibial tuberosity)

**↑ Q angle** — ↑ lateral subluxation force → pain, mechanical symptoms, wear, dislocation · ↑ need for **lateral release**

### Errors that ↑ Q angle

| Component | Bad: ↑ Q angle (maltracking) | Good: ↓ Q angle (good tracking) |
|---|---|---|
| **Femur** | **Internal rotation** · **medialisation** | **External rotation** · **lateralisation** |
| **Tibia** | **Internal rotation** · **medialisation** | **External rotation** · **lateralisation** (with adequate ER) |
| **Patella** | **Lateral placement** | **Medial / central** placement |
| **PF space** | ↑ composite thickness (under-resection), **anterior femoral translation** → tight lateral retinaculum (medial approach) | — |

**Lateral release rates (OB)** — IR femur: **24% varus, 33% valgus** · ER off TEA: **7% varus, 29% valgus**

![Femoral IR and lateral patellar subluxation](../../MCQ/attachments/q3274-illb-ir.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Malrotation** — ✓ **CT scan of the knee**\
✗ Wrong options: 3-joint standing film · fluoroscopic EUA · 45° flexed PA · MR arthrogram

**Merchant / tangential** — lateral tilt + subluxation

## 5 Management

### Femoral rotation landmarks (posterior condylar axis, hypoplastic lateral condyle)

| Axis | Definition | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **AP (Whiteside's line / trans-sulcus)** | Deepest trochlear groove → top of intercondylar notch | **Perpendicular** = neutral rotation · fewer maltracking reoperations in valgus knees (Whiteside & Arima) |
| **Transepicondylar (TEA)** | Medial ↔ lateral epicondyle · **⊥ Whiteside's line** · parallel to cut tibia | Posterior cut ∥ TEA → **rectangular flexion gap** · more reliable than PCA |
| **Posterior condylar (PCA)** | Tips of posterior condyles · ✓ **~3° IR to TEA** (range **1–10°**) | Cut in **3° ER** from PCA |

**Posterior condylar axis normal relation** → ✓ **3° internally rotated to TEA**\
✗ Wrong options: parallel · ⊥ Whiteside · 3° or 7° ER

**Hypoplastic LFC + posterior referencing** → ✓ **femoral component IR** → lateral tilt, maltracking, anterior pain in flexion · fix: add ER, posterior lateral augments, check against TEA / Whiteside\
✗ Wrong options: tibial rotation errors

**Femoral IR also** → **tight medial / loose lateral flexion gap** → stiffness \
✗ Wrong options: joint-line change · poly thickness change · ↓ Q

**Gap-balanced flexion (tensioner) — varus (A) vs valgus with hypoplastic LFC (B)** → ✓ **B needs more ER relative to PCA** · posterior **medial** resection thicker than lateral when ER correct · B would need posterior **lateral** augments if any\
✗ Wrong options: more IR to trans-sulcus

**Valgus knee with lateral tilt after TKA** → ✓ **posterior condyles used as rotation reference**\
✗ Wrong options: excess ER · femoral lateralisation · gap balancing (not linked) · TEA matching

### Component internal rotation & maltracking

**Most common intra-op error causing maltracking** → ✓ **femoral component IR**\
✗ Wrong options: gap balancing vs measured resection · tibial ER · femoral lateralisation · over-resected patella (**under**-resection overstuffs)

**Optimise tracking** → ✓ **ER of femoral and tibial components** · medialise patellar button

**↓ Need for lateral release** → ✓ **femoral ER** · ✓ **tibial ER** (medialises tubercle)\
✗ Wrong options: mobile bearing (no benefit) · anterior tray (force not tracking) · CR device

**Lateral subluxation after medial approach** → ✓ **femoral IR** (also tibial IR, ↑ composite patellar thickness, anterior femoral translation)\
✗ Wrong options: tibial ER · posterior femoral translation · ↓ patellar thickness · lateral tibia

**Patellar tilt — all cause EXCEPT** → ✓ **lateralisation of the femoral prosthesis**

### Patellar component & tibial position (medialisation, Q angle)

**Patella** — centred or ✓ **medialised** (replicates median eminence) · ideal **40–45%** (medial edge → central peg ÷ resected width) · uncovered **lateral facet** → excise to avoid lateral facet syndrome · or **oval** button with apex medial

**Most risky for maltracking** → ✓ **lateral placement of patellar component**\
✗ Wrong options: resection thickness · CR · medial placement · pre-op tilt

**Decreases Q angle** → ✓ **medialisation of patellar component**\
✗ Wrong options: medialise femur/tibia · IR femur/tibia

**Tibia** — **neutral rotation**, centred over **medial ⅓ of tibial tubercle** (junction medial–middle third) · may leave posteromedial tibia uncovered, posterolateral overhang · tibial IR = relative tubercle ER → ↑ Q

**Tray at medial/middle ⅓ with 10° ER, still lateral tilt** → ✓ **lateralise the tibial component**\
✗ Wrong options: medialise femur · lateralise button · IR femur/tibia

**Tibial size** — not related to tracking

![Q angle and component position](../../MCQ/attachments/q212913-illa-q-angle.jpg)

### Tourniquet effect on tracking

**Lateral subluxation with perfect components** → ✓ **deflate tourniquet and re-evaluate** before lateral release\
✗ Wrong options: lateral release first · revise components

### Patellar resurfacing vs patelloplasty

**Absolute indications to resurface** — **inflammatory arthritis** (✓ consistent benefit) · **patellar maltracking** · **PF OA as main indication** · severe patellar deformity

**Strategies** — always · never (± **patelloplasty**: excise marginal osteophytes, reshape) · **selective**

| Resurfaced vs not | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Anterior knee pain** | **Less with resurfacing** (Orthobullets; OB4743) · **no difference** (OB215498) — read the options |
| **Revision / secondary surgery** | **Unresurfaced → ↑ secondary resurfacing (8.7%)** · secondary resurfacing gives **inferior** results |
| **Tibial/femoral revision** | No difference |
| **Fracture, AVN, tendon injury** | No difference |
| **Infection** | No difference |
| **Satisfaction / function** | Similar · TKA improves function either way |
| **Metal-backed button** | Not lower loosening — **higher** |

**Patelloplasty EXCEPT** → ✓ **"no difference in anterior knee pain"** = false

**"Patella-friendly" trochlea** — **thinner anterior flange** · anatomic groove

### Late instability – realignment

**Instability after fall, components correctly ER on CT, tiny patellar fragment** → ✓ **lateral retinacular release + proximal realignment** (excise fragment)\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF + brace · tibial revision · distal tubercle medialisation (not first line) · patellar revision

**Rule** — malpositioned components → **revise** · well-positioned → **soft-tissue procedures** (lateral release first)

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior condylar axis** | **TEA** | **Whiteside's line** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Relation | 3° IR to TEA | Reference | ⊥ TEA |
| Pitfall | **Hypoplastic LFC → IR** | Epicondyles hard to palpate | Trochlear dysplasia |

## How it's asked

**"Femoral IR results in?"** → **increased need for lateral release**

**"Decreases need for lateral release?"** → **external rotation of femoral component**

**"Patellar tilt — all cause EXCEPT?"** → **lateralisation of femoral prosthesis**

**"Valgus knee — error causing lateral patellar instability?"** → **femoral component IR**

**"Femoral IR may lead to?"** → **net lateral patellar tilt + lateral subluxation**

**"Risk for lateral maltracking?"** → **IR of tibial component**

**"Most common intra-op error causing maltracking?"** → **IR of femoral component**

**"Lateral subluxation after medial parapatellar TKA — cause?"** → **IR of femoral component**

**"Optimal implant position for tracking?"** → **ER of femoral and tibial components**

**"Enhance tracking and minimise lateral release?"** → **ER of tibial component**

**"Missed hypoplastic LFC + posterior referencing?"** → **IR of femoral component**

**"Normal posterior condylar axis relation?"** → **3° IR to TEA**

**"Valgus knee, posterior referencing — error?"** → **IR of femoral component**

**"Valgus knee, lateral tilt on Merchant — technique?"** → **posterior condyles used as rotation reference**

**"Varus (A) vs valgus (B), gap-balanced?"** → **B needs more ER relative to PCA**

**"MOST places knee at risk of maltracking?"** → **lateral placement of patellar component**

**"Aids correction of tracking?"** → **medialisation of patellar component**

**"Decreases Q angle?"** → **medialisation of patellar component**

**"Tray ER 10°, still lateral tilt?"** → **lateralise the tibial component**

**"Malrotation best diagnosed by?"** → **CT scan of the knee**

**"Perfect components, patella subluxes — next?"** → **re-evaluate after tourniquet deflation**

**"Patelloplasty vs resurfacing — EXCEPT?"** → **no difference in anterior knee pain**

**"True about the patella in TKA?"** → **resurfacing benefits inflammatory arthritis**

**"Post-fall instability, components well rotated?"** → **lateral release + proximal realignment**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Periprosthetic Fracture

*Recon · 52 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Sites** — **distal femur** · **proximal tibia** · **patella** · overall prevalence **0.39%** (OB)

**Decision drivers** — **fracture location** · **implant stability** · **bone stock** · comorbidity · implant design (CR / open-box PS / closed-box PS / stemmed)

### Risk factors & intraoperative fractures

| Category | Risk factors |
|---|---|
| **Bone quality** | **Osteopenia** · age **>70** · **female** · **chronic steroids** · **RA** · stress-shielding |
| **Stress risers** | Screw holes · **osteolysis** · stiffness · **revision TKA** · navigation pin sites |
| **Neurological** | **Epilepsy** · **Parkinson's** · cerebellar ataxia · myasthenia · **polio** · CP |
| **Femur-specific** | Modulus mismatch · **rotationally constrained** components · **anterior notching** — debatable |
| **Not risk factors** | **Male gender** · **anterior femoral notching** (TO: controversial; >3 mm ↓ strength in biomechanics, not clinically) |

**Notching conflict** — **not a risk factor** (TO) · **listed as risk** (OB210125, OB220387) · **debatable** (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Intra-op fractures** — ✓ **medial femoral condyle commonest** (incomplete sagittal cut between notch and MFC) → then **lateral condyle → supracondylar femur → proximal tibia** · ↑ in **PS (box cut)** · **femoral > tibial** · patella rare · most need **fixation** (wires/screws/plates) · occur in exposure/bone prep > trialing

**Navigation / robotics** — ✓ **femoral shaft fracture through tracker pin sites** = complication unique to navigation (TO) · ✓ **transcortical / bicortical diaphyseal, large pins** ↑ risk · safer: **two 3 mm unicortical metaphyseal pins**, self-drilling/self-tapping · fractures within ~**3 months**; thermal necrosis · 5 mm pin removes **2.78×** bone of 3 mm · drill holes ↓ bending strength up to **40%**\
✗ Wrong options: PS implant risk

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-rays** — diagnosis · **CT** — bone stock, **loosening**, planning · identify **CR (no box, notch recess) vs PS (box)** on X-ray

**Supracondylar periprosthetic femur** — within **15 cm** of joint line or **5 cm** of top of a stemmed component

**Acceptable outcome** — ROM **>90°** · shortening **<2 cm** · V/V **<5°** · F/E malalignment **<10°**

### Lewis & Rorabeck (distal femur) — most used

| Type | Displacement | Component |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Non-displaced** | Intact |
| **II** | **Displaced** | **Intact** |
| **III** | Displaced | **Loose / failing** |

### Su (distal femur) (TO)

| Type | Location | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Proximal** to femoral component | **Retrograde or antegrade IMN** / plate |
| **II** | Starts at **proximal edge of component**, extends proximally | **Retrograde IMN** or fixed-angle plate |
| **III** | Any part **distal to upper edge of anterior flange** | **Fixed-angle/locked plate** or **revision / DFR** if poor bone · no rIMN |

**Su used for** — ✓ **periprosthetic femur** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: patella · humerus · tibia

![Su classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5027-su-classification.jpg)

### Other femoral classifications

| System | Types |
|---|---|
| **Neer (1967)** | I non-displaced (<5 mm/<5°) · II >1 cm (IIa lateral, IIb medial shaft displacement) · III displaced + comminuted |
| **DiGioia & Rubash (1991)** | I extra-articular non-displaced · II extra-articular displaced (>5 mm/>5°) · III lost cortical contact / angulated 10°, ± intercondylar/T |
| **Chen (1994)** | I non-displaced · II displaced/comminuted |

### Felix (tibia)

| Type | Location | Subtype |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Tibial plateau** (implant interface) | **A** stable component |
| **II** | **Adjacent to stem** (meta-diaphysis) | **B** **loose** component |
| **III** | **Shaft distal** to component | **C** **intra-operative** |
| **IV** | **Tibial tubercle** | — |

**Felix treatment (TO)** — **IB, IIB → revision** (most postoperative I/II are B) · IIA, IIIA, IVA → usual fracture principles · IC → intra-op fixation · IIC → graft / immobilise / restricted WB · IIIC → conventional

**Unified Classification System (TO)** — A apophyseal · B implant bed · C clear of bed · D between two arthroplasties · E two bones, one arthroplasty · F faces unresurfaced implant

![Felix tibial classification](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356187-1.png)

### Patellar classifications

| **Ortiguera & Berry** | EM | Component | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Intact | **Stable** | **Cast/brace in extension** |
| **II** | **Disrupted** | Stable or loose | **ORIF** (good bone, ± mesh augment) · partial patellectomy + repair · patellectomy needs allograft/mesh reconstruction |
| **IIIa** | Intact | **Loose**, bone **≥10 mm** | Component **revision** or resection arthroplasty |
| **IIIb** | Intact | Loose, **<10 mm** / comminuted | **Component removal + patelloplasty** or patellectomy |

| **Goldberg** | Description |
|---|---|
| **I** | Not involving implant/cement interface or quad mechanism |
| **II** | Involves interface and/or quad mechanism |
| **IIIA / IIIB** | Inferior pole **with / without** patellar ligament rupture |
| **IV** | Fracture-dislocation |

![Ortiguera and Berry classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q214846-illa-ortiguera-and-berry.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Periprosthetic distal femur fracture
├─ Non-displaced + stable component → cast / brace
├─ Displaced + stable component → fix
│      (rIMN if CR / open box + Su I–II; locked plate if closed box, stem, Su III)
└─ Loose component / poor bone → revision (long stem) or DFR
       └─ Elderly, very distal, comminuted → distal femoral replacement
```

### Supracondylar femur – locked plating vs retrograde nail

**Retrograde IMN** — needs **CR or open-box PS** (8–12 mm recess) · Su I/II · ≥**2 distal interlocks**, end cap · insert **deep** (no patellar abrasion) · not with **closed-box PS**, **stemmed** femur, loose component, ipsilateral long antegrade/cephalomedullary nail or hip stem (stress riser), insufficient distal bone · not Su III (distal to flange) · ~**15% revision** (nonunion)

**rIMN malunion** — femoral component forces **posterior start point** → ✓ **extension (recurvatum) deformity** (OB) · **valgus + recurvatum** (TO) · distal **10° bend** nails improve alignment · hyperextension tolerated, valgus poorly tolerated (TO)

**Locked plate** — polyaxial screws around lugs/stems · independent of design + distal bone · **MIPO/submuscular** · not non-locked condylar buttress (no varus resistance; nonunion up to **50%**) · blade plate/DCS hard around PS

| Plate vs rIMN | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Union / nonunion** | ✓ **Equivalent** · similar secondary surgery |
| **Malunion** | **↑ with IMN** (plating ↓ malunion) · ✓ **IMN higher malunion** than ORIF or DFR |
| **Weight bearing** | **IMN faster** to full WB |
| **Soft tissue** | IMN less injury |

**Nonunion risk** — ✓ **extensile lateral approach > submuscular** · MIPO ↑ malunion (Orthobullets)\
✗ Wrong options: no BMP (not approved) · no lag/cerclage · plate vs antegrade nail · titanium vs steel

**Commonest reason ORIF fails** — ✓ **nonunion (persistent fracture line)** (~10% revised) → revision ORIF or DFR\
✗ Wrong options: aseptic loosening · PE wear · infection · dislocation

**Nail + lateral plate vs dual plating** — ✓ **earlier weight bearing** · dual plating stiffer (axial + torsion) + more medial dissection · infection same

![Posterior start point — extension deformity](../../MCQ/attachments/q218932-illc-illustration-c.jpg)

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| Far-distal fracture, well-fixed CR, community ambulator | ✓ **distal femoral locking plate** (✗ Wrong options: cast · buttress plate · DFR · antegrade nail) |
| PS TKA, displaced fracture just above, osteopenic, well fixed | ✓ **MIPO locked plating** (✗ Wrong options: cast/brace · rIMN (PS) · DFR in 62 yo) |
| **Su I, CR knee, well fixed** | ✓ **retrograde IMN** (✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · recon plate · stem revision · antegrade) |
| **Stemmed revision femur, well fixed** | ✓ **lateral locked plate** (✗ Wrong options: rIMN/antegrade (stem blocks)) |
| **PS box, 5 days post-op, well fixed** | ✓ **PS design → lateral locking plate** |
| Bridge plating vs rIMN | ✓ **similar nonunion, ↓ malunion** |

### Loose components or poor bone – revision / distal femoral replacement

**Loose femoral component** → ✓ **revision TKA** (long stem or DFR) · loose + medial condyle fracture → **ORIF + femoral revision**\
✗ Wrong options: nail · plate · cast · ex-fix

**DFR indications** — **elderly / sedentary** · **loose or malpositioned** component · **poor bone stock** · very distal / comminuted (Su III) · requires **rotating hinge** (collaterals lost) · tibia often revised too

**DFR vs ORIF** — ✓ **similar complication rate** · similar reoperation, function, return to ambulatory status · **immediate weight bearing** · shorter surgery · ROM similar or **slightly less** with DFR\
✗ Wrong options: better outcomes · higher reoperation · greater ROM

**84 yo walker user, very distal comminuted** → ✓ **DFR** (best chance ambulatory at 1 yr)\
✗ Wrong options: plate · dual plate · nail · ex-fix (seeding risk)

**Distal to flange, poor bone, medial condyle free** → ✓ **revision arthroplasty only (DFR)**

![Distal femoral replacement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5027-distal-femoral-replacement.jpg)

### Tibial periprosthetic fracture

**Incidence** — **0.4–1.7%** · risks: prior **TTO**, **loosening**, malposition, **long-stemmed** tibial components

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Non-displaced, stable | Cast/brace (non-WB) |
| Unstable fracture, stable component | **ORIF** / IMN |
| ✓ **Displaced + loose tibial component** (Felix IIB) | **Revision with long stem bypassing fracture** (TO) (✗ Wrong options: locking plate, LCP, LISS, ex-fix, IMN, brace) |
| Loose + poor bone, prior pain | ✓ **ORIF + revision TKA** (✗ Wrong options: cast · strut graft · IMN · proximal tibial replacement (salvage)) |

### Patella fractures

**Incidence** — resurfaced **0.2–21%** (Orthobullets) · **0.68–5.2%** (OB) · unresurfaced **0.05%**

**Risks** — patellar ON · asymmetric / over-resection · central **single peg** · **uncemented** · **metal backing** · **inset** component · lateral release · ↑ BMI

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| Displaced, **no SLR**, well-fixed component, good bone | ✓ **ORIF** (✗ Wrong options: patellectomy · allograft · component revision · brace) |
| Transverse, **active extension intact**, well fixed | ✓ **long-leg cast / brace in extension** (✗ Wrong options: immediate ROM · component removal · ORIF) |
| Displaced mid-patella, no SLR | ✓ **ORIF** (✗ Wrong options: hinged ROM · 2 wk immobilise · 6 wk cast (lag) · partial patellectomy (poles/comminution)) |
| Type II at 10 wk, stable button, good bone | ✓ **ORIF** (± mesh augmentation) |
| Intact EM, stable component | ✓ **knee brace locked in extension / cylinder cast** |
| Non-displaced transverse, SLR intact | ✓ **immobilise in extension 4–6 wk** |

**Type II/III outcomes** — **50% complications**, **20–40% reoperation** · **total patellectomy** = salvage, **>50% loss of extension strength**

### Extensor mechanism failure

**Chronic EM disruption after revision (6 mo, wide pole diastasis)** → ✓ **extensor mechanism allograft**\
✗ Wrong options: fusion (salvage) · patellectomy + repair · ORIF · cast

## X vs Y

| | **CR femoral component** | **PS open box** | **PS closed box / stemmed** |
|---|---|---|---|
| rIMN possible | Yes (notch recess) | Yes (8–12 mm) | No → **plate** |

| | **Lewis–Rorabeck** | **Su** |
|---|---|---|
| Based on | Displacement + **component stability** | **Fracture location** vs component |

## How it's asked

**"Complication unique to navigated TKR?"** → **femoral shaft fracture** (TO)

**"Not a risk factor for periprosthetic fracture?"** → **anterior femoral notching** (TO)

**"Risk factors for supracondylar fracture — EXCEPT?"** → **male gender**

**"True about intra-op fractures?"** → **medial femoral condyle most common**

**"Robotic TKA, femur fracture at 3 wk — greatest risk?"** → **transcortical tracking pins**

**"Most common intra-op fracture?"** → **medial femoral condyle**

**"Malunion after rIMN for Su II?"** → **valgus and recurvatum** (TO)

**"Far-distal fracture, well-fixed implants?"** → **distal femoral locking plate**

**"Nonunion after locked plating — risk?"** → **extensile lateral approach (vs submuscular)**

**"PS TKA, displaced fracture, well fixed?"** → **minimally invasive plate osteosynthesis**

**"Bridge plating vs rIMN?"** → **similar nonunion, decreased malunion**

**"Su I, CR, well fixed?"** → **retrograde IMN**

**"Stemmed femur, well fixed?"** → **ORIF with lateral plate**

**"Design + treatment 5 days post-op?"** → **PS; ORIF with lateral locking plate**

**"Nail-plate vs dual plating?"** → **earlier weight bearing**

**"Locked plate ORIF failure at 1 yr — commonest finding?"** → **persistent fracture line (nonunion)**

**"CR knee, considering rIMN?"** → **higher potential for extension deformity**

**"Revised stemmed femur, well fixed, adequate bone?"** → **lateral locked plating**

**"IMN vs ORIF vs DFR — difference?"** → **IMN higher malunion**

**"Malalignment after rIMN?"** → **extension deformity**

**"Plate vs IMN?"** → **union rate equivalent**

**"Loose femoral component after fall?"** → **revision TKA**

**"Loose femoral component + medial condyle fracture?"** → **ORIF + femoral component revision**

**"Elderly, loose component, poor bone?"** → **revision with distal femoral replacement**

**"84 yo walker user, very distal fracture — best ambulation at 1 yr?"** → **conversion to DFR**

**"Tibial fracture, prior activity pain, loose implant?"** → **ORIF with revision TKA**

**"Fracture distal to flange, poor bone?"** → **revision arthroplasty only**

**"DFR vs ORIF?"** → **similar complication rate**

**"Felix 2B tibial fracture?"** → **revision with long stem** (TO)

**"Tibial fracture at stem with loose component?"** → **long-stem tibial revision bypassing fracture**

**"Patella fracture, no SLR, well-fixed button?"** → **ORIF**

**"Transverse patella fracture, active extension intact?"** → **long-leg cast**

**"Displaced patella fracture, no SLR, 1 yr post-op?"** → **ORIF**

**"Type II patellar fracture, stable button, good bone?"** → **ORIF**

**"Intact EM, stable button — initial management?"** → **brace locked in extension or cylinder cast**

**"Non-displaced transverse patella, SLR intact?"** → **immobilise in extension 4–6 wk**

**"Chronic EM disruption after revision?"** → **extensor mechanism allograft**

**"Su classification used for?"** → **periprosthetic femur fracture** (TO)

# TKA Peroneal Nerve Palsy

*Recon · 53 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **0.3–2%** (Orthobullets) · **0.3–1.3%** (OB) · **0.3–9.5%** after valgus TKR (TO)

**Prognosis** — **≥50%** improve/recover without further treatment

### Risk factors (valgus & flexion contracture)

| ✓ Risk factor | Note |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Valgus + flexion contracture** | **Highest risk** (✗ Wrong options: valgus only · varus ± FFD · FFD only) |
| **Valgus >12°** · **correction of 20° valgus** | Nerve tethered at fibular head → stretch |
| **FFD >10–15°** | Stretch |
| **Tourniquet >120 min** | — |
| **Post-op epidural analgesia** | Unprotected limb → compression |
| **Aberrant retractor** | — |
| **Pre-op neuropathy** (central or peripheral) · lumbar laminectomy (double crush) | — |
| ✗ Wrong option: **excessive medial release** | **Not** a risk factor |

**Lateral OA + FFD >15° + valgus >12° — counsel for** → ✓ **CPN injury** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: DVT · instability · quadriceps contracture

**Large valgus + FFD — higher risk than typical for** → ✓ **peroneal nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: DVT · wound · loosening · quad rupture

### Anatomy & release risk

**At tibial cut level** — CPN lies ✓ **superficial to (on) lateral head of gastrocnemius** (protected by it) · in interval between **biceps femoris and lateral gastrocnemius**\
✗ Wrong options: deep to arcuate ligament · on popliteus · on bone

**Distance from posterolateral tibia** — **9–15 mm** (Orthobullets) · mean **14.9 mm**, never <**9 mm**, ↑ in larger legs (OB MRI) · as close as **7–9 mm in extension** (cadaver)

**Greatest risk** — ✓ **release of posterolateral capsule with knee extended**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior capsule · PLC in flexion · ITB (anterior to nerve)

**Danger zone (TO)** — PLC release in triangle of **popliteus, tibial cut surface, posterior ITB fibres** · ITB pie-crusting = **safe zone** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Signs** — no ankle dorsiflexion or toe extension · ↓ sensation **dorsum of foot + first web space**

## 5 Management

```
Foot drop after TKA
├─ Recovery room → remove dressings + flex knee 20–30°
├─ Complete foot drop → AFO + PT (keep passive DF)
├─ No recovery at 3 mo → EMG; consider decompression / exploration (3–6 mo)
└─ Persistent palsy, tibialis posterior 5/5 → tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum
```

### Immediate management

✓ **Unwrap compressive dressings + flex knee**\
✗ Wrong options: AFO acutely · immediate EMG · exploration · reassurance

### Late management

**EMG** — after **3 months** if no improvement

**Exploration / neurolysis** — no recovery at **3–6 months** · explore at posterior border of biceps femoris

### Tendon transfer for persistent palsy

**Failed AFO + neurolysis, 18 mo, passive DF intact, inversion 5/5** → ✓ **tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum** (through interosseous membrane → **lateral cuneiform**)\
✗ Wrong options: continue AFO · repeat neurolysis · peroneus tertius ± TAL (same anterior compartment, paralysed)

### Valgus TKR technique (TO)

**Tibial cut** — referenced **6–8 mm off medial compartment** after removing lateral osteophytes (TO)

**Femur** — IM entry **more medial** · distal valgus cut **3°** (not 5–7°) · lateral distal resection minimal (1–2 mm) or none · medial ≤10 mm (usually 7–8) (TO)

**Rotation** — hypoplastic LFC → use **~6° ER** or anterior referencing · Whiteside line unreliable (trochlear dysplasia) → **TEA** or parallel to tibial cut (TO)

**Lateral release orders (TO)**

| Author | Sequence |
|---|---|
| **Ranawat** | **PCL** → PLC (at tibial cut, cautery) → ITB + LCL pie-crust · popliteus preserved |
| **Favorito** | **LCL** (tightest) → popliteus → PLC → ITB → lateral gastrocnemius |
| **Whiteside** | Tight in **flexion → LCL, popliteus** (near epicondyle) · tight in **extension → ITB, PLC** · LHG only for FFD |

## X vs Y

| | **ITB release** | **PLC release (extension)** |
|---|---|---|
| Nerve risk | **Safe zone** (ITB anterior to nerve) | ✓ **Danger zone** |

## How it's asked

**"Lateral OA, FFD >15°, valgus >12° — counsel for?"** → **CPN injury** (TO)

**"Deformity with most risk of peroneal palsy?"** → **valgus + flexion contracture**

**"Peroneal palsy risk factors — EXCEPT?"** → **excessive medial release**

**"Large valgus + FFD — higher risk of?"** → **peroneal nerve injury**

**"Recovery room, cannot dorsiflex?"** → **unwrap dressings and flex the knee**

**"Where is CPN at tibial cut level?"** → **superficial to lateral head of gastrocnemius**

**"Lateral tight in flexion + extension — greatest nerve risk during?"** → **posterolateral capsule release with knee extended**

**"Persistent foot drop at 18 mo, inversion 5/5?"** → **tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum of foot**

# TKA Polyethylene Wear & Manufacturing

*Recon · 54 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — macroscopic premature PE failure from excess loading + mechanical loosening

**Catastrophic failure** — typical of **TKA** (vs **osteolytic** failure in THA)

**Primary variables** — **PE thickness** · **articular geometry** · **kinematics** · **sterilisation** · **manufacturing** · **surgical technique** (tight flexion gap ↑ sliding wear; tight PCL + anterior tibial slope ↑ stress)

**Treatment** — revision TKA or isolated **PE exchange** if implants stable · X-ray: narrowing of tibiofemoral implant interface

### Polyethylene thickness

**Rule** — keep thinnest PE **>8 mm** · <8 mm → loads exceed PE yield strength (**12–20 MPa**) → catastrophic failure (older PE data)

**Labelled vs true** — "8 mm" insert may be **4–5 mm** PE on ~**3 mm** tray · deeper tibial cut avoids thin insert · young + active + thin PE = ↑ risk

### Articular surface design and geometry

| | **Congruent ("dished")** | **Flat** |
|---|---|---|
| Rollback | Sacrificed | ↑ rollback, ↑ flexion |
| Anatomy | Less anatomic | More anatomic, PCL sparing |
| Contact area | **↑** | ↓ |
| Contact stress | **↓** | **↑** → catastrophic wear |

**Best** — **biplanar congruency** (coronal + sagittal) · maximise contact area, minimise contact stress

### Insert conformity & stability

**Anterior-stabilised (AS) vs CR** — ✓ **↑ contact area + ↑ stability** in PCL-deficient knee · highly conforming + **large anterior lip** blocks anterior femoral translation · no box cut

### Kinematics

**Sliding wear** — **ACL sacrificed + PCL retained** · worst in **CR + flat insert** · least in **PS or AS + congruent insert**

**Varus alignment** → catastrophic failure · **excess rollback** → dyskinetic sliding → cracking

**Solutions** — correct varus · congruent design · ↑ posterior slope · **PS** for incompetent PCL · posterior contact point

## 2 Mechanism

### Sterilisation – gamma in air & oxidation

**Gamma radiation** — commonest sterilisation · breaks C–H bonds → **free radicals**

| Environment | Pathway | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **Air (O₂ rich)** | **Oxidation** (chain scission) | ✓ **Early failure**: **subsurface delamination**, **pitting**, **cracking**, fatigue · ↑ wear + **osteolysis** · abandoned |
| **Inert (argon, nitrogen, vacuum)** | **Cross-linking** | ↑ resistance to **adhesive + abrasive wear** · ↓ ductility + fatigue resistance · ✓ **↓ pre-implantation oxidation** |
| **Ethylene oxide / gas plasma / H₂O₂** | No radicals, **no cross-linking** | No oxidation, but no wear benefit |

**Oxidised PE mechanics** — ✓ **↑ elastic modulus + ↓ strength** (and ↓ ductility) → brittle

**Gamma in nitrogen produces** — ✓ **free radicals**\
✗ Wrong options: annealing · gas plasma · uncross-linking · remelting

**Shelf life** — gamma-in-air packaged PE stored **5 yr** → ✓ **substantially oxidised** (still sterile, no creep) · oxidation ∝ time exposed to O₂ · (✗ Wrong options: OR FiO₂ matters (no radiation))

**Doses** — sterilisation **2.5–4 Mrad** (conventional 2.5 in air) · HXLPE **5–10 Mrad** (5–15 Mrad) in inert environment, separate earlier step · little benefit **>10 Mrad**

![Gamma irradiation in air — oxidation](../../MCQ/attachments/q226-illa-gamma-in-air.jpg)

### Cross-linking & thermal processing (annealing vs remelting)

**Most improvement in wear** — ✓ **gamma irradiation in argon (inert)** · ✓ **radiation** has most positive effect on wear resistance\
✗ Wrong options: ram bar · gas plasma · EtO · air

**↑ Irradiation dose** — ✓ **↑ volumetric wear resistance** · ↓ tensile strength, fracture toughness, crack resistance · ✓ principal negative: **↓ fatigue strength**\
✗ Wrong options: yellow · unstable · absorbs fluid

**Radiation + heating** — ✓ **↓ wear but ↑ fracture risk** (↓ ductility) · forms interpenetrating network · fewer, smaller particles vs smooth counterface · **worse vs rough counterface** (↓ abrasive resistance)

**Free radicals removed by** — ✓ **thermal processing** (or **vitamin E**; ✗ Wrong options: vitamin C)

| | **Remelting** | **Annealing** |
|---|---|---|
| Temperature | **Above** melting point | **Below** melting point |
| Free radicals | ✓ **Most removed** (largest reduction) | **More remain** → oxidation risk |
| Crystallinity | **Partial crystalline → amorphous** | Preserved |
| Mechanical properties | **↓** | ✓ **Better maintained** |
| Wear | **Equivalent** | Equivalent |
| In vivo oxidation | Seen in both | Seen in both |

**Annealed vs remelted XPE** → ✓ **more free radicals, better mechanics, equivalent wear**

**Preferred** — annealing (better mechanics) (OB8309) · remelting most effective radical removal

**HXLPE vs conventional (THA RCT)** — head penetration **0.003 vs 0.051 mm/yr** · ✓ **superior wear** · ↓ osteolysis · larger head: volumetric wear equal or slightly higher

### Manufacturing – compression moulding

| Method | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Ram bar extrusion + machining** | Powder pushed through heated barrel → rods → machined · variable quality |
| **Calcium stearate additive** | **Fusion defects** · ↓ mechanics |
| **Sheet compression moulding** | Sheets up to 8" → machined |
| ✓ **Direct compression moulding (net shape)** | Powder → final mould · **no machining**, high gloss · **lowest fatigue crack formation + propagation** · wear **~50%** of machined (Orthobullets/Miller) · wear **equal** to ram extrusion (Comprehensive Ortho Review) — read the options · slow, costly |

**Machining** — shear stretches chains **1–2 mm** below surface (amorphous > crystalline) → radiation-susceptible → **white band of subsurface oxidation** → delamination + fatigue cracks

**Crystallinity** — ideal **~50%** · **>70%** ↑ failure

**"Perfect storm" for catastrophic wear** — metal-backed tray + **thin PE** · **flat coronal** design · **CR + flat insert** · **ram bar + calcium stearate** · **gamma in air** · **machined surface**

![Subsurface white band of oxidation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5002-subsurface-line-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Oxidised PE** | **Cross-linked PE** |
|---|---|---|
| Made by | Gamma **in air** | Gamma in **inert** gas / vacuum |
| Wear | ↑ delamination, pitting | ↓ adhesive + abrasive |
| Mechanics | ↑ modulus, ↓ strength | ↓ ductility, ↓ fatigue strength |

## How it's asked

**"Catastrophic delamination at 3 yr — cause?"** → **gamma irradiation in air**

**"Gamma in air vs inert — result?"** → **accelerated wear and failure in air**

**"Gamma in nitrogen results in?"** → **free radicals**

**"Gamma causes rapid wear when combined with?"** → **irradiation in air**

**"Gamma in air degrades wear because of?"** → **oxidation**

**"Properties after PE oxidation?"** → **↑ elastic modulus, ↓ strength**

**"Greatest PE oxidation?"** → **gamma radiation in air**

**"Gamma-in-air cup stored 5 yr — why not implant?"** → **may be substantially oxidised**

**"Processing step → ↑ wear + osteolysis?"** → **gamma irradiation in air**

**"Inert vs air sterilisation — true?"** → **decreased pre-implantation oxidation**

**"Irradiation in O₂-rich environment — effect?"** → **early failure**

**"MOST improvement in wear?"** → **gamma irradiation in argon**

**"Radiation then heating — effect?"** → **lower wear but ↑ fracture risk**

**"Annealed vs remelted XPE?"** → **more free radicals, better mechanics, equivalent wear**

**"↑ Irradiation dose improves?"** → **volumetric wear**

**"Principal negative of high cross-linking?"** → **loss of fatigue strength**

**"Most positive effect on wear resistance?"** → **radiation**

**"Partial crystalline → amorphous?"** → **remelting**

**"Free radicals removed by?"** → **thermal processing**

**"Largest reduction of free radicals?"** → **remelting**

**"Annealed HXLPE in THA — counsel?"** → **superior wear to conventional PE**

**"Annealed vs remelted — difference?"** → **annealing changes mechanical properties less**

**"Lowest fatigue crack susceptibility?"** → **direct compression moulding**

**"Anterior-stabilised vs CR?"** → **increased contact area, increased stability**

# TKA Postoperative Inpatient Management

*Recon · 55 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Team** — surgeon, PT, OT, case manager, nursing, patient + family

## 5 Management

### Pain management

| Phase | Measure |
|---|---|
| **Pre-op** | NSAIDs + opioids immediately before surgery ↓ post-op pain |
| **Intra-op** | **Regional (spinal/epidural) preferred** over GA · **peripheral nerve blocks** · **periarticular multimodal injection** (low risk) |
| **Post-op** | **Multimodal oral therapy = gold standard** |

| Drug class | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Opioids** | **Mu agonist** → neuronal hyperpolarisation, ↓ excitability |
| **NSAIDs** | Inhibit **COX-1 + COX-2** → ↓ PGs, TXA, AA mediators |
| **Selective COX-2** | Block AA → PG precursors · ↓ GI effects · may impair bone healing |
| **Gabapentin / pregabalin** | ↓ hyperexcitability of voltage-dependent **Ca²⁺ channels** · pregabalin better oral bioavailability |
| **SNRIs** | Inhibit serotonin + noradrenaline reuptake (CNS) |

![Multimodal analgesia — pathway targets](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422740-multimodal-therapy-defined-as-altering-the-pain-pathway-in-v.jpg)

### Regional anaesthesia – adductor canal block

**ACB vs femoral nerve block** — ✓ **ACB → earlier ambulation** · **equivalent analgesia** · ACB = **sensory** block (saphenous n. + nerve to vastus medialis, distal to quad motor branches) · FNB → **quad weakness, falls, delayed ambulation**\
✗ Wrong options: FNB better pain control · ACB blocks quads · longer spinal → earlier ambulation · CPM → early ambulation

### Tourniquet use

**Tourniquet-less TKA** — ✓ **↑ intra-op blood loss** (consistent) · no difference in 1-yr flexion, operative time, DVT · thigh pain with tourniquet · ~**90%** of surgeons use one

### Physical therapy & ROM

**Start therapy on day of surgery** — ✓ **↓ length of stay** (AAOS **strong**) · ↓ pain, ↑ function vs POD1 (moderate)

| Activity | Flexion needed |
|---|---|
| **Swing phase of gait** | **65°** |
| **ADLs** | **90°** |
| **Stairs** | **95°** |
| **Rising from chair** | **105°** |

### Continuous passive motion

![CPM — no long-term benefit](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422740-use-of-a-cpm-has-not-shown-to-have-any-long-term-benefit-fol.jpg)

**CPM** — ↑ **early** flexion only (2 wk) · ✓ **no difference in outcomes at 1 yr** · ✓ **no long-term ROM difference** · (✗ Wrong options: ↓ contracture · ↓ MUA · ↑ analgesic need · ↓ VTE (insufficient evidence) · ↑ passive flexion at 6 mo · ↓ SSI) · some studies ↓ LOS · Salter 1970

### AAOS guidelines (early rehab, drains)

| Recommendation (AAOS knee OA surgical CPG) | Strength |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Rehab on day of TKA ↓ LOS** | **Strong** |
| **CPM does not improve outcomes** | **Strong** (against CPM) |
| **No surgical navigation** (no outcome/complication difference) | **Strong** |
| **No difference with/without tourniquet** | **Strong** |
| **No difference patellar resurfacing vs not** | **Strong** |
| ✓ **Drains — no difference in complications/outcomes** (↑ cost, ↑ LOS) | **Strong** (OB213939) · **Moderate** against (2022 CPG, OB219801) — read the options |
| **Cryotherapy devices do not improve outcomes** | **Moderate** |
| **Pre-op PT improves pain/function** | **Limited** |
| **Tourniquet ↓ short-term function** | **Limited** |
| **Robotic vs conventional — no difference** | **Limited** |
| **Simultaneous bilateral TKA** — avoid in high cardiopulmonary risk | **Consensus** |
| **PCA** | No recommendation |

### Post-operative anaemia

**Tourniquet** ↓ intra-op blood loss · **earlier discharge to rehab** → better outcomes

### Outpatient TKR pathway (TO)

**Components (TO)** — **TXA** · periarticular injection (liposomal/plain bupivacaine ± ketorolac, morphine, dexamethasone) · **ACB + iPACK** (infiltration between popliteal artery and capsule) → best early function, ↓ LOS vs FNB · **PT at 2 h**, **WB at 6 h** · minimally invasive · ✓ **no tourniquet** (TO)

**NOT a component (TO)** — ✓ **tourniquet**

**Discharge criteria (TO)** — walk safely with 2 crutches, stairs, home exercises, self-care, **≥90° flexion** · no brace

**Patient selection (TO)** — **age <75**, **BMI <35**, no anaemia, COPD, CHF, cirrhosis, VTE, spinal stenosis, BPH, chronic narcotics · primary TKA, first/second case · RAPT **>10**, near hospital, private insurance

### Drain management

**Drains** — no difference in complications or outcomes · ↑ cost

### Discharge

| Destination | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **Home** | Medically stable · **80–90° active flexion** · **ambulate 75–100 ft** · stairs |
| **Rehab / SNF** | Earlier discharge → better outcomes |

## X vs Y

| | **Adductor canal block** | **Femoral nerve block** |
|---|---|---|
| Nerves | Saphenous + nerve to VM | Femoral (motor + sensory) |
| Quads | Spared | **Weak** → falls |
| Ambulation | **Earlier** | Delayed |
| Analgesia | Equivalent | Equivalent |

## How it's asked

**"CPM after TKA — true?"** → **no difference in clinical outcomes at 1 yr**

**"CPM in uncomplicated TKA — true?"** → **no long-term difference in ROM**

**"Highest-strength AAOS recommendation?"** → **rehab on day of TKA reduces LOS**

**"Patient requests — strong evidence against?"** → **drain placement**

**"Moderate AAOS recommendation against?"** → **intra-articular closed suction drain**

**"ACB vs FNB — accurate?"** → **ACB gives earlier ambulation**

**"Tourniquet-less TKA — true?"** → **increased intra-op blood loss**

**"Not a component of outpatient TKR?"** → **tourniquet** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Postoperative Rehabilitation & Outpatient Management

*Recon · 56 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Phases** — **inpatient extended care** (rehab/SNF) · **outpatient care** · earlier discharge to rehab → better outcomes · home discharge criteria as in hospital

## 5 Management

### Rehabilitation (cryotherapy, home PT)

**Outpatient PT** — **2–3×/week for ≥2 weeks** · ✓ **closed-chain concentric** exercises · crutches → cane → unassisted

**Self-directed home PT vs formal outpatient PT** — ✓ **similar clinical outcomes** (ROM, MUA rates) + **cost savings** in selected (motivated, equipped) patients · up to **⅓** need formal PT later\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ cost · ↓ ROM · 50% need OPPT · OPPT saves money

| Modality | Point |
|---|---|
| **Cryotherapy** | Local temperature ∝ synovial **PGE2** · ✓ **better ROM at discharge** · ↓ blood loss but not transfusion · (✗ Wrong options: pain, swelling, analgesia · long-term ROM) · AAOS: moderate evidence no outcome benefit |
| **Aquatic** | Buoyancy ↓ compressive load + gives resistance |
| **Balance training** | Proprioception, postural control |
| **NMES** | Overrides CNS-related activation deficits |

### Opioid use risk factors

**↑ post-op opioid use** — ✓ **cigarette smoking** · **pre-op opioid** (chronic use) or **benzodiazepine** use · **↑ BMI** · psychiatric comorbidity · **younger age (<55)** ·\
✗ Wrong options: valgus alignment · robotic TKA may ↓ need

### Return to sport

**Preferred** — **low-impact closed chain**: elliptical, **biking**, **golf** · **running discouraged** (impact ↓ implant longevity)

**Golf after TKA** — ✓ **handicap change <5 strokes** · play frequency usually ↑ · drive length **↓ ~12 yd after TKA** · **↑ ~3 yd after THA** · lead (opposite-handed) knee stressed\
✗ Wrong options: unable to return · fewer rounds · >20 yd longer

### Driving

| Side | Return |
|---|---|
| **Right TKA** | **4 weeks** |
| **Left TKA** | **<4 weeks** |

## How it's asked

**"Cryotherapy after TKR — demonstrated effect?"** → **improved ROM at discharge**

**"Self-directed home PT vs outpatient PT?"** → **similar clinical outcomes**

**"Golf after TKA — true?"** → **handicap change <5 strokes**

**"Variable linked to ↑ post-op opioid use?"** → **cigarette smoking**
