# TKA Prosthesis Design

*Recon · 57 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### History

| Era | Milestone |
|---|---|
| **19th c** | Soft-tissue interposition |
| **1950s** | **Walldius** — first **hinged** knee |
| **1958** | **MacIntosh & McKeever** — acrylic tibial plateau |
| **1960s** | **Gunston** — first cemented surface arthroplasty |
| **1970** | **GUEPAR** hinge (↑ motion, ↓ bone loss) · first-gen hinges → **high loosening from over-constraint** |
| **~1973** | **Total condylar** — first to resurface all 3 compartments · PCL sacrificing, no cam-post → flexion ~**90°** |
| Later | **Duo-patellar (kinematic) = CR** · **Insall–Burstein PS** · LCS rotating platform · medial pivot · ultracongruent (TO) |

### Concepts in prosthetic design

**Femoral rollback** — ✓ **posterior translation of the femur with flexion** → ↑ quad lever arm + flexion by **preventing posterior impingement** in deep flexion (TO) · native: controlled by **ACL + PCL** · CR: native PCL · PS: **tibial post contacts femoral cam**

**Constraint** — V/V + F/E stability despite **ligament laxity or bone loss** · more constraint → more force to fixation → **aseptic loosening**

| Least → most constrained |
|---|
| **Bicruciate-retaining** (keeps ACL; ↑ op time + complications) → **CR** → **PS** → **VVC / CCK (non-hinged)** → **rotating hinge** |

**Modularity** — metal tray + modular insert, augments, stems · customise intra-op · but **↑ osteolysis** + **backside wear** (micromotion insert–tray) · more expensive

## 2 Mechanism

### Kinematics (medial pivot, paradoxical motion)

**Native knee** — ✓ **femur pivots about a medial axis** in flexion (lateral condyle rolls back more: larger radius / convex lateral plateau) · flexion → **femoral ER (tibial IR)** + rollback · terminal extension → **femur IR, lateral condyle translates anteriorly** (screw-home = tibial ER last 15°) · lateral condyle posterior **12.7 mm** vs medial **2.9 mm** (TO)

**Paradoxical translation** — ✓ **anterior translation of tibiofemoral contact point from extension → flexion** · commonest in **CR** (no ACL, posterior resting point; PCL not tight until ~**45°**) · also PS before cam engages · CR shows it in first **~40°**

**Medial-pivot / medial-stabilised / medial congruent (MC)** — ✓ **ball-and-socket medial** (**spherical single-radius MFC**), less congruent lateral → lateral rollback · asymmetric liner · raised anterior lip · higher PROMs in some studies

**NOT true of medial pivot (TO)** — ✓ **"spherical variable radius MFC"** (it is **single** radius)

**Flexion → extension in medial-congruent design** → ✓ **medial pivot, femur internally rotates + translates anteriorly**

**Insert truths** — ✓ **standard CR can show paradoxical anterior femoral translation** · AS lip prevents femur rolling **forward** when PCL weak (✗ Wrong options: replaces ACL) · **dual-pivot** = lateral pivot early, **medial pivot deep** · mobile bearing (✗ Wrong options: medial pivot) has insert–tray motion · PS **promotes** rollback (✗ Wrong options: minimises)

![Paradoxical anterior translation](../../MCQ/attachments/q216390-illa-paradoxical.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**CR vs PS** — ✓ **PS shows box / cam outline** on lateral X-ray · CR has none (notch recess) (TO: A is CR, B is PS)

![CR (A) vs PS (B) — box visible in PS](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17105562-1.png)

## 5 Management

### Cruciate-retaining vs posterior-stabilised

| | **CR** | **PS (cruciate-substituting)** |
|---|---|---|
| Needs | **Intact functional PCL** | PCL **sacrificed** → flexion gap ↑ |
| Indications | Minimal bone loss/laxity · **varus <10°**, **valgus <15°** | **Patellectomy** · **inflammatory arthritis** (late PCL rupture) · deficient PCL · severe deformity (allows **lateralisation** of components → better tracking) (TO) |
| X-ray | **No box** | **Box / cam** |
| Advantages | No post impingement/dislocation · less distal femur cut · **proprioception** · closer kinematics (debated) | Easier balance without PCL · arguably more ROM · easier exposure · ✓ **tolerates joint-line change better** |
| Disadvantages | Tight PCL → **accelerated PE wear** · loose PCL → **flexion instability** · ↑ PF contact pressure · **does not tolerate joint-line alteration** (TO) | **Cam jump** · **tibial post wear** · ✓ **patellar clunk** · **more bone resected** · ↑ **intra-op fracture** (box) |
| Survivorship / flexion | **Equal** | **Equal** |
| Cementless | Available | Available |

**Low-volume surgeons (<20 TKA/yr)** — use PS (CR balancing demanding) (TO)

**PCL resection simulates** → ✓ **excessive posterior femoral resection** (↑ flexion gap)\
✗ Wrong options: distal resection (loose extension) · augments/oversize (tight flexion)

**CR — recreate PCL tension EXCEPT** → ✓ **increasing posterior tibial slope** (loosens flexion, under-tensions PCL) · do: match AP femoral size, conservative distal cut + soft-tissue release, anatomic rotation (femoral IR / tibial ER alter tension most), resect only what is replaced

**PCL injured intra-op, gaps balanced → CR with conforming (UC/AS) insert** → ✓ **no difference in functional outcomes vs PS**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion instability · paradoxical translation (only with standard insert) · clunk · PJI

**Patellectomy** → ✓ **PS** (AP instability with weak extensor) · gold standard, though newer CR data similar\
✗ Wrong options: UKA · CR · constrained

**Best CR candidate** → ✓ **OA with 10° valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: RA · patellectomy · bicruciate rupture · steroid-treated SLE

**PS vs CR — true** → ✓ **higher patellar clunk**\
✗ Wrong options: less bone · better survivorship · fewer fractures · cementless only

**Flexed femoral component + excess tibial slope (PS)** → ✓ **excessive tibial post wear** (anterior post impingement → osteolysis)\
✗ Wrong options: recurvatum · coronal instability · clunk · patellar instability

![PS knee — cam and box on lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5019-ps-knee-radioraph.jpg)

### PS complications

| Complication | Mechanism | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Cam jump** | **Loose flexion gap** or hyperextension | Closed reduction (**anterior drawer at 90°**) → revise flexion gap |
| **Post wear** | Femoral flexion, slope, hyperextension | Avoid excess distal resection, flexion, slope |
| **Patellar clunk** | Scar nodule (posterior distal quad tendon / superior pole) catches in box ~**30–45°** extension · small femur, flexed femur, undersized button · within 12 mo | Observe or **arthroscopic/open excision** · longer trochlear groove designs ↓ |

### Constraint selection (VVC, hinge)

| Design | Mechanism | Indications |
|---|---|---|
| **CCK / VVC (constrained non-hinged)** | **Large tall tibial post in deep femoral box**, no axle · V/V + rotational stability (partial) · functions as PS otherwise | **MCL or LCL attenuation/deficiency** · **V/V laxity >5 mm** · **flexion gap laxity** · **moderate bone loss in neuropathic** knee · **severe deformity (>20°) with collateral incompetence** · irreparable intra-op MCL · post-polio (some) · stems advised |
| **Rotating hinge** | **Linked** components (axle) · tibial bearing rotates on yoke (↓ constraint) · **extension stop** | ✓ **Global ligament deficiency** · **hyperextension instability / recurvatum (>5°)** (polio, tumour) · tumour resection · **massive bone loss in neuropathic joint** · **gross flexion–extension mismatch (>15 mm)** despite releases/upsizing · chronic MCL loss in **elderly low-demand** · periprosthetic fracture / DFR |

**CCK cannot control hyperextension** → ✓ **only hinge for hyperextension deformity** (TO) · hinge **can** be used in primary TKR (TO) · CCK is not "deep dish + small post"

**CCK identification** — picture of large post = **CCK** (TO) · CCK = "semi-hinge", low-budget back-up for on-table collateral transection (TO) · TO also states on-table **MCL transection → hinge ideal** vs OB: repair + CR/PS + brace or CCK — read the options

**Both constrained** — ↑ femoral bone resection · ✓ **aseptic loosening** from constraint (first-gen **GUEPAR hinge** → high loosening)

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| **Charcot valgus**, still medial lax after ITB/popliteus/LCL release + thicker poly | ✓ **convert to constrained TKA** (✗ Wrong options: lateral retinacular release ± MPFL · MCL imbrication · valgus recut) |
| Diabetic **Charcot knee** — initial implant | ✓ **hinge / constrained** (✗ Wrong options: CR · PS · fusion · UKA) |
| Charcot, PS used → instability, maltracking, AP laxity | ✓ **should have used hinged TKA** |
| Charcot valgus with bone loss — counsel | ✓ **↑ constraint needed for global instability** (↑ loosening risk; cones + long stems) |
| **84 yo, grossly attenuated MCL** (valgus) | ✓ **VVC** (96% at 10 yr) (✗ Wrong options: MCL repair/scaffold + PS · LCL release · lateral UKA) |
| Obese, **MCL-deficient** revision | ✓ **constraint resisting varus–valgus loads** |
| **Absolute indication for non-hinged constrained** | ✓ **varus >20° with LCL incompetence** (✗ Wrong options: arthrodesis conversion · hyperextension (hinge) · repairable MCL · UKA revision) |
| **Post-polio, hyperextension 15°, global instability** | ✓ **hinged TKA** (✗ Wrong options: CR + reconstruction · PS · DFO · AKA) |
| **Polio, quad weakness, valgus + recurvatum** | ✓ **rotating hinge** (extension stop) (✗ Wrong options: PS with under-resection · VVC) |
| **CCK kept available because** | ✓ **27° valgus** (MCL likely incompetent) (✗ Wrong options: RA · 22° recurvatum (hinge) · LLD · patellar dislocation) |
| **Strongest hinge indication** | ✓ **aseptic revision with >15 mm F/E gap mismatch despite femoral upsizing** (✗ Wrong options: 15° deformity + erosion (wedges) · after dynamic spacer (collaterals intact → CCK) · baja) |
| **Elderly RA, 20° FFD, valgus laxity, + posterior drawer, low demand** | ✓ **cemented rotating hinge + metaphyseal cones** (✗ Wrong options: press-fit · MCL reconstruction) |
| **Implant for polio genu recurvatum** | ✓ **rotating hinge** (✗ Wrong options: intra-op LCL/MCL disruption · diabetic neuropathy · aseptic loosening) |
| **CCK on X-ray — indication** | ✓ **MCL insufficiency + 13° valgus** (✗ Wrong options: hyperextension · 6° valgus with 2 mm laxity (CR) · unresurfaced patella · PCL over-resection (UC or PS)) |
| **Rotating hinge figure — constrained motions** | ✓ **partial V/V + partial IR/ER** · use least constraint that gives stability |
| **Overconstrained, high loosening** | ✓ **GUEPAR (first-gen hinge)** |

![Rotating hinge](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5019-rotating-hinge-radiograph.jpg)

### Mobile bearing & polyethylene conformity

**Mobile bearing** — PE **rotates on tibial baseplate** · PCL removed · ↑ contact area → theoretically **↓ PE wear + ↓ bone–implant stress** · relative indication: young active · ✓ **no improvement in survivorship** vs fixed (flexion 112° vs 108°; recovery, strength, wear not proven)\
✗ Wrong options: 15° more flexion · faster recovery

**Bearing spin-out** — ✓ mobile-bearing-specific · **loose flexion gap** → tibia rotates behind femur · lateral X-ray without cam outline (TO) · closed reduction at 90° + anterior drawer → revise flexion gap

**↑ Conformity (fixed bearing)** — ✓ **↓ contact stress** in PE · ✓ **↓ femoral rollback** · ↑ shear to fixation\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ cracking · ↑ delamination · ↓ loosening · PF stability change

### Modular vs all-polyethylene tibia

| | **Modular (metal tray + insert)** | **All-polyethylene (monoblock)** |
|---|---|---|
| Wear | ✓ **More backside wear** (articular side unchanged) | No backside wear → ✓ **less osteolysis** |
| Cost | Higher | ✓ **Less expensive** |
| Flexibility | Poly exchange possible · sizing after cementing | Size chosen before cementing · full tibial revision if problem |
| Outcomes | — | **Equivalent function** (2–10 yr) · equivalent loosening (Orthobullets) · recent: better survival, ↓ infection, instability, loosening, fracture (OB) · OK in BMI <37.5 |
| Load distribution | More even (biomechanics) | — |
| Prep / bone removal / stability | Same | Same |

![Backside wear on modular insert](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5019-backside-wear.jpg)

### Fixation – cemented vs cementless, cement viscosity

**Most consistent tibial fixation** — ✓ **cement metaphysis + keel**\
✗ Wrong options: cement plateau only + press-fit keel → ↑ loosening

**Cementless** — trabecular/porous ingrowth · ✓ **equivalent functional outcomes** (RCTs to 2 yr) · **shorter** op time, ↑ cost · no difference in femoral loosening, DVT, Hb drop · ideal: ✓ **young, active, obese** (e.g. 52 yo, BMI 44)\
✗ Wrong options: **elderly** (esp. elderly white females), **osteoporosis**, **irradiated bone**, **inflammatory arthritis**, metabolic bone disease

**High-viscosity cement** — ✓ **longer working time** · shorter mixing/waiting · bone penetration equal or less · loosening data conflicting (possibly ↑) · pulmonary complications same

### Patellar resurfacing

**Routine resurfacing vs retention** — ✓ **↓ revision risk** (retention: **20–30% ↑** revision in registries; 5.7% revised for anterior pain)\
✗ Wrong options: no complications · ↑ anterior pain · ↓ fracture

### Gender-specific implants

**Women with standard components** — ✓ **greater implant survivorship** than men · no evidence of inferior outcomes · female designs: narrower ML, thinner anterior flange, ↑ trochlear angle

### Metal allergy

**Nickel allergy** → ✓ **oxidised zirconium femur + titanium (or all-poly) tibia** · CoCr + stainless contain nickel · **titanium** unsuitable as articulating femur (metallosis)

### Patient-specific instrumentation

**PSI** — custom guides from CT/MRI · ✓ **no IM femoral hole needed** (also navigation) → theoretical ↓ fat embolism (TO) · claimed ↓ time, blood loss, trays (TO)

**Only proven benefit (OB)** — ✓ **fewer instrument trays** · (✗ Wrong options: cost · alignment · function · pain/satisfaction) · custom guides → **no difference in ROM, alignment, outcome scores**

### Highly congruent liners & minimum thickness

**Highly congruent (medial-pivot) liners** — medial concavity + lateral translation · equivalent short/mid-term outcomes + survival

**Minimum PE insert thickness** — ✓ **8 mm** (TO)

## X vs Y

| | **Cam jump (PS)** | **Bearing spin-out (mobile)** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Loose flexion gap / hyperextension | Loose flexion gap |
| X-ray | Box visible | No cam outline |
| Reduction | 90° + anterior drawer | 90° + anterior drawer |
| Definitive | Revise flexion gap | Revise flexion gap |

| | **CCK / VVC** | **Rotating hinge** |
|---|---|---|
| Link | None | **Axle** |
| Hyperextension | Not controlled | ✓ Extension stop |
| Collaterals | Attenuated / one deficient | **Globally absent** |

## How it's asked

**"CR vs CS — true?"** → **CR does not tolerate joint-line alteration** (TO)

**"Post-op X-rays A and B?"** → **A is CR, B is PS** (TO)

**"Prior patellectomy, degenerative knee — implant?"** → **posterior stabilised TKA**

**"Who benefits most from CR TKA?"** → **OA with 10° valgus**

**"Patellectomy, stable ligaments — most predictable implant?"** → **PCL-substituting knee**

**"Recreate PCL tension — EXCEPT?"** → **increasing posterior tibial slope**

**"PCL resection simulates?"** → **excessive posterior femoral resection**

**"PS vs CR — true?"** → **higher incidence of patellar clunk**

**"Flexed femur + excess slope — predisposes to?"** → **excessive tibial post wear**

**"PCL injured, CR with conforming insert — outcome?"** → **no difference vs PS**

**"Constrained TKR — true?"** → **only hinge for hyperextension deformity** (TO)

**"Severe valgus — prosthesis allowing component lateralisation?"** → **PS** (TO)

**"Implant in picture?"** → **CCK type** (TO)

**"Charcot valgus, still lax after releases?"** → **convert to constrained TKA**

**"Rotating hinge — constrained motions?"** → **partial V/V + partial IR/ER**

**"Charcot knee — initial implant?"** → **hinge (Figure B)**

**"High loosening from overconstraint?"** → **GUEPAR hinge (Figure C)**

**"Charcot, PS used, unstable — should have used?"** → **hinged TKA**

**"84 yo, attenuated MCL?"** → **varus–valgus constrained TKA**

**"Obese MCL-deficient revision — essential feature?"** → **constraint resisting varus–valgus loads**

**"Absolute indication for constrained non-hinged?"** → **varus >20° with LCL incompetence**

**"Post-polio, hyperextension, global instability?"** → **hinged TKA**

**"Polio recurvatum — technique?"** → **rotating hinge**

**"CCK available — clinical factor?"** → **27° valgus**

**"Strongest rotating-hinge indication?"** → **revision with >15 mm F/E mismatch despite upsizing**

**"Elderly RA, MCL + PCL incompetent?"** → **cemented rotating hinge + metaphyseal cones**

**"Indication for hinge on X-ray?"** → **poliomyelitis with genu recurvatum**

**"CCK on X-ray — indication?"** → **MCL insufficiency + 13° valgus**

**"Charcot valgus — discuss?"** → **increased constraint for global instability**

**"Lateral view — what complication?"** → **bearing spin-out in mobile-bearing TKA** (TO)

**"Modular vs monoblock tibia?"** → **more backside wear with modular**

**"Design that reduces PE wear + interface stress?"** → **mobile-bearing TKA**

**"↑ Conformity — effect?"** → **decreased contact stress**

**"Mobile vs fixed bearing?"** → **no improvement in survivorship**

**"↑ Conformity — fixed bearing effect?"** → **decreased femoral rollback**

**"All-poly vs metal-backed tibia?"** → **less expensive**

**"Monoblock advantage?"** → **less osteolysis**

**"True about femoral rollback?"** → **posterior femoral translation preventing posterior impingement** (TO)

**"Medial pivot — NOT true?"** → **spherical variable-radius MFC** (TO)

**"Normal knee kinematics extension → flexion?"** → **femur pivots about a medial axis**

**"Medial-congruent design, flexion → extension?"** → **medial pivot, femur IR + anterior translation**

**"Paradoxical translation?"** → **anterior contact-point translation with flexion**

**"Insert facilitating lateral condyle rollback?"** → **medial congruent**

**"Tibial insert designs — true?"** → **standard CR can cause paradoxical anterior translation**

**"Most consistent tibial fixation?"** → **cement metaphysis and keel**

**"Cementless TKA — true?"** → **equivalent functional outcomes**

**"High vs low viscosity cement?"** → **longer working time**

**"Ideal cementless candidate?"** → **52 yo active male, BMI 44**

**"Routine patellar resurfacing associated with?"** → **decreased risk of revision**

**"Women with standard components?"** → **greater implant survivorship**

**"Nickel allergy — implant?"** → **titanium tibia + oxidised zirconium femur**

**"IM femoral hole not needed in?"** → **patient-specific instrumentation** (TO)

**"PSI vs standard — true?"** → **fewer instrument trays**

**"Custom cutting guides — outcome difference?"** → **no difference in ROM, alignment or scores**

**"Minimum PE insert thickness?"** → **8 mm** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Revision

*Recon · 58 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Causes of failure (loosening, wear, osteolysis, metallosis)

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Infection** | **Most common overall** (national databases, TO) · ✓ **most common early (<2 yr)** · always exclude first · **neutrophil**-predominant |
| **Aseptic loosening** | **2nd overall** · ✓ **most common late (>2 yr)** (OB5949: late = **>10 yr**) · **tibial > femoral** · femoral lysis hidden (posterior condyles) → oblique views, **serial X-rays** |
| **Instability** | **~8%** · MCL/LCL incompetence · flexion instability (PCL attenuation in CR, unbalanced gap, ↑ slope, small femur, femur in extension) |
| **Periprosthetic fracture** | **~5%** (3–5%) · supracondylar femur commonest |
| **Arthrofibrosis** | **~5%** (0–3% OB) · **fibroblasts** |
| **PF maltracking** | **Component malposition** |
| Joint-line problems · patellar clunk (arthroscopic excision) · metal hypersensitivity (**lymphocytes, plasma cells**) | — |

**TO top four** — **infection → aseptic loosening → periprosthetic fracture → instability** (TO)

**Trends** — ✓ **PE wear no longer the major cause** (HXLPE) · instability/malalignment share ↓ · stiffness common early\
✗ Wrong options: infection commonest late · loosening commonest early

**Osteolysis** — PE, PMMA, metal particles → **macrophages + giant cells** · femur: condyles near collateral attachments · tibia: periphery + cancellous access channels · commonest in **uncemented** + **backside wear** · modern cementless ↓ aseptic revision in morbidly obese

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| 15 yr, pain + instability, osteolysis + loosening, infection excluded | ✓ **revision TKA** (✗ Wrong options: protected WB · bisphosphonate · observe · liner exchange + graft) |
| 15 yr, cemented PS, HXLPE 13 mm — likely cause of revision | ✓ **aseptic loosening** |
| 16 yr, well-defined femoral metaphyseal lysis, medial narrowing | ✓ **particle-mediated osteolysis** (✗ Wrong options: metastasis · stress shielding · sepsis · sarcoma) |
| 13 yr, metal-backed patella, squeaking/grinding | ✓ **extensive metallosis** (worn-through PE → metal-on-metal, black tattooing) |
| 12 yr, femoral migration into varus, normal labs | ✓ **polyethylene wear** (commonest contributor to late lysis/loosening) |
| 6 yr, tibia 5° varus, medial PE narrowing, lateral laxity | ✓ **revise all components + soft-tissue balancing** (✗ Wrong options: poly exchange · isolated tibial (PCL then incompetent)) |
| Commonest early revision — cell lineage | ✓ **neutrophil** (✗ Wrong options: macrophage (PE lysis) · lymphocyte/plasma (metal allergy) · fibroblast (arthrofibrosis)) |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — original indication · pre-op ROM, ambulation · infection, thrombophlebitis, falls · **THA** · implant records

**Temporal pattern** — pain **since index** vs new pain (acute vs chronic infection) · **weight-bearing pain → mechanical**

**Exam** — gait (stiff-legged, no full extension in stance) · ROM · skin, effusion, warmth (**infection vs CRPS**) · ligaments · patellar tracking

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Use |
|---|---|
| **Serial AP + lateral** | Timeline of migration/loosening |
| **Weight-bearing films** | Asymmetric wear |
| **Skyline** | Patellar tracking |
| **Standing long-leg** | Alignment |
| **AP pelvis** | Exclude hip pathology |
| **CT** | Component rotation (vs femoral neck version) · bone defects |
| **Bone scan** | **Positive up to 2 yr** after TKA · positive = nonspecific (loosening, infection, stress fracture) · ✓ **negative rules out loosening** · diffuse uptake → **CRPS** |

### Genes & lab markers

**CBC, ESR, CRP + aspiration** (cell count, culture) before any revision

**Antibiotics already started** → ✓ **stop antibiotics and repeat aspiration**\
✗ Wrong options: continue 2 wk · add gram-negative cover · revise · PT

**Painful nonunion after periprosthetic ORIF with effusion + loosening screws** → ✓ **inflammatory labs + aspiration first**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · bone stimulator · revision ORIF / DFR before infection excluded

### AORI (Anderson) bone defect classification

| Type | Description | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Minor defects, **intact metaphysis**, stability not compromised | **Cement** or **impaction allograft** (no metaphyseal augmentation) |
| **2A** | Metaphyseal damage, **one** condyle/plateau | Cement, **augments**, small graft |
| **2B** | Metaphyseal damage, **both** condyles/plateaus | Cement, augments, small graft |
| **3** | **Massive** loss of condyle/plateau ± **collateral / patellar tendon** attachments | **Bulk allograft**, custom, megaprosthesis, **tantalum cones**, **sleeves**, **rotating hinge** |

![AORI classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q218064-illa-aori.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Goals & steps

**Goals** — extract with minimal bone loss · restore bone · ✓ **restore joint line** · balance ligaments · stable implants · soft-tissue cover

**Sequence** — extensile exposure → remove implants → **tibia first** (establish tibial joint line **1.5–2 cm above fibular head**, use contralateral X-ray) → femur matched to tibia → **flexion–extension gaps** → **medial–lateral gaps** → PF tracking (patella **>12 mm**)

### Exposure (rectus snip, tibial tubercle osteotomy)

**Rectus (quad) snip** — extension of MPP superolaterally across rectus tendon · ✓ **no difference in outcomes** vs MPP · ✓ **enhances exposure without changing rehab or outcomes** · **most common** extra-exposure method\
✗ Wrong options: lateral arthrotomy (devascularises patella) · full MCL release · patellectomy · mobile bearing

**TTO** — **8–10 cm** medial osteotomy hinged on lateral periosteum · ✓ **patella baja** (contracted tendon; prevents avulsion; allows **proximal transfer**) · well-fixed long stems/sleeves/cones · RA, valgus, anteromedial scarring · ✓ **very low nonunion (~100% union)** · fixed with screws or **16-gauge wires/cerclage** (long stems do not preclude) · OK in infected/staged cases · ROM restricted 4–6 wk · used if snip insufficient (V-Y → lag)\
✗ Wrong options: well-fixed standard baseplate alone · slope · CR · BMD predicts

| Figure | TTO needed? |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Patella baja** | **Yes** |
| Posterior dislocation, PT avulsion (alta), spin-out, recurvatum | No |

**Post-HTO baja, PJI explant, flexion 65°, stiff after release** → ✓ **TTO** (best exposure, preserves patellar supply)\
✗ Wrong options: snip + lateral release (lateral geniculate) · V-Y turndown (blood supply) · snip alone (insufficient) · modified V-Y

### Bone loss (augments, cones, sleeves, stems)

**Stems** — load-share to diaphysis · bypass metaphyseal defects

| | **Press-fit diaphyseal stem** | **Cemented stem** |
|---|---|---|
| Pros | Scratch fit, aids alignment, no cement to remove later | **Bowed femur**, antibiotic delivery, **osteopenic bone** |
| Cons | No ingrowth · **iatrogenic fracture** · not for bowed femur · ✓ **end-of-stem pain** (modulus mismatch) | Harder future revision |

**Shin pain at stem tip after hybrid (press-fit) stem** → ✓ **fully cemented stem would have prevented**\
✗ Wrong options: hinge · spacer · teriparatide · short press-fit

**Medial baseplate overhang with long stem → pain** → ✓ **offset tibial stem**\
✗ Wrong options: ingrowth augment · no stem · allograft · cement

| Defect | Option |
|---|---|
| **Cavitary <1 cm** | **Cement** (structurally better than graft) |
| **Cavitary >1 cm, contained** | **Metaphyseal sleeve** |
| **Large, uncontained / asymmetric** | ✓ **Trabecular (tantalum) cone** + press-fit diaphyseal stem (intact diaphysis) |
| **Small asymmetric uncontained** | Metal augment |
| **Small symmetric contained** | Impaction graft |
| **Large symmetric contained + poor diaphysis** | Sleeve + **cemented** stem |
| **Elderly, massive tibial loss incl. posterior cortex** | ✓ **metal-augmented revision tibia** (structural allograft for younger) (✗ Wrong options: hinge · ICBG · cement) |

| | **Metaphyseal sleeves** | **Trabecular metal cones** | **Structural allograft** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data | Mid–long term good | Short–mid term good | Mid–long term OK |
| Link to implant | ✓ **Morse taper** (modular, attached to stem/tray) | **Independent**, unitised with cement | Cemented |
| Vendor | **Manufacturer-specific** | ✓ **Any system** | — |
| Defect | **Contained only** (✗ Wrong options: large uncontained) | ✓ **Uncontained** OK | Custom shape |
| Other | Simple, acts as cutting guide · expensive, hard to remove | **Brittle**, can be cemented to bone if scratch fit poor · soft-tissue irritant · expensive | Time, disease transmission, **resorption**, infection |

**Porous cones vs cortical allograft** → ✓ **higher long-term survival**\
✗ Wrong options: implant-specific · ductile Morse taper (sleeves) · cannot be cemented · AORI 1

**Cone purpose** → ✓ **↑ metaphyseal contact for osseointegration**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotics · diaphyseal fit · no cement · easier future revision

**Stage 2, massive metaphyseal defects, intact collaterals, healed TTO** → ✓ **uncemented porous metaphyseal sleeves + uncemented stems**\
✗ Wrong options: impaction graft · allograft without stem/cement · "diaphyseal" defects

**Sleeve relative contraindication** → ✓ **large uncontained metaphyseal defect**\
✗ Wrong options: hinge · stem · RA · capacious diaphysis (sleeve helps)

![Trabecular metal cone](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5021-tm-cone.jpg)

### Constraint in revision (VVC, hinge)

| Implant | Indication |
|---|---|
| **CR** | Intact PCL (keep PS available) |
| **PS** | PCL deficiency |
| **Constrained non-hinged** | V/V instability, LCL/MCL attenuation (MCL controversial — post may break), flexion laxity (tall post) |
| **Rotating hinge** | **Global ligament deficiency** · flexion laxity with mismatch · multiply revised / post-traumatic · ✓ **hyperextension (polio)** — absolute · tumour / infection resection · Charcot (relative) · **collateral origin bone loss** |
| **DFR** | Salvage: fracture, massive bone loss |

**Rule** — **least constraint** that gives stability

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| 1990 TKA, two-component osteolysis + instability | ✓ **revise femur + tibia with stems/augments** (✗ Wrong options: tibia only ± LCL reconstruction · immobiliser · no stems) |
| Irreparable saw MCL transection, valgus laxity >1 cm (RA) | ✓ **unlinked VVC** (least interface stress) (✗ Wrong options: PS fixed/mobile · CR · hinge) |
| 80 yo, MCL deficient + anterior drawer + no SLR (multiplanar) | ✓ **hinged revision** + EM repair (✗ Wrong options: poly upsize · MCL reconstruction · femoral only · high-post) |
| Absolute indication for rotating hinge | ✓ **hyperextension instability** (✗ Wrong options: LCL attenuation (CCK) · EM deficiency · PCL deficiency (PS) · contained condylar defect (AORI 2A)) |
| Stage 2 — strongest hinge indication | ✓ **medial femoral cortical loss involving medial epicondyle** (MCL origin) (✗ Wrong options: cancellous loss (cones/sleeves) · cortical loss sparing collaterals (augments)) |

![Rotating hinge revision](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5021-hinged-tka.jpg)

### Flexion instability

**Revision options** — ✓ **upsize femoral component** · ↓ tibial slope · more distal femur + thicker poly · correct rotation\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ slope · more posterior femur · more tibia (both gaps) · distal augments (extension only)

### Infection-related (cement, host, workup)

**Stage 2 for MRSA** → ✓ **pre-mixed gentamicin cement + hand-mixed vancomycin** · pre-mixed better elution + mechanics · FDA: antibiotic cement approved only for **stage 2 of two-stage**; primary use off-label\
✗ Wrong options: plain cement

**Failure of repeat two-stage** → ✓ **MSIS host grade C** (none cleared) → consider **fusion / amputation** · also MRSA, polymicrobial\
✗ Wrong options: WBC >25k · anaerobic cocci (low virulence) · semi-constrained · static spacer

**Revision infection risk** — **4–7%** (double primary) · ↑ with MSIS C · PJI 1–2% primary, up to 4% revision

### Cementless TKA indications

**Best evidence** → ✓ **younger, obese males** (↓ tibial aseptic loosening, HA-coated; short–mid term)\
✗ Wrong options: osteopenic · RA · elderly ♀ · elderly smokers

### Wound & extensor problems

**Skin** — incorporate prior scars · supply medial → lateral edge hypoxic → **use most lateral incision** · full-thickness defect → **medial gastrocnemius flap**

**iNPWT** — ✓ **revision TKA** clearly benefits (↓ wound complications)\
✗ Wrong options: UKA · primary TKA · primary THA any approach

**EM disruption** — Achilles bone-block allograft (residual lag common) · **mesh** may give better mid-term function + survival

**Other** — pain worse than primary (activity pain ~6 mo) · stiffness · peroneal palsy (valgus + FFD correction)

### Distal femoral replacement outcomes

**DFR for fracture around primary components** → ✓ **lower revision rates** than DFR after prior revision components or failed ORIF

## X vs Y

| | **Early failure (<2 yr)** | **Late failure (>2 yr)** |
|---|---|---|
| Commonest | **Infection** | **Aseptic loosening** |
| Cells | Neutrophils | Macrophages (wear) |

| | **Sleeve** | **Cone** |
|---|---|---|
| Defect | Contained | **Uncontained** |
| Attachment | Morse taper, vendor-specific | Independent, any vendor |

## How it's asked

**"Oblique rectus snip vs MPP in revision?"** → **no difference in outcomes**

**"Enhance exposure without changing rehab?"** → **extensile rectus snip**

**"Which X-ray most likely needs TTO?"** → **patella baja (Figure D)**

**"Post-HTO baja, PJI explant — best exposure preserving patellar supply?"** → **TTO (Fig D)**

**"Radiographic predictor of needing TTO?"** → **patella baja**

**"True about TTO?"** → **very low nonunion rates at 1 yr**

**"Stage 2, metaphyseal defects, intact collaterals?"** → **uncemented metaphyseal sleeves + uncemented stems**

**"Medial tibial overhang pain with long stem — prevention?"** → **offset tibial stem**

**"84 yo, massive tibial loss — management?"** → **metal-augmented revision tibia**

**"Relative contraindication to metaphyseal sleeve?"** → **large uncontained metaphyseal defect**

**"Tantalum cone + press-fit stem — appropriate for?"** → **large asymmetric uncontained metaphyseal defect, intact diaphysis**

**"Highly porous cones — true?"** → **higher long-term survival than cortical allograft**

**"End-of-stem shin pain — prevention?"** → **fully cemented stem**

**"Purpose of metaphyseal cone?"** → **increase metaphyseal contact for osseointegration**

**"Two-component osteolysis + instability?"** → **revise both with stems and/or augments**

**"Irreparable MCL transection — least interface stress?"** → **unlinked VVC**

**"Elderly, MCL deficient, EM deficient, multiplanar instability?"** → **hinged revision**

**"Absolute indication for rotating hinge?"** → **hyperextension instability**

**"Stage 2 — strongest hinge indication?"** → **cortical loss involving medial epicondyle**

**"Most common cause of TKR revision?"** → **infection** (TO)

**"15 yr, osteolysis + loosening, infection negative?"** → **revision TKA**

**"Late revision at 15 yr — likely cause?"** → **aseptic loosening**

**"Well-defined femoral lysis at 16 yr?"** → **particle-mediated osteolysis**

**"Metal-backed patella, squeaking at 13 yr — finding?"** → **extensive metallosis**

**"Varus tibia, medial PE wear, lax laterally?"** → **revise all components + balance**

**"Femoral migration into varus at 12 yr, normal labs?"** → **polyethylene wear**

**"Revision causes vs historical?"** → **PE wear no longer the major cause**

**"Commonest early revision — cell lineage?"** → **neutrophil**

**"Flexion instability revision?"** → **upsize femoral component**

**"Stage 2 MRSA — cement?"** → **pre-mixed gentamicin + hand-mixed vancomycin**

**"Failure of second two-stage — risk factor?"** → **MSIS type C host**

**"Nonunion after periprosthetic ORIF, effusion — next?"** → **inflammatory labs + aspiration**

**"Possible PJI, already on antibiotics, normal aspirate, high CRP?"** → **stop antibiotics and repeat aspiration**

**"Best evidence for cementless TKA?"** → **younger obese males**

**"Procedure clearly benefiting from iNPWT?"** → **revision TKA**

**"DFR for fracture around primary vs prior ORIF?"** → **lower revision rates**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Sagittal Plane Balancing

*Recon · 59 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Gap definitions (TO)

**Extension gap** — ✓ **distal femoral cut ↔ proximal tibial cut** (in extension) (TO)

**Flexion gap** — **posterior femoral cut ↔ proximal tibial cut** (at 90°) (TO)

**Goal** — gaps **rectangular and equal** (TO)

### Rules

| Change | Affects |
|---|---|
| **Distal femoral cut / distal augment** | **Extension gap only** |
| **Posterior femoral cut / femoral AP size / posterior augment / AP position** | **Flexion gap only** |
| **Tibial cut / poly thickness** | **Both gaps** |
| **Tibial slope** | **Flexion gap** (↑ slope opens flexion) |
| **Posterior capsule release** | Extension |
| **PCL recession / release** (CR) | Flexion |

**Symmetric problem** (both tight / both loose) → **tibia / poly** · **asymmetric problem** → **femur**

**Femoral AP position** — **anterior** shift → ↑ flexion gap · **posterior** shift → ↓ flexion gap (and tightens posterior tissues) · proximal/distal shift → extension gap

**Downsizing femur** — changes **AP dimension only** (no proximal–distal change) · with anterior referencing → removes more posterior condyle → opens flexion

**Gap-balancing technique (TO)** — remove osteophytes → precise tibial cut → **release ligaments before femoral cuts**

## 5 Management

### Sagittal balancing grid (TO + OB)

| | **Tight in flexion** | **Balanced in flexion** | **Loose in flexion** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tight in extension** | ✓ **Cut more proximal tibia** (or thinner poly) | ✓ **Cut more distal femur** · release posterior capsule | ✓ **Cut more distal femur + thicker poly** · or more distal femur + **upsize femur** |
| **Balanced in extension** | ✓ **Downsize femur** (same poly) · ↑ **posterior slope** · CR: **recess / cut PCL** · anteriorise femur | Done | ✓ **Upsize femur ± posterior augments** · move femur posteriorly · more distal femur + thicker poly · less slope + thicker poly |
| **Loose in extension** | ✓ **Downsize femur + thicker poly** | **Distal femoral augment, same poly** (OB) · downsize femur + thicker poly or more slope + thicker poly (TO) — read the options | ✓ **Thicker poly** |

![Gap balancing grid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5016-grid.jpg)

### Tight in extension – resect distal femur

**Flexion contracture, flexion balanced** → ✓ **resect more distal femur** (or release posterior capsule)\
✗ Wrong options: PCL recession · ↑ slope (flexion problems) · smaller femur · more tibia (loosens flexion) · thinner poly (both) · posterior augments (tighten flexion)

**Pre-op 25–95°, after capsule release + osteophytes still 20° short, flexion fine** → ✓ **resect 2 mm more distal femur**\
✗ Wrong options: 2 mm tibia · downsize + thicker poly · ↑ slope · femur 4 mm posterior (tightens flexion + posterior tissues)

**Flexion contracture steps (OB)** — 1: ML balance + osteophytes + distal femur over-resected **2 mm** · 2: **posterior capsule release** · 3: distal femur up to **4 mm** more · 4: **hamstring tenotomy** · <15° → steps 1–2 suffice · steps 3–4 for some >35° · residual contracture >15° less likely to improve

**Flexion contracture treatments EXCEPT** → ✓ **downsize femoral component** (flexion gap only)

**Revision: 2 mm more tibia for 10° contracture** → ✓ **flexion instability** (common pitfall; cut distal femur instead)

### Loose in flexion – upsize femur & posterior augments

**Causes** — **excess posterior femoral resection** (✓ loose flexion gap) · ↑ slope · undersized femur · **PCL resection** · popliteus injury

**Loose flexion, balanced extension — EXCEPT (TO)** → ✓ **downsize femur + thicker spacer** · valid: more distal femur + thicker spacer · upsize femur, same spacer · less slope + thicker spacer (TO)

**Cruciate-sacrificing, flexion instability, extension fine** → ✓ **upsize femur + posterior augments** (or move femur posterior, or thicker poly then fix tight extension)\
✗ Wrong options: downsize femur/tibia · upsize tibia · femur anterior

**Loose flexion, stable extension** → ✓ **move femoral component posterior**\
✗ Wrong options: thicker poly alone · downsize · anterior + distal augment · ER both

**Revision: upsized femur + posterior augments because** → ✓ **balanced in extension, loose in flexion**

**CR TKA, AP instability at 90°, femur anterior, liftoff** → ✓ **revise to larger PS implant**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAID · brace · PT · thicker poly

**Revision with distal augment → dislocated at 1 mo** → ✓ **flexion gap instability** (distal augments treat extension; thin patella / EM weakness aggravates)\
✗ Wrong options: joint line · PT rupture · tibial IR · femoral hyperextension

**Robotic plan 8 mm, 13 mm posterior condyle removed** → ✓ **difficulty rising from a chair** · flexion instability: recurrent effusions, **quad weakness**, stairs, ↑ (not ↓) terminal flexion\
✗ Wrong options: hyperextension · ↓ flexion · coronal instability in extension · hamstring weakness

### Tight in flexion – downsize femur or increase slope

**Downsizing femur helps** → ✓ **tight flexion + normal extension** (TO) · keep anterior cut (avoid notching) → removes more posterior condyle

**PS revision, full extension, tight flexion** → ✓ **downsize femoral component** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: flex femur (tightens flexion) · thinner tibia (both) · more distal femur (extension)

**CR knee, tight flexion gap (TO)** → ✓ **cut PCL** (convert to PS) · other: ↑ slope (limited), downsize femur (may need anterior referencing) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: more tibia (opens extension too) · upsize femur · distal femur

**Anterior referencing, 8 mm posterior cut, still tight flexion (TO)** → ✓ **downsize implant** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: distal femur · ↓ slope · thinner poly

**Full extension, flexion only to 90°** → ✓ **downsize femur**\
✗ Wrong options: more tibia / thinner poly (loosen extension) · distal augment · distal femur

**IM referencing, stable extension, won't flex past 90°** → ✓ **downsize femur** · also **PCL recession**, **↑ slope**\
✗ Wrong options: thinner insert · posterior augments · distal femur · medial tibial reduction osteotomy (valgus laxity)

**CR, recut tibia with ↑ slope because** → ✓ **balanced extension, tight flexion** (slope opens flexion via rollback)

**Tibial tray lift-off in flexion** = tight flexion · easy extraction at 90° = loose flexion

![Tight in flexion — downsize femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5016-tight-in-flexion-jpg.jpg)

### Femoral referencing (TO)

| | **Posterior referencing** | **Anterior referencing** |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed | **Posterior cut** (~8–9 mm) → flexion gap fixed | **Anterior cut** (no notching) |
| Sequence | Distal cut → size → anterior/posterior cuts → chamfers + notch | Anterior cut first (Whiteside rotation) → size |
| Size change alters | **Anterior** resection | **Posterior** resection (flexion gap) |
| Between sizes | **Choose larger** (smaller → notching) | **Choose smaller** (wider flexion gap, better flexion; rare mid-flexion instability) |

![Posterior vs anterior referencing](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356211-1.jpg)

### Tight in both – resect tibia

**Cannot extend + tray lifts off past 90°** → ✓ **resect more proximal tibia** (or thinner poly)\
✗ Wrong options: distal augment · distal femur only · downsize femur only · thicker poly

### Loose in extension – distal femoral augment

**Balanced flexion, loose extension** → ✓ **distal femoral augment, same poly**\
✗ Wrong options: distal resection · downsize + thinner poly · tibial resection · augment + thicker poly

### Combined gap mismatch

**Tight extension + loose flexion (TO)** → ✓ **distal femoral cut + thicker poly**\
✗ Wrong options: downsize + slope · downsize + thicker poly · tibia + thicker poly

**Post-op flexion contracture + loose flexion (revision)** → ✓ **resect more distal femur + upsize femur**\
✗ Wrong options: thicker poly (worsens extension) · more tibia · anterior femur + thinner poly · distal femur + tibia

**CR TKA, recurvatum + flexion only 80° (loose extension, tight flexion)** → ✓ **downsize femur + thicker insert**\
✗ Wrong options: semi-constrained · more tibia · anteriorise femur alone · ↑ slope alone

**Extension gap > flexion gap — release? (TO, FNB 2024)** — options anterior sMCL, posterior capsule, POL, deep MCL · **answer not provided by the source** · TO rule (chapter 41): **anterior half of sMCL + pes → flexion space**; POL, posterior capsule, semimembranosus → extension space

**Revision joint-line elevation** — 23 mm spacer, full extension, stable flexion, **patella impinging on poly** → ✓ **upsize femur + distal and posterior augments** (lower joint line, shrink flexion gap) · error: femoral component seated on residual bone → too small + too proximal\
✗ Wrong options: proceed · tendon Z-lengthening · posterior augments only · distal augments only

### PCL deficiency in CR knee – anterior femoral subluxation (TO)

**CR TKR, anterior femoral subluxation on X-ray** → ✓ **PCL deficiency** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ACL deficiency · PCL impingement · PLC instability

**CR contraindications (TO)** — PCL insufficiency · posterolateral instability · **flexion >20°**, **coronal >15°** deformity · inflammatory arthritis + EM deficiency (now relative) · prior ORIF/HTO/UKA (poor ligaments → PS easier) · bone defects needing augments/stems · **excessive native slope** (flexion tight → convert to PS) (TO)

**CR technique (TO)** — avoid over-resecting distal femur (CR tends to flexion tightness) · choose **smaller** femur between sizes (PS: **larger**) · PCL inserts **5–10 mm** below tibial surface → protect (osteotome / bone block) · tight PCL → release / convert to PS (TO)

**Kinematics (TO)** — CR: anterior femoral translation **30–60°** WB · PS: constant contact, rollback 60–90° NWB · both show early paradoxical translation · clinical scores, ROM, mid-term survival, quad recovery **no different** · **bicruciate-retaining** preserves screw-home partially (TO)

### Genu recurvatum correction (TO)

**Steps (TO)** — **minimal distal femoral resection** · **move joint line distally** · ✓ **smaller femoral component** (anterior referencing → bigger flexion gap) · thicker poly fills both gaps · **appropriate constraint**

**EXCEPT (TO)** → ✓ **one size bigger femoral component**

## X vs Y

| | **Downsizing femur** | **More distal femur** | **More proximal tibia** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flexion gap | **↑** | — | ↑ |
| Extension gap | — | **↑** | ↑ |
| Fixes | Tight flexion | Tight extension / FFD | Both tight |

## How it's asked

**"CR TKA, 15° FFD, flexion balanced?"** → **resect more distal femur**

**"Stable in flexion, cannot fully extend?"** → **resect more distal femur**

**"Flexion contracture techniques — EXCEPT?"** → **downsize femoral component**

**"Pre-op 25–95°, still 20° short, flexion balanced?"** → **resect 2 mm more distal femur**

**"Cannot fully extend after releases, flexion balanced — next?"** → **resect additional distal femur**

**"Loose flexion, balanced extension — EXCEPT?"** → **downsize femur + thicker spacer** (TO)

**"Cruciate-sacrificing, flexion instability, extension fine?"** → **upsize femur + posterior augments**

**"Excessive posterior femoral resection leads to?"** → **loose flexion gap**

**"Loose flexion, stable extension — option?"** → **move femoral component posterior**

**"Revision upsized femur + posterior augments because?"** → **balanced extension, loose flexion**

**"CR TKA, AP instability at 90°?"** → **revise to larger PS implant**

**"Distal augment, dislocation at 1 mo?"** → **flexion gap instability**

**"Excess posterior condyle cut by robot — symptom?"** → **difficulty rising from a seated position**

**"Downsizing femur helps when?"** → **tight flexion, normal extension** (TO)

**"PS revision, full extension, tight flexion?"** → **downsize femoral component** (TO)

**"Tight flexion gap in CR knee?"** → **cut PCL** (TO)

**"Anterior referencing, 8 mm posterior cut, still tight in flexion?"** → **downsize implant** (TO)

**"Full extension, flexion to 90° only?"** → **downsize femoral component**

**"IM referencing, won't flex past 90°?"** → **downsize femoral component**

**"Recut tibia with more slope because?"** → **balanced extension, tight flexion**

**"Cannot extend + tray lift-off in flexion?"** → **resect more proximal tibia**

**"Balanced flexion, loose extension?"** → **distal femoral augment, same poly**

**"Tight extension + loose flexion?"** → **distal femoral cut + thicker poly** (TO)

**"Revision: FFD + loose flexion?"** → **more distal femur + upsize femur**

**"2 mm more tibia for revision contracture — effect?"** → **flexion instability**

**"Recurvatum + flexion to 80° after CR TKA?"** → **downsize femur + thicker insert**

**"Revision: patella impinging on 23 mm spacer?"** → **upsize femur + distal and posterior augments**

**"CR TKR, anterior femoral subluxation?"** → **PCL deficiency** (TO)

**"Recurvatum correction — EXCEPT?"** → **one size bigger femoral component** (TO)

**"Extension gap refers to?"** → **distance between distal femoral and proximal tibial cuts** (TO)
