# TKA Stiffness

*Recon · 60 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — ✓ **flexion <90°** or **flexion contracture 10–15°** · poor function + satisfaction

**Incidence** — **1.3–12%** (up to 6%; arthrofibrosis ~1%)

### Risk factors

| Phase | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Pre-op** | ✓ **Poor pre-op ROM (most important)** · **patella baja** · **younger age (<55)** · smoking · comorbidity (DM) · low pain tolerance · **prior surgery** (distal femur ORIF, plateau ORIF, multi-ligament surgery → 2–3× arthrofibrosis) · post-traumatic OA · socioeconomic |
| **Technical** | **Overstuffed PF joint** · **malrotation** · **tight flexion/extension gaps** · **joint-line elevation** · **extensor mechanism tightened at closure** (close in flexion) · **tight PCL** (CR) · oversized components · anterior tibial slope · inadequate resections |
| **Post-op** | Delayed rehab · poor effort · **infection** · HO · hamstring spasm (usually resolves by **6 mo**) |
| **Not** | Skin staples · SNF discharge · CPM use · PS vs CR · ipsilateral THA · no tourniquet |

### Predictors of postoperative ROM

**Most important determinant of final ROM** — ✓ **pre-op ROM** (TO, OB) · also intra-op ROM, flexion arc, lower tibiofemoral angle\
✗ Wrong options: CPM/rehab · slope + capsular release · balancing

**Gap errors (OB7594)** — **excess distal femoral resection + thick poly → tight flexion → loss of flexion** · **inadequate distal resection + retained osteophytes + tight posterior capsule → flexion contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: the reversed pairings

**Post-traumatic OA (prior plateau ORIF) TKA** → ✓ **limited knee flexion / stiffness needing MUA** · also wound problems\
✗ Wrong options: worse function (gain larger) · more loosening · HO · fatal PE

**Prior distal femur ORIF** → ✓ **arthrofibrosis 10–85° at 3 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: good pre-op ROM · PS · ipsilateral THA · no tourniquet

### Functional ROM requirements

| Activity | Flexion |
|---|---|
| **Stairs** | **90°** (TO, OB) · 95° (Orthobullets) |
| **Rise from chair** | ✓ **95–100°** (TO) · 105° (Orthobullets) — read the options |
| **Lifting an object** | **≥120°** |
| **Squat, kneel, cross-legged** | **110–165°** |
| **Efficient gait** | Near-full extension |

**Minimum flexion to rise from chair (TO)** → ✓ **95°**

## 3 Presentation

**Difficulty kneeling** · compare with pre-op ROM in records

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray / CT** — exclude malposition, malalignment, oversizing, notching

### Genes & lab markers

**ESR / CRP ± aspiration** — **exclude infection** before MUA

## 5 Management

```
Stiff TKA (flexion <90°)
├─ Always rule out infection (ESR, CRP, aspirate)
├─ <12 wk → manipulation under anaesthesia
├─ >12 wk / failed MUA → arthroscopic (or open) lysis + MUA
└─ Identifiable technical cause → revision TKA
```

### Manipulation under anaesthesia – indications & timing

**Indication** — **flexion <90° within 12 weeks** despite PT\
✗ Wrong options: 110° threshold

**Timing** — ✓ **best before 12 weeks / within 3 months** (6 wk no better than 12 wk; max gains 12–14 wk) · late (>3 mo) less effective, more risk

**Loss of flexion** responds better than loss of extension

**Gains** — average **67° → 111°** · early MUA +**20–30°** · resume aggressive PT immediately

**Risks** — **fracture** (<1–2%), **extensor mechanism disruption** · ✓ **notching** (undersized femur with **posterior referencing** → anterior over-resection; **3 mm** notch ↑ fracture risk; anterior femur stressed most in extension)\
✗ Wrong options: unequal gaps · ↑ offset · femoral IR · press-fit

| Scenario | Answer |
|---|---|
| ROM 0–60° at **2 mo** (pre-op 5–100°) | ✓ **best MUA indication** (✗ Wrong options: 0–60° at 8 mo · 30–120° (extension loss)) |
| 6 wk, good intra-op motion, poor flexion | ✓ **MUA** (✗ Wrong options: resurface patella · thinner poly · arthroscopy (>3 mo) · CPM) |
| 0–80° at 6 wk — MUA best before | ✓ **12 weeks** |
| 10–70° at 10 wk, normal ESR/CRP | ✓ **MUA** (✗ Wrong options: aspiration (labs normal) · steroid + PT · poly exchange · revision) |

![Notching — fracture risk during MUA](../../MCQ/attachments/q219945-illa-screenshot-2024-12-26-at-5-19-06-pm.jpg)

### Late stiffness – workup & revision

**Deteriorating motion 9 mo after revision** → ✓ **investigate for PJI**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · MUA · revision · resection

**9 mo, 5–80°, painful, CR well aligned, infection negative** → ✓ **arthroscopic lysis of adhesions + manipulation**\
✗ Wrong options: dynamic splint · open release · MUA alone (late) · revision to CS

**Revision for stiffness** — only with **identifiable technical cause** · results modest (arc +~20°; 40% good–excellent; 66% satisfied) · ✓ **main post-op focus = regain near full extension** (more satisfying than arc gain)\
✗ Wrong options: 75–80% reach 0–90° · pre-op arc irrelevant · CR→PS much better · MUA reliable after revision

**Other options** — open adhesiolysis ± poly exchange, quadricepsplasty, component revision

### CPM

✓ **May improve early ROM, not final ROM** · no benefit in pain, ambulation, extension · cost questioned

## X vs Y

| | **< 12 weeks** | **> 12 weeks** |
|---|---|---|
| First choice | **MUA** | **Arthroscopic lysis + MUA** |
| Risk | Low | ↑ fracture, tendon rupture |

## How it's asked

**"When is MUA best indicated?"** → **0–60° at 2 months**

**"6 wk, poor flexion despite aggressive rehab?"** → **manipulation under anaesthesia**

**"MUA gives largest flexion gain if before?"** → **12 weeks**

**"10–70° at 10 wk, normal labs?"** → **manipulation under anaesthesia**

**"True about MUA?"** → **best results within 3 months**

**"Fracture during MUA — technical error?"** → **undersized femur with posterior referencing (notching)**

**"Most important factor for final ROM?"** → **preoperative ROM** (TO)

**"ROM after TKA — true?"** → **pre-op ROM is the principal predictor**

**"TKA after plateau ORIF — increased risk of?"** → **limited knee flexion**

**"10–85° at 3 mo — which history?"** → **prior distal femur ORIF**

**"Deteriorating motion after revision?"** → **investigate for infection**

**"Stiff revision candidate — counsel?"** → **focus on regaining near-full extension**

**"9 mo, 5–80°, painful, infection negative?"** → **arthroscopic lysis + manipulation**

**"Routine CPM outcome?"** → **may improve early ROM, not final ROM**

**"Minimum flexion to rise from chair?"** → **95°** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Templating

*Recon · 61 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — anticipating implant **size + position** pre-op · predicts sizes needed in OR · reliable starting point

**Accuracy** — **manual 92–100%** within ± one size · **automatic only 51%**

**Steps** — radiographs → analysis → check **scale (20% magnification)** → femur → tibia

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Role |
|---|---|
| **AP weight-bearing knee** | Required · bony defects + osteophytes easiest here |
| ✓ **Lateral knee** | **Most important view for templating** · slope, patellar height |
| **Patellofemoral (sunrise)** | Not needed for templating · tilt/shift |
| **Full-length hip-to-ankle WB AP** | Optional · rule out **extra-articular deformity**, coronal laxity, plan cuts vs mechanical axis |

### Radiographic analysis

| Step | Assessment |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Mechanical axis** (hip-to-ankle) — neutral bisects knee centre |
| **2** | Coronal deformity — **tibiofemoral angle** |
| **3** | **Femoral resection (valgus cut) angle** = mechanical vs anatomic femoral axis |
| **4** | **Tibial cut** ⊥ mechanical axis |
| **5** | Bony defects + osteophytes (AP) |
| **6** | **Tibial slope** (lateral) |
| **7** | **Patellar height** (lateral) — **baja** → harder exposure |
| **8** | Patellar shift/tilt (skyline) |

![Valgus cut angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12303-valgus-cut-angle.jpg)

### Alignment strategies

| | **Mechanical alignment** | **Kinematic alignment** |
|---|---|---|
| Goal | Neutral axis **0 ± 3°** | Restore pre-arthritic joint line + kinematics |
| Femur | ⊥ femoral mechanical axis (**5–7° valgus** to anatomic) | Cylindrical axis of posterior condyles · **0.7–1.8° more valgus** |
| Tibia | ⊥ tibial mechanical axis (0°), avoid accidental varus | Pre-arthritic joint line · **1.2–2.2° more varus** |
| Slope | — | Native · ~**1.2° more** |
| Rotation | **TEA** or **3° ER** to posterior condyles | — |
| Soft tissue | Releases anticipated for rectangular gaps | Few/no releases · equal medial/lateral resections |

## 5 Management

### Template the femur

**Size on lateral** — restore **posterior condylar offset** · avoid **notching**

**AP** — ML position, no overhang (overhang → consider downsizing)

**Pitfall** — ✓ **oversizing the femur** · femur **less accurate** than tibia

### Template the tibia

**Size on lateral** — no overhang · match native slope · confirm on AP

**Pitfall** — **equally likely to over- or undersize** · intra-op tendency to **downsize**

![Tibial template — lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12303-tibial-template-lateral.jpg)

### Extra-articular deformity

**Healed femoral fracture with hardware, mechanical axis ~3° valgus, 14° deformity not affecting joint** → ✓ **routine knee prosthesis** · avoid hardware removal with **extramedullary jigs / navigation**\
✗ Wrong options: custom components · special IM jigs · hinge · osteotomy (only for severe deformity)

## X vs Y

| | **Femoral templating** | **Tibial templating** |
|---|---|---|
| Accuracy | Lower | Higher |
| Error | **Oversizing** | Over = under · surgeons downsize |

## How it's asked

**"Prior femoral fracture, axis 3° valgus — restore mechanical axis with?"** → **routine knee prosthesis**

**"Most important view for TKA templating?"** → **lateral knee**

**"Manual vs automatic templating accuracy?"** → **92–100% vs 51% (± one size)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Vascular Injury & Bleeding

*Recon · 62 / 65*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **0.017–0.2%** · rare, devastating · commonest = **direct popliteal artery injury**

**Risk factors** — **revision** · **peripheral vascular disease** (consider TKA without tourniquet)

### Popliteal artery anatomy & retractor injury

**Origin** — continuation of **SFA** at **adductor hiatus** · anchored by adductor magnus insertion

**At the joint** — ✓ **lateral** structure · directly behind **posterior capsule** · posterior to **posterior horn/root of lateral meniscus** · ✓ **posterolateral to PCL tibial insertion** · with popliteal vein + tibial nerve

**Relations** — ✓ **anterior to popliteal vein** · **9 mm** posterior to posterior tibial plateau at **90° flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: 15 mm · posterior/medial/lateral to vein

**Branches** — above joint: **medial + lateral sural**, cutaneous, **middle genicular** · at joint: **medial + lateral geniculars** · exits under **soleus** tendon · divides into **anterior + posterior tibial** at distal popliteus

**Flexion vs extension** — furthest from plateau at **60–90° flexion** · **hyperflexion** displaces it posteriorly ✓ · **hyperextension brings artery closer** + traction → injury during cuts

![Popliteal artery relations](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12751-labeled-anatomic-structures.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Direct laceration** — sharp dissection in posterior compartment

**Blunt** — **posterior retractor lateral to PCL** or **>1 cm** into posterior soft tissue · hyperextension traction

**Thrombosis**

**Safe practice** — single-prong posterior retractor **medial**, never lateral to PCL, ≤1 cm deep

| Retractor | Structure at risk |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Posterior, lateral to PCL / posterolateral to PCL insertion** | **Popliteal artery** (✗ Wrong options: biceps · lateral meniscus root · semimembranosus · peroneal n.) |
| Posterior, 1 cm, **medial** to PCL | Safe |
| **Lateral, around fibular head 3 cm** | **Common peroneal nerve** (within 3 cm of posterior fibular head) |
| **Medial along tibial plateau 3 cm** | **Saphenous nerve** stretch (2–3 cm posterior to medial tibia) |
| Lamina spreader medial | Not linked |

![Posterior retractor lateral to PCL](../../MCQ/attachments/q219781-illa-popartery.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Intra-op** — brisk bleeding · loss of pulses

**Post-op** — ischaemia · sensory change · **absent distal pulses** · **skin mottling** · severe pain + global motor loss

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Intra-op** — angiogram via vascular surgery

**Post-op** — **CT angiogram** for ischaemic signs\
✗ Wrong options: MR angiogram (delay)

## 5 Management

```
Suspected popliteal injury
├─ Intra-op bleeding / pulse loss → immediate vascular consult ± angiogram
└─ Post-op absent pulses → back to OR, vascular consult, intra-op arteriogram
       └─ Stent / bypass / endarterectomy ± prophylactic fasciotomy
```

### Management of absent pulses

**Asymmetric absent pulses + poor refill post-op** → ✓ **return to OR + vascular consult + intra-op arteriogram** (localise before exploring; stent possible)\
✗ Wrong options: observe 4 h · compartment pressures · MRA · blind exploration

**Valgus TKA, 5 h, pain, no DF/PF, asymmetric pulses after flexing + loosening dressing** → ✓ **vascular surgery consultation**\
✗ Wrong options: serial exams + EMG · ACB · peroneal exploration (global deficit) · thrombectomy without vascular decision

**Options (vascular)** — observation · **stent** · **bypass** · **endarterectomy** · **prophylactic fasciotomy** (reperfusion compartment syndrome)

**Complications** — compartment syndrome · amputation · mortality · ↑ LOS + cost

### Drain & transfusion

**Closed suction drain in primary TKA** → ✓ **↑ transfusion**\
✗ Wrong options: dehiscence · ↓ infection · ↓ hematoma return · ↓ pain

## X vs Y

| | **THA vascular risk** | **TKA vascular risk** |
|---|---|---|
| Vessel | External iliac (anterosuperior screw), obturator (TAL retractor), femoral (anterior retractor) | **Popliteal artery** (posterolateral to PCL) |
| Protective position | — | **Flexion** |

## How it's asked

**"Associated with closed suction drain?"** → **increased transfusion**

**"Popliteal artery anatomy?"** → **anterior to popliteal vein, 9 mm behind posterior tibial plateau at 90°**

**"Sharp Hohmann posterolateral to PCL — injury?"** → **popliteal artery**

**"Absent pulses in PACU, blunt popliteal injury — retractor?"** → **posterior retractor 1 cm deep, lateral to PCL**

**"Asymmetric absent pulses after TKA — next?"** → **OR + vascular consult + intra-op arteriogram**

**"Valgus TKA, global motor loss, asymmetric pulses after flexing knee?"** → **vascular surgery consultation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# TKA Wound Complications

*Recon · 63 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Risk factors (diabetes, obesity, smoking, nutrition)

| Category | Factors |
|---|---|
| **Systemic** | **Diabetes mellitus** · vascular disease · **inflammatory arthritis (RA)** · immunosuppressives / **steroids** · **tobacco** · renal + liver disease · **poor nutrition** (albumin <3.5 g/dL, TLC <1,500/µL) · perioperative **anaemia** · **morbid obesity (BMI >40)** |
| **Local** | **Previous incisions** · knee deformity · skin adhesions · poor local blood supply · traumatic arthritis |
| **Technique** | **Large subcutaneous flaps** · short incision → vigorous skin traction · not preserving subcutaneous fat layer · long surgical time · long tourniquet time (Orthobullets) |
| **Post-op** | **Haematoma** · infection · **early (first 4 days) flexion >40°** in a high-risk knee |

**Diabetes** — ✓ **most strongly associated** with early wound complication needing surgery (Galat: > inflammatory arthritis, smoking, PVD, steroids, BMI >30, previous open surgery)

**Tourniquet** — Orthobullets lists long tourniquet time · OB: tourniquet use + duration **not correlated** with wound complications — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: "tourniquet 70 min" as strongest factor

**Previous knee surgery** — **open** surgery = risk · esp. if a more **medial** incision used → **lateral wound necrosis** (may need gastrocnemius flap)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy

**DVT prophylaxis** — **aspirin** → **fewer** wound complications than LMWH / NOACs\
✗ Wrong options: aspirin as risk factor

**Early surgical intervention for wound** → ↑ major subsequent surgery (**5.3%**) + deep infection (**7.0%**) → aim for primary healing at index surgery

**Modifiable before surgery** — smoking cessation · nutritional optimisation · stop harmful immunomodulators · glucose control · weight loss

### Genes & lab markers – nutrition

| Marker | Malnutrition cut-off |
|---|---|
| **Albumin** | **<3.5 g/dL** |
| **Total lymphocyte count (TLC)** | **<1,500 /µL** (cells/mm³) |
| **Prealbumin** | **<15 mg/dL** |
| **Transferrin** | **<200 mg/dL** |

**TLC + prealbumin for a chronic wound** → ✓ **assess nutrition**\
✗ Wrong options: rule out infection · detect inflammation (ESR/CRP) · immune status (complement/immunoglobulins) · anaemia (Hb, Hct, RBC)

### Smoking

**Smoker vs non-smoker TKA** → ✓ **higher readmission** · smoking = single greatest predictor of **30-day readmission + reoperation** after outpatient TJA · ↑ deep + superficial SSI · ↑ **MUA** · ↑ wound breakdown, loosening\
✗ Wrong options: better SF-36 · better pain relief · no difference in SSI or MUA

**Cessation** — **4–6 wk before and after** TJA → complications ↓ **50%** (↑ blood O₂-carrying capacity)

**MUA risk factors (Issa)** — diabetes · hypercholesterolaemia · preop ROM **<100°** · TKA for osteonecrosis · smoking

### Obesity

**BMI >40 TKA** → ✓ **more post-op radiolucent lines** · ↑ complications · ↑ revision, inferior survivorship · poorer function\
✗ Wrong options: no difference in function / complications / revision

### AAOS 2016 knee OA surgical guideline

| Strength | Statement |
|---|---|
| **Strong** | Obese patients **less improvement** after TKA · **no difference** in pain/function with **patellar resurfacing** · **no difference CR vs PS** · **periarticular + peripheral nerve** anaesthesia ↓ pain + opiates |
| **Moderate** | **Diabetes** ↑ complications · **neuraxial** anaesthesia improves perioperative outcomes + complications |
| **Limited** | ✓ **Hepatitis C / cirrhosis ↑ complications** |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — multiply operated knee · prior infection

**Symptoms** — persistent knee pain · **persistent drainage beyond 7 days** (early post-op period)

**Exam** — wound breakdown · erythema · warmth · drainage · **peri-incisional eschar**

![Full-thickness peri-incisional necrosis after TKA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12752-f2-large.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Role |
|---|---|
| **Aspiration** | Exclude **deep infection** before wound surgery |
| **Radiographs** | Help rule out deep infection — look for **bone resorption** |
| **Bone scan** (radionuclide) | Infection suspected but **aspiration + serology not confirmatory** |
| **Angiography** | **Sural artery flow** in question **before gastrocnemius flap** |

## 5 Management

```
TKA wound complication
├─ Small superficial wound / not a surgical candidate → local wound care ± antibiotics
├─ Proximal necrosis over patella → local wound care + SKIN GRAFT
└─ Full-thickness necrosis → debridement + rotational flap
       ├─ Anterior / medial (patellar tendon, tubercle) → MEDIAL GASTROCNEMIUS
       └─ Lateral defect → LATERAL GASTROCNEMIUS
```

### Operative criteria – incision planning

| Rule | Value |
|---|---|
| Skin bridge between incisions | **>5–6 cm** |
| Crossing a previous incision | Never at an angle **<60°** |
| Which old incision | ✓ **Most lateral** usable — anterior knee superficial supply mainly from **medial geniculates** |

### Soft tissue coverage - gastrocnemius flap

**Exposed patellar tendon after superficial debridement, aspiration negative** → ✓ **medial gastrocnemius flap + skin graft**\
✗ Wrong options: split-thickness graft alone · wet-to-dry Dakin's · VAC to secondary intention · latissimus free flap

**Ries** — necrosis over **patella** → local care + **skin graft** · over **tibial tubercle / patellar tendon** → ✓ **muscle flap** (prevents extensor mechanism disruption + deep infection) · all TKAs salvaged

**Flap artery** — medial gastrocnemius = ✓ **medial sural artery** · lateral gastrocnemius = **lateral sural artery**\
✗ Wrong options: inferomedial geniculate (medial plateau + patella) · recurrent anterior tibial (patellar anastomosis) · profunda femoris (thigh) · descending branch of circumflex femoral (joins patellar anastomosis, not below knee)

**Reach** — proximally based → rotates up to **15 cm above the joint line** · low failure rate

**Muscle flap types (Markovich)** — **prophylactic** (before reconstruction) · **treating** (infected prosthesis, deficient cover — least successful) · **salvage** (early dehiscence / necrosis)

![Medial gastrocnemius rotational flap](../../MCQ/attachments/q218012-a-pmfaam17-gastrocnemius-part7.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Haemarthrosis – commonest early complication (TO)

**Commonest post-op complication of knee arthroplasty** → ✓ **haemarthrosis** (TO) · a little over **1%** · esp. after **lateral retinacular release** or **liberal synovectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · compartment syndrome · tourniquet palsy

**Wound complication sequelae** — deep infection · extensor mechanism disruption (exposed tendon) · ↑ reoperation + cost

## X vs Y

| | **Medial gastrocnemius flap** | **Lateral gastrocnemius flap** |
|---|---|---|
| Artery | **Medial sural** | **Lateral sural** |
| Defect covered | **Anterior + medial** (patellar tendon, tubercle) | **Lateral** |

| Test ordered | What it assesses |
|---|---|
| TLC, albumin, prealbumin, transferrin | **Nutrition** |
| ESR, CRP | Inflammation / infection |
| Complement, immunoglobulins | Immune status |
| Hb, Hct, RBC | Anaemia |

## How it's asked

**"Exposed patellar tendon after debridement of TKA skin necrosis — next?"** → **medial gastrocnemius flap + skin graft**\
✗ Wrong options: STSG · VAC · Dakin's · latissimus

**"Artery of the rotational flap for distal medial necrosis?"** → **medial sural artery**

**"Comorbidity most associated with early wound complication needing surgery?"** → **type 2 diabetes**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · aspirin · BMI 31 · tourniquet 70 min

**"BMI >40 effect on TKA?"** → **more post-op radiolucent lines**

**"Active smoker vs non-smoker after TKA?"** → **higher readmission risk**

**"Purpose of TLC + prealbumin for chronic TKA wound?"** → **assess nutrition status**

**"AAOS 2016 statement WITHOUT strong recommendation?"** → **hepatitis C / cirrhosis ↑ complications** (limited)

**"Commonest post-op complication of knee arthroplasty?"** → **haemarthrosis** (TO)

**"Multiple previous incisions — which to use?"** → **most lateral; bridge >5–6 cm; cross at ≥60°**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Unicompartmental Knee Replacement

*Recon · 64 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **5%** of knee arthroplasties · **medial** compartment most common

**Classic candidate** — older (**>60**), lower-demand, thin (**<82 kg**) · only **6%** of patients meet these criteria with no contraindication · indications expanding to younger, heavier, more active patients

### Implant types – fixed vs mobile bearing

| | **Fixed-bearing** (Miller-Galante, Zuk) | **Mobile-bearing** (Oxford) |
|---|---|---|
| Design | PE **locked** in tray · **flat** tibial surface | Fully **congruent, unconstrained** PE glides between components |
| Contact | **Flat-on-round** → point loading, high contact stress | Large contact area → low stress |
| PE wear | Theoretically **↑** (fails in **later** years) | **↓** · excellent survivorship to 2nd decade |
| Dislocation | **Impossible** | ✓ **Bearing dislocation (spin-out)** + impingement (fails in **early** years) |
| Technique | Easier · forgiving of laxity / small gap mismatch | **Technically demanding** · flexion gap must **equal** extension gap · stability from **MCL** tension |
| ACL deficiency | Controversial | **Contraindicated** (spin-out) |
| Loosening | Component loosening more common | Poor bone preparation / thermal necrosis |

**Mobile-bearing specific disadvantage** → ✓ **higher risk of bearing dislocation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PCL sacrifice · limited femoral sizing · ↑ PE wear

**After UKA + twisting injury — true?** → ✓ **mobile-bearing UKA has more dislocation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: lateral UKA more linear wear · medial more dislocation · fixed less linear wear

### Anteromedial OA (AMOA) concept (TO)

**AMOA** — wear confined to **anterior + middle thirds** of the medial tibial plateau · needs a **functional ACL** + competent **MCL** · varus is **intra-articular + correctable** once cartilage height restored ✓\
✗ Wrong options: justifies fixed bearing · eliminates PF symptoms · allows PCL sacrifice

**Why** — intact ACL keeps femur anterior on tibia in flexion · ACL deficient → tibia subluxes anteriorly → femur articulates further back → **posterior tibial wear**

**True AMOA** — **bone-on-bone in extension** · joint space partly preserved in **deep flexion** (posterior cartilage spared) · **full-thickness lateral cartilage**

### Advantages

| vs **TKA** | vs **Osteotomy (HTO)** |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Faster rehab + recovery** · ↓ blood loss · ↓ morbidity · ↓ cost · ✓ **↓ PJI + wound complications** · ↓ MI, PE, VTE, mortality · ✓ **near-native kinematics** (ACL, PCL, other compartments kept) · ✓ **better ROM** · smaller incision → less pain, shorter stay · bone stock preserved | Faster rehab · better cosmesis · ↑ initial success · fewer short-term complications · **lasts longer** · **easier conversion to TKA** |

**Disadvantages vs TKA** — **higher revision rate** · lower survivorship · ↑ aseptic loosening · **technically more demanding** · **OA progression** (unique) · **2×** early (90-day) periprosthetic fracture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Lateral compartment** — confirm **full-thickness cartilage** on **valgus stress view** (TO)

**Lateral view of medial OA** — anterior / central tibial wear = AMOA ✓ · ✓ **posterior-third tibial wear = ACL incompetent** (TO) → contraindication to standard (mobile-bearing) UKA\
✗ Wrong options: PCL incompetent · global OA · MCL contracted

**Mobile bearing** — contains a **radiographic marker** → shows bearing dislocation

**Stress fracture** — X-ray may be normal · ✓ **bone scan uptake under the tibial component**

**PFA work-up** — standing AP + lateral · **Rosenberg** (45° flexion PA) · **skyline** (Merchant) · standing full-leg

### Diagnostic criteria – Kozinn & Scott vs Oxford (TO)

| Criterion | **Kozinn & Scott 1989** (fixed bearing) | **Oxford** (mobile bearing) |
|---|---|---|
| Age & weight | **>60 yr, <82 kg (180 lb)** | **No limits** |
| Activity | Low demand / sedentary | No restriction |
| Deformity | **Varus <10°, valgus <5°** | Varus intra-articular + **manually correctable to neutral** |
| Flexion | **>90°, contracture <5°** | **>90°, contracture <15°** |
| Ligaments | Intact ACL | Intact **ACL + competent MCL** |
| PF joint | No PFJ arthritis | Ignored unless **severe lateral facet grooving + bone loss + anterior pain** |
| Lateral compartment | Pristine | **Full-thickness cartilage** (valgus stress view) |

**No longer a barrier (modern / Oxford)** → ✓ **high BMI** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: lateral cartilage loss · fixed flexion deformity · inflammatory arthritis (still barriers)

## 5 Management

```
Isolated compartment knee OA, failed nonop
├─ Inflammatory / multicompartment / unstable → TKA
├─ Young, active, mild OA, malaligned → OSTEOTOMY
│      └─ Lateral OA in correctable valgus knee → VARUS-PRODUCING DFO
└─ Older, bone-on-bone, intact ACL + MCL → UKA
       ├─ Medial tibiofemoral → MEDIAL UKA
       └─ Isolated patellofemoral → PFA
```

### Indications & contraindications

**Indications (TO)** — isolated medial or lateral OA · **osteonecrosis** · age **>60** · weight **<82 kg** · deformity **<15°**, passively correctable to neutral · flexion contracture **<5°** · ROM **>90°**

| Contraindication | Threshold / note |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Inflammatory arthritis** (RA, psoriatic) | **Absolute** — involves all compartments → progression (TO, OB) |
| **Fixed varus** | **>10°** |
| **Fixed valgus** | **>5°** → ✓ fixed valgus **10°** = absolute CI (TO) |
| **Flexion contracture** | **>5–10°** (Orthobullets) · **>10°** (TO list) · **≥15°** (TO) · **15°** = CI (OB) · >5° relative (OB) — read the options |
| **Arc of motion** | **<90°** |
| **ACL deficiency** | **Absolute for lateral UKA** · controversial for medial fixed-bearing · **contraindicated for mobile-bearing** (spin-out) · TO: **relative** — reconstruct ACL at same sitting |
| **Previous meniscectomy, other compartment** | e.g. complete **lateral** meniscectomy for a medial UKA |
| **Tricompartmental OA** | Diffuse / global pain |
| Opposite-compartment cartilage erosion | Weight-bearing area |
| **Grade IV PF chondrosis** | Anterior knee pain · exposed subchondral bone under patella |
| Young, high-activity, heavy labourer | Relative |
| **Joint subluxation ≥5 mm** | Relative (OB) |
| **Obesity** | Relative historically (BMI >30, OB) · no longer a barrier (TO, OB) |

**NOT contraindications** — **SONK / osteonecrosis of MFC** (UKA works: 90% good at 5.5 yr, **96%** survivorship at 12 yr) · prior **ACL reconstruction** with functioning graft (**grade 1A Lachman**) · prior **medial meniscal transplant** · **contralateral knee OA** · asymptomatic / medial PF chondromalacia · **age >70** · age <60 with modern implants · **prior HTO** · previous trauma / plateau ORIF (scar only) · **fixed varus 5°** · **9° varus** · BMI 36, weight 80 kg · **Q-angle 13°** in a male (normal)\
✗ Wrong options: traps

**ACL found torn at arthrotomy for planned UKA (55 yr)** → ✓ **reconstruct ACL + proceed with UKA** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: continue + intervene later · convert to TKR and tell after · simply fixed-bearing UKA

**Most important relative CI to mobile-bearing UKA** → ✓ **prior failed ACL reconstruction** (ACL deficiency → spin-out)\
✗ Wrong options: medial PF OA · age >70 · flexion contracture 10° (OK if <15°) · previous HTO

**Relative CI** → ✓ **Outerbridge IV MFC + grade IIb Lachman** (non-functioning ACL)\
✗ Wrong options: meniscal transplant · ACLR with grade Ia Lachman · Outerbridge IV medial alone (that is the indication)

### UKA vs HTO – patient selection

| | **UKA** | **HTO or UKA** | **HTO** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Age | >55 yr | 55–65 yr | <65 yr |
| Activity | Low demand | Moderately active | Active |
| BMI | <30 | Any | — |
| Alignment | 0–5° | 5–10° | 5–15° |
| AP instability | None–grade I | None–grade I | Any |
| ML instability | None–grade I | None–grade I | None–grade II |
| ROM | Arc 90°, FC <5° | Arc 100°, FC <5° | Arc 120°, FC <5° |
| Arthrosis | Any | Ahlbäck II | Ahlbäck I–II |

**HTO prerequisites (TO)** — <60 yr, active · varus **5–15°** · < grade 3 medial involvement · intact lateral + PF · flexion **>120°** · no laxity · expanded: posterolateral laxity, varus thrust, ACL deficiency (slope change)

**Kissing bone-on-bone lesion, good ligaments, one compartment** → **UKA** over HTO · **KL 4 + other compartments / instability / gross malalignment** → **TKA**

**UKA** — older, less active, **minimal varus, more severe OA**, little instability · **HTO** — younger, active, **milder OA, more malalignment**, AP instability

**40 yr, lateral OA after lateral meniscectomy, correctable 5° valgus, stable** → ✓ **varus-producing DFO** · osteotomy CIs: inflammatory, cruciate deficiency, FFD **>15°**, flexion **<90°**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus HTO · TKA · arthroscopic debridement · AMZ

**68 yr, isolated medial Outerbridge IV, worse after arthroscopy** → ✓ **medial UKA**\
✗ Wrong options: opening-wedge HTO (younger, milder) · bicompartmental (medial + PF) · PFA · lateral UKA

**71 yr, BMI 28, mild varus, stable, wants to kneel** → ✓ **UKA** — best ROM\
✗ Wrong options: HTO · CR / PS / rotating-platform TKA (equivalent ROM to each other)

### Technique

**Alignment** — ✓ **undercorrect mechanical axis by 2–3°** · overcorrection overloads unresurfaced compartment → OA progression · Orthobullets: correct varus to **1–5° valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: overcorrect into valgus 2–3°

**Steps** — remove peripheral + **notch osteophytes** · minimal bone resection · **avoid extensive releases** (never release the **MCL**) · avoid edge loading · correct ML position → no tibial spine impingement · **avoid a varus tibial cut** (↑ loosening) · care with proximal tibial **guide pins** (stress fracture)

**Gaps (mobile bearing)** — flexion gap set by **posterior femoral cut** · flexion = extension gap · tibial slope parallels native **3–7°**

**Bearing unstable in mid-flexion, tight in full extension** → ✓ **inadequate posterior femoral resection** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: tibial over-resection · excess slope · MCL over-release (lax in both flexion + extension)

**Small incision without patellar dislocation** → ✓ **faster recovery** (2× standard UKA, 3× TKA)\
✗ Wrong options: conversion option · better positioning · better ultimate ROM · ↑ 10-yr survival

### Patellofemoral arthroplasty – indications & failure (TO)

**Ideal PFA candidate** — **<65 yr**, debilitating **isolated PF OA**, no patellar malalignment · pain localised to PFJ, failed NSAIDs / injection · flexion **120°**, FFD **<10°** · not if **valgus >8°** or **varus >5°** unless osteotomy first

**Not an indication** → ✓ **BMI >30** (implant overload) (TO) · inflammatory arthropathy = CI · **tibiofemoral OA** = principal CI

| | **Inlay** (1st gen, resurfacing) | **Onlay** (2nd gen, anterior cut) |
|---|---|---|
| Trochlea | Replaces cartilage only; sits in native trochlea | Uses TKA anterior femoral cut; replaces **entire anterior trochlea** |
| Main problem | **Patellar instability** (short-term failure) | Instability minimised |

**Commonest failure (overall + long-term)** → ✓ **progression of tibiofemoral OA** (TO, OB) · up to **22%** at 5–15 yr\
✗ Wrong options: PF instability (inlay short-term) · patellar tendon rupture · aseptic loosening (<0.5% at 7 yr)

**Early PFA complications** — higher than TKA · persistent anterior pain (extensor malalignment, **oversized femoral component**) · catching / snapping · extensor rupture

**TF progression after PFA** → **convert to TKA** · PFA **not** superior to medial/lateral UKA\
✗ Wrong options: add medial UKA

## 6 Complications

### Failure modes (OA progression, loosening, bearing dislocation)

| Timing | Commonest cause |
|---|---|
| **Early (<5 yr)** | ✓ **Aseptic loosening** — **25–45.3%** of early failures (✗ Wrong options: OA progression · PE wear · stress fracture ~2%) |
| **Mid (5–10 yr)** | **OA progression (38%)** |
| **Late (>10 yr)** | ✓ **OA progression (40%)** · then aseptic loosening **29%**, PE wear **10%** |
| **Overall (TO)** | Contralateral arthritis **39.5%** · aseptic loosening **25.4%** · instability **15.3%** · PE wear **14%** · arthrofibrosis **2.5%** · pain **1.8%** · infection **1.5%** |

**Late (>5 yr) causes (Orthobullets)** — OA progression (idiopathic, **over-correction**, more with mobile-bearing) · component failure (**under-correction** overload) · component loosening (fixed-bearing) · **patellar impingement** on femoral component · PE wear

**Unique to UKA vs TKA** → ✓ **OA progression**\
✗ Wrong options: periprosthetic fracture · PJI · liner dissociation · PE wear (all shared)

**Price & Svard (682 Oxford UKA)** — 29 revisions: **10 OA progression** · 9 loosening · 5 infection · 2 bearing dislocation · 3 unexplained pain

**Survivorship** — fixed-bearing 10 yr **87.4–96%** (failure ~**1%/yr** first decade) → 2nd decade **79–90%** · mobile-bearing **93% at 15 yr** · lateral = medial long-term · UKA revision results worse than TKA revision

![Overcorrection → lateral compartment OA after medial UKA](../../MCQ/attachments/q5665-illa-overcorrection.jpg)

### Aseptic loosening

**Risk** — **all-PE tibia** · **fixed-bearing** · component malposition (**varus tibia**) · poor initial fixation · weight **>82 kg** · tibial side more common

**43 yr, BMI 43, loose varus tibial component at 1 yr, normal ESR + CRP** → ✓ **revise UKA to TKA** (no aspiration needed when markers normal)\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · explant + spacer · tibial-only or both-component UKA revision

### Bearing dislocation (mobile bearing)

**Cause** — almost always **technical error**: **gap mismatch** · **MCL damage / release** · impingement by retained **cement or osteophytes** · component malalignment

| Technical error | Effect |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Flexion gap 1–2 mm looser than extension** (bearing sized to tight extension gap) | **Posterior spin-out in deep flexion** (squatting) — most classic |
| Flexion gap tighter than extension | Stiffness + deep-flexion pain · no dislocation |
| Retained **anterior** femoral / notch osteophytes | Push bearing **posteriorly** in **terminal extension** |
| Retained **posterior** condylar bone | Pushes bearing **anteriorly** in deep flexion |
| **MCL** injury / pie-crusting | Medial opening → bearing escapes |
| **Excess posterior slope (>10°)** | Bearing slides posteriorly in flexion |
| Too little / reverse slope | Pushes bearing anteriorly |
| Internally rotated tibial tray | Edge-loading, twists out |

**Presentation** — pain, giving way on stairs, **clicking**, effusion, ↓ ROM, stable ligaments, normal markers → ✓ **bearing dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: PJI · ACL rupture · lateral OA · lateral meniscal tear

**Treatment** — closed reduction · open exploration + **bearing exchange** · convert to fixed UKA · convert to TKA

**Anteromedial knee pain after mobile-bearing medial UKA** → ✓ **anterior impingement** (TO) — technique / malposition\
✗ Wrong options: bearing dislocation · loosening · instability

![Dislocated mobile bearing after medial UKA](../../MCQ/attachments/q217342-a-uka.jpg)

### Medial vs lateral UKA (TO)

**Lateral UKA** — traditionally ↑ dislocation + failure · LCL allows **6.7 mm** lateral distraction in flexion (vs **2 mm** medial) · lateral tibial condyle **convex** (medial **flat**) · lateral femoral condyle subluxes posteriorly in high flexion · newer designs → **equivalent** survival, pain relief, function

### Tibial plateau stress fracture

**Features** — **always tibia** · high activity + heavy patient (**>200 lb**) · **pain-free interval → spontaneous activity pain** · **blood on aspiration** (RBC 45,000, WBC 6,500) · normal X-ray + **bone scan uptake under component**\
✗ Wrong options: infection (WBC >20,000, ↑ ESR/CRP) · loosening · crystals · synovial entrapment · pes bursitis · CRPS

**Technical risk factors** — guide pin **penetrating posterior tibial cortex** · **peripheral medial cortical pin** · **re-drilling** guide holes (≥3 holes) · **undersized tibial component** · **vertical sagittal saw cut too deep** past the horizontal cut (TO)

**NOT a cause of the post-op stress fracture** → ✓ **excessive impaction force** → **intra-operative** fracture, seen at once

**Rate** — ~**2%** of UKA failures · nondisplaced → protected weight bearing · displaced → ORIF or revision

![Medial tibial plateau stress fracture under UKA](../../MCQ/attachments/q213791-b-53c.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **UKA** | **TKA** |
|---|---|---|
| Recovery / rehab | ✓ **Faster** | Slower |
| Kinematics | ✓ **Near-native** | Altered |
| ROM | **Better** | Less |
| Blood loss, pain meds, stay | Less | More |
| PJI, wound, stiffness | **Lower** | Higher |
| VTE, MI, mortality | Lower | Higher |
| Early periprosthetic fracture (90 d) | **2× higher** | ✓ Lower |
| Revision / survivorship | **Higher revision**, lower survival | ✓ Lower revision, better survival |
| Aseptic loosening | Higher | Lower |
| OA progression | **Yes** — commonest revision cause | No |
| Technical demand | **More** | Less |
| Mobile bearings | Successful | Successful |

| | **Early failure (<5 yr)** | **Late failure (>10 yr)** |
|---|---|---|
| Commonest | **Aseptic loosening** | **OA progression** |
| Typical design | Mobile bearing (dislocation, impingement) | Fixed bearing (PE wear) |

## How it's asked

**"Contraindication to UKR?"** → **RA with medial compartment involvement** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · medial OA · previous patellectomy

**"Absolute contraindication to UKA?"** → **fixed valgus 10°** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: FC 10° · fixed varus 10° · ACL deficiency

**"ACL found torn at UKA arthrotomy — next?"** → **reconstruct ACL + proceed with UKA** (TO)

**"Posterior-third medial tibial wear on lateral X-ray — implication?"** → **ACL incompetent** (TO)

**"Traditional criterion no longer a barrier (Oxford)?"** → **high BMI** (TO)

**"Significance of AMOA?"** → **deformity is correctable** (TO)

**"All are contraindications to medial UKA EXCEPT?"** → **osteonecrosis of the MFC**

**"Strongest contraindication to UKA, 65 yr active woman?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI ≥30 · fixed varus 5° · FC 5° · contralateral OA

**"Failed UKA at 4 yr — preoperative factor?"** → **inflammatory arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: old ACLR · SONK · 80 kg · 5° varus

**"Relative contraindication to UKA?"** → **Outerbridge IV MFC + grade IIb Lachman**

**"Not a UKA candidate?"** → **50 yr with psoriatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: BMI 36 · 9° varus · Q-angle 13° · prior medial meniscectomy

**"Most important relative CI to mobile-bearing UKA?"** → **prior failed ACL reconstruction**

**"58 yr, old plateau ORIF, grade 1A Lachman, FC 15° — CI?"** → **flexion contracture**

**"Lateral OA, 40 yr, correctable 5° valgus?"** → **varus-producing DFO**

**"Isolated medial Outerbridge IV, 68 yr — best treatment?"** → **medial UKA**

**"Wants maximum ROM / kneeling, 71 yr?"** → **UKA**

**"Advantage of small incision without patellar eversion?"** → **improved rate of recovery**

**"Benefit of UKA over TKA?"** → **faster rehabilitation**\
✗ Wrong options: better 1-yr outcomes · better 10-yr survival · better alignment

**"True of UKA vs TKA?"** → **UKA more closely replicates normal kinematics**\
✗ Wrong options: TKA higher reoperation · mobile bearings fail in one

**"True of UKA?"** → **lower PJI risk than TKA**\
✗ Wrong options: mobile-bearing for ACL deficient · age <45 · overcorrect 2–3° · stress fracture commonest failure

**"Potential advantage of UKA over TKA?"** → **faster rehab + recovery**\
✗ Wrong options: lower revision · less loosening · higher survivorship · less demanding

**"Risk after UKA but not TKA?"** → **osteoarthritis progression**

**"After TKA (vs UKA) expect?"** → **decreased risk of early periprosthetic fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: reduced mortality · higher revision · lower PJI · shorter stay

**"Most likely reason for revision of medial UKA?"** → **progression of osteoarthritis**

**"Most common cause of late (>10 yr) mobile-bearing UKA failure?"** → **progression of OA**

**"Most common cause of early UKA failure?"** → **aseptic loosening**

**"Most accurate statement on onlay PFA?"** → **commonest long-term failure = OA progression to other compartments**

**"Loose varus tibial UKA component, BMI 43, normal ESR + CRP?"** → **revise UKA to TKA**

**"Giving way, clicking, effusion 12 months after medial UKA?"** → **bearing dislocation**

**"Anteromedial pain after mobile-bearing UKA?"** → **anterior impingement** (TO)

**"Posterior bearing dislocation in deep flexion — technical error?"** → **flexion gap 2 mm looser than extension** (TO)

**"Bearing unstable mid-flexion, tight in extension?"** → **inadequate posterior femoral resection** (TO)

**"Pain 8 months after UKA, normal X-ray, uptake on bone scan?"** → **stress fracture**

**"Heavy patient, 4 months after UKA, bloody aspirate WBC 6,500?"** → **stress fracture**

**"Errors causing post-op tibial stress fracture EXCEPT?"** → **excessive force impacting the tibial component**

**"Not an indication for PFA?"** → **BMI >30 in a 50-year-old** (TO)

**"Commonest cause of PFA failure overall?"** → **progression to tibiofemoral OA** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Wear & Osteolysis Basic Science

*Recon · 65 / 65*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Osteolysis** — **histiocytic (macrophage) response to wear debris** → bone loss · same biology as aseptic loosening, faster + focal

**4 steps** — (1) **particulate debris formation** → (2) **macrophage-activated osteoclastogenesis + osteolysis** → (3) **prosthesis micromotion** → (4) **particulate debris dissemination**

**Aseptic loosening** — ✓ commonest cause of **late** THA failure · supra-acetabular osteolysis usually **>10 yr** after index surgery · ↓ since HXLPE

**Closest correlate of osteolysis** → ✓ **linear wear rate** of the bearing couple · activity, cup position, PE oxidation, bearing all act by ↑ wear\
✗ Wrong options: weight · head material · offset

### Wear mechanisms (adhesion, abrasion, backside wear)

| Mechanism | What happens | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Adhesive** | Surfaces bond under load → weaker material (PE) pulled off | ✓ **Most common / most important** in THA osteolysis (TO) |
| **Abrasive (two-body)** | Harder, rough surface cuts softer — **cheese grater** | Rough head scratches PE · ploughing, cutting, fragmentation |
| **Third-body** | Trapped particles between bearing surfaces | Type of abrasive wear · **PMMA** mainly (bone, metal beads, HA, Al oxide, BaSO₄, taper corrosion debris) |
| **Fatigue / delamination** | Cyclic subsurface stress → microcracks, pits → large particles | ✓ **TKA** PE failure (TO) — max stress just **below** surface |
| **Fretting** | Small oscillation between surfaces not meant to move | **Backside wear** (shell–liner, tray–insert) |
| **Corrosive** | Sliding in corrosive / oxidative environment | — |

**Hip vs knee PE** — hip: ✓ **adhesion + abrasion** at the surface · knee: **fatigue cracking + delamination**\
✗ Wrong options: fretting · crevice corrosion (under screw heads) · oxidative for hip

**Third-body wear — NOT true** → ✓ **"particle size more important than hardness"** (TO) · hardness / composition, concentration matter more · implant hardness **<0.8×** particle → severe abrasion · **>1.2×** → resistant · vitamin E PE has better surface properties

**Wear modes (McKellop)** — **1**: bearing surfaces as intended · **2**: bearing vs non-bearing surface · **3**: third body between bearing surfaces · **4**: two non-bearing surfaces

**Wear stages** — primary (run-in) → secondary (steady state; most of life) → tertiary (rapid failure)

**Determinants of wear** → **wear factor**, **sliding distance**, **load** — ✓ **all independent determinants** (TO)

**PE cup wear pattern sequence** → ✓ **foldings first** (loading force) → **ripples** (loading-shear; adhesive; slowly worn cups) → **fibrils** (abrasive; rapidly worn cups) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: fibrils · ripples · ditches

**Optimally positioned cup — wear site** → ✓ **anterosuperior quadrant** (TO) · excess inclination ↑ edge wear · greatly anteverted cup → posterior (neck impingement in extension)

### Linear vs volumetric wear

| | **Linear wear** | **Volumetric wear** |
|---|---|---|
| Definition | **Head penetration into the liner** (thickness lost) | Total volume removed → ✓ **number of particles** |
| Measure | **Livermore** — shortest distance head centre → acetabular surface | **V = πr²w** (∝ **radius²** of head) |
| Main factor | Orthobullets: independent of head size · TO: **22 mm > 32 mm** — read the options | ✓ **Head size** · **total contact area** (TO) |
| Failure | Wear-through of PE cup | **Osteolysis** |
| Threshold | **>0.1 mm/yr** → osteolysis + loosening (rare below) | — |

**Head size** — **22 mm**: ↑ linear, ↓ volumetric, ↑ central wear + dislocation · **32 mm**: ↓ linear, ↑ volumetric, thinner PE, ↑ ROM · **28 mm** = compromise → most systems (TO)

**Conventional PE** — 32 mm heads significantly more volumetric wear than 22 or 26/28 mm · **1 mm ↑ head size → ~10% ↑ volumetric wear**

**HXLPE** — wear **independent of head size 22–46 mm** → 32 vs 28 mm = ✓ **equivalent wear** · head **>40 mm** no proven longevity gain\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ dislocation · ↓ ROM · ↓ osteolysis · ↑ periprosthetic fracture

![Jump distance rises with head size](../../MCQ/attachments/q4556-illa-jump-distance.jpg)

### Bearing wear rates

| Bearing | Wear rate / particles |
|---|---|
| **Conventional UHMWPE** | **0.1–0.2 mm/yr** (✗ Wrong options: 0.01–0.02) |
| **HXLPE** | Lower · smaller particles · penetration **0.003 vs 0.051 mm/yr** (McCalden) |
| **Ceramic-on-ceramic** | ✓ **Lowest** of any couple — **0.5–2.5 µm / component / yr** |
| **Ceramic-on-PE** | 0–150 µm |
| **Metal-on-metal** | **2.5–5.0 µm/yr** · **nanometre** particles, smaller than MoP |

**Order** — **CoC < MoM < MoP** (TO)

**Conflict** — OB3311 calls **0.02–0.04 mm/yr** "conventional THA" wear (so 0.03 mm/yr ≈ conventional) · Orthobullets/OB218172: conventional UHMWPE **0.1–0.2 mm/yr** — read the options

**New liner at 0.03 mm/yr (RSA, 4 yr)** → ✓ **osteolysis + loosening minimal if rate continues**\
✗ Wrong options: greater than conventional · positioning irrelevant · significantly lower than conventional · better satisfaction

### Risk factors for wear

**Patient** — **age <50** · **male** · high activity · high BMI

**Implant / surgical** — PE thickness **<6 mm** · head **>32 mm** · ↑ contact stress · **cup inclination >45°** · component malalignment\
✗ Wrong options: >6 mm

**TKA, PE gamma-sterilised in air** — ↑ **shelf age** · younger patient · male · **rough tibial baseplate**\
✗ Wrong options: **PCL-retaining design** (not a risk)

### Particle type & size

**Commonest particle** — ✓ **UHMWPE** (up to **90%** of debris volume; colourless, mostly **spheroid**) · also PMMA, Al oxide, Zr oxide, CoCr, Ti, third body

**Size implicated** — ✓ **<1 µm (submicron)**, **0.1–1 µm** · mean PE **0.5 µm**, metal **0.7 µm** (Maloney) · most stimulatory **0.2–7/8 µm** · most particles **<5 µm**, random shape (TO) · active **0.5–10 µm** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 10, 100, 1000 µm

**Size vs type** — **size more important than type** for biological activity (Orthobullets)

**Severity of response ∝** → ✓ **particle number** (dose-dependent)\
✗ Wrong options: size · composition · molecular weight

## 2 Mechanism

### Osteolysis biology (macrophages, RANKL, cytokines, particle size)

```
Submicron wear particle
├─ Macrophage phagocytosis / toll-like receptor contact → ↑ NF-κB
│      └─ TNF-α, IL-1, IL-6, IL-8, PGE2, MCP-1 → ↑ RANK + RANKL
└─ Osteoclast precursor + RANKL (from osteoblasts) → OSTEOCLAST BONE RESORPTION
       └─ Osteolysis → micromotion → more wear → LOOSENING
```

| Bearing / process | Cell driving response |
|---|---|
| **PE debris (MoP, CoP)** | ✓ **Macrophage (histiocyte)** — also fibroblasts, giant cells |
| **Metal-on-metal** | ✓ **Lymphocyte (T cell)** — type IV hypersensitivity, ALVAL |
| Infection | Neutrophil |
| Eosinophilic granuloma | Langerhans-type histiocytes (not wear) |

| **Promote osteolysis** | **Inhibit osteolysis** |
|---|---|
| ✓ **TNF-α** (key; from macrophages) · **IL-1** · **IL-6** · IL-8 · **PGE2** (COX-2) · PDGF · **RANKL** · **VEGF** · MCP-1 (recruits monocytes) · osteoclast activating factor · acid phosphatase · GM-CSF → ROS (H₂O₂, oxide radicals) | **OPG** · ✓ **interferon-γ** · **IL-4** · **IL-10** · calcitonin |

| Pro-osteolytic target | Inhibitor |
|---|---|
| RANKL | **Denosumab** (mAb) · OPG |
| TNF-α | **Etanercept** (decoy receptor) |
| COX-2 → PGE2 → IL-6 | **Celecoxib** / NSAIDs |
| IL-6 | **Tocilizumab** (mAb) |
| **IL-1** | **Anakinra** (receptor antagonist) (✗ Wrong options: "IL-10, anakinra") (IL-10 itself inhibits osteolysis) |
| Osteoclast | Bisphosphonates |

**RANK–RANKL–OPG** — RANKL made by **osteoblasts** (✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts) · RANK on **osteoclast precursors** · **OPG binds RANKL** (✗ Wrong options: RANK) · interleukins from **macrophages** (✗ Wrong options: osteoclasts) · RANK/RANKL transcription **↑** (✗ Wrong options: ↓) · TNF-α ↑ RANK

**UHMWPE-induced osteolysis** → ✓ **both VEGF and RANKL ↑** (VEGF enhances RANK/RANKL)

![Cytokine cascade and inhibitors in periprosthetic osteolysis](../../MCQ/attachments/q5883-illa-cytokine.jpg)

### Micromotion & dissemination

**Micromotion** — osteolysis → micromotion → ↑ particle wear + loosening · joint-fluid pressure → bone resorption

**Effective joint space** — anywhere joint fluid reaches bone · fluid + particles follow **pressure gradients / path of least resistance** · ↑ **hydrostatic pressure** (inflammation) spreads debris · above cup, **through screw holes**, down the stem · cementless cup → **expansile** lysis from implant–bone interface

**Pelvic lysis around cup screws 10 yr after THA** → ✓ **PE particles tracking through the effective joint space**\
✗ Wrong options: osteosarcoma · galvanic corrosion · sepsis · occult fracture

**Systemic** — ↑ serum metal ions with MoM · **no clear link** to neoplastic, toxic or metabolic disease\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ sarcoma

![Osteolysis behind the cup via screw holes](../../MCQ/attachments/q1032-a-osteolysis.jpg)

### Osseointegration & micromotion (TO)

| Micromotion | Tissue formed |
|---|---|
| ✓ **<20 µm** | Predominantly **bone** (ideal) |
| **40–150 µm** | Bone + fibrous tissue |
| **>150 µm** | **Fibrous tissue** |

**Osseointegration (Albrektsson 1981)** — lamellar bone on implant without fibrous tissue · **4–12 wk**, continues up to **3 yr**

**Engh signs of osseointegration** — no radiolucent lines around porous surface · **spot welds** (new bone bridging endosteum → porous coating)

## 3 Presentation

**Osteolysis** — often **clinically silent** until pathological fracture · **start-up pain** with loosening · groin pain (cup) · thigh pain (stem)

**Labs** — aseptic: **ESR + CRP normal** · **urine N-telopeptide ↑**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Finding |
|---|---|
| ✓ **Urinary N-telopeptide** | **↑** — type I collagen breakdown = bone turnover (✗ Wrong options: ↓ in osteolysis) |
| Serum ALP, urine hydroxyproline, deoxypyridinoline | ↑ in high turnover (Paget's) |
| ESR / CRP | **Normal** in aseptic osteolysis |
| Urine cAMP ↑ | Pseudohypoparathyroidism type 2 (wrong option) |
| Urine phosphoethanolamine | Hypophosphatasia (wrong option) |
| Urine Bence Jones protein | Myeloma (wrong option) |
| Serum bone sialoprotein | Mineralised tissue component (wrong option) |
| ✓ **Serum cobalt >1 ppb on two samples** | Diagnostic of **MACC-associated ALTR** (✗ Wrong options: >0.5 ppb) · Co ↑ more than Cr |
| MoM serum ions ↑ | **Cup abduction >55°** · **smaller component** |

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding / role |
|---|---|
| **Plain X-ray** | Monitors progression · ✓ **underestimates** size, misses small lesions · femur: **endosteal scalloping** = hallmark (TO) · round lysis **behind cup / around screws** |
| **Livermore method** | Max head penetration (manual) |
| **Computer-assisted** | 2-D radiographs → 3-D wear estimate |
| ✓ **RSA** | **Most accurate + precise** wear / migration measure · **tantalum beads** in bone · paired radiographs → 3-D position |
| ✓ **CT** | **Preferred for extent** of osteolysis · 3-D lesion + component position · surgical planning |

**Best study of a new bearing's wear** → ✓ **prospective trial with RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: serial X-rays · annual CT · edge-detection radiographs

![Radiostereometric analysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5001-radiosteriometric-analysis.jpg)

### Loosening signs – cemented femoral stem (TO)

| Sign | Note |
|---|---|
| Radiolucency stem–cement, **Gruen zone 1** (superolateral) | Debonding, early deformation |
| Radiolucency cement–bone | Loosening |
| **Subsidence** of stem ± mantle | Loosening |
| Stem shifts into **varus** | Loosening |
| Cement fragmentation, **zone 7** (superomedial) | Loosening |
| Cement fracture near tip, **zone 4** | Loosening |
| Stem **deformation / fracture** | Loosening |
| ✗ Wrong option: **parallel radiodense line around stem** | **Not** loosening |

### Barrack cementation grade (TO)

| Grade | Finding |
|---|---|
| **A** | Complete fill — **white-out**, no radiolucency |
| **B** | Radiolucency **≤50%** of cement–bone interface |
| **C** | ✓ Radiolucency **50–99%** or incomplete mantle |
| **D** | **100%** radiolucency and/or **no cement beyond stem tip** |

### Cementless stem loosening

**Subsidence + thick distal pedestal + no calcar stress shielding, 10 yr** → ✓ **femoral loosening** (failed ingrowth)\
✗ Wrong options: acetabular / femoral osteolysis alone · acetabular loosening (radiolucent lines + cup migration)

## 5 Management

### Highly cross-linked polyethylene

```
UHMWPE resin (MW 2–6 million)
├─ Shaped → COMPRESSION MOULDING / RAM EXTRUSION / ISOSTATIC PRESSING
├─ Gamma or e-beam 5–10 Mrad, no oxygen → CROSS-LINKS + FREE RADICALS
│      └─ Free radicals removed → REMELT / ANNEAL / VITAMIN E
└─ Terminal sterilisation → GAMMA, E-BEAM, ETHYLENE OXIDE, GAS PLASMA
```

| Property | **HXLPE vs conventional** |
|---|---|
| Wear resistance (adhesive + abrasive) | ✓ **↑** (TKA in vitro: wear ↓ **30–80%**) |
| Particle size | **Smaller** (more bioreactive) |
| Particle number | Fewer (process dependent) |
| Tensile strength | **↓** (✗ Wrong options: ↑) |
| Fatigue strength / crack-propagation resistance | **↓** |
| Fracture toughness | **↓** |
| Ductility / elongation to break | **↓** |
| 10-yr outcome | ✓ **↓ wear + ↑ survival** (✗ Wrong options: no difference) |
| Head size effect | None, **22–46 mm** |

**Toughness** — affects mechanical strength, **not** wear rate

**Conventional PE — the false statement** → "conventional generates smaller particles" is false (conventional = **larger**) · true: higher head penetration, more adhesive wear, ↑ toughness, ↑ tensile strength

| Free-radical step | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Remelting** (above **135 °C**) | Radicals **virtually eliminated** · **↓ crystallinity** → ↓ mechanical properties |
| **Annealing** (just below melt) | Less effective on radicals · crystallinity preserved |
| **Vitamin E** (α-tocopherol) soak / blend — 2nd gen | Scavenges radicals without remelting · ↑ oxidation + delamination resistance |
| Sequential irradiation + annealing ×3 — 2nd gen | ↓ radicals, good mechanics |
| ✗ Wrong option: **ethylene oxide / gas plasma** | Terminal **sterilisation** only |
| ✗ Wrong option: **electron beam / gamma irradiation** | **Creates** cross-links + free radicals |

**HXLPE insert — false** → ✓ **"sterilized by ether"** (TO) (✓ true: compression moulding, vitamin E, less delamination)

**Gamma in air** (historic) → oxidation → early failure

**Young active THA patient asks about longevity** → ✓ **HXLPE liners successful at 15 yr** in her age group\
✗ Wrong options: dual mobility for longevity (it is for stability) · 40 mm head · liner <3 mm (use **≥5–6 mm**) · vertical cup

### Stem design, stress shielding & fatigue failure (TO)

**Distal femoral osteolysis prevention** → ✓ **proximal circumferential porous coating** (proximal **33–40%** of stem; seals interface)\
✗ Wrong options: long stem · collared · fully cemented · diaphyseal fill · extended offset

| Coating | Osteolysis site |
|---|---|
| **Fully (extensively) coated** | ✓ **Proximal** (often no loosening) |
| **Proximal circumferential** | **Proximal** |
| **Patch-coated** | **Distal** — fluid reaches diaphysis |

**Stress shielding** — stiff stem carries load → proximal bone resorption (Wolff) · ✓ **cemented < uncemented** · **fully (HA) coated > proximally coated** · extensively coated: **25–30%** show shielding, calcar rounding, distal **spot welds**\
✗ Wrong options: fully coated ↓ shielding

**Stem material to ↓ stress shielding** → ✓ **titanium** (low modulus, highest fatigue strength) · CoCrMo **~10×** stiffer than femur · alumina **~19×** · flexible, **short, hollow** stems ↓ shielding

**Bending cantilever fatigue → stem fracture** → ✓ **proximal loosening of a cemented stem** with distal stem fixed (TO)

### Cemented component loosening (TO)

**Where loosening starts** — cemented **cup → cement–bone** · cemented **stem → cement–prosthesis** (mechanical) · site unspecified → ✓ **any site** (TO)

**Stem technique errors** — soft cancellous bone left on medial neck · thin / incomplete mantle · **removing all trabecular bone** · too little cement · no pressurisation · **stem motion while curing** · non-neutral stem · voids \
✗ Wrong options: **H₂O₂ canal preparation** (not a cause)

**Cup technique errors** — poor superior/posterior support · cartilage / soft tissue left, too few anchor holes · no pressurisation · cement not around entire cup · movement while curing · **undersized cup** in a large mantle · **malposition** (horizontal, not anteverted) \
✗ Wrong options: **serial reaming + removing all cartilage** (correct technique)

**Causes of aseptic loosening (THR)** — wear debris (major) · lack of primary osseointegration · insufficient press-fit \
✗ Wrong options: **decreased BMD** (TO)

### Clinical osteolysis & polyethylene failure

**Aseptic loosening TKA — ESR 3, CRP 0.1, aspirate 157 WBC, 18% PMN** → ✓ **one-stage revision**\
✗ Wrong options: DAIR + PE exchange · spacer · two-stage · antibiotics

**Osteolysis** — revise if **painful** · asymptomatic → monitor

**TKA 16 yr, distal femoral lysis** → ✓ **macrophage activation by PE particles**\
✗ Wrong options: PE toughness · more with HXLPE · infection · immunosuppression

**TKA 18 yr, no joint space, metal debris, well-fixed** → ✓ **polyethylene failure** (metal-on-metal articulation)\
✗ Wrong options: loose components · particle osteolysis

### Knee backside wear (TO)

**Backside wear** — micromotion tray ↔ insert → osteolysis **under the tibial tray + around screw holes**

**Cemented TKA, lysis under baseplate, no articular wear, no stem** → ✓ **backside fretting PE debris**\
✗ Wrong options: stress shielding (no stem) · articular PE or metal debris · cement debris

**↓ Backside wear** — **polished tray** · **robust locking mechanism** (most important — no micromotion) · **monoblock** · not **rough tray** (↑ wear) · obesity ↑ · modularity ↑

**Least backside wear, same insert** → ✓ **cruciate-retaining** (flat, less conforming, no cam-post) · most → **highly constrained** · PS intermediate

**Cementless tibia with patch porous coating + smooth tracks** → ✓ **↑ metaphyseal osteolysis** · continuous porous coating seals metaphysis\
✗ Wrong options: early loosening · hypersensitivity · fracture · instability

![Patch (A) vs continuous (B) porous coating of tibial tray](../../MCQ/attachments/q218124-illa-screenshot-2023-08-30-at-12-14-16-pm.jpg)

## 6 Complications

### Metal-on-metal debris & lymphocyte response

**MoM particles** — **nanometre**, ✓ **smaller than MoXLPE** · ↓ volumetric wear vs MoP · **↑ serum ions** · wear ↓ after **year 1** (steady state) · volumetric wear > metal-on-ceramic · no ↑ sarcoma\
✗ Wrong options: lower than MoXLPE

**Response** — ✓ **lymphocyte (T cell)** · particles + native proteins → **haptens** → **type IV delayed hypersensitivity** · perivascular lymphocytes + **ALVAL** (aseptic lymphocyte-dominated vasculitis-associated lesion) = pseudotumour histology

**Lymphocyte-driven soft-tissue reaction, well-positioned THA** → ✓ **metal-on-metal**\
✗ Wrong options: MoP, CoP (macrophage) · CoC (lowest wear)

**MoM failure** — **2–3×** higher than MoP · **4–5%** aseptic loosening at 6 yr

**↑ serum metal ions (MoM resurfacing)** → ✓ **smaller implant diameter** · **cup abduction >55°** (edge loading)\
✗ Wrong options: smaller abduction · anteversion 10–20° · function scores · preop OA severity

**MOMHRA pseudotumour mechanism** → ✓ **edge loading + loss of fluid-film lubrication** (malpositioned cup) · incidence **<1%**, usually asymptomatic\
✗ Wrong options: lymphocyte reactivity to nickel (no difference) · third body · P. acnes

**ALTR fluid** — watery, **yellow / grey**, **basic pH**, **low cell count**

**Titanium as bearing surface** — high failure: poor wear resistance + **notch sensitivity**

### Taper corrosion (MACC) & trunnionosis (TO)

**Trunnionosis** — wear at the femoral **head–neck** interface · risk: **modularity**, **large CoCr heads** (↑ torque), **taper design**, mixed alloys, MoM · **small head** is not a risk

**MACC** — mechanical abrasion + chemical corrosion at head–neck → ions + debris → **ALTR** · ✓ **also in MoP THR** (≥1 CoCr component) · **Co ↑ > Cr** · **Co >1 ppb ×2** diagnostic · **MRI** for pseudotumour\
✗ Wrong options: only MoM · Co >0.5 ppb · more with small heads

### Ceramic damage (stripe wear)

**Stripe wear** — CoC · ✓ **lift-off separation** of head (edge loading in gait / deep flexion)\
✗ Wrong options: "unique to metal-on-poly"\
✗ Wrong options: third body · neck–socket impingement · insertion · infection

**"Lead pencil" metal marks on ceramic head** → ✓ **recurrent dislocations** (metal transferred from shell) → ↑ head roughness + PE wear\
✗ Wrong options: infection · LLD · ceramic fracture · ALVAL

**CoC debris** — results from malposition / instability rather than causing failure

![Stripe wear on ceramic head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob5001-stripe-wear.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **PE osteolysis** | **ALVAL / pseudotumour** | **Stress shielding** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bearing | MoP, CoP | MoM, MACC (CoCr head) | Stiff stem |
| Cell | **Macrophage** | **Lymphocyte** | — (Wolff's law) |
| Particle | Submicron PE | Nanometre metal + ions | — |
| Labs | ESR/CRP normal · NTx ↑ | **Co, Cr ↑** | — |
| Imaging | Expansile lysis, scalloping · CT | MRI | Proximal bone loss, calcar rounding |

| | **22 mm head** | **28 mm head** | **32 mm head** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linear wear | **Highest** | Intermediate | Lowest |
| Volumetric wear | Lowest | Intermediate | **Highest** |
| Failure mode | Wear-through | — | Osteolysis |
| Stability / ROM | Lowest | Better | Best |

| | **Hip PE** | **Knee PE** |
|---|---|---|
| Wear mechanism | **Adhesion + abrasion** (surface) | **Fatigue + delamination** (subsurface) |
| Backside wear | Shell–liner | Tray–insert → lysis under tray |

## How it's asked

**"VEGF and RANKL in UHMWPE osteolysis?"** → **both increased**

**"Pelvic lysis at cup screws 10 yr after THA?"** → **PE particles tracking through the effective joint space**

**"Particle size implicated in osteolysis?"** → **<1 µm (submicron)**

**"Distal femoral lysis 16 yr after TKA — pathogenesis?"** → **macrophage activation by PE particles**

**"OPG binds to?"** → **RANK-ligand**\
✗ Wrong options: RANK

**"Marker of type I collagen breakdown / bone turnover?"** → **increased urinary N-telopeptide**

**"Molecule associated with macrophage-induced osteolysis?"** → **IL-1**\
✗ Wrong options: BMP-7 · IL-10 · SOX-9 · OPG

**"Promoter + inhibitor pairs — EXCEPT?"** → **IL-10, anakinra**

**"Severity of response to PE debris proportional to?"** → **particle number**

**"Supra-acetabular lysis 20 yr after THA — process?"** → **macrophage activation by particulate debris**\
✗ Wrong options: lymphocytes (MoM) · trunnion wear · stress shielding

**"Osteolysis 10 yr after THA — true?"** → **RANK activity increased**\
✗ Wrong options: X-ray accurate · NTx ↓ · TNF-α ↓ · ESR/CRP ↑

**"Osteolysis 16 yr after THA — true?"** → **TNF-α released by macrophages**

**"Cytokine that inhibits osteolysis?"** → **interferon-γ**

**"Well-fixed THA with peri-acetabular lysis — cause?"** → **macrophage activation by 0.1–1 µm particles**

**"HXLPE TKA insert — EXCEPT?"** → **sterilized by ether** (TO)

✗ Wrong options: larger particles · ↑ ductility · ↑ crack resistance

**"Lysis under cemented tibial baseplate, no articular wear?"** → **backside fretting PE debris**
